# Changelog

Notable changes to Eregion. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) — before 1.0, signals, schemas and interfaces may still change. Milestones and lots: [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md) (French).

## [Unreleased]

## [0.5.12] — 2026-11-13 — "always heard"

### Security
- **`glorfindel restore` says the VM by its name and resource group: its own lines never show the subscription's id** (IML1c, #752; `docs/banc-t1486.md`, step 6). The `Resource :` line showed the VM's full id, the subscription's included (read in the output of `make bench-restore`, 12/11); it now says « <vm> (groupe <rg>) », the vault by its short name, and so do the `glorfindel jobs` hint and the `recovery_complete` line. The signal keeps the full id. The guides lose their « do not paste it » warning. Azure's own error text, the refusal of an ambiguous short name, the decision rendered after the recovery with `--wait` without `--keep-isolated`, and the other commands that printed a full id (`glorfindel release`, `unblock`, `snapshot`, `list`, `door`, `jobs`) are covered by MSK2 (#762).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_entries_calls.py` (the dry run, fire-and-forget, `--wait` with and without `--keep-isolated`: no subscription's id in the output). Mutants M4878–M4879.
- **No output of the CLI meant for a human shows a subscription's id** (MSK2, #762; IML1c's follow-up; `docs/operations.md`, `docs/banc-t1486.md`, step 6, `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`). An ARM id is said by its resource's name and its group (« vm-01 (groupe rg-a) »), a subscription's id left bare (« The subscription '…' could not be found », or the one the environment names) by « <abonnement> » (`glorfindel/masking.py`), at the display only. This holds for Azure's own error text at the CLI's boundary (a 403 cites « over scope '/subscriptions/…' ») and for every refusal; for the refusal of an ambiguous short name, which lists the VMs by their name and group (two of one name and one group in two subscriptions told apart by a rank); for the decision rendered by `respond` and by `restore --wait` without `--keep-isolated` (explanation, reasoning, escalation reason, « Glorfindel agent error »); for `glorfindel release` and `unblock` (their `Resource :` line, their result, what stays after a partial lift), `snapshot` (`Resource :`, `Vault :`, its `glorfindel jobs` hint, its verification), `jobs` (its error, its poll error, and its second step, `glorfindel release`, which names the job's own VM by a name that takes it back — never a homonym of another group, whose quarantine it would lift), `list` (its hold of a mode and its `activate` hint too), `door` (its scopes, its unreadable reads; `--json` keeps the ids, asked for), `reset` (what stays), the cards of `glorfindel pending` and `pending --watch` (the reason, masked before it is cut at 120 characters, the model's suggested steps, and its own steps, which name the VM by its short name only when that name comes back to the card's very resource — otherwise, two VMs of that name, a VM no longer known here while another of its name in another group is, a NSG or an account, they ask for the id: the short name would have restored the other VM, `--yes` asking nothing), the rules `pending` proposes (the model's analysis, explanation and KQL query, written with the full id in its prompt; `approve-rule` takes the proposal's id) and the reasons of `glorfindel activate` (`other_subscription` names two subscriptions: the connector's and the VM's are masked even when the environment names neither). The watch's own log lines (`glorfindel watch`, docker logs) are the machine's journal: not masked, Azure's error text keeps its ids there. The model's words are filtered where they are shown, never where they are made: neither `_build_user_message()`, nor `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, nor `few_shot_examples.yaml` changes, and only `glorfindel/cli.py` imports the masking. The signal, the state files and the machine's journals (JSONL) keep the full id, and every command still takes one.
  - **`glorfindel list --ids`**: the VMs' full ids (this terminal only). Without it, a VM is said by its name and group, and the commands it suggests (`release`, `unblock`, `reset`, `activate`) take the short name only when it comes back to that VM; a name two VMs hold, the hint asks for the id (`<id: glorfindel list --ids>`), since every command refuses that name.
  - The guides' reservation of IML1c is lifted for what MSK2 covers; what stays to read again before pasting: the names themselves (VM, group, vault) and, in Azure's message, a principal's object id or a tenant's id, which nothing masks.
  - **The masking** reads an id encoded whole (`%2Fsubscriptions%2F<guid>`), the JSON of an error body (`"subscriptionId": "<guid>"`) and « subscription identifier '<guid>' », with no environment naming the subscription; the « / » before an id stays when a path precedes it (`…/resource/groupe rg`). Known limits, when the environment does not name the subscription (`AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`, mandatory, covers the main one): `"subscriptionId" : "<guid>"` (a separator of 5 characters), `%3D<guid>`, a double encoding (`%252Fsubscriptions%252F`) and a JSON list (`subscriptions: ["<guid>"]`) pass. A step the model wrote, once masked (« glorfindel restore vm (groupe rg) --yes »), no longer copies: the shell refuses it (bash: a syntax error; zsh: a pattern error); nothing runs. The short name is chosen when the output is shown: a command copied and run much later may meet a state or a registry that changed (a VM lifted, a homonym discovered) — run `glorfindel list` again before an old command.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_cli_masking.py` (each path, the journal, the card and the signal keeping the full id; the guard that only `glorfindel/cli.py` imports the masking sees every form of import), `tests/unit/test_entries_calls.py` (the ambiguous short name). Mutants M4950–M4959, M4980–M4989, M4990–M4994, M5000–M5003.
- **No message of the Discord bot nor of the webhook shows a subscription's id** (MSK3, #772; MSK2's follow-up, decided by Jonathan on 12/11: « as the CLI »; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « alerting webhook + bot Discord »). A message posted in a channel is pasted from it in an issue of the public repository: the bot and the webhook now say an ARM id by its resource's name and group (« vm-01 (groupe rg-a) ») and a subscription's id left bare by « <abonnement> » (`glorfindel/masking.py`), at the display only. The commands to copy of the 📋 Command button (`restore … --before`, `revert`, `activate`, and an action taken alone's `release` / `unblock`), and a card's steps that name its VM (written by the code with its id or its short name), name the VM by `<group>/<name>` (`glorfindel restore rg-a/vm-01 --yes --before …`): never its full id, never its name alone — the bot checks on its host, but the command is pasted on another (an operator's terminal, whose discovery may know only a homonym of another group, which a short name would have taken, `--yes` asking nothing). A VM whose id names no group: `<id: glorfindel list --ids>`. The card's embed masks the model's explanation, the reason, the suggested steps, the target (its name, its path, its limit), the evidence and the check of an action taken alone, an identity card's plan (its prose; a command of the plan that would name a subscription is not shown — the console's card holds it: a command is shown whole or not at all, never altered), the rule's name and the footer's run; a bare id of a subscription the card names anywhere, or the environment's, is masked in every text of it. The thread's name, its opening ping and `/pending` say a card filed on a subscription « <abonnement> », never its id; a thread opened before MSK3 and named by a subscription's id is renamed when it is used again (only then: a name an operator chose is kept). What a button says it ran (« ⚡ … → `glorfindel revert vm-01 --yes` », the restore's confirmation), the command's output, its error, the restore's form and its points (a vault's unreadable error names its scope), a verdict's error are masked too. The notices in the VM's thread (a cut's closure, an action's announcement) and the webhook's messages — the fallback when the bot is mute (Discord's endpoint and Slack's format), an escalation and an announcement without a bot (`escalations._notify`, its action, TTP and severity too; `_action_words`, masked first, then cut, and the text kept in the notices' store never cut inside a GUID) — as well.
  - What keeps the full id: the buttons' arguments (they act: `revert <id> --yes`, `restore <id> --yes --before …`), the escalations' store and the notices' (the text as written, masked where it is shown), the signals, the bot's and the watch's journals. The action queue's receipt (« File d'actions (<8 characters>) ») names the request, never a resource. What the model sees is never filtered: neither `_build_user_message()`, nor `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, nor `few_shot_examples.yaml` changes; the guard of `tests/unit/test_cli_masking.py` now allows the CLI and the bot, and the masking of `vm_notices.py` and `escalations.py` inside their renderings only (`_short`, `_shown`; `_action_words`, `_notify`), never at a module's level.
  - **`<group>/<name>` in the CLI** (`_resolve_resource_id`): resolved on the host it runs on, from the active state and the discovery, the group and the name matching whatever their case; unknown there, refused (« inconnue de ce poste — glorfindel list --ids »), never passed to Azure as it is; one group and one name in two subscriptions, refused too. A full ARM id is taken as before, a short name as before (MSK2's own outputs keep it: same host, same registry). **The VM resolved is read back before anything is done, `--yes` too**: `restore`, `release`, `unblock` already said it (« Resource : »), `reset`, `revert` and `activate` now do (« VM : »); `snapshot`, which took its argument as an id, now resolves it too (a card's step `glorfindel snapshot <group>/<name> --yes` acts). Only a VM's own id (`…/virtualMachines/<name>`) is said `<group>/<name>`; a group holding a character the shell reads (« ( ) ») is quoted. A text is rewritten, then masked with the subscriptions learned from it as written (`masking.shown`, in the bot, the notices and both webhook renderings): a bare id of a subscription only a command's id named is masked too. The help of these commands says the three forms.
  - Known limits: the masking's own (MSK2: a few encodings of an id pass when nothing names its subscription); a host that knows, of that group and that name, only the VM of another subscription takes it (two subscriptions, one group, one name) — the line read back says it by its name and group, not by its subscription; and, in Azure's text, a principal's object id or a tenant's id, which nothing masks.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bot_masking.py` (the review's case: the bot's command pasted where only the homonym is known, refused; `<group>/<name>` resolved, unknown, ambiguous, the full id; the VM read back with `--yes` by each command; a step's command; a thread renamed; the cut; each path: the commands, the homonym, the buttons keeping the id, the embed and the store, an executed card's evidence, an identity plan's command held, a card filed on a subscription, a button's run and its error, a restore's confirmation and form, the notices and the store keeping their text, the fallback on both webhooks, an escalation and an announcement by the webhook). Mutants M5020–M5029, M5098–M5104.

### Added
- **The bench in one gesture: the calm and « restore first » verified by the preflight, the clean point taken by the 📸 route, the preflight replayed** (BNC3b, #770; `docs/design/banc-autonome.md` §3 rows 3 and 5, §3.1, §3.2; `docs/banc-t1486.md`). `make bench-preflight` reads the target's activity log over 89 days and the vault's jobs, under the operator's `az login`: the calm (started 10 min ago at least, no Run Command for 10 min) is a ✗ that says the minutes left, never a wait; « restore first » refuses a VM that carries any Run Command since its last restore, whatever its caller, but a session drain of Imladris within [−2 min, +7 min] of a verified isolation of the watch, and a run of the current Orodruin whose technique reads as non-destructive (its journal in the channel, then its report, against the catalogue). A read that fails, a read cut at its bound, a last restore beyond the horizon, a restore in progress: a ✗, never a ✓ (`scripts/bench_history.py`). Once the request is confirmed, `make bench-run` keeps the newest recovery point only when it was taken after the VM's last restore and outside every quarantine over every run (`scripts/bench_h0_reader.py history`, in its disposable, network-less container); otherwise it asks for one by `POST /api/action/snapshot/<vm>` of Imladris's console, through a short port forward it opens itself (host key pinned, no multiplexed master, closed whatever the end), the console's token read in `host.env` after `CONSOLE_GUARD` and handed by the environment only, as a Bearer, without a proxy; under FS1 its receipt is followed (`not_taken`, `untrusted`, `refused`, `failed`, `unknown`, expired stop the run); the vault's job is followed to `Completed`, 60 min at most, before anything goes to Orodruin; then the preflight is replayed just before the attack (`scripts/bench_snapshot.py`). Nothing is read for the point, nor written, before the confirmation. Only a restore proven in place counts as the last restore, as REC1 proves it (measured on the bench): its container names this VM, its job's property bag says « Job Type » « Replace disks » (`glorfindel.recovery_watch.IN_PLACE_TYPES`), and the VM's current OS disk was created during the job — a « Restore disks » or a new VM made by hand counts for nothing, and the ✗ says what to read when `make bench-restore` has just succeeded. The point the 📸 route took is judged again, its quarantines read again. One line of Orodruin's journal answers one Run Command; a Run Command without its own line is destructive. The calm counts a write on the VM and the end of its last restore as a start, and is unreadable when no start reads and the VM's creation does not. A partial history of Imladris (a line or a file unread, a mount missing, no cycle file) is no quarantine absent. The forward is a bounded remote command, closed on SIGTERM too; the console's token is taken out of every child's environment once read. Limits, written: a managed Run Command, a Custom Script extension, an SSH session and a restart by the platform are not seen; the activity log's ingestion delay is a hypothesis to measure; under FS1 the watch's gestures for the queue leave no cycle, caught by the drain they start and by an open quarantine. Verified on fakes; not yet measured on the bench.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_preflight.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_run.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_snapshot.py`.
- **Design: a measure that arrives late is judged** (ING1, #738; `docs/design/mesure-en-retard.md`).
  Founded on the bench on 11/11: after bree's lift, its `Perf` samples arrived in one block, up to
  27 minutes after their time, while `Heartbeat` kept arriving within seconds. Every shipped rule
  selects its lines by `TimeGenerated`, and so does the query window the RulePoller asks for: the
  samples above the threshold from 15:56 to 16:06 were never in the rule's window, and the detection
  came only because the write was still going on after 16:07. The design inventories the eighteen
  shipped rules and the bench's three copies, with their window, their source and what is known of
  its lateness (measured, documented or unknown), and weighs the ways to judge a late line, including
  a catch-up window that would cover every rule unchanged. It recommends judging a line at its
  arrival (`ingestion_time()`, written by the platform), up to a lateness horizon the rule declares
  (`late_s`, two hours proposed), while aggregating on its time, so that a pair of samples or ten
  failures in ten minutes keep their meaning, whatever the order of arrival; an aggregate is judged
  when its last line arrives. Deduplication is split: an event's identity is kept for the whole
  window, so a line is never judged twice, and an aggregate's for the selection window as today, so a
  second burst on the same VM after a lift still makes a card. The correlation looks for logins from
  the time of the failures, so a login delivered late with its brute force is not lost. No query
  more per pass; the server reads thirteen times more lines for the ten-minute rules (twenty-five for
  the five-minute one) and returns as many as before; the model's message keeps its shape, the
  lateness facts kept in a file of their own. If the quarantine holds the guest's telemetry back (a
  hypothesis to measure first), a detection before the lift cannot come from the guest, and a late
  human lift needs a catch-up read with absolute bounds. The lateness itself is shown per VM and per
  table, read over 48 hours of event time, with a card only when entering the state of lines beyond the horizon, or of a sampled table
  gone silent while `Heartbeat` arrives (never for `Syslog`, which follows events), and as a fact of
  the isolation record. Twelve hypotheses to measure, most of them read-only on data kept until early
  December; the code lots ING2 to ING7; ten questions for the operator. Linked from
  `docs/glorfindel.md`. No code yet.
- **The bench protocol that measures how late each source arrives** (ING5, #758; `docs/banc-retard.md`).
  The design of ING1 rests on twelve hypotheses, RH1 to RH12, that nobody has measured: whether the
  quarantine holds the guest's telemetry back, what the agent delivers of a 37-hour quarantine, how
  long the catch-up after a lift lasts, the normal lateness of `Perf`, `Syslog`, the Activity log,
  Entra and `StorageBlobLogs`, the silence of `Syslog` and the period of `Heartbeat`, whether the
  agent dates a line on its arrival, and whether the platform fills the arrival time. The page gives
  each its exact Log Analytics query, read-only, on the data the bench workspace keeps for 30 days,
  each with its window in absolute dates and its deadline, in the order the data leaves: the anchors
  of every isolation and lift since 2 November first (an unknown one is excluded from the normal week), then RH5, RH3, RH4 and RH6, the first deadline on 8 December.
  The isolation record's DH2 and DH11 join the same sitting, DH11 read from the backup job's own tasks. Each hypothesis says what confirms it,
  what refutes it, what changes if it falls, and where its result is reported, in figures only: no
  query returns an identifier. RH9 is a non-destructive gesture (three log lines dated by hand on
  gondolin); RH10 waits for ING3. Nothing has been run yet. Linked from the design (§10, §11), the
  isolation record (§9.1), `docs/glorfindel.md` and the bench guide.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_banc_retard.py`.
- **A disruptive action Glorfindel takes alone makes a card to judge, and everything is said in the VM's Discord thread when the bot is configured** (JUG1, #760; `docs/observation.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`, « alerting webhook + bot Discord »). On the bench on 12/11, gondolin's autonomous isolation (the T1486, `non_disruptive`) was verified and left no card: TO JUDGE stayed empty, the indicator of the disruptive actions taken wrongly could not count it, and its announcement — and the release's — went by the webhook into the channel, while the bot's cards and the closure of the session cut were in the VM's thread. Now an `isolate_vm` or a `block_suspicious_ip` run alone (`non_disruptive`, `full_auto`) and verified makes a card « exécutée » (`autonomous_action`, `glorfindel/agent.py`, `_record_executed`): the action, the VM, the evidence (a few values of the detection's row), the check read back on Azure and the mode. It enters TO JUDGE, is judged like any other (Right, Wrong, Unsure) and counts among the disruptive actions of `glorfindel report` and of the overview's indicator.
  - **One card per action**, by its key (`escalations.executed_key`: the cycle's signal, the VM, the action, the address), even after a restart of the watch, the card judged or acknowledged since or not; a snapshot or a release makes none; a second action, on the same VM too, has its own card, never folded into another's: each is judged and counted.
  - **It changes nothing of the action nor of the autonomy**: its type is not approvable (no one-click execute, no proposal signed, no Revert or Restore button in Discord); its steps and its command name the usual way back (`glorfindel release`, `glorfindel unblock`). Discord titles it « ✅ VM isolée du réseau — exécutée seule, à juger », with its mode, its check and its evidence, escaped (SEC6).
  - **Where it is said**: the announcement of every autonomous action goes as a notice (`glorfindel/vm_notices.py`, DRN2's form), under the action's key — posted by the bot in the VM's thread, beside the card, when the bot is configured, nothing by the webhook; without the bot, by the webhook as before, the card to judge named, and the card's own notification quiet: one message in the channel. An autonomous action always alerts somewhere: the notices' store refused (a full disk), the webhook still says it — without the bot, or with the bot when its card could not be written either (the bot would have nothing to post); with the bot and the card written, the bot says it in the thread, nothing in the channel. A notice decides nothing: forged under an action's key, it silences the announcement, never the card — without the bot, the announcement is the channel's only message, so a forged notice silences it: a known limit, bounded to whoever writes `~/.glorfindel` and guesses the cycle's signal id.
  - **The ping** (Jonathan, 12/11): the card of an action taken alone pings the role (`DISCORD_PING_ROLE`) in the VM's thread, new or already open, its allowed mentions the role's alone; one ping per action — the announcement and the cut's closure never ping, even when the announcement, read first, opens the thread, and the card is posted once, after a restart of the bot too. Any other card as before: the role pinged when its post opens the thread. Known limits: a bot stopped between the card's post and `bot_posted.json` posts it again at its restart, as every card, and this one with its ping; and, rarely, the card refused (its store in error), the notices' store written: the announcement goes to the thread without a ping.
  - With the bot configured but its container stopped, its card and its announcement wait for the bot (announcements older than 24 h are dropped; the card stays pending in the console) — lifted by JUG2 (#765): the bot mute, the webhook says the action.
  - `glorfindel restore <vm> --yes` without `--before` never takes its moment from the card of an action taken alone (`restore_plan.card_of`): a VM isolated alone, without another card, is still refused (SEC4c: no card, no moment).
  - **The console**: a line of TO JUDGE opens its card (its details, its verdict, a note); a tile that folds several cards (the six cards of an NSG, « ×6 », on 12/11) shows each of them in its details, every one with its own reason, explanation, plan (an identity card's, with its Verify) or steps, proposed query and way to its verdict — before, the first one's only. An action taken alone is never grouped with a card of another type (a held isolation): a group's Ack never acknowledges it unread. `/api/to-judge` carries the card's steps and what an action taken alone did. Everything from outside still goes through `_attr` (`tests/unit/test_console_escaping.py`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_judge_card.py` (the real graph on the in-memory Azure, a fake webhook and a fake Discord thread: the isolation and the block, the verdict in the report, a restart, a second action on the same VM, a snapshot and a release, a mode hold, a check that fails, a card refused, nothing approvable, the bot's card, the webhook without the bot, the thread with it, a forged notice, a notices' store refused, a full disk with the bot, the card's ping in a thread new or open, once per action, a restore without a moment, the escaping), `tests/unit/test_judge_console.py`. Mutants M4910–M4929 and M4940–M4946 in `scripts/mutants/JUG1.py`.
- **The Discord bot silent, the webhook announces an action Glorfindel takes alone — once, the role pinged** (JUG2, #765; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « alerting webhook + bot Discord »). Since JUG1, with the bot configured, an autonomous action is announced in its VM's thread and nothing goes by the webhook; with the bot's container stopped, nothing said it any more — its card and its announcement waited for the bot, and an announcement older than 24 h was lost. Now the bot writes a heartbeat at each turn of its loop (`~/.glorfindel/bot_heartbeat.json`, `glorfindel/bot_beat.py`, every 5 s once its channel is read) and removes it when it stops, on a SIGTERM too (`docker stop`: the bot is PID 1 of its container, where Python installs no handler — it is now read as a Ctrl-C); a SIGKILL or a host cut leave it, and the fallback then waits for it to age, 3 min and a pass of the watch. The watch reads it.
  - **Before announcing an action taken alone** (`vm_notices.say`): the beat absent, unreadable, dated more than 30 s in the future or **older than 3 min** (`MUTE_AFTER_S` = 180 s, some 36 turns), the bot is mute. With a webhook set, the notice is recorded `via: webhook` with `relayed` (when, why, whether it pinged) and the webhook says it in the channel — on a Discord webhook by Discord's own endpoint (`/api/webhooks/` or `/api/v<n>/webhooks/`, the `/slack` suffix taken off), the bot's message escaped as the bot escapes it (SEC6), the role `DISCORD_PING_ROLE` first and the only mention allowed (`allowed_mentions`); on another webhook (Slack, Teams), its Slack message, no ping. The announcement's text names the card and the run before the explanation, so that no cut (Slack's 800 characters) loses them. The watch now receives `DISCORD_PING_ROLE` (an id, no secret; `docker-compose.yml`).
  - **At the passes after** (`vm_notices.relay_pass`, the watch's housekeeping, about every minute): an announcement left to the bot that the bot has not posted (its key absent from `bot_notices_posted.json`) goes by the webhook the same way when the bot reads mute — the bot dead after the action, before its post — or, whatever its beat says, once it is 10 min old (`CONFIRM_S`). Within 24 h, as the bot's own; the posts of one pass spaced (`RELAY_SPACING_S`, 0.5 s).
  - **A webhook that fails is never counted as said**: only a 2xx answer is a post, and the notice is then noted `relayed.sent`. Refused, unreachable, or rate limited (a 429 waited for once when it asks no longer than 5 s; else the rest of the pass waits for the next one), the notice goes back to the bot: the next pass tries again, or the bot posts it at its return and its card pings — a rare double rather than a silence. A mark never noted sent (the watch stopped mid-send, its note refused) is taken back after 2 min (`RESEND_AFTER_S`).
  - **Once per action when the bot comes back**: the notice is rewritten before the webhook is called, and the bot does not post it (it reads each notice again just before its post). The card to judge is posted at the bot's return in the VM's thread with its buttons, **without a ping** when the webhook pinged for it and the post was noted sent; the webhook pings no one when the bot posted the card already. The bot alive, nothing changes from JUG1.
  - **DRN2's boundary holds**: the beat, and the two stores of the bot the watch reads (`bot_notices_posted.json`, `bot_posted.json`), decide only the announcement's path and its ping — never the card, written before any of them is read, nor any state. A beat forged fresh by a writer of `~/.glorfindel` (the console, the bot) only delays the fallback: 3 min once written, 10 min at most if kept fresh; a beat dated in the future reads mute. A notice forged under an action's key, relayed and noted sent, silences the announcement and can silence the card's ping too — never the card (`docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`).
  - Known limits: without a webhook the announcement still waits for the bot; a post of the bot in flight while the watch relays (a `thread.send` held by Discord's rate limits) says the announcement twice, and the same race on the card can ping twice; a webhook that delivered but whose answer was lost has it said again; a bot that posts an announcement and stops before writing `bot_notices_posted.json`, then read mute, has it said again by the webhook; the notices' store refused (a full disk) with the bot mute, the webhook says the action with its ping but nothing records it, so the card pings again at the bot's return. A session cut's closure (DRN2) falls back too since JUG2b.
  - JUG1's wording made exact: `.envrc.example` (the role also pinged at each action taken alone), and the two known limits of its ping (`docs/glorfindel.md`, `changes/JUG1.md`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bot_fallback.py` (the real graph on the in-memory Azure, a fake webhook, a fake Discord thread: the bot stopped, stale, alive, back; a bot silent after the action; a card or an announcement it posted already; its read before a post; a webhook that answers 429, 404, 500 or raises, then the bot back; a relay tried again at the next pass; a short 429 waited for; a burst of actions; a pass rate limited; a mark never noted sent; a forged notice and the card's ping; a long explanation on Slack; a beat forged fresh, in the future, unreadable; no webhook; a Slack webhook; a full disk; a role that is not an id; the escaping; Discord's endpoint, versioned too; the bot's beat written, removed, and removed by a real SIGTERM). Mutants M4970–M4979 and M4995–M4999 in `scripts/mutants/JUG2.py`.
- **A pair's secret turned in one command, `make pair-rotate PAIR=<watch|console|bot>`, and reminded by Imladris in `#deploy` before it ends** (ROT1, #737; `docs/operations.md`, « Faire tourner un secret »; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Les rappels d'échéance des paires »). Before, a rotation was an `az ad sp credential reset` whose `password` Jonathan pasted into `.envrc` by hand: nothing said which pair serves where nor what to restart, nothing checked the new secret, and nothing reminded him — the console's secret, on its service principal (PAIR1), ends around 08/12. The command, refused in a code session and without a terminal, reads both objects of the pair as PAIR1 does and turns the secret of the one that holds it; says what it will do (the pair, its appId masked, the object, the duration — 90 days by default, Jonathan's decision of 12/11 —, the lines of `.envrc`, the service, the other readers) and asks « [o/N] »; adds a new secret beside the old one (`--append`: nothing is cut); verifies it (a token of the pair, then a read of Azure Resource Manager, retried while Entra propagates it) before anything else; writes it into `.envrc` (a 0600 copy first, the file rewritten beside it and renamed over it, its mode kept, every copy of the old value replaced); recreates the compose service that reads the pair, the new value in its environment; and only then removes the old secret by its key id. It refuses before anything when the loaded environment is not that `.envrc` (a service would be recreated without its id). A failure leaves everything in place, an exception or a Ctrl-C included: before `.envrc`, only the new secret moved, and it is removed; after, `.envrc` is put back from its copy, the service recreated on the old secret, the new one removed; the copy is kept, ignored by git, only when `.envrc` cannot be put back, and that is said. The secret never appears on the screen, in a log or in a process's command line. `make pair-expiry-note PAIR=<pair>` notes the end in place on Imladris without turning anything — once for the console's current secret, which the command did not set.
- **The reminders**: the end date, a date and never a secret, is written on Imladris by the workstation's ssh (`~/.eregion-cd/expiry/<pair>.json`); the CD's timer reads it at each pass and posts in `#deploy`, with Jonathan's mention, at 14, 7 and 1 days before and on the day — the pair, the date, the exact command to type. Once per tier, even after a reboot; a date noted late sends the tier it is in; a new date starts over; a post Discord refuses is tried again at the next pass; without the webhook a tier is never counted sent; the journal gets a line only when something happens (a post, a refusal, a date that does not read, no webhook while a tier is due). `#deploy`'s webhook is already the workshop's only secret on Imladris (Jonathan's decision, 12/11): no new secret, no new timer, no new identity, nothing on Microsoft Graph (`scripts/identity_map.py check`). A rotation made outside the command is not seen: `make azure-pairs-expiry` and the bench's preflight remain the net. The CD's agent changes: `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` from an up-to-date `main`, right after the merge (the agent does not depend on the release).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_pair_rotate.py`, on a fake `az`, a fake `docker`, a fake `ssh` to a fake Imladris running the real agent, a fake `.envrc` and the fake Discord.
  - Known limits: a Ctrl-C at the « [o/N] » question or during the first reads ends in a Python traceback — nothing has been done then, and no secret is in it. On Imladris, if `reminders.json` cannot be written right after a reminder was posted (a full disk), the tier is not recorded and the reminder is posted again, with the mention, at each pass until the disk is freed.

### Changed
- **The bench's report composes itself after the run's cycle and after the lift** (BNC3a, #753 ;
  `docs/banc-t1486.md`, steps 6 and 8 ; `docs/demarrer/mobile.md` ; `docs/operations.md`, « Le banc
  lisible »). On 12 Nov `make bench-run` composed its report 7 s after the detection: the isolation and its
  cycle were not there yet, and the report said « an action held back, or a recommendation », which was
  false. The watch wrote the cycle 5 min 22 s after the detection, the session cut included. Now
  `make bench-run` reads Imladris again every 30 s until the run's cycle is written, 10 min at most, then
  reads Azure. `make bench-restore`, once the restore is done, follows the lift by Imladris the same way,
  30 min at most, then composes the run's report alone: the run of `RUN=`, or the one the moment came
  from. A lift held by the watch's recovery also ends the wait: its `release_hold` card still pending,
  except `stopped`, which the watch reads again. The report gives that card's kind and time, never its
  reason. Only the lift and the cards written after this restore started count (`--since`): a restore
  relaunched on the same run is never stopped by the first one's card. No read of Imladris runs past the
  bound. Past the bound, or on Ctrl-C, the report is composed anyway and says what is not yet written;
  a Ctrl-C before anything is read says so, never a Python trace. A report composed
  before the cycle says « not yet written », never an action held back. Everything is read-only, under
  the operator's environment (never the console's pair). `bench-restore-go` now gets Imladris's ssh
  command too.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_report.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_run.py`,
    `tests/unit/test_bench_restore.py`; mutants M4880–M4889 and M4900–M4904 (`scripts/mutants/BNC3a.py`).
- **The commands of Imladris are all `make imladris-*`** (IML1b, #747; Jonathan's decision of 12/11; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`, `docs/operations.md`). `make help`, the guides, the README of Imladris and the CD's messages show the new names only; the former ones still work, silently, until IML2 (#689) takes them away with the resource: `make glorfindel-host-<x>` is `make imladris-<x>` (plan, up, down, env, push, client-rules, start, stop, logs, status, pull, model, report, campaign-report, containment, fault-rate, ssh, the `cd-*`, watch-key, queue-keys, and the internal known and init), `make mobile-check` is `make imladris-tunnel-check`, and `make mobile-tunnel-<x>` is `make imladris-tunnel-<x>` — a rule without a recipe each, so the same recipe, the same variables and the same guards (`YES=1`, `REF=`, the host key, the refusal of a code session, which now names the new one).
  - **The console of Imladris**: `make imladris-tunnel-install`, `-on`, `-off`, `-remove`, `-check` (the workstation's tunnel, MOB2, unchanged); `make imladris-url` always gives the console's URL with its token, alone on the output, and says on the error output when the workstation's tunnel is not armed (« make imladris-tunnel-on », and `imladris-tunnel-install` when it is not set); `make imladris-tunnel` is the foreground tunnel of before (`glorfindel-host-tunnel`), held by the terminal until Ctrl+C, listed as the fallback — a workstation without a user systemd (macOS), or a look without arming the 12 h.
  - **`make console-url` sees the workstation's tunnel.** While it holds 127.0.0.1:7007, the console there is Imladris's, not the local one: what `console-url` then gives is IML1c's. The phone's guide uses `make imladris-url`.
  - **The CD's agent changes** (`eregion_cd.py`, `install.sh`, its units): its messages in `#deploy` and its journal give `make imladris-*` and name the host Imladris (« ✅ Imladris tourne sur … », « ⛔ … refusée : Imladris reste sur … »). Nothing of the checks, the verification or the deployment changes. The release that carries it is deployed by the agent of before, which still speaks under the former names: `#deploy` asks for the update as `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` (it still works), at every deployment until the agent is reinstalled; run `make imladris-cd-install`, from an up-to-date `main`.
  - **The guard of the name** (`scripts/host_name.py`, IML1) now reads the CD, and refuses a former command in a live text: « make », blanks (a line's end too), then `glorfindel-host-` or `mobile-`. Not read: the history (`CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/historique.md`, the fragments of `changes/`), the workflows, the tests, the mutants and the check itself; kept, each with its reason: a published release's line in `ROADMAP.md` (and the line of `CLAUDE.md` that cited `make glorfindel-host-start REF=…`, until IML1c changed it).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_name.py` (each former name, by `make -n` with every binary that could reach Azure, Imladris or a Terraform state refusing, runs the very recipe of its new one; `make help`; the guard), `tests/unit/test_mobile.py` (`imladris-url`, `console-url`, the refusal of a code session under the former names). Mutants M4840–M4849.
- **`make console-url` gives Imladris's console while the workstation's tunnel holds 127.0.0.1:7007** (IML1c, #752; Jonathan's decision of 12/11, back on IML1b's exit 1; `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`, `docs/operations.md`, `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`). The tunnel's unit active and the port 7007: its URL, as `make imladris-url` gives it (the token of `host.env`), exit 0, and a line on the error output, « le tunnel d'Imladris tient 7007 : c'est sa console ». Otherwise the local console's, as before; the refusal in an agent session stays, first. The console's token page names both commands.
  - **The follow-ups of IML1b's review**: `make imladris-url`, the tunnel not armed, also names the fallback (« ou make imladris-tunnel au premier plan »: a workstation without a user systemd, or the foreground tunnel already running); `deploy.sh`'s refusal during a deployment says « le CD d'Imladris » (one of the CD agent's files: the same `make imladris-cd-install` sets it); the README of Imladris says that `#deploy` asks for the agent's update under its former name, `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` (it still works), at every deployment until the agent is reinstalled; `docs/design/banc-autonome.md` no longer cites a removed target.
  - **The guard of the name** (`scripts/host_name.py`) sees a former command after options and variables (`make YES=1 glorfindel-host-up`, `make -C . -s glorfindel-host-…`) and in an argv (`["make", "glorfindel-host-…"]`); a word that is neither never stands between. `CLAUDE.md` is no longer kept: its line cites `make imladris-start REF=…` (Jonathan's agreement, 12/11), and its test count is the current one (~10 700).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mobile.py` (`console-url` under the tunnel, set and not armed, without `host.env`, on another port; `imladris-url`'s fallback), `tests/unit/test_host_name.py`, `tests/unit/test_host_deploy.py`, `tests/unit/test_console.py`. Mutants M4870–M4877.
- **The bot mute, a session cut's closure falls back on the webhook too — every one, a drained cut too, never pinging** (JUG2b, #768, decided by Jonathan on 12/11; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Sa clôture, une fois » and « alerting webhook + bot Discord »). Since JUG2 only the announcement of an action taken alone fell back; a closure (DRN2: drained, refused, sessions left open, never ended, no longer followed) still waited for the bot, and was lost after 24 h. It now takes the same path (`vm_notices.RELAY_KINDS`): the same heartbeat, the same passes, a webhook that fails gives it back to the bot, once per closure, nothing posted twice at the bot's return. No ping: only an action taken alone pings (JUG1) — `relayed.ping` false, `allowed_mentions` empty. Its line says the bot is silent and that the VM's isolation and its cards are in the console. It is never posted from the reassertion: said under its VM's lock, it is recorded for the bot, and the next pass of the fallback says it, in its own thread, at the next pass, about a minute later (two when a pass still runs) — a webhook slow or unreachable holds neither that lock (an isolation of the same VM) nor the reassertion of the next VMs. It decides nothing: the cut's outcome is the one read on Azure, written in the isolation's state (DRN2). The known limit « a session cut's closure still waits for the bot » (JUG2) is gone.

- **The phone's guide says what the bench of 11/11 measured, and what is still to measure** (MOB3, #733; `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`, `docs/operations.md`, the threat model's frontier 9). Played with Jonathan from 12:20 to 13:50 UTC, on an iPhone:
  - Termius on iOS, measured: the key under *Vaults* (the `<` arrow), *Keychain*, **+**, **New Face ID Key** — an `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` key, Face ID shown at the connection; its public key by *Export*, then « Send by email » or « Copy public key »; « Export to host » does not work, the workstation refusing the password (as meant); the console's forward by *Port Forwarding*, then *Local*. Blink is « Blink Shell, Build & Code » on the App Store, and paid; not played, still not verified;

The `/run/sshd` gesture stays (BNC1b);

### Fixed
- **The vault's jobs of the day are read** (BNC3a, #753). `az backup job list --end-date` with today's
  date leaves today's jobs out (measured on 12 Nov). The report then showed the previous night's backup
  as the restore point, and missed the morning's clean point and the restore job. The bound is now the
  next day.
- **The console's live feed says the day of a line that is not today's, and puts the latest on top, each line in its place by its full moment** (CON-D, #735; `docs/design/console.md`). On the bench on 11/11, a « snapshot » line of the day before was taken for one of that afternoon and sent an incident's investigation down a false trail: the feed said « 16:15:45 UTC », never the day. A line that is not of today, in UTC, now says « yesterday 16:15:45 » or its date (« 10/11 16:15:45 », its year when it is not this one); a line of today keeps its time alone; the full date and time on hover. Past midnight UTC, the page's lines say their day again. The server reads each line's moment in UTC (`_feed_when` in `eregion_console/api.py`: `at`, `day`, `time`; a time with no offset read as UTC, a timestamp that does not read goes to the bottom) and orders by it: the history, sorted until now on the text of the timestamp, and each turn of the live feed, until now file by file. The page puts each line above the first one not newer than it: a late line (a signal pulled from Orodruin) goes below the newer ones. Why the feed of 11/11 read 16:15, 21:05, 15:54, 16:22, 18:36 (oldest on top) is a hypothesis, which nothing kept from the bench shows: a right order over two days, 16:15 and 21:05 the day before — the operator's own actions are shown whatever their age, and a run's file recent by its mtime can hold older lines. Whatever it was, their date now says it.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_console_feed_day.py` (around midnight UTC, offsets, the order of the history and of a turn; the page read in its text). Mutants M4820–M4823 in `scripts/mutants/CON-D.py`.
- **A session cut announced pending is closed, once: its outcome and when it was read so, in the VM's Discord thread when the bot is configured, else by the webhook, and on the console's card** (DRN2, #754; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La coupure suivie sur Azure »). On the bench on 12/11 the cut of gondolin's isolation ran 304 s on Azure: the autonomous isolation's notification said « coupure en attente … suivie », the reassertion read it drained at its next pass, 23 s after its end — and no message said so; the Discord card stayed on « en attente ». Every cut the watch announced pending now gets a closure (`glorfindel/vm_notices.py`, called by `glorfindel/drain_watch.py`): cut (« Coupure des sessions aboutie sur …, relue N s après la demande »), refused, sessions left open, never ended, or no longer followed (its VM released, or isolated again, before its end). The bot posts it in the thread where the VM's cards are, reopened or created without pinging the role; without the bot, the webhook carries it; a failed outcome keeps its `verification_failed` card, which the closure names. The console's ISOLATED badge says the cut: « cut pending », then « sessions cut · N s », « ⚠ cut refused », « ⚠ sessions open » or « ⚠ cut never ended », what the outcome says on hover (`/api/state`, `drain_view`, from the isolation's state alone; a cut that ended within its first wait adds nothing).
  - **Once per cut, by its nonce** (the VM and `asked_at`), even after a restart: the notice is recorded in `~/.glorfindel/vm_notices.jsonl` before it goes anywhere, and the same key says nothing again; the bot keeps the keys it posted (`bot_notices_posted.json`) and posts no notice older than 24 h. Its card, then the state — the outcome read on Azure, and a mark `drain_closure_due` —, then the closure, then the mark taken off (L1A): a card refused leaves the cut pending; a closure that cannot be said (a full disk, the watch stopped) is said from the state at the next pass that finds the mark. At most once: a webhook that fails is not retried. A cut asked again by the same watch before its end is closed « reprise avant sa fin ». Rare and said: a closure due, not said yet, of a VM released or isolated again before the next pass is not said (its mark goes with the state); the activity log keeps the outcome.
  - **A notice decides nothing**: the console and the bot write `~/.glorfindel` too, so the watch only reads in the store whether a closure was said, never an outcome — a forged « aboutie » never changes a cut's state, and the console's card is the state's alone. A forger can still silence a closure, have one said twice, or have the bot post an escaped text in a VM's thread — never change the state nor the console's card; open until FS1f (`docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`, `docs/design/file-actions-signee.md` §3.2). A store with bytes that are not UTF-8 stops neither the follow of the cuts nor the bot's turn.
  - The comment of `DRAIN_DEADLINE_S` no longer says the guest agent is held 15 min 45 under the quarantine as a rule: that was after a restore, on 09/11; on 12/11, before any restore, it was read `Ready` under the quarantine once, at 09:36:46, the delay not measured (`docs/azure.md`, L17).
  - JUG1 will put the autonomous actions' announcements in the VM's thread the same way (`vm_notices.say`); until then the isolation's announcement still goes by the webhook, to the channel.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_drain_closure.py` (the bench's sequence, each outcome, a release before the end, a cut asked again before its end, a restart of the watch, a watch stopped between the state and the closure, a card refused, a forged notice, a store unreadable, the store's bound, the bot's thread, its restart, a post that fails, an old notice, the escaping of both chats; a fake webhook and a fake Discord thread), `tests/unit/test_drain_console.py`. Mutants M4890–M4899 and M4905–M4909 in `scripts/mutants/DRN2.py`; M4715 and M4719 (DRN1) follow the code they mutate.
- **The test suite never touches the operator's real `~/.glorfindel`, even on a workstation where it exists and a Glorfindel writes there** (HY2, #769; `docs/operations.md`). On 12/11 two tests wrote there (a store before its redirection; a heartbeat after a `monkeypatch.undo()` that undid the conftest's redirection), and the guard saw a write there only where the folder did not exist when the suite started (CI). Now `HOME` is a throwaway folder from the conftest's import, before any module of the repository (the session refuses to start, and names it, if one was imported first): every path computed from `HOME`, a forgotten redirection's, an undone monkeypatch's, lies under it, and the subprocesses inherit it. The shell's `XDG_*` are dropped; the one link to the real `HOME` is ChromaDB's model (`~/.cache/chroma`, made at its first fetch under FLK1's lock). The process that made the folder removes it (the link, never its target). The guard of the real folder: every write the test process makes there fails the run and names the file and the test (never fooled by the workstation's own Glorfindel); its photograph (names, sizes, dates) before and after fails the run for a file created, changed or removed when no live Glorfindel's heartbeat moved, and only says it when one did. `monkeypatch.undo()` is forbidden in the tests (`far_zone` takes its own `MonkeyPatch.context()`).

The setup: Db--01's primary NIC on the data subnet, web blocked on the app subnet, then Db--01 blocked on its own subnet, its 13th call answered 429. The console's promotion window says both on their own lines; it said a NIC uncovered and dropped the pair gone beside it. The machine's oracle is tightened: it reads the pair as the fake Azure holds it when the block answers, and a pair gone must be said by name (`missing_rules`, or `pair_unread`), `blocked_partial`. It excuses the subnet's other VMs only for what a third party the test sets acting took off (`world_deleted`, a regression's hook), way by way, and nothing else; otherwise « I3: perimeter block lost its own pair … ». A pair said unread (`pair_unread`) is accepted only after a fault that played in the operation. With the pair's listing unread, a NIC added since and left to it (a promotion took its own block off) is unknown (« périmètre illisible »), never « no rule of this block on it » nor put back on a guess (A0); the console's promotion window says the pair unread on its own line.

- **The webhook fallback never holds the watch's loop** (JUG2b, #768; `docs/glorfindel.md`). `relay_pass` ran in the watch's main loop: a webhook that did not answer cost 10 to 25 s per announcement at each pass, delaying the reading of `runs/`, the watch's heartbeat, the reassertion, the TTL and the answer to « Verify ». It now runs in a thread of its own (`cli._relay_notices`, as the reassertion or the door's facts), one pass at a time (the thread not started again while one runs, and `vm_notices._PASS`). Each POST is bounded: 5 s to connect, 5 s to read, and 10 s for the whole of it, the DNS resolution included (`POST_DEADLINE_S`: the POST in a thread its caller waits for, then abandons). A pass stops at the first webhook rate limited, refusing them all (a 401, 403, 404 or 410: a token revoked, a webhook deleted, a Slack channel archived refuse the next ones too), unanswered within the 10 s, unreachable (no answer, or none within 10 s) or that nothing reached — the rest goes back to the bot, or to the next pass — and starts no post after 60 s (`RELAY_PASS_BUDGET_S`). The real bound: the watch's loop, nothing; a pass, its budget and one announcement (0.5 s of spacing, two POSTs of 10 s and a short 429's wait of 5 s), some 86 s, under the 2 min after which a mark is taken back.
  - **The tries of a notice are capped**: a post that may have been delivered — a read timeout, the connection cut once the request was sent, a 5xx — counts a try, and so does a 4xx of the notice's own (a 400, a 413: the pass goes on, and the notice gives way to the next ones in the end) (`tries`, beside `relayed`; a mark taken back counts one too). After 3 (`RELAY_MAX_TRIES`) the webhook says it no more, said once in the watch's log — a webhook that delivered but whose answer was lost no longer pings at every pass for 24 h; the notice waits for the bot, whose card pings at its return. What delivered nothing counts none: a post from which nothing left — the name not resolved, the connection refused or not made in time (`vm_notices._nothing_sent`) — stops the pass without a try, so a network or DNS cut, however long, never silences the fallback; nor does a refusal of the whole webhook (a 401, 403, 404, 410, a 429). **A post unanswered within the 10 s settles its notice itself**: the deadline stops waiting for it, not the post, which may still deliver (an IPv6 route that loses its packets, then the IPv4; a dead resolver first in `resolv.conf`). The pass stops on it (`pending`), no try, its mark kept — the notice is not due again and the next ones go at the next pass; when the post ends, its own thread settles the notice by the usual rule (a late 2xx noted sent; nothing sent — every address of a host a black hole — given back without a try; a read timeout or a 5xx, a try), and only under the mark it was posted with. A post that never ends is the mark's take-back's, after 2 min (a try). Known limits: a post pending past those 2 min, the notice said again, then delivered after all, says it twice with its ping (a try counted, the cap holds); the bot back while a post is pending posts the card with its ping — two pings if the post delivers. A known limit older than this lot: without the bot configured, a cut's closure and the cards still go by the webhook from the reassertion's thread.
  - **The bot's SIGTERM handler is put back** once `run()` returns (`bot._restore_sigterm`): a caller that goes on — a test's worker — no longer keeps it.
  - `post_webhook`'s docstring said a webhook that fails is never tried again: a short 429 is, once.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bot_fallback.py` (the real loop of `glorfindel watch` with a webhook that never answers: its heartbeat written and its reassertion started while the POST waits; one pass at a time; a pass stopped at a webhook unreachable, refusing them all, at its budget; a notice refused for itself giving way; a host whose addresses all drop counting no try; a post delivered past its deadline noted sent by itself, the next ones said at the next pass; one ending in a read timeout counting a try; a late answer after its mark was taken back changing nothing; the bot back while a post is pending; what left nothing told from what may have been delivered; a network cut counting no try; a POST past its deadline; its connect and read bounds; the bound said in the doc; the cap on a read timeout and a 502, none on a 404 or a long 429, a mark taken back; the SIGTERM handler put back; every closure by the next pass without a ping, a closure that never waits for the webhook, the bot alive, a closure left to the bot then relayed, the bot back posting none), `tests/unit/test_bot.py` (the handler put back), `tests/unit/test_drain_closure.py` (the bot alive, the closure waits for it). Mutants M5005–M5014, M5060–M5064 and M5080–M5082 in `scripts/mutants/JUG2b.py`; M4998 re-anchored in `scripts/mutants/JUG2.py`.
- **The bench tool that measures the lift says what the bench of 12/11 read, without the three defects seen there** (LEV2b, #763; `docs/banc-lev2.md`).
  `scripts/bench_lev2.py report` said the disk's refused `grant-access` as « Conflict conflict : un
  échec, il compte », the code lowercased, which read as a comparison by case. There is none: the code
  was `Conflict`, not `OperationNotAllowed`, the only refusal Glorfindel leaves out (REC1b), so
  Glorfindel counts it and the verdict was right; it is the guide that expected it left out. The
  report now says the status and the code as Azure wrote them, and why a `Conflict` under another code
  counts; the guide's gesture 8 says the same, and whether the door should leave this refusal out goes
  to LEV3. The mask no longer rewrites the name of a Microsoft.Resources operation
  (`microsoft.resources/subscriptions/resourcegroups/write` read `microsoft.resources<ressource>`): only
  the shape of an operation is kept, right after its namespace (type words of a closed list, then its
  verb, whatever the case); any other word, a group's or a VM's name, and the path is masked as an id
  (`scripts/bench_shared.py`, every bench report). A resource that is none of the VM's is named by its
  type, its words read in a closed list, and its place (of the VM's group outside the VM, of another
  group, of the VM's subscription, of another subscription), never by its name: on 12/11 it was the disk gesture 15 creates, which the guide now notes. Found
  while fixing: each round reads the same events again, and each copy was judged; a `Started` read one
  round more than its refusal was left over, and the refusal was no longer one. Each event is now
  judged once, as Glorfindel reads it, and a refusal whose operation Glorfindel still keeps is never
  said « écarté ». Recomposed on the reads of 12/11, the report changes in those three lines only.

- **The workstation's commands work as written, under zsh as under bash** (MEN3, #734 ; `docs/operations.md`,
  its head ; `docs/demarrer/banc.md`, `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`,
  `README.md`). Four traps the bench met on 11/11, under Jonathan's login. `az role assignment list --role
  "<name>" --all` fails (« ValueError: No value for given attribute », in `_resolve_role_id`: without a
  scope, az cannot look the role up by its name) — measured without `--assignee`, deduced from az's code
  with it; and `--role` without `--all` nor `--scope` lists the subscription's scope alone, never a VM's (az's help; not measured).
  Each one cited (Restore Staging, steps 2 and 5; SEC11, step 3; SEC17, step 5; removing a role; the
  threat model) now filters the output, `az role assignment list --all --query
  "[?roleDefinitionName=='<name>']"`. `$SUB` was used by blocks that never set it: empty, the roles' `sed`
  wrote « /subscriptions/ » and az answered « InvalidSubscriptionId 'providers' »; each block now reads it
  (`SUB=$(az account show --query id -o tsv)`, the README's too, no longer typed) and runs what uses it
  inside an `if` that checks it is a GUID without printing it — pasted at once, an `if … fi` is read whole
  before it runs, where `return`/`exit` would close the terminal under bash and do nothing under zsh. A `!`
  between double quotes is the shell's history (« event not found »), a backquote a command substitution
  (`` "[?a == `false`]" `` becomes `[?a == ]`): a query carrying either goes between single quotes, or
  escapes each backquote; no query of the documents carries one. A Terraform plan is in colours, so `grep
  '^Plan:'` found nothing: where a document gives the plan's line, the target runs under
  `TF_CLI_ARGS_plan=-no-color` (Imladris' plan through `tee`, then `grep '^Plan:'`).

- **Two texts of JUG2b put right** (JUG2b's review, round 4). `docs/glorfindel.md` no longer says a bounded POST past its deadline is abandoned with its answer never read: the caller stops waiting, the post goes on, and a notice of the fallback is settled at its end. The docstring of `escalations.notify_action` names `pending`, which `vm_notices.say` may now answer (relayed by the webhook, its post unanswered within the deadline).
- **`make azure-pairs-expiry` and the bench's preflight read a pair's secrets on its service principal too** (PAIR1, #736; `docs/operations.md`, « Faire tourner un secret »). On the bench, 12/11, the preflight said « aucun secret lu sur son application » of the console's pair, while its rights, read under that same pair, proved it connected: its secret was on its service principal (none on its application, 26 days left), and `az ad app credential list` reads the application only — the check would never have seen it expire, around 08/12, and the restore from the workstation would have stopped in silence. Each pair is now read on both objects, `az ad app show` then `az ad sp show`, its secrets (`passwordCredentials`) and its certificates (`keyCredentials`), the end dates only. Each date is said with where it lives (« sur son principal de service »); the nearest of the two objects decides; « aucun secret ni certificat » is said only of two objects read and empty, now a ✗; an object az can't read is said so, a ✗, never read as « aucun », and no ✓ stands beside it. An old expired secret is to remove by the command of its own object. Neither a secret, a client id nor a key id is printed. The rotation's doc says the two forms, and a dated note says the console's secret is to rotate before 08/12.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_credential_expiry.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_preflight.py`, on a fake `az`.
- **The bench reader's check and the canary's lookup read a credential on both objects** (ROT1, raised by PAIR1's review; `docs/operations.md`). « No line: no password » was read by `az ad app credential list`, which reads the application only: a password set on the service principal would have passed unseen. Both now read the application and the service principal.

## [0.5.11] — 2026-11-12 — "named and mapped"

### Security
- **The bench's network flow logs go to a storage account of their own, which no identity reachable from a watched VM can write** (INF-FL, #654 ; `docs/operations.md`, « Le compte des flux réseau (INF-FL) » ; `docs/design/corroboration.md`, CR15). The blobs of the VNet flow logs hold one record per connection, the proof SEC-A3f will read that an address really connected to a VM's port 22. They went to the exfiltration exercises' account, where each enrolled VM's managed identity holds `Storage Blob Data Contributor` on the whole account: a local user on that VM, through IMDS, could forge flows or erase the real ones. Celebrimbor now poses `azurerm_storage_account.flows` (`stcelebrimborflows`, or `stcbrflow<hash>` for a named instance), in the same region, Standard LRS, encrypted as before. No role assignment is given to a VM's identity on it or above it. Its shared key is refused (`shared_access_key_enabled = false`, through `flow_logs_shared_key_enabled`, false by default: the way back, in `terraform.tfvars`, if the bench shows that Network Watcher does not write without it), and public containers are refused. Its one assignment is `Storage Blob Data Reader` for the operator who applies, for the bench's measurements. The flow log's `storage_account_id` targets it, an in-place update; traffic analytics (10 minutes, `NTANetAnalytics`) and the 7-day retention are unchanged. The exercises' account keeps what the exercises need: the enrolled VMs' write, its diagnostic setting and `exfil-target`. The azurerm provider no longer reads any account's data plane with its key (`features.storage.data_plane_available = false`): otherwise an account without a key fails every plan. No account of the module has `queue_properties` or `static_website`. Outputs `flow_logs_storage_account` and `flow_logs_storage_account_id`; `make celebrimbor-flows-plan` and `celebrimbor-flows-up [YES=1]` plan and apply the account, its read and the flow log alone. Written, not yet applied: the plan and the apply are Jonathan's. The history of flows stays in the old account, not a proof. Writing without the key (Network Watcher, traffic analytics) is a hypothesis to be measured on the bench.
  - Tests: `infra/terraform/tests/flow_logs.tftest.hcl` (mock providers: the flow log's target, no VM identity on the account or above it, the key refused, the way back), `tests/unit/test_bench_flow_logs.py` (read in the files, the default is the refusal: every role assignment of the module, whatever its principal, is scoped to a resource of a closed list strictly below the group, or named in a closed list with its exact scope and its reason — the operator's read alone today; a group, the subscription, a literal id, a `local`, a `var` or the flows account are refused, so a VM's identity passed through a `local` does not get round it; the Make targets against a fake `terraform`). Mutants M4760–M4764 and M4770 in `scripts/mutants/INF-FL.py`.
- **The workstation's tunnel to the console of Imladris, armed on demand for 12 hours at most, never at boot, never by a code session** (MOB2 ; `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`, `docs/operations.md`, the threat model's frontier 9). Decision of Jonathan (11/11): drive from the iPhone or the iPad without going back to the desk, and never leave the console reachable on the workstation without a gesture. A user unit of systemd, `infra/poste/eregion-console-tunnel.service`, holds what `make glorfindel-host-tunnel` held by hand: `ssh -N -L 127.0.0.1:7007:127.0.0.1:7007` to Imladris, the port bound to 127.0.0.1 alone; the phone still opens its own SSH tunnel to that 127.0.0.1.
  - `make mobile-tunnel-install [DRY_RUN=1]` writes the unit into `~/.config/systemd/user/` and neither enables nor starts it: no `[Install]` section, nothing for `systemctl enable` to do. The address of Imladris is Terraform's at the install; the host key is pinned: `infra/glorfindel-host/known_host.sh` checks it by `HOST_SSH_FINGERPRINT` (required: never accepted at first contact), the one key copied into the tunnel's own known_hosts, the fingerprint named by the unit (`X-Eregion-HostKey`), `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, `GlobalKnownHostsFile=/dev/null`, `UpdateHostKeys=no`; `-F none`, `BatchMode=yes`, `ExitOnForwardFailure=yes`, `ServerAliveInterval=30` × `ServerAliveCountMax=3`, `Restart=on-failure` after 15 s. The unit is checked as `mobile-check` reads it before it is written.
  - `make mobile-tunnel-on` arms it, from the workstation or from the phone in `tmux`; `make mobile-tunnel-off` stops it; `make mobile-tunnel-remove [DRY_RUN=1]` stops it and removes the unit, its known_hosts and its deadline. The gesture writes the deadline (now + 12 h), and the unit alone holds to it: its `ExecCondition` refuses any start or restart without a deadline, past it, or with one more than 12 h ahead (within the window, a `systemctl --user start` by hand does start it, until the deadline); its `ExecStart` runs ssh under `/usr/bin/timeout` for the time left (12 h at most), so a run ends at the deadline (124) and the restart meets the condition; `RuntimeMaxSec=12h` ends any single run. A transient timer of systemd stops the unit at the deadline too (`systemd-run --user --on-active=43200s`, `eregion-console-tunnel-expiry.timer`; without it nothing is armed): a fourth bound the three others do not need. What they do not hold: a process of the same Unix user can write another deadline and start the unit (the guard stops a mistake, not a will); under WSL, a sleep of the host pauses the monotonic clocks, so those 12 h count the time the workstation is awake. `make mobile-tunnel-on` refuses a unit `mobile-check` would refuse, or whose address is no longer Terraform's (Imladris redeployed: `make mobile-tunnel-install` writes the new one).
  - `make mobile-check` reads the tunnel too: the unit the model's (no `[Install]`, no link in a `*.wants/`, no key or section said twice, `RuntimeMaxSec` at most 12 h, the deadline read, ssh under the model's timeout, one forward on 127.0.0.1 alone, nothing more than the model says, the host key pinned, the address of Imladris Terraform's), then `systemctl --user show` — what systemd loaded: its file alone (`FragmentPath`), no drop-in (`DropInPaths`), `RuntimeMaxUSec` at most 12 h, not changed since it was read (`NeedDaemonReload`), nothing that pulls it in (`WantedBy`, `RequiredBy`, `BoundBy`, `UpheldBy`, `TriggeredBy`, `OnFailureOf`, `OnSuccessOf`), else a ✗, armed or not — and `ss -Hltnp`: stopped, « ℹ not armed », never a ✗; failing, restarting, enabled, armed past its deadline, for more than 12 h or without its timer, a ✗; armed, since when, until when, and the unit's processes — its cgroup's, else MainPID and its children, since MainPID is timeout's and its child ssh listens — listening on 127.0.0.1:7007 alone (the port held outside the unit: a ✗; unreadable: a ⚠).
  - `make console-url` gives the console of Imladris's URL with its token while the tunnel runs (the way to it from the phone's terminal); otherwise the local console's, as before (`glorfindel-start` and its neighbours print it), with a line that names `make glorfindel-host-url` or `make mobile-tunnel-on` when the tunnel is set but not armed. It refuses in a code session, the guard alone first, and so do `make glorfindel-host-url` and `make glorfindel-host-tunnel`: the token never reaches a code session. Every `mobile-tunnel-*` target refuses in a code session too (`bench_shared.session_refusal`, as MOB1's), before make expands the address of Imladris.
  - Said in the threat model: while the tunnel is armed, any local process of the workstation — a code session included — and, WSL sharing its loopback with Windows, a process of Windows reach the console; what guards it then is the console's own gate (`_TokenAuthMiddleware`: the `Host` checked, a change from another origin refused, the token required, a `SameSite=Strict` cookie, a change without its origin refused, JSON required). Without the token, a refusal.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mobile.py` (on fakes of `systemctl`, `systemd-run`, `ss`, `terraform`, `ssh-keyscan`; the unit's `ExecCondition` run by `/bin/sh` on deadlines past, future and absent), `tests/unit/test_host_console.py` and `tests/unit/test_console.py` (the targets that print the token run from a copy of the operator's checkout); mutants M4776–M4780, M4784–M4786 and M4787 (`scripts/mutants/MOB2.py`).

### Added
- **Design: the bench that measures itself** (BNC2, #684; `docs/design/banc-autonome.md`). The
  destructive T1486 run stays launched by the operator (`make bench-run`, from the workstation or the
  phone through it), and its restore stays a second human gesture, from the isolation card once FS1g
  and TOT1 exist, from `make bench-restore` until then. What surrounds them is automated: the
  preflight; a calm check that refuses rather than waits; a refusal ("restore first") on a VM that
  carries a destructive run without a restore since, the runs listed from Azure's activity log of
  the VM (every Run Command since its last restore, whatever its caller; only the host's own
  session drain left out, when it starts within minutes of a verified isolation the watch recorded
  on that VM; an unreadable isolation refuses), the technique of the current Orodruin's runs from its journal (an
  unreadable one counting as destructive), and the restores from the vault's jobs; after the typed name, the newest recovery
  point checked clean (taken after the VM's last restore and outside any quarantine) and, when it
  is not, a new one asked through the snapshot button's own route of the host's console (a signed
  request the watch executes under the action queue), followed to its end, then the preflight played again before the attack; the lift by the host;
  and a masked report composed alone on the workstation and then on the host at the end of the
  run, with the rights already there. A dated slot plays a non-destructive drill of DRL1 once, at an
  armed hour, one slot at a time; the weekly drill's Logic App wakes Orodruin for it by reading the
  slot's permit in the exercise channel, its read bounded to that one blob, and let through the
  channel's firewall by a resource instance rule for that Logic App alone, the bypass kept at None. The design questions are
  settled by the operator's decisions of 11/11. Linked from the weekly drill design and the T1486
  bench guide. No code yet.
- **Design: the record of an isolation, the facts read after the action so a human only has to
  judge** (DOS1, #727; `docs/design/dossier-isolation.md`). Founded on the bench night of 10/11: bree
  isolated itself on two 10-second write samples, the first dated 31 s after its nightly backup
  started, while the host's metric read far lower. Every guard was right about what it reads, none
  reads the platform, and under Linux the investigation's backup-agent query always returns nothing.
  The design defines a record for every isolation and every card that proposes one: facts, never a
  verdict, each with its source, its author (the platform; the guest, a `Syslog` line a local user can
  forge, or a true fact he can provoke, such as a `Heartbeat` gap or an extension's state; or
  Glorfindel) and its state (read, empty, not applicable, unreadable, pending; unreadable is never
  empty). The facts cover the platform's context (a vault job, an extension, a reboot, the resource's
  health and a planned maintenance; Service Health stays out of reach without a subscription-wide
  reader, and says so), a second opinion from the host compared minute by minute, the recurrence over
  ten days and against the backups, the accepted connections, the aftermath (Glorfindel's own
  operations, the session cut, the check, the cards) and the decision as it was taken (the model and
  the sampling, which the debug line does not say plainly today, the version, the rule). It is
  composed after the action, in a thread of its own, under the rights Imladris already holds, and
  bounded. It shows on the console's card and in Discord, and never in what the model sees. Whether a
  fact becomes a guard is the operator's question; the design recommends judgment only until the
  host's divergence is measured. Nine questions for the operator, twelve hypotheses to measure, most
  of them read-only on data kept until early December, and the code lots DOS2 to DOS8. Linked from
  `docs/glorfindel.md`. No code yet.
- **The identity map: who holds what, from where, under which identity — Azure, Entra ID, GitHub, Discord, the workstation, the phone, Imladris (formerly H0) and Orodruin** (IDM1, #687 ; `docs/design/identites.md`, `scripts/identity_map.py`). Four mermaid diagrams (the network, the defence's identities, the red team's and the bench's, the workshop), each link saying what crosses it and under which identity. Every role read action by action, as its file grants it, not as the code uses it: Response can rewrite any network interface, NSG and rule of its resource group and attach any public IP there, so it can make a VM more reachable; Restore can rewrite any VM of its group (profile, attached disks, userData, system identity), any disk, and the configuration of any storage account (firewall, shared key, public access), which amounts to replacing a VM's OS disk; Session Drain and Restore Neutralize run any command as root on their VM. A matrix, one line per identity — 38, among them 4 planned (the Stratus identity of R2/R2c, the restore executor of FS1g, the TOTP secret of TOT1, the dedicated Graph identity of 0.6.2) —: where it lives, where its secret lives or that it has none, who uses it, its rights by kind of scope, what it cannot do, its rotation and expiry, the threat model's boundary that holds it, and whether the repository declares it, Eregion's code draws it, or it is made by hand. A register ties each declaration of the repository to its lines: every role of `infra/identities/`, every Terraform identity or right (role assignments of any type that names one, PIM and Cosmos DB included, keyed by their role and the expressions of their scope and of their principal, so that any of them changed is said; role definitions, user-assigned identities, key vault access policies, azapi role assignments, every `azuread_*` resource, every `identity`, `admin_ssh_key` and `access_policy` block, `dynamic` or not, in `.tf` and `.tf.json`), every identity variable of the compose and of the example environments, every secret, `secrets: inherit` and write permission of the workflows (`.yml` and `.yaml`). Each role's actions must be exactly those of its rows in the action tables, role by role (a Terraform definition's joker `*` read); every not-action, Graph permission and directory role must be named in the matrix. `python3 scripts/identity_map.py check` says what is missing, `list` what the repository declares. No real identifier: roles, kinds of scope, generic names. How `make bench-preflight` would compare the live assignments to the map is proposed as a follow-up (§5); what the check cannot see is said (§6). The threat model, `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/operations.md` (« Identités Azure ») point to it.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_identity_map.py` — the map holds every declaration; one added without its line fails (a role file, an assignment, a definition, an Entra ID resource, a user-assigned identity, a key vault access policy, an azapi role assignment, a `.tf.json` assignment, a managed identity on a resource — nested, `dynamic` —, an SSH key, a pair in the compose, a token, a key, a password, a SAS, a connection string, a fingerprint, a certificate, a commented pair, a workflow's secret in either form, `secrets: inherit`, a `.yaml` workflow, a job's OIDC token, a top write permission); an assignment's role or scope changed, an action added to a role file or a Terraform role definition, a Graph permission or a directory role not named in the matrix, a stale row, an unknown line, a « dépôt » line nothing feeds, a planned line already declared, a missing column or boundary, a role not named on its lines fail. Mutants M4765–M4769, M4773–M4775, M4781–M4783.

### Changed
- **The VM that carries Glorfindel is Imladris (formerly H0), in everything people and sessions read** (IML1, #688 ; `scripts/host_name.py`, `docs/operations.md`, `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`). Jonathan's decision of 10/11: the house where Glorfindel lives, beside Eregion, Celebrimbor, Orodruin, Dagorlad and Amon Sûl. The README, `docs/` and its guides, `CLAUDE.md` and the role files, the texts of the console, of the Discord notices and of the CLIs (`annatar`, `glorfindel`, `make bench-*`, `make help`, and what `make glorfindel-host-*` prints: `host_config.py`, `host_env.py`, `host_client_rules.py`), the public manual, the replay and the adoption page say Imladris; each document's first mention says « Imladris (anciennement H0) ». What does not change: the Azure resource, the `make glorfindel-host-*` targets, the `infra/glorfindel-host/` directory, the Terraform, the CD, the scripts deployed on the host, the role definitions, the environment variables (IML2 renames them with the resource), a shipped rule's query (its KQL comment keeps the former name: the rule's WL1 fingerprint covers it, and every client copy would be set aside), the doc's quotations of an output IML2 will rename (a CD message, a workflow's step), and the history (`docs/historique.md`, the CHANGELOG of published versions).
