The lot keeps its name: « le lot H0 », the pre-observation, `lots: [H0]`. `scripts/host_name.py` (and `tests/unit/test_host_name.py`) refuses the former name for the host outside the history: by its place on the line (after « lot », in a list of lots, a table's first cell, a value alone), else by a lot's phrase named one by one, or a text kept in its file with its reason (it must still be there, and a quotation in its source); Terraform, the CD, the host's deployed scripts, the role definitions, the workflows, the tests and the mutants' anchors are not read, each with its reason. `make imladris-<x>` runs `make glorfindel-host-<x>`, the same recipe and the same guards; the targets the bench's guides use are untouched.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_name.py` ; mutants M4710–M4714 and M4771–M4772 in `scripts/mutants/IML1.py`.

### Fixed
- **On the simulated AWS, an ENI whose original nothing holds any more is judged as Azure judges a bare NIC: the oracle was wrong, not the adapter** (AWS1b, #708 ; `tests/unit/test_invariants_aws.py`, `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions_aws.py`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). The night of 10/11 (« Invariants (deep) », macOS, 10 000 examples, seed 894570835) found four failures of I3 on the AWS machine — « release said released, i-web2 is not back to its design » and « verify_release True » — replayed on Linux by the same seed, one sequence: i-web2 on `default` and its own group, isolated, its original on the quarantine group's tag and the state never written (a full disk at the state write); someone takes eni-web2 out onto `default`; the quarantine group's tags are lost. Nothing records its original any more: the re-isolation from `default` notes `default`, the release puts `default` back and says « released » — the AWS analog of a NIC left bare, re-isolated as bare (U2), whose release the Azure machine accepts, the operator putting its group back. The AWS oracle let it through until the re-isolation wrote its state, then read « a state held its original » from that `default`; Azure's reads a record of « none » as nothing. A placement whose original is the VPC's `default` alone, on an ENI someone left there, now records nothing either, but only when the tags of the quarantine noted at its recording were already lost as the state was seen: nothing held the original then (`_holds_an_original`). A first fix excused that `default` whatever that moment, and the review refused it because it weakened I3. A faulty re-isolation in the manner of M606 ignores the tag still there, notes `default` over it and overwrites it; followed by the loss of the tags, it went through, where the base's oracle caught it. Bounded by the moment of the loss, the oracle refuses what the base's refused. A state that held the real original makes the ENI traceable, and so does a `default` noted while the tag still held it, whether its tags are lost afterwards or not; a release that leaves the ENI on `default` then fails I3. Four sequences are frozen: the night's; its counterpart by the state (the state written); its counterpart by the tag (the tags lost after the re-isolation), both of which catch M606; and the oracle facing `default` noted over the tag. No mutant of the adapter: it had no defect. Two mutants of the oracle (M4751, the defect back; M4752, any original naming `default` counts as nothing), and M4753 (the excuse whatever the moment of the loss, the first fix), in `scripts/mutants/AWS1b.py`.
- **The bench's preflight reads the recovery point, says az's error, and no longer takes another project's « watcher » for a local watch** (BNC1b, #724; `docs/banc-t1486.md`, `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`). Measured on the bench on 11/11:
  - the point: `az backup recoverypoint list -c <properties.containerName>` was refused (`BMSUserErrorContainerNameIncorrectFormat`: the three-segment name). `-c` and `-i` are now read from the item's own id: the segment between `/protectionContainers/` and `/protectedItems/` (the full container, `IaasVMContainer;…`) and the last one (the item's name). Measured on the bench, read only: that form answers, and the id's segments are `"IaasVMContainer;" + properties.containerName` and `name`, to the exact case. An id without them is an unreadable ✗ that says so — never a fallback to a form not measured;
  - `first_error` skips az's `WARNING:` and its indented lines, as it skipped ssh's `Warning:`, and says the first `ERROR:`; a warning goes on neither over a blank line nor over a line that says `ERROR`, indented or not; only warnings is said so, never an empty line (it was « point : illisible (RuntimeError: WARNING: ) »);
  - the workstation's check reads a watch of Eregion by its command, `glorfindel watch …` (`docker ps --no-trunc`), whatever its image and its name — compose's service, `make glorfindel-watch` (a name drawn by docker, no compose label), an instance of another name on a rebuilt image —, and still by the compose service `watch` of the image `eregion-glorfindel` and by the name `eregion-watch` / `eregion-<team>-watch`; never by a name that only holds « watch », so another project's « …-watcher » is none. A watch typed inside `make glorfindel-shell` is not seen (the container's command is its shell);
  - the workstation's SSH server (Ubuntu 24.04): `sudo install -d -m 755 /run/sshd` before `sudo sshd -t`, which refuses without it while `ssh.service` has not started (`/run` is a tmpfs, the service's `RuntimeDirectory` creates it).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_preflight.py`; mutants M4800–M4808 (`scripts/mutants/BNC1b.py`).
- **The jobs that install Terraform refresh HashiCorp's apt source alone** (CI11, #728 ; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release-gate.yml`, `canary-azurerm.yml`). On 11/11, Ubuntu's mirrors did not resolve for hours on GitHub's runners (`security.ubuntu.com`, in a window: `main` and the canary were green on the same image just before), and a pull request's `terraform-test` failed twice on `apt-get update -o APT::Update::Error-Mode=any`, its code nothing to do with it. CI's `terraform-test`, the release gate's `terraform` and the azurerm canary now refresh `/etc/apt/sources.list.d/hashicorp.list` alone (`Dir::Etc::sourcelist`, `Dir::Etc::sourceparts=-`): an outage of Ubuntu's mirrors no longer fails them, and `Error-Mode=any` still holds for HashiCorp's index. No image is pinned: the image was not the cause.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (each job that installs Terraform, found by what it runs — HashiCorp's apt repository, or `apt-get install … terraform` — and no other, refreshes that source alone). Mutants M4814–M4815 (`scripts/mutants/CI11.py`).
- **The test suite fetches ChromaDB's embedding model by one process at a time, and only when a test needs it** (FLK1, #723; `docs/operations.md`, « Ce que la CI retente »). In `pytest -n 4` with an empty throwaway HOME, two workers fetched the model into the same `~/.cache/chroma` at once (a real `CycleMemory` in `test_agent_nodes.py`, the campaign report's index in `test_containment.py`); ChromaDB writes the archive in place and removes it when its SHA-256 does not match, so one removed the other's half-written file (`FileNotFoundError` on `onnx.tar.gz`), reproduced at the first full run. In each process of the suite, the conftest now wraps ChromaDB's own check-then-fetch (`ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2._download_model_if_not_exists`) in a file lock beside the model, when ChromaDB is imported (`scripts/chroma_model.py`, `lock_downloads`): a session that never embeds a text fetches nothing; the first process that needs the model fetches it, the others wait, then find it there. The lock is re-entrant within a process; a cache that is not writable (one root still owns) or a ChromaDB without that method is said as a warning, never an error of the suite. `python scripts/chroma_model.py` (CI8) and the mutation harness's preparation (CI6b) take the same lock.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_chroma_model.py` (three workers embedding at once on a fake ChromaDB that writes its files in place, slowly: fetched once; a session that embeds nothing fetches nothing; a second process waits for the lock, by a handshake; a read-only cache; the locked ChromaDB still has the method), `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`. Mutants M4790–M4793, M4810–M4813.
- **The guard of Imladris's name reads `git ls-files -z`** (FLK1, #723; `scripts/host_name.py`). Without `-z`, git quotes a name that is not ASCII, which names no file: its content was skipped, unsaid (the second review of IML1).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_name.py`. Mutant M4794.
- **Seven documents no longer contradict what the bench measured** (MEN2, #722). `docs/banc-t1486.md`
  said nothing of it had been played on the bench: it now says what the 09/11 run showed (the
  detection at 70.3 s, the autonomous isolation, the 302 s session cut, the restore from the
  workstation under the console's principal refused at its trigger, the lift held by REC1's watch),
  what fixed each since (DRN1, RST1, REC1b; BNC1 and MOB1 for the gestures typed by hand), and what
  has not been replayed yet; the version it runs is the published release, no longer « 0.5.8, in
  service ». `docs/operations.md` said the Activity Log and Entra ID settings were neither planned nor
  applied: the Activity Log runs on the bench since 05/11, Entra ID since 06/11 at the latest (AL2,
  AL1c, C2b), the full measure of ID1 still ahead; `docs/azure.md` no longer calls AL1 and C2
  unmeasured. `docs/design/preuves.md` counted about 870 unit tests: more than 10,000, the exact count
  in each CI run. `CLAUDE_INFRA.md` and `ROADMAP.md` said O0's enrollment tags « not applied yet »: they were read on
  Azure on 11/11, on gondolin and on its resource group (`eregion-target` the bench's id on both,
  `eregion-zone` `bench`); the reading says nothing of the legacy `annatar-test` tag, which still
  enrolls, nor of a role assignment set by hand, which Orodruin's contract does not tell apart.
  `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md` said SEC5's Terraform « not applied yet »: Orodruin's `armed`
  contract of 05/11 implies it (deduced from it, not yet read on Azure), the contract refusing a write
  on the whole bench group; SEC5's other resources, by a full apply.
- **The azurerm canary's real hour and the measured form of its OIDC sign-ins** (MEN2, #722, #673;
  `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Les connexions OIDC du canari (CAN3) » ; `docs/glorfindel.md`).
  The canary is scheduled at 06:00 UTC, and GitHub delays it by hours: on 11/11 its last six scheduled
  runs started between 11:57 and 14:27 UTC (`gh run list --workflow canary-azurerm.yml` gives the real
  hour). Measured on the bench on 11/11: `ClientCredentialType` is `federatedIdentityCredential` and
  `FederatedCredentialId` is present; `ServicePrincipalCredentialKeyId` and `…Thumbprint` are filled
  too, constant, and are not the federated credential's id, so they never said a secret or a
  certificate was used — `ClientCredentialType` says it, and whether the canary still has none is
  read on its application and service principal, as counts (`az ad app show`, `az ad sp show`). The
  bench copy of the rule reads neither field.
- **M14 is caught on every run, whatever the seed** (MUT10, #715 ; `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`). The first night of the fragile mutants (MUT7, 11/11, 500 sequences, three seeds) saw M14 (« release does not read the NIC back ») caught on two seeds and survive on 356818481: the invariant machine alone was its target. A0's own frozen sequence, written for it — another host putting the quarantine back during the release, which only the read-back after the write tells — already caught it without being linked to it, and so did the unit test of a release whose write Azure didn't apply, the customer's NSG not back on the NIC: both are added to M14's tests (`TESTS_ALSO` of MUT3, as MUT5, MUT6, MUT8 and MUT9 did); the mutant and its anchor do not change.

## [0.5.10] — 2026-11-10 — "within reach"

### Security
- **`make orodruin-exercise` wants a terminal for the names it has typed** (MOB1, #686): its first
  prerequisite, `orodruin-exercise-guard`, refuses without one before `glorfindel-host-known` and
  `orodruin-known` — nothing read from H0, nor a drill's VMs from Azure, nor Terraform's state; a name
  piped into `eregion_core.exercise_request confirm` is refused too, nothing shown nor written.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_exercise_request.py`, `tests/unit/test_makefile_exercise.py` (now run under
    a pseudo-terminal).

### Added
- **Design: the lift after a restore, alone and on probation, unless the door is proven open** (LEV1, #690;
  `docs/design/levee-probation.md`, linked from `docs/glorfindel.md` and the threat model). A design only,
  nothing coded. One rule for every lift after a restore (the watch, `glorfindel release`, the console, the
  bot; a human's lift reads the restore on Azure itself), with three verdicts, none given before the
  restore job's end plus a margin for the activity log's latency:
  - an exercise proven by the control plane (Orodruin's identity acting on the VM within the run's
    published window, no third party), on the bench or the twin only (the zone noted at isolation, the
    most cautious reading wins), never in production, lifts without probation;
  - a real incident lifts into probation;
  - the quarantine is kept, with a card, when a detection on the VM is dated after the restore's end (a
    human alone lifts then), or when a third party that acted on the VM keeps its rights, a right that
    grants rights included, or its rights cannot be read in full. No right is added to H0: a right
    through a group, a PIM eligibility, a group's ownership or a deleted principal reads as unreadable,
    and the bench measures how often. The activity log is read with its child resources (extensions,
    managed Run Commands, NSG rules, tags). Glorfindel and the restoring principals are excused only for
    their own operations, targets and windows, and expected callers only per operation (never a Run
    Command, VMAccess, an AAD login extension or a disk export); an extension written by a third party
    counts whatever its type. The card carries the revocation plan in IR1a's form and a Verify
    button. A hold placed by the operator is removed only by a traced human gesture.

  The mode still decides who executes. Probation: 24 h by default. The administration ports are closed from
  the Internet by a named Glorfindel rule; its state is written before it, so that the control-plane
  detection, the reassertion, the orphan sweep and `reset` recognize it, and it is laid and read back before
  the quarantine is removed. Probation-only rules and thresholds live in the rules file. A detection in the
  guest dated after the restore's end re-isolates according to the mode, decided by code at the head of
  `decide`, guard by guard,
  without the model (a card in `human_only`); a control-plane detection is always a card, never a
  re-isolation alone (Jonathan's decisions of 10/11). These cycles never go to the memory. The original
  disks are named on every card as evidence, never touched. The model's input stays byte-identical. Also:
  the vocabulary (quarantine, probation), eight invariant properties, the bench exercise (T1486 "as a real
  incident", then T1136.001 during probation), the lots LEV2 to LEV8, and Jonathan's decision on every
  question.
- **The door's facts, for information: who acted on an isolated VM by the control plane, and whether they keep their rights** (LEV2, #690 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La porte, pour information » ; `docs/design/levee-probation.md` §4). Nothing lifts or holds on them yet: the lift, the release precondition and the model's message are unchanged, to the byte.
  - `observed_actors` reads the closed table of gestures (a Run Command, any extension, a write of the VM, its disks, NICs, NSG rules, a public IP joined, an identity, the tags) in the activity log, whatever their status, a platform refusal aside: one exact read per resource and one read per group filtered on the VM's children, each bounded at 20 pages. On a shared NSG only a rule that covers the VM counts. A rule deleted and the VM's subnet written are gestures too (Q16, Jonathan's decision of 10/11): a rule deleted from a shared NSG does not count only when the rules the isolation noted (`nsg_rules_at_isolation`) show it never covered the VM. A user is known by its object id and UPN, never by its token's appid (the client tool's, the same for the whole tenant). A read cut or failed, or an answer not of the shape ARM documents, makes the actors **unreadable, never empty**.
  - `observed_rights` reads the role assignments, PIM eligibilities and role definitions at each scope of a gesture, judged by `action_held` (wildcards, notActions, dataActions, and the rights that give one back): held, absent or unreadable — a group, an eligibility, a management group, a deleted principal, a principal of a type absent or unknown, a role without its permissions are unreadable. Read only, under the Reader H0 already holds: no right added, in ARM nor in Graph.
  - The watch reads them for each isolated VM (`GLORFINDEL_DOOR_FACTS_INTERVAL_S`, 900 s) into `~/.glorfindel/door/`; the isolation card shows them, escaped. `glorfindel door <vm>` reads them on demand, writing nothing.
  - The VM's disks (`disks_at_isolation`) and the rules of each customer NSG the isolation plan reads (`nsg_rules_at_isolation`) are noted at the isolation, from reads it already made; an older state reads back without them.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_door_facts.py`, `tests/unit/test_door_console.py`, `tests/unit/test_recovery_watch.py` (the lift unchanged); the fake Azure gains the full activity log (pages, children, status, late events), assignments, definitions and eligibilities.
- **The bench measure of H7, H9 and H12 prepared for Jonathan** (LEV2, #690 ; `docs/banc-lev2.md`): for each operation of the table, a no-effect write on gondolin and its rollback, then `scripts/bench_lev2.py`, a read-only reader (it refuses in a code session and outside the `bench` zone) whose report says, without an identifier, the resource of each event, whether the exact filter gives the children, the pages, and the latency. H1 and H2 by `glorfindel door` under H0's identity.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_lev2.py`, on sample logs and a fake `az`.
- **The bench driven from the phone, always by commands typed on the workstation** (MOB1, #686;
  `docs/demarrer/mobile.md`, `docs/operations.md`, the threat model's frontier 9). Only the path between
  the phone and the workstation changes: Orodruin still listens to the workstation's SSH alone, nothing
  changes on H0. Two independent barriers, for the terminal as for the console: a private network
  (Tailscale, an access rule that opens only phone → workstation:22), then the workstation's SSH server
  in WSL, by a key alone, kept in the phone's secure chip. The console of H0 reaches the phone through
  the phone's own SSH tunnel to the workstation's 127.0.0.1:7007, where `make glorfindel-host-tunnel`
  holds H0's: the console's `Host` stays `localhost`, its token still required.
  - `make bench-run TARGET=<vm> SCENARIO=<id> [DRY_RUN=1]` (`scripts/bench_run.py`): a terminal on stdin
    first (a name piped is no name typed), the preflight (one ✗ and nothing leaves), a one-wire request
    read by the requests' one reader, the target's name typed — destructive or not — then the launch by
    Orodruin as `make orodruin-exercise` does it (the name typed removed from Orodruin at the end), then
    `make bench-report` for each run named, and the restore's command with its `RUN=`.
  - `make bench-restore TARGET=<vm> [RUN=…] [BEFORE=…] [DRY_RUN=1]` (`scripts/bench_restore.py`): a
    terminal, the console's pair (required), `--before` the run's T0 (its `attack_started`, as
    `make bench-report` wrote it in `runs/bench/`: `RUN=`, else the target's one report — two or more are
    listed and refused) unless `BEFORE=`, always `--keep-isolated` (the lift is H0's), the VM's name
    typed, a dry run, then « [o/N] »; the repository's own `glorfindel`, under the console's pair alone.
  - Both refuse inside an agent session (`EREGION_AGENT_SESSION`, a lot's worktree), the guard alone
    first, before any command towards H0 or Orodruin is expanded; the internal `-go` targets check the
    variables' origin again; `TARGET`, `SCENARIO`, `BEFORE`, `RUN` and `POINT_MAX_H` from the environment
    are refused. A long-lived `tmux` session can be driven by any process of the same user: said in the
    guide and the threat model, with the advice not to keep the pair loaded in it.
  - `make mobile-check` (`scripts/mobile.py`), read-only: the SSH server listening on the private address
    alone; password and keyboard-interactive closed, the key alone, from `.ssh/authorized_keys` alone, no
    `AuthorizedKeysCommand`, no `TrustedUserCAKeys`; `AllowTcpForwarding local`, `PermitOpen
    127.0.0.1:7007` alone, `GatewayPorts no`, no socket, agent nor X11 forwarding; the console's port on
    127.0.0.1 alone; Tailscale SSH off (unread: a ✗); no address printed. These bound sshd's own
    forwarding only: with a shell, the phone relays as the operator does.
  - The workstation's models, applied by the operator: `infra/poste/sshd_config.d/00-eregion.conf`,
    `infra/poste/ssh.service.d/eregion.conf`, `infra/poste/tailscale-acl.hujson`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_run.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_restore.py`, `tests/unit/test_mobile.py`;
    mutants M4696–M4705, M4737–M4744 and M4756–M4758 (`scripts/mutants/MOB1.py`).
- **The mutants only an invariant machine kills, measured each night before the release gate finds them** (MUT7, #699 ; `scripts/mutation_night.py`, `.github/workflows/mutants-night.yml`, `docs/operations.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). On 10/11 the 0.5.9 gate was held five times, one mutant after the other (M21, M606, M09, M13, M33), each let through by some draws of the seed. A mutant is taken when its target reaches the test that runs a machine, word by word: a plain test of a machine's file, or a `-k` that leaves the machine out, is no machine (file by file, `mutation_check.machine` counts nine such targets). `alone` when the target runs nothing else (42 on 10/11, after MUT9); `beside` when it also names fixed tests (199), which run first, once: if they catch the mutant, the machine is not run on it — unless they draw (Hypothesis) or race, then the whole target runs on each seed, never a false green. Each night takes a seventh of the list (a mutant's night is fixed by its id, the whole list in a week) at the gate's 500 sequences on three seeds drawn, after each unmutated machine on each seed. Each mutant is said `CAUGHT n/n`, `flaky` or `SURVIVES`; a fragile one comes with the seeds it survived on and, for each, the command that measures it as the gate does. The workflow, pushed by Jonathan (the sessions' App has no Workflows right), opens or comments a neutral issue per fragile mutant from a job of its own (`issues: write`, no checkout, no action, what the night said checked against its form first); the remedy is a frozen sequence linked by `TESTS_ALSO`, as MUT5, MUT6 and MUT8 did. Estimated budget: 15 to 45 min of wall time a night on one runner.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mutation_night.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`. Mutants M4731–M4736.

### Fixed
- **M33 is caught on every run, whatever the seed** (MUT9, #704 ; `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`). The 0.5.9 release gate (500 examples, seed 637433567) saw M33 (« an unowned old fixed-name rule on a non-dedicated NSG ignored ») survive one round of two: the invariant machine alone catches it on seed 637433567 and lets it through on 637433568. A0's own frozen sequences already caught it without being linked to it — an older version's fixed-name deny-all, its state lost, on an NSG shared since, or whose sharing can't be read: the release must not say « released » while it cuts the VM off — and so did the unit test of a release without state that leaves the deny-all on another VM's NSG, said: the three are added to M33's tests (`TESTS_ALSO` of MUT3, as MUT5, MUT6 and MUT8 did); the mutant and its anchor do not change.

## [0.5.9] — 2026-11-10 — "one gesture"

### Security
- **A detection row is never attributed by its host name: a rule whose rows carry neither `_ResourceId` nor `CallerIpAddress` is refused at load and at approval** (SEC-A4, #633; audit of 06/11, V-04; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « L'attribution d'une ligne »). A row without `_ResourceId` was routed by its `Computer`, which root on the VM chooses: a rule without it — a rule proposed by the model and approved, for one — made the watch isolate or block in another VM's name, and the two-homonyms guard only held between two passes of the discovery (every 30 min). `RowAttribution` no longer reads `Computer`: a row is routed by the resource Azure writes (`_ResourceId`, or `ResourceId`) or by the private address that made a call (`CallerIpAddress`); a row that carries only its host name is unattributed (an anchored card, the actions held). At load, in any layer (the base, the client's layer, hence the approved rules), a rule of the data plane whose query keeps neither key is refused and said; a refused copy in the client's layer leaves the shipped rule of its name. `glorfindel approve-rule` and the console refuse it with the reason, the proposal kept pending, nothing written. The query is read on its text (`eregion_core/rule_routing.py`), the strictest reading, which refuses at the slightest doubt rather than follow a value: a key is kept only while it holds what Azure wrote — a table carries `_ResourceId`, a storage table (`StorageBlobLogs`, `StorageFileLogs`, `StorageQueueLogs`, `StorageTableLogs`) carries `CallerIpAddress` too, a `union` what all its members carry; `project`, `distinct`, `summarize … by`, `arg_max`/`arg_min` keep a key only if they name it and it is still there; `project-away`, `project-keep`, `project-rename` drop it. A query that writes a routing key anywhere, in any case, is refused, whatever it writes from (`extend`, `project X = …`, `project-rename`, `summarize X = …`, a named output of `arg_*`, `parse … with … X:string`, `print`, `datatable`, a `let`) — but one rewrite, read as none: the port taken off the storage caller's address, exactly `CallerIpAddress = tostring(split(CallerIpAddress, ":")[0])` (`data-exfiltration-blob`). An assignment of a form it does not read (a bracketed name, a tuple, a top-level `=` whose left is not a bare name), an unknown operator, a source it cannot read (`datatable`, `print`, `evaluate`, `union withsource=`), a string not closed: refused, never assumed. `CallerIpAddress` on any other table — a custom table fed from the VM, root's word — is refused. And what the load proves is all the run reads: a row of the data plane is read by the keys its rule's query proves only (`_ResourceId` if proven, `CallerIpAddress` if proven, `ResourceId` never) — a custom table fed through the ingestion API, its `_ResourceId` empty, no longer routes by a `ResourceId` or a `CallerIpAddress` root writes. An `attack_started` is read by the keys the query it polls proves (a line of `runs/` never brings its own). The control plane and identities are read as before. The control plane and identities, filed on what their rows name (AL1c), are not concerned. Every shipped rule is conformant and none changes: the eight rules of the data plane keep `_ResourceId`, `data-exfiltration-blob` is routed by `CallerIpAddress`. The analysis AI's triage (`analysis/triage.py`) reads the same refusals.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_rule_routing.py`, `tests/unit/test_analysis_rules_parity.py`, `tests/unit/test_detection_rules.py`, `tests/unit/test_unexpected_decision.py`; mutants M4541–M4543, M4600–M4604 on the reader, M4605–M4606 on what a row is read by (M1335 and M1363, the host name's reading, retired). A limit to measure on the bench: a row with an empty `_ResourceId` is no longer attributed by its host name — its isolation held, what the model reads changed.
- **A call made from an appliance's address is never pinned on the appliance: every action is held, and the card says the source is behind it** (SEC-A4, #633; audit of 06/11, V-05; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « L'attribution d'une ligne »). Behind a network appliance that SNATs toward a private endpoint (required when a third-party appliance inspects that traffic), the storage service sees the appliance's address: `data-exfiltration-blob` attributed the exfiltration to the appliance, and the card proposed — in `non_disruptive`, ran — the isolation of a whole hub, the VM that exfiltrates untouched. `private_ip_owners` now reads `enable_ip_forwarding` on each network interface: every address of a VM one of whose NICs forwards packets is marked. A row from such an address is unattributed (`attribution_via: appliance`), its card anchored on the appliance when the rule watches it, and `decide`'s attribution guard holds every action — the isolation, the block, the snapshot, the release —, whatever the model or an earlier gate said; the card says the source VM is behind the appliance, to be found in its logs. The same reading for an `attack_started` and for a card of the control plane; a rule bound to one asset never takes such a row for its own. Limits, in the documentation: the source VM is not found (no source reads an appliance's logs yet), an explicit proxy without IP forwarding is not recognised.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_rule_routing.py` (the in-memory Azure of the invariants, `tests/unit/fake_azure.py`, now carries `enable_ip_forwarding`); mutants M4540, M4544.

### Added
- **Eregion by role: a sheet per reader, on the project's site, every figure dated and sourced** (ADO2,
  #671 ; `docs/adoption/`, served at `https://grootcyber.github.io/eregion/adoption/`, linked from the README
  and the home page ; `docs/sessions.md`, « Les fiches d'adoption »). Nine sheets, in English: a CISO, a
  CDIO, a CTO, a security engineer, an SRE or platform team, a DPO, an MSSP or MSP, an auditor or an
  insurer, an executive or a CFO. Each says the reader's fear, what changes for them, three proofs, what to
  show them in five minutes, their objection and its answer, and the next step with its document. A section
  says what the project does not promise: Azure VMs today; identities and the control plane in detection
  only; not a SOC; `full_auto` planned, not available; autonomy on a real fleet not recommended yet; the
  ransomware times from a June run, to be replayed, and a second human step after a restore with the hosted
  watch; bench measures on small samples; no hosted offer, no price. The page runs no script, under a content
  policy that allows only itself; its fonts are the replay's; it prints one sheet per page and reads on a
  phone.
  - **One source for the site's figures, `docs/proofs.json`**: each figure has its value, what it says, its
    date (the day or the month of the measure or, when the source dates no measure, the day the source
    states it), its kind (measured, an estimate, a setting, or a measure to take again, which the page says)
    and its source in the repository — a file, the sentence or sentences of it that give the figure, and the
    heading it sits under or the run it comes from. `python3 scripts/adoption.py check` refuses a figure
    without its date or its source, a source the repository does not hold, a quote its file no longer says, a
    number of the value, of what it says or of its note that no quote gives, a figure on the page shown
    otherwise than its source says, a card that says anything outside its fields, a digit on the page outside
    a figure of the source — in the text, a title, a label, an image's text or a meta's content (a technique's
    id, the license and an issue's number aside) —, and a figure no page shows. On a failure it says where to
    fix it: `docs/proofs.json` and the page, in the same pull request. The figures of the private preview the repository does not hold (vendors' prices, a test
    count, a catalogue size, a proof a month) are not on the page.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_adoption.py` (the rule, the real source and page, the page's policy and links,
    what it does not promise).
- **`make bench-preflight`: the bench's step 0 in one verdict** (BNC1, #682; `docs/banc-t1486.md`, step 0; `docs/operations.md`, « Le banc lisible »). The T1486 replay of 09/11 took about 4 h: every check of the step 0 was typed by hand, and a missing right (`listKeys` on the staging account) was seen only at the restore. One line ✓, ⚠ or ✗ per check, then a summary; exit 1 on a ✗, a ⚠ does not stop the run. Read only, on Azure, H0 and Orodruin, under the operator's `az login` and the pairs of their environment:
  - H0's deployed reference, its containers and its CD, as `make glorfindel-host-status` and `make glorfindel-host-cd-status` say them; no local watch (`docker ps`);
  - Orodruin's contract read on it by `check()`, never published, armed and its cap; deallocated, a warning;
  - the pairs, by `scripts/credential_expiry.py` (RST1): the console's, which restores, is required;
  - the target: its group, its mode and readiness on H0, the quarantine, no isolation already; the age of its newest recovery point (a warning past `POINT_MAX_H`, 24 h);
  - the effective rights: the console under its own pair, by `Microsoft.Authorization/permissions` with the restore's own reader (`restore_rights.py`), the neutralization's Run Command included; H0 and Orodruin by their role assignments and the roles' definitions, wildcards and `notActions` read as Azure reads them, their ids read from Terraform's outputs;
  - the known drifts: « Eregion Glorfindel Restore » that still holds `runCommand/action` (before SEC17) warns; « Eregion Glorfindel Restore Staging » not on the console at the staging account fails.
- **`make bench-report RUN=<run_id>`: the chronology of a run, composed alone** (BNC1; `docs/banc-t1486.md`, step 8). It reads, on H0 by ssh, the run's report, its cycles, the lifts and the isolation state; then the activity log of the target and of its NICs, each read apart (a read that reaches its cap is said cut), the vault's jobs and the instance view. It prints the form of the 09/11 report — the figures from T0, the `restore_time` in two numbers (the job, the start, and whether the quarantine held it until the lift), the chronology, each Run Command with its caller as a role (the console, the bot, H0, Orodruin, a human, unknown) and its duration — and writes the same in JSON (`runs/bench/<run_id>.json`, or `OUT=`), ready to feed `docs/proofs.json` later. The Dagorlad request of a wire is linked when the run carries it.
  - H0's reader is read-only by construction: never run inside the watch (`docker exec`), but in a disposable container of the deployed watch's image (`docker inspect` gives its id and its mounts), `docker run --rm --network none --read-only --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges`, with the watch's runs, its state and its configuration mounted `readonly` — no network, so neither IMDS nor a token. It sends back neither a reasoning nor a raw signal. Nothing goes to H0 nor to Orodruin until their host key is verified by its fingerprint. A later step: a read-only sub-command shipped in the image.
  - The isolation, once H0's state is gone, is dated by H0's write on one of the target's NICs; an isolation by an NSG's rules alone does not show there. An assignment at a management group is not read: a missing right says so. The JSON and the Markdown are written side by side, `OUT=x.md` included. A failure of any kind is said in one masked line, never a Python trace.
  - No identifier is printed by either target: each line says a role, a kind of scope or a public bench name, and goes through one mask (GUID, IPv4, UPN, ARM id) before it is printed or written. `TARGET`, `RUN`, `OUT` and `POINT_MAX_H` reach the scripts through the environment, never expanded by make, checked there.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_preflight.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_report.py`, on a fake `az`, `terraform`, `docker`, `make`, Orodruin and H0 read by ssh (the reader run for real on a fake H0), and a fake Azure Resource Manager; with the shapes measured on 09/11, the preflight sees the missing `listKeys`, an absent pair, a point too old and a role from before SEC17, and the report composes again T0, the detection at 70.3 s, the isolation, the 302 s drain, the 152 s neutralization, the 15 min 17 job, the lift and the agent `Ready`. Mutants M4676–M4685, M4706–M4709 and M4730 (`scripts/mutants/BNC1.py`).
- **The public manual: where each part of Eregion stands, on the project's site** (MAN1, #664 ;
  `docs/manuel/`, served at `https://grootcyber.github.io/eregion/manuel/`, linked from the README and the
  home page ; `docs/sessions.md`, « Le manuel public »). Each component is shown as bricks laid on its
  chain (detect, investigate, decide, guard, act, verify…), each brick at its state: planned, in development,
  in review, on `main`, released with its version; the road from 0.2 to 1.0, release by release, and what
  each one lays. Its source is `docs/manuel/modules.yaml`, which a lot's pull request updates;
  `make manuel` writes the `modules.json` the page reads. On load, the page reads GitHub's public API
  without a token (open pull requests, open issues, the last release; kept ten minutes): an open pull
  request on a brick shows it in review. Without the API, it shows what the repository says, and says so.
  The page runs only its own script, under a content policy that allows GitHub's API alone; nothing it
  reads is written as HTML; its fonts are the replay's.
  - The public rule, held in the YAML itself: a security brick (its lots, in any case, a flag of its own, or
    its issue or pull request in the list of the audit's issues) has a neutral title only, no note, and,
    until released, a neutral place; the page shows neither its issue nor its pull request; no text of the
    manual names a finding; there is no field for an operator's gesture or a review; a note only on a
    brick released. A version cut but not yet published on GitHub reads « to be published ».
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_manuel.py` (the schema, the rule, the page, and a fragment `changes/<LOT>.md`
    that no brick on `main` names fails).
- **An interactive replay of the loop, on the project's site** (PRES1, #658 ; `docs/replay/`, served at
  `https://grootcyber.github.io/eregion/replay/`, linked from the README and the home page). A ransomware
  simulation on a bench VM, in ten chapters: Annatar's checks before any cloud call, the attack, the
  `ransomware-disk-write` detection, the questions Glorfindel asks before deciding, the guards on its decision,
  the quarantine NSG on every network card (your own NSG kept intact), the proof read back from Azure, the
  restore, the release and the memory. It plays in `human_only` (every step approved by you), `non_disruptive`
  (contained alone, the restore approved by you) and `full_auto`, said planned and not available today. The
  operator's screen shows what reaches you, in the formats of the bot and the console (texts of the bot
  translated from French). Times shown as measured are the README's (55–71 s, about a minute, 21 min 29 s);
  everything else is said illustrative. The page is static and loads nothing from elsewhere: its fonts are
  served with it (SIL Open Font License, `docs/replay/fonts/`), its images carry no metadata.
- **A step-by-step to replay the ransomware (T1486) on the bench and date its figures** (RAN1, #665 ;
  `docs/banc-t1486.md`, linked from `docs/demarrer/banc.md` and `docs/demarrer/a-rejouer.md`). It runs on
  gondolin: bree is not enrolled. The path is a Dagorlad request and `make orodruin-exercise`, the target's
  name typed; a declared campaign refuses T1486. The clean recovery point is taken from H0 before the attack.
  The restore runs from the operator's machine, under the console's principal, with `--before` and
  `--keep-isolated`. The release is done on H0. The page says where to read T0, the detection, the
  containment read back from Azure and the restored service, with one read-only script on H0. It also covers
  putting the bench back, the cost, and what can go wrong. Every command was checked in the code or run dry;
  nothing was played on Azure yet. The page names one gap: on the bench, the loop does not close alone. H0
  never restores, and its watch does not see a restore run elsewhere; a release made outside H0 would be put
  back by its reassertion. Two human steps remain, so the README's figures stay as they are until the run.
- **The watch reads on Azure the recovery of a VM it isolated, and lifts the isolation itself** (REC1, #668; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La reprise lue sur Azure »). H0 never restores (SEC17): a restore runs from the workstation, whose `recovery_complete` stays on that host, so H0's watch never released on its own, and its reassertion put back a release made elsewhere — two human gestures on the bench, restore then release. Every `GLORFINDEL_RECOVERY_INTERVAL_S` (60 s), for each isolation of its state, the watch reads through the adapter (`observed_restore`, `glorfindel/providers/azure/recovery.py`), never from another host's file nor from the model, and holds itself at least as strict as the workstation, which proves the neutralization by its own gesture and restores in place:
  - the VM's last restore job since its isolation, finished and `Completed`, in its container. A job naming no container counts only when it names its point by its id.
  - **A restore in place, by the control plane**: the job's type « Replace disks », and the VM's OS disk created during the job. A restore elsewhere (« Restore disks », a new VM) carries the same operation, name and container, yet leaves the isolated VM's disk: its card says not to release.
  - The point it restored, matched to one of the VM's recovery points, before the start of the incident: the isolation, or the first detection of its pending card when earlier (`restore_plan.incident_start`, the rule of `--before`).
  - The VM running.
  - **The neutralization, by the activity log**: the last succeeded Run Command on the VM before the job started was made by a principal that restores (`GLORFINDEL_RESTORE_PRINCIPALS`: set on the workstation, in `.envrc`, copied into `host.env`), and no Run Command event by anyone else after it, whatever its status (one that failed or was cut by the deallocation left its settings in the VM model). An activity log longer than its bound is unreadable, never read as whole. Then, one more condition and never enough alone: the guest's word after the restore is the harmless output. The guest writes it; an attacker's script can print the public marker, and root can rewrite it.
  - Then the watch builds its own `recovery_complete`, with the same fields as `restore --wait` and vouched for in its process, and hands it to the VM's queue. The release follows as on a workstation: autonomous where the mode allows, a `mode_hold` card in `human_only`, the release precondition unchanged. Without the activity log's fact, the neutralization is unknown and the release is held.
  - A failed job, a restore not in place, no container nor named point, a point not before the incident or unreadable: one `release_hold` card (action `review_recovery`, nothing to approve in one click), no release. A VM not running 10 min after its job ended: one card, and the release follows once it runs.
  - Once per job (`~/.glorfindel/recoveries.json`, written before the signal leaves; the records of a VM no longer isolated dropped). A release running, a restore claimed on this host, or a job ended less than `GLORFINDEL_RECOVERY_GRACE_S` (300 s) ago: left alone, or waits.
  - Reads only, under the Reader role H0 already holds on the bench's group, where the vault is. Unmeasured on the bench: the job's « Job Type » and how it names its point, the caller id the activity log gives the console's principal, and the guest's word after a restored disk boots. Any of them missing gives a card, never a release.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_recovery_watch.py`, on the fake Azure. `tests/unit/fake_azure.py` now carries a vault's restore jobs and their type, recovery points, power state, OS disks and their creation, the activity log and its callers, and the guest's word. Its state machine has a hostile guest and an attacker printing the marker. R1: never a `recovery_complete` without a recovery proven on Azure. R2: never two for one job, nor two cards for one job and one reason. Also `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py` (the principals reach the watch alone). Mutants M4615–M4619, M4628–M4632, M4639–M4641 (`scripts/mutants/REC1.py`).
- **The staging account's keys, a role of their own, on that one account** (RST1, #680; `docs/operations.md`, « Identités Azure »). The first restore from the console, on the bench on 09/11, was refused at the trigger: 403 `LinkedAuthorizationFailed`, `Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts/listKeys/action` missing on the staging account (`restore_staging_storage`). On 05/10 the restore ran under the former shared principal, which held `Contributor`.
  - « Eregion Glorfindel Restore Staging » (`infra/identities/glorfindel-restore-staging.json`): `listKeys` alone, given to the console and the bot on the staging account only — never on the group, which holds other storage accounts, never to the watch nor to H0's identity.
  - On the bench, H0's Terraform gives it (`restore_staging_storage` in `infra/glorfindel-host/restore.auto.tfvars`, read on Azure by its type and name in the bench's group, never through `azurerm_storage_account`, which would copy its keys into H0's state; `terraform output restore_staging`); elsewhere, the `az` commands of the documentation. A role set by hand before (the bench, 09/11) is imported, not recreated.
  - Tests: `infra/glorfindel-host/tests/restore_staging.tftest.hcl`, `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`, `tests/unit/test_azure_roles.py`.
- **`make azure-pairs-expiry`: when the secrets of the workstation's three pairs end** (RST1; `docs/operations.md`, « Faire tourner un secret »). It reads `az ad app credential list` under the operator's `az login`, the end date only. It warns under 14 days, says « aucune paire » for an absent pair, prints neither a secret nor a client id, and exits 1 on an unreadable date or when no secret is valid; an expired secret beside a valid one is said as an old one to remove. `check-azure-env` and Glorfindel's start never call Graph. The bench's step 0 checks the console's pair three ways (`docs/banc-t1486.md`), and `.envrc.example` says the workstation that restores needs it.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_credential_expiry.py`, on a fake `az`.

### Changed
- **The azurerm canary's OIDC sign-ins no longer make an `entra-sp-unusual-signin` card on the bench** (CAN3, #673 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Les connexions OIDC du canari azurerm » ; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Les connexions OIDC du canari (CAN3) »). The canary (`.github/workflows/canary-azurerm.yml`, no secret) signs in from GitHub's hosted runners, whose address changes every run: the shipped rule, a principal's sign-in from an address never seen in 14 days, made a card at every run on H0. Jonathan's decision (09/11): in the bench's client layer (`infra/glorfindel-host/client_rules.bench.yaml`), the shipped rule copied as it is (`base_sha256`) plus one exclusion, read on each sign-in line before the lines are grouped by principal and address, the 14-day baseline untouched. A line is set aside only when both hold: its client application is the canary's — the appid taken from the watch's environment by `env_guids: [EREGION_RULE_CANARY_APPID]`, CAN2's variable, already on H0, never in the repository — and it was made with a federated identity credential (`ClientCredentialType =~ "federatedIdentityCredential"`, Microsoft Graph's value, and `FederatedCredentialId` not empty). The canary's principal by a secret, a certificate or a bare assertion, and any other principal, still make a card; the variable absent or not a GUID, the copy is set aside and the shipped rule runs, said. No code: `env_guids` (0.5.8) is enough, and the copy reaches H0 by `make glorfindel-host-client-rules`, without a release. To measure on the bench at the canary's next run: the form the workspace writes in `ClientCredentialType`, and that `FederatedCredentialId` is filled — another form keeps the cards (nothing hidden) and is fixed in the copy. Limits: any federated credential of the canary's application is read as the canary's (adding one makes an `entra-credential-added` card, not set aside); the principal is known by its appid, not its object id.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_canary_signin.py` (the shipped rule and its copy run as written on lines in `AADServicePrincipalSignInLogs`' form, then through the RulePoller and the Entra adapter); `tests/unit/kql_subset.py` now reads `let`, `join kind=inner|leftanti`, `distinct`, `between`, `dcount`, `make_set`, `bin`, `strcat_array` and `project X = …`. Mutants M4636–M4638 (`scripts/mutants/CAN3.py`).
- **`make release` releases the manual's bricks on `main`** (MAN1, #664 ; `scripts/release.py`,
  `scripts/manuel.py`). In the version's commit, each brick on `main` becomes released with the version,
  its release step is published with its last version and its date, and `modules.json` follows; a manual
  it cannot read is refused before anything is written. Tests: `tests/unit/test_release.py`.
- **The public manual is in English, like the rest of the project's site** (MAN1b, #676 ; `docs/manuel/`,
  `docs/sessions.md`, « Le manuel public »). The page (its titles, states, stages, legends, the workbench,
  the road, what it says when GitHub's API answers, limits or stays silent) and every text of
  `docs/manuel/modules.yaml` (the components, their stages, the release steps and the 110 bricks, their
  names and notes) now read English; the ids, the keys and the states (`prevue`, `livree`…) stay the
  repository's words, which `make release` reads, and the page translates them (planned, in development,
  in review, on main, released). A security brick is named « Security hardening », and the page shows that
  name for any brick the public rule reads as security, whatever the YAML says. `scripts/manuel.py` refuses
  a text of the manual that reads French (an accented letter, a guillemet, a space before a colon, a common
  French word): a lot's pull request writes its brick in English. The links to the manual (README, the
  home page, Eregion by role) no longer say « French ».
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_manuel.py`.
- **The night and the release gate draw a new Hypothesis seed each round, said in the report; a pull
  request and a push stay derandomized** (SEED1, #655; `docs/design/preuves.md`, `docs/operations.md`).
  In CI, Hypothesis loads its `ci` profile (`derandomize=True`): the night's 10 000 sequences were the
  same every evening, and the gate's two rounds were one — yet `mutation_check.py` said each round drew
  new seeds. Measured: under that profile `--hypothesis-seed=N` changes nothing. A test's own seed does
  come first: `tests/unit/hypothesis_seed.py`, from the conftest, sets it on every property test and on
  every invariant machine, the tests' settings untouched (the machines' database still read and written).
  `EREGION_HYPOTHESIS_SEED` asks for it: a number (a replay), `draw`, or `profile`. When nothing is
  asked, a scheduled or dispatched run on GitHub draws one; a pull request and a push keep the profile.
  xdist workers share it, and a test's own `@seed` is kept. It is said under each failing property test
  or machine (never under a plain test), in the job's summary, and at the end of the run when pytest is
  given files of `tests/unit/` (under `pytest tests/ -n N` the xdist controller does not load the
  conftest: there, the summary and the failures say it). A replay runs under `CI=true` with the same
  database, or none. `scripts/mutation_check.py` draws the run's seed S, or takes `--seed S`: the unmutated
  runs on S, round r on S + r − 1. The seed is said at the start and at the end, and written in each
  shard's JSON (`seed`, `round_seeds`). The total says each shard's, and the command that replays a
  mutant that survived. The mutation shards (the gate's, the Monday's) and the night need no workflow
  change; the gate's two invariant jobs, which run on the version's pull request, draw once their step
  says `EREGION_HYPOTHESIS_SEED: draw` (a workflow edit, pushed by hand, with a `seed` input to replay
  a night on GitHub). Measured at 60 sequences under `CI=true`: M38, which survives twice when
  derandomized, is caught by one round of two, and its replay by seed gives the same verdicts.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_hypothesis_seed.py`, `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`.

### Fixed
- **A session cut still running on Azure is followed, never read as an empty output** (DRN1, #677; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La coupure suivie sur Azure »; `docs/azure.md`, L17). On the bench on 09/11, under the quarantine, the cut of an autonomous isolation took 302 s on Azure (Started, Accepted, Succeeded); the watch waited 300 s, read what the last poll said, no output, and declared the isolation unverified (« sortie de Run Command illisible ») while the NSG held and the cut succeeded 2 s later.
  - The choice: the isolation is verified on the NSG, as before, and the cut is followed apart. The VM's worker still waits 300 s: a 600 s wait would hold every later signal of that VM for 10 min, and the reassertion's thread when it puts an isolation back. Past it, the cut is `pending` and its poller kept by the watch; the cycle keeps the NSG's verdict, its notification and `glorfindel list` say the cut is pending.
  - Every pass of the reassertion reads the Run Command's status again on Azure, under the VM's lock. While it runs, one card « coupure en attente » (`drain_pending`, action `review_drain`): no decision, nothing to approve. Once the sessions are cut, that card closes with « isolement vérifié ». Refused, failed or with sessions left, a `verification_failed` card says « coupure refusée » or « sessions restées ouvertes ». Still running 30 min after it was asked, or no longer followed (the watch restarted, or another process isolated the VM: `glorfindel respond`; the state's `asked_at` is the drain's nonce), it says « coupure jamais aboutie », the sessions « peut-être pas coupées ». The card is written before the state: a card refused (a full disk), the drain is read again at the next pass. With the reassertion off (`GLORFINDEL_REASSERT_INTERVAL_S=0`), nothing reads a pending cut again: the cycle is not verified, « coupure en attente, non suivie ». The cycle's own card, for a cut that ended within 300 s, says the same cases.
  - The cut's cards have their own id (`reassert-drain:…`, a separator no Azure VM name holds) and never fold into another card of the reassertion on the VM, nor the reverse, nor group with it in the console. The continuation token of the poller is never written to disk: `~/.glorfindel` is writable by the console and the bot, and a forged token would make the watch poll a chosen URL with its ARM token. A VM released while its card waits closes the card. For BL1, a cut that ends badly after the isolation was verified reads « contenu, puis l'isolation n'a pas tenu », never « contenu seul ».
  - The restore's neutralization still waits 600 s (152 s measured under the quarantine). Not done, its status is read on Azure and said (`run_command_pending`, `run_command_status`); it is never read as neutralized, nor as refused.
  - The quarantine's rules 110 (DNS) and 111 (IMDS) are untouched: the hypothesis that they hold the guest agent is strongly supported, not told apart, and the test that would settle it is Jonathan's to run on the bench.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_drain_follow.py`, on the in-memory Azure (a Run Command held running, then ended by the test); the invariant machine gains L4 (`guest_agent_held`, `run_commands_end`: a cut still running is said waiting, one that ended is read). Mutants M4646–M4649, M4672–M4675, M4715–M4719 (`scripts/mutants/DRN1.py`).
- **M21 is caught on every run, whatever the seed: a block counted on an NSG that no longer governs its NIC** (MUT5; `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`, `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`). The 0.5.9 release gate saw it survive one round of two (500 examples: the machine alone lets it through on seed 89917386, catches it on 89917387): the invariant machine reaches the sequence only when its draw does. A sequence written from the mutant's description is frozen next to L26's (a block on the NIC's own NSG, the VM isolated by quarantine, the quarantine taken off the NIC and the NIC left bare, then a verification), and both are added to M21's tests, as MUT3 did for the other mutants caught by a frozen sequence.
- **M606 and M09 are caught on every run, whatever the seed: an ENI left on `default` re-isolated with `default` as its original, a missing original tag read as « no NSG »** (MUT6, #694 ; `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions_aws.py`, `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`, `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`). The 0.5.9 release gate (500 examples) saw M606 survive both rounds (seeds 276700698 and 276700699: the AWS machine never reaches it) and M09 survive one round of two (the machine alone catches it on seed 766752177, lets it through on 766752178): the invariant machines reach these sequences only when their draw does. Two sequences written from the mutants' descriptions are frozen: on AWS, an ENI on its own group isolated, taken out of the quarantine onto `default` by someone, isolated again, then released (I3: « released » puts its own group back); on Azure, a NIC isolated by quarantine whose state and tags are lost, then released (I2: its own NSG is never taken off for « none »). Each is added to its mutant's tests (`TESTS_ALSO` of MUT3, as MUT5 did for M21); the mutants and their anchors do not change.
- **M13 is caught on every run, whatever the seed, and the audit's concurrency test no longer depends on the runner's clock** (MUT8, #694 ; `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`, `tests/unit/test_audit.py`). The 0.5.9 release gate (500 examples) saw M13 (« reassertion ignores a release that has started ») survive one round of two: the invariant machine alone lets it through on seed 873889299 and catches it on 873889300. A frozen sequence already caught it without being linked to it — M35's, a `reset --from-azure` cut off after its first write on Azure, then a reassertion — and so did the unit tests of a release and an unblock in progress: the three are added to M13's tests (`TESTS_ALSO` of MUT3, as MUT5 and MUT6 did); the mutant and its anchor do not change. The same gate saw `test_run_checks_run_concurrently` fail on a slow runner (0.75 s for a 0.7 s threshold): each connector check now waits at a barrier of three that only the other two, running at the same time, can open (10 s guard delay) — run in parallel they meet at once, run one after the other the barrier breaks, whatever the runner's speed.
- **The replay says what a restore without a human would take, in the conditional** (PRES1b, #658 ;
  `docs/replay/index.html`). In the planned `full_auto` and in the panel of the road ahead, letting a restore run
  alone *would be* a separate setting, `allow_destructive`, empty by default and not read by the decision today
  (it was said as if it already worked). The page's sharing description ends on "record", as its end card does.
- **The watch decides a neutralization by the control plane alone, and the platform's refusals hold nothing** (REC1b, #679; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La reprise lue sur Azure »). On the bench (09/11) the instance view never carries Run Command v1: `RunCommandLinux` is `Ready` in `vmAgent.extensionHandlers` but absent from `instanceView.extensions`, before a restore, after it, and after a replayed neutralization. REC1's watch waited 15 min for that word, never found it, and held every release with a card, so the loop never closed with the restore alone.
  - The activity log decides: the last succeeded Run Command before the job came from a principal that restores, after the isolation, and none from another principal since. The guest's word is now information only. Its absence holds nothing, and the watch no longer waits for it (`GLORFINDEL_RECOVERY_REPLAY_WAIT_S` is gone). If it is present, dated after the job, and is anything but the harmless output, it contradicts the control plane and the release is held.
  - **A Run Command refused by the platform is not counted, whoever ran it**: an event `Failed` with HTTP status `Conflict` and error code `OperationNotAllowed`. This was measured on a deallocated VM: the command never reached the guest and left the VM model as it was. The adapter reads the status in `properties.statusCode`, else `subStatus`, and the code in the top-level error of `properties.statusMessage`. Each refusal takes with it one `Started` at most: one within the window, of the same operation (`operationId`), by the same caller, at most 60 s earlier (about 2 s on the bench). A `Started` left over or outside that window, or any other status in the operation (`Accepted`, `Succeeded`), still holds the release: where `operationId` comes from is unmeasured, and an id a client could choose would otherwise let a command cut by the deallocation hide behind a later refusal. Any other failure of another principal still holds the release (REC1's rule m1): another code, another status, a code under `details`, or a message that is unreadable or missing. The query now selects `subStatus`, `properties` and `operationId`. Where exactly ARM writes that status and that code is unmeasured. If they are missing, a refusal reads as another failure, and the release is held.
  - Measured and now written in the docs: the activity log names a principal with a secret the same way as a managed identity (`caller` and `claims.oid` = object id, `claims.appid` = appId), and a restore in place has the « Job Type » « Replace disks ». The operator's rule (`docs/banc-t1486.md`, `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`): an integrity read by Run Command comes after the release, or runs under a principal that restores. Run before the release by anyone else, it holds the automatic release.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_recovery_watch.py`. A view without Run Command and a control plane that proves the neutralization lifts once. Refusals from the restoring principal and from others hold nothing. Six other failures from another principal hold the release. The bench's activity log of 09/11 lifts with the restore alone. Two `Started` for one refusal, or one ten minutes before it, hold the release. `tests/unit/fake_azure.py` changes: the instance view carries no Run Command by default (`view_run_command`), a Run Command on a VM that does not run is refused and logged, and each operation gets its own id. The state machine draws the view, the deallocation before the restore, refusals from each principal and a contrary word. Mutants M4650–M4653, M4668 and M4671 (`scripts/mutants/REC1b.py`).
- **The tests of the release and of the public manual no longer hang on the manual's current state** (REL4, #694 ; `tests/unit/test_manuel.py`, `tests/unit/test_release.py`). They read the tree's `docs/manuel/modules.yaml` and assumed a brick still on `main`, which the release itself passes to `livree`: the version's pull request turned four of them red, and the cut of 0.6.0 would have broken a fifth. Each now runs on the manual as it is, as the patch 0.5.9 leaves it and once the step 0.6 is cut, with a brick of its own on `main` that it must deliver, so none checks nothing.
- **A restore reads its rights before it touches the VM, and says Azure's refusal whole** (RST1, #680; `docs/azure.md`, « Le restore depuis la console »). On 09/11 the CLI neutralized the last Run Command and deallocated the VM, then met the refusal; the VM stayed stopped three hours, unsaid, and the refusal, cut at 300 characters, lost the action Azure named.
  - `Microsoft.Authorization/permissions` is read on the vault, the VM and the staging account, before the neutralization and the deallocation. The rights read: the vault's restore, `deallocate` and `start` on the VM, `listKeys` on the staging account. Wildcards and `notActions` are read as Azure reads them. A missing right refuses the restore early, naming it and its role, the VM untouched. Run Command missing on the VM is no refusal (SEC17). Unreadable rights are said, and the restore goes on as before: a hypothesis to measure on the bench. Deny assignments, locks, conditions and PIM are not returned by the API.
  - An `AuthorizationFailed` or `LinkedAuthorizationFailed` refusal names each missing action and the kind of its scope, never an id; another refusal is said with its ids masked; the snapshot's too. Past the deallocation, every failure says the VM is deallocated and the gesture: start it again after a refusal of authorization or validation (a 4xx other than 409: no restore left); after a 5xx, a 409, no answer or no job, read the vault's jobs first. A refused trigger leaves no trace in the activity log: only the CLI's output keeps it.
  - `glorfindel restore` says which kind of identity it runs under (`Identity :`): the console's pair, the bot's (in their containers, by `GLORFINDEL_AZURE_PAIR`, a constant of the compose; the watch gets none), the watch's, another, or no complete pair, when `DefaultAzureCredential` silently takes another identity. Never an id.
- **No `restoreDiskLunList` on a restore in place** (RST1). Azure now refuses the field on `OriginalLocation`, even complete: the job failed in 4.5 s, code `400625`. The same payload passed twice on 05/10; Azure hardened its validation between the two dates. Every disk of the point is restored.
- **A job that fails at once is read with its error** (RST1). The search after a trigger (`_find_backup_job`, also the watch's snapshot) kept an InProgress job only, for 45 s: a job failed at once read « not found after trigger », which pushed to trigger again. It now keeps a finished job too, says its `errorDetails`, and does not take a finished job started more than 30 s before the trigger (a previous attempt's). The search lasts by operation: 2 min for a restore (8 × 15 s), still 3 × 10 s for the watch's autonomous snapshot, which a ransomware's signal waits behind. Without a job it says to read the vault's jobs before triggering again, and, for a restore, that the VM is deallocated. A `--wait` ends on `CompletedWithWarnings` too.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_restore_console.py`, on a fake Azure (the permissions API per scope, the VM's writes in order, the trigger's answer, the vault's jobs). Mutants M4654–M4663, M4669–M4670 (`scripts/mutants/RST1.py`).

## [0.5.8] — 2026-11-09 — "within bounds"

### Security
- **A campaign whose kill switch cannot be read stops, and a kill switch the runner has seen is never overwritten** (KS1, #648; `docs/operations.md`). `annatar campaign run` re-reads its manifest's `kill_switch` through the bounded reader of `runs/` (SEC-A1) before each run and before each write of the manifest; a stop once seen is kept and never overwritten by what the runner writes from memory. A stop written by hand while the runner rewrites the manifest (a few milliseconds between its re-read and its write, or at ratification) can still be lost: no lock is shared with a hand edit (#651); check that the campaign ended `aborted` after a stop. Previously the runner rewrote the whole manifest during and after each run, erasing a `kill_switch` set meanwhile. A manifest that is absent (deleted from outside: the runner never leaves it absent), refused by the reader (a pipe, a link, a file too large) or unreadable (not JSON, nested too deep, an I/O error, not an object, a `kill_switch` that is neither true nor false, `null` included) now stops the campaign (`aborted`, exit code 1) instead of letting it continue; so does a stop seen during the last run. The reason is said once in the runner's output and recorded in the manifest as `stop_reason` (`"kill switch set"` for the operator's stop). A campaign directory deleted while the campaign runs is never recreated. When the manifest is loaded, `kill_switch` counts as set only when it is `true`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_campaign_runner.py`. Mutants M4573–M4578.
- **Fail closed on the id and the allow-list** (R2a, part of #79; Stratus, not delivered yet: R2b, #608): a technique id not shaped `azure.<tactic>.<name>` (non-Azure platform, path separator, option-looking segment, space, upper case) is refused before it reaches a process; an id Stratus knows but the catalog does not is refused too. The binary's sha256 is re-verified before every phase.
- **Bench only, enrolled, on the control plane** (R2a, part of #79; Stratus, not delivered yet: R2b, #608): `Engine._run_stratus` guards the bench group — inside the bench, enrolled (`eregion-target`/`eregion-zone`, the group standing for the target), and in zone **`bench` only** (a Stratus technique can create real cloud resources, so it never runs in twin or production). Whether Stratus provisions inside that group or creates its own (then outside the inscription and the exercise window) is a design question for R2b/ID1, not settled here.
- **Every phase goes through the Orodruin contract and is journaled** (R2a, part of #79; Stratus, not delivered yet: R2b, #608) before it runs, and counted against the cap (`Session.before_call`), like the VM executor. With no armed contract: passive, nothing touched (`Passive`). The **Graph probe is read-only**: held to a GET of a Microsoft Graph v1.0 path (it can only read the directory), it checks the initial state before `detonate` and after `cleanup`, journaled as a declared read (never counted against the cap). `warmup` creates the prerequisites, so **cleanup always runs** (even when warmup or detonate raised); revert runs only after a detonation.
- **Orodruin has no Internet** (R2a, part of #79; Stratus, not delivered yet: R2b, #608): `warmup` provisions with Terraform (pinned `1.3.10`, which the catalog auto-installs otherwise) and its provider plugins; both are to be staged and pinned in the image, **not delivered yet** (R2b, #608), and the adapter points Terraform at them offline (`airgap_env`: a run-scoped `HOME`, `TF_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR`, a `TF_CLI_CONFIG_FILE` filesystem mirror) — once they are there, `terraform init` never reaches the network (`annatar/stratus/README.md`, `annatar/stratus/PIN`).
- **The exercise windows, the signals `glorfindel respond` reads and a campaign's manifest are read through the bounded reader of `runs/`** (SEC-A1, #630; `docs/glorfindel.md`). `load_windows`, `close_window`, `glorfindel.signals.load_signals` and `CampaignManifest.load` now go through `eregion_core.channel_reader`: a regular file only, never through a link, never blocking, bounded in size (`WINDOW_BYTES` for a window, its mtime taken from the file read). At most `MAX_WINDOW_FILES` window files are read at a time; a JSON nested too deep is refused like any unreadable one. A refused file is said once and skipped, or the command stops with exit code 1; it is never waited on.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bounded_reads.py`. Mutants M4520–M4522, M4564, M4566.
- **A local exercise window is bounded in the past by its file's mtime, and windows chained on one VM are bounded together** (SEC-A1, #630; `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`). A window written on the host (no `channel_created`, or in an injector's own directory, for the watch and the console alike) is refused when its last declared moment is more than `FUTURE_SKEW_S` before its file's mtime; at the watch, its record (SEC14) bounds it too. The windows on one VM cover at most `CHAIN_MAX_S` (8 h) of the `CHAIN_HORIZON_S` (24 h) before each one starts; a window past that bound no longer marks the VM (`chain_refusals`, computed once per VM). At the watch, the chain counts every window its record saw, its file deleted or not. A window the console refused attributes no cycle.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bounded_reads.py`, `tests/unit/test_console_exercises.py`. Mutants M4523, M4524, M4563, M4565.
- **The watch's decision queue is bounded: a burst of forged detections no longer delays a grave one nor drives calls to the model without limit** (SEC-A2, #631; audit of 06/11, V-03; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La file de décision, bornée »). Each detection of the data plane waited for its decision in a first-in, first-out queue per VM with no ceiling, and each decision is at least one call to the model; an aggregated row is identified by its text columns, so values written in log lines by a local account on a watched VM each made a detection. `glorfindel/decision_queue.py` now holds four bounds, before any call: the queue of a VM holds at most 50 detections (beyond, the least grave and newest leaves it, said in the watch's log and counted on a card); the gravest first (critical severity, then the techniques where data is destroyed or leaves: T1486, T1485, T1490, T1041, T1567 — by technique, never by the rule's text); one decision of the model per VM, rule, gravity and source in a window of 300 s, opened only by a decision that contained or put a card before a human (never a failed cycle, nor a decision with no action and no card), the other detections of the RulePoller deduplicated without a call; a ceiling of 10 calls to the model per minute for the whole watch, each class counted apart with the draws of D-1, the low class taking at most 6 and the grave ones keeping their own room. Beyond the ceiling, a low detection becomes a card and a grave one waits in its queue until the room comes back; a critical one (a compromise, COR1) is never held by the ceiling and is deduplicated by its address. What B3a's floor reads is never lost: a held detection still writes the facts it confirms on its VM's incident, and once both facts are confirmed on the VM, its detection is never held (one per pass of its rule). A detection held by these bounds becomes a card `undecided_detection`: no action, no investigation, nothing in the memory, one card per VM and rule naming its latest lines and counting them by reason. Cards of the control plane and of identities, decided by the code, are not held. Limits stated in the documentation: a detection held this way goes through none of `decide`'s guards; the counts and the windows restart with the watch.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_decision_queue.py`; mutants M4525–M4528, M4567–M4572.
- **The older lines of a pass are named on a card, none marked sent unsaid** (SEC-A2, #631; audit of 06/11, V-13). For a rule of event lines (sudo, `useradd`, crontab), only the newest line of a pass made the detection and the others were marked sent: a decoy line written just after the real one hid it. The RulePoller still sends the newest line as it is, and now one more signal of the same VM carries the others (one line, or a summary), marked for a card only: the queue makes it a card `undecided_detection` that names them, never a call to the model. What the model reads is unchanged: neither `_build_user_message()`, nor `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, nor `few_shot_examples.yaml` is touched. auditd's fold (AUD1) is unchanged.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_decision_queue.py`, `tests/unit/test_detection_rules.py`, `tests/unit/test_forged_logs.py`; mutant M4529.
- **The threat model names two more boundaries: the watch's Microsoft Graph read and the Stratus binary** (SEC-D, #636; security audit of 06/11, P-02; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §5, `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`). The watch's Graph read (IR1a): four read-only application permissions, set by hand outside Terraform, that apply to the whole tenant and, on H0, to the watch, the console and the bot, which share the VM's identity; what holds it (a GET-only reader, the console never reading Graph, no write permission ever, nothing by default) and what stays open (no start-up contract re-reads the watch's Graph rights; planned: #638). The Stratus Red Team binary launched from Orodruin (R2a): what holds it (closed catalog allow-list, pinned sha256 re-checked before every phase, each phase journaled and counted under the cap from an armed contract, enrolled bench group in zone `bench` only, offline Terraform) and what stays open (no target input, Orodruin's environment and identity inherited, so its reach is Orodruin's RBAC; the binary is not shipped yet; planned: R2b, #608, then R2c). H0's line no longer says its identity holds « nothing else » without naming Graph. §4 now names what the correlation copies from Syslog onto the line sent to the model, fields that a local user can write: `SuccessfulLogins`, read under sshd's tag (an account and a method, 64 characters at most each); the reason of `SessionOrigin`, addresses read as such and, for an account taken by another service, that service's name and the account that took it, read from a session line of any tag, with no length bound (planned: #632).

### Added
- **A sister rule for VM restore points, Azure Backup excluded by its public application id: `restorepoint-by-unexpected-caller`** (AL2, #598 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Les points de restauration » ; `docs/operations.md`, ID1 « Les opérations de test »). Measured on the bench on 05/11 (ID1's hypothesis A2): an on-demand Azure Backup of gondolin — what Glorfindel's `snapshot` action does — goes through restore points (`RESTOREPOINTS/WRITE`, then `RETRIEVESASURIS/ACTION`), never a snapshot nor `BEGINGETACCESS`, so `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` makes no card at each backup; but `RETRIEVESASURIS` hands out SAS URLs to a restore point's disks, the same exit as a snapshot followed by download access, and no rule read it. Decided by Jonathan on 06/11: a sister rule rather than extending the snapshot rule, which does not change (its sha256 is held by a test). The new rule (`source: azure_activity`, detection only, `plane: control`, T1578.001 and T1530, `high`, `critical` under Glorfindel's identity) reads `RESTOREPOINTS/WRITE`, `RESTOREPOINTS/RETRIEVESASURIS/ACTION` and `RESTOREPOINTS/DISKRESTOREPOINTS/BEGINGETACCESS/ACTION` (the same URL through the public API, disk by disk) — not a collection written alone, which holds nothing; one row per operation, its latest line, failures left out. **Azure Backup is excluded, and it alone, by its public application id** (`262044b1-e2ce-469f-a196-69ab7ada62d3`, the same in every tenant of the public cloud: Microsoft Learn, « Back up and restore Azure VMs with PowerShell »), in `Claims.appid` or as the `Caller`: an operation makes a card when one of its lines names a caller other than Azure Backup; a line that names no one decides nothing — a backup's last line may carry neither, and its Start line may be out of the window or not yet ingested: anyone else's card comes at the pass where their named line lands. A correlation id is the client's to choose (`x-ms-correlation-request-id`), so a backup's id taken up by someone else hides no one; Azure Backup's id is matched whole, never as a term of the `Caller`.
