The row carries the caller that decided it, and its address: its latest line's when that line names someone other than Azure Backup, else what the lines naming someone else carry (a bare latest line, or Azure Backup's) — so Glorfindel's Start followed by a bare Success is critical, and a human's names the human, with the human's address; when several other callers share an operation and its latest line names none of them, the card may mix their ids (each column is taken on one of their lines), and it is critical when an id it carries is Glorfindel's; the correlation id leads to the others. Its card is decided by the code, no action, like every control-plane card. It does not see a restore or a file recovery from the vault, a storage SAS (`LISTSERVICESAS`), the download itself, what Azure Backup does under its own identity, an operation none of whose lines names a caller, nor — a known limit of the family of activity-log rules, followed by AL1d (#600) — an operation read by its latest line, whose failure is left out. The bench proof is written for Jonathan: a backup makes no card; a restore point of gondolin made by hand, then download access to its disk, make two; the `az` commands are reversible, and a query confirms Azure Backup's caller on the 05/11 lines (hypotheses ID1-A9, ID1-A10; the query also shows whether an operation's last line is bare). The test reader `kql_subset` now follows `parse_json(…).member`, `["key"]`, `in~`, `!in~`, `arg_max(…, *)` followed by named aggregations, `take_anyif` and `iif`. `!~`, which no rule uses, is left out of it.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_restore_points.py` (the rule as written over forged AzureActivity lines, then the real AzureActivityDetector and the RulePoller; no Azure, no model). Mutants M4280–M4289, M4305–M4309, M4390–M4392 and M4410.
- **A read-only identity for the Claude session « Banc », off by default** (BRO1, #614;
  `docs/operations.md`, « L'identité de lecture du banc (BRO1) »). The session reads the bench's
  detections and reports (KQL on the bench's Log Analytics workspace) without asking the maintainer
  each time; it is never for a code lot. `infra/terraform/bench_reader.tf` (`bench_reader_enabled =
  false`) sets a single-tenant application and its service principal, with no permission asked and no
  enrolment tag, a public certificate, and one role assignment: Log Analytics Reader, scoped to the
  bench's workspace alone. The private key is drawn on the operator's machine (`make
  celebrimbor-reader-key`, 30 days by default, 90 at most) and never enters the Terraform state. A
  certificate file holding anything but one `CERTIFICATE` block stops the plan, and the plan's
  diagnostic never shows that file (a sensitive value). The credential's end is the certificate's,
  set explicitly (`end_date`, read from the `reader.end` file the key generation writes; the plan
  refuses an end more than 91 days away): left out, the API would pick one, « typically around 2
  years ». The canonical instance only; no new provider, the lock file unchanged. `make
  celebrimbor-reader-plan`, `celebrimbor-reader-up [YES=1]` and `celebrimbor-reader-profile`
  (targeted, the instance checked before any call); the `bench_reader` output holds identifiers only.
  - `scripts/bench_reader.py`: `keygen`, `profile`, `status`, `login`, `query`, `verify` (one query
    read, one write refused), `logout`. It runs `az` in the reader's own `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR`, without the
    caller's `AZURE_*` or `ARM_*` variables, against the profile's workspace only. Files others can
    read are refused. Every command but `status` refuses inside a code session (`EREGION_AGENT_SESSION`,
    a lot's worktree); the session's commands also want `EREGION_BANC_SESSION=1` and `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR`
    set to the reader's own, so that a bare `az` in the session never reads the maintainer's sign-in;
    `login`, `query` and `verify` refuse a session that inherited a credential from the shell that
    launched it (`ARM_*`, `AZURE_*`, `GH_*`, `GITHUB_*`, `*SECRET*`, `*TOKEN*`, and a non-empty
    `GIT_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS` or `VSCODE_GIT_*`, through which a VS Code terminal's git would get
    the maintainer's GitHub sign-in; names said, never values). `status` says a posed end already past.
  - The Banc session's own settings, documented for the maintainer to write (`AZURE_CONFIG_DIR`, an
    empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, a `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` without a credential helper, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`,
    empty `GIT_ASKPASS` and `SSH_ASKPASS`),
    and a launch from a fresh terminal where `.envrc` was never sourced, its environment's names
    checked first: otherwise, a session launched outside the repository holds the maintainer's
    credentials.
  - The rule in `docs/sessions.md`; a new boundary in `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` (8).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_reader.py`, `infra/terraform/tests/bench_reader.tftest.hcl`
    (`terraform test`, mock providers); mutants M4420–M4424, M4495–M4499 and M4507 (`scripts/mutants/BRO1.py`).
- **The bench's client layer sets the azurerm canary's runs aside from `nsg-change-outside-glorfindel`, by its group and its principal together** (CAN2, #605; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Le canari azurerm »). The scheduled `canary-azurerm` run creates then deletes its throwaway NSGs (`nsg-customer-<suffix>`, `nsg-glorfindel-quarantine-canary-<suffix>`) in `rg-eregion-canary` under its own OIDC principal (P-04): twelve cards on H0, judged right (Jonathan's decision, 06/11). Of the shipped `source: azure_activity` rules, only `nsg-change-outside-glorfindel` reads what the canary does (held by a test). `infra/glorfindel-host/client_rules.bench.yaml` holds that rule copied as it is (`base_sha256`, WL1's drift), plus one exclusion, read on the operation's row as AL1d makes it (its latest line, a bare one's caller completed when the operation's lines name one alone), after that completion and before `project`: the group is `rg-eregion-canary` (`ResourceGroup` and `_ResourceId`, without case, as ARM reads them) **and** the caller is the canary's principal (its appid in `Claims.appid` or as `Caller`, and no user's name as the caller). Another caller in that group, a user signed in through the canary's application, that principal in another group: a card, as before. `make canary-jit` on a workstation runs under the operator's session: its runs keep their cards. The shipped base is unchanged. Limits (`docs/glorfindel.md`): the link to the principal stays approached (its appid, and no `@` in `Caller`); another caller writing the same NSG of `rg-eregion-canary` under the canary's own `CorrelationId` would be set aside with it, when the canary's line is the latest — a collision the caller must choose, negligible.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_canary_exclusion.py` (forged AzureActivity lines, Start, Accept and Success, run as written by `tests/unit/kql_subset.py` — which now reads `contains` and `!contains` — then through the RulePoller and the activity adapter; the row as AL1d completes it: a bare latest line of the canary, the only caller its operation's lines name, set aside; among two callers, a card); mutants M4360–M4364, one per property, and M4508–M4509 (the exclusion read on the row as AL1d completes it).
- **A client rule may take a GUID from the watch's environment, and nothing else** (CAN2). The canary's appid never enters the repository: the copy declares `env_guids: [EREGION_RULE_CANARY_APPID]` and writes `${EREGION_RULE_CANARY_APPID}` in its query. At load (`load_config`, after the drift and before the layers merge), the watch puts in place of that placeholder — and of it alone — the value of its environment variable, checked to be a GUID and written lowercase: no other character ever reaches the query (no KQL injection). Only `EREGION_RULE_…` variables, named in a list by the rule that takes them (else the entry is skipped and said); any other `${…}` stays as written, as every query since SEC2. Absent, empty or no GUID: the copy is set aside and the shipped rule of its name runs — more cards, never fewer — and it is said (a note at the watch's start and under `glorfindel rules`, the variable named, never its value); if that shipped rule would not load, the client rule runs with its placeholder left as written (it equals no identifier), and that is said too. `docker-compose.yml` passes `EREGION_RULE_CANARY_APPID` to the watch and the console (which shows the rules as the watch loads them); `infra/glorfindel-host/host_env.py` copies it from the operator's environment, and `--check` says a value that is no GUID, never refusing. `make glorfindel-host-client-rules` reads such a rule back with a stand-in GUID (H0's appid is never on the workstation) and says H0's watch needs the variable. Jonathan's gesture, once a release with CAN2 is deployed: `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le canari azurerm (CAN2) » — the appid written by the shell into H0's `host.env` from the exact filter `az ad app list --filter "displayName eq 'eregion-canary'"` (never copied from the screen), one line and one GUID checked, cross-checked against the `CallerAppId` of a canary card of 06/11; then `docker compose up -d watch war-room` on H0 (a `restart` does not reread `.env`), then `make glorfindel-host-client-rules`; at the canary's next scheduled run (06:00 UTC), no card of the canary.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_canary_exclusion.py`, `tests/unit/test_host_client_rules.py`, `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`.
- **The weekly bench drill, designed: one exercise a week, played by a timer on Orodruin** (DRL1, #613; `docs/design/drill-banc.md`, indexed in `CLAUDE.md`). Jonathan's decision of 06/11, so that the age of the last proof (under 30 days) holds by itself. The design, reviewed once in Opus 5.5 and corrected, covers:
  - **The content.** A shipped drill, `drills/banc-hebdo.yaml`, with one new block, `schedule` (shipped drills only, every wire in zone `bench`, techniques implemented, non-destructive, from a public library). Version 1 holds one wire: T1136.001, the pinned Atomic Red Team test already proven from Orodruin. What the watch should see lives apart, in `expectations/banc-hebdo.yaml`, which the injectors' image never holds. The drill's digest covers its whole file.
  - **The timer.** A systemd timer, installed and enabled by the operator's arming, never before. Three inputs decide an occurrence: the verified image, the arming file and the clock. What comes from H0 or the channel (the watch's heartbeat, the arming permit) can only refuse. It runs in zone `bench` only, judged again before each wire. It runs at most once in six days, its registry read and written under Orodruin's lock, the lock handed down to the runner. It is never caught up, and never again after an emergency stop until re-armed (a permit, below). It goes through the same judge as a Dagorlad request. A stopped target is started by the drill itself, after the contract, the watch and the journal, its isolation read first. The time budget is set out (`RuntimeMaxSec` 1 h), and the stop of the service closes the windows.
  - **The emergency stop.** An arming permit, closed by default: the operator writes it in the channel when arming, the kill and the disarming remove it, and an occurrence requires it, equal to the local arming. The channel's retention can only let it expire, which closes. The kill also disables the scheduled start of Orodruin right after the revocation, and marks the drill disarmed, so that H0 raises no false absence alarm. On a stop, the service relays SIGTERM to the running wire and waits for it, and the wire's window lasts no longer than its time left.
  - **The report.** Each occurrence is judged on H0, which also raises the alarm when nothing arrived, only when the permit and its own copy of the drill agree (else "re-arm expected"). The Banc session reads the workspace as an independent witness, the rule's relative time filters rewritten onto the occurrence's window.
  - **The rest.** The bias of the last-proof indicator (one technique keeps it green; rotation comes next), the cost (how Orodruin is powered on, and the narrow role of its scheduled start), the threat model frontier it adds (proposed, not yet written there; the public schedule said), and what VP1 reads.
  - **Jonathan's decisions of 06/11**, recorded in its §10: the scheduled start, Tuesday 19:00 UTC, T1136.001 alone, alerts by Glorfindel's bot, the digest over the whole drill file, the kill disabling the scheduled start, the public schedule, gondolin left in its real mode.
  - No code, no change of behaviour.
- **The Stratus Red Team adapter (R2a, part of #79)**: Annatar is ready to run an Azure control-plane technique by **calling the public Stratus Red Team binary** (DataDog, Apache-2.0, v2.38.0) by its technique id — no home-grown offensive logic. `annatar/engines/stratus.py` resolves an id against a **closed allow-list** (the `engine: stratus` entries of `technique_catalog.yaml`) and, for an allowed technique, hands the runner the exact argv of each phase of Stratus's own cycle; `annatar/executors/stratus_host.py` runs them from an **armed Orodruin**. The `warmup → detonate → revert → cleanup` cycle is **mapped onto the injector interface** (prepare, inject, revert, clean up). Modelled on the Atomic adapter (R1, `annatar/engines/atomic.py`). The binary is **pinned** (`annatar/stratus/PIN`) and its sha256 **re-checked before every phase** (SEC5) — a binary that is not the pinned one, or one swapped mid-run, runs nothing. **The binary is not delivered yet** (R2b, #608): it is in no image and not on Orodruin, so without it the resolution refuses and **no Stratus technique runs today**. `stratus` joins `KNOWN_ENGINES` (`annatar/runner/parser.py`) and the runner dispatches it (`Engine._run_stratus`). Three Azure techniques in the catalog (`azure_control_plane`, `status: planned`): `azure.execution.vm-run-command` (T1651), `azure.persistence.create-bastion-shareable-link` (T1133), `azure.persistence.backdoor-managed-identity-fic` (T1098). They show in the Azure Activity Log (`azure_activity`, detection lot AL1); the real bench measurement is **ID1 (#281)**, so `attack_started` is emitted but the detection is not waited on (`detection: unknown`, said in the report).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_annatar_stratus.py` (hermetic, fake Stratus, `dry_run`, no cloud/LLM call). Mutants M4315–M4354 (`scripts/mutants/R2a.py`).
- **The bench's client layer sets bree's scheduled maintenance aside from `sudo-privilege-escalation`, and nothing else** (WL1, #595; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La maintenance programmée de bree »). The cron of H0's pre-observation workload (`infra/terraform/scripts/workload.sh`: `nginx -t`, `systemctl reload nginx`, `journalctl --vacuum-time=14d` as `celebrimbor`, without a terminal) made a card every morning, judged `wrong` (Jonathan's decision, 06/11). `infra/glorfindel-host/client_rules.bench.yaml` holds the shipped rule copied as it is, plus one exclusion: bree alone (its host name and its resource), the account `celebrimbor`, no `TTY=`, nothing between `USER=root` and `COMMAND=`, these three commands with exactly these arguments. Another command, other arguments, another account, a terminal, another VM: a card, as before. The shipped base is unchanged.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_routine.py` (forged lines in sudo's and the agent's forms); mutants M4265–M4269, one per property.
- **A client rule copied from a shipped one is set aside once the shipped rule changed, and the shipped rule runs** (WL1). The copy carries `base_sha256`, the fingerprint of the whole base rule it was taken from (`base_rule_sha256`: canonical JSON, keys sorted, UTF-8 — any field, not the query alone); at load, before the layers merge, a copy whose shipped rule changed is set aside and the shipped rule loads in its place — more cards, never fewer. It is said as drift (`DetectionConfig.drifted`, a note, in red in the watch banner and under `glorfindel rules`). A copy whose base rule is gone keeps running, and is said; so does a stale copy whose shipped rule would not load either (`enabled: false` in the base, or named in the layer's `disable`), or whose name another entry of the layer holds too (the last one wins): its technique is never left undetected. A malformed fingerprint skips the copy (said) and the shipped rule loads, if it is not disabled — otherwise nothing, as for any invalid entry of the layer; so does the retired field `base_query_sha256` (the query alone), never a copy loaded uncompared. Mutants M4300–M4304, M4365–M4368. In the repository, the bench's copy is held to the base by its test: the pull request that changes the base fails until the copy is redone.
- **`make glorfindel-host-client-rules`: the bench's rules merged into H0's client layer** (WL1, in `make help`; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « La couche du client du banc »). H0's `~/.glorfindel/client_rules.yaml` is read by ssh, each rule added or replaced by its name (rules approved on H0 stay), read back as the watch will (`infra/glorfindel-host/host_client_rules.py`: nothing sent for a rule the watch would skip, a stale copy, or an unreadable layer), put in place under the layer's lock only if it did not change since it was read, then the watch restarts and `glorfindel rules` is shown. Jonathan's gesture, once a release with WL1 is deployed.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_client_rules.py` (the recipe against a fake H0).

### Changed
- **A brute force that succeeded, then root on the same VM: the decision proposes at least `isolate_vm`** (B3a, #609; `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/correlation.md`). The CD harness of H0 drew `block_suspicious_ip` one time in three on its paired case (a brute force after a sudo to root on the same VM), above the gate: the source blocked, the VM where root was taken left standing. A deterministic floor in `decide`, never in the prompt: when the correlation (COR1) marked a brute force with a login from its address (`ssh-login-after-brute-force`) and a sudo to root (T1548.003) was detected on the same VM — in either order, kept by the VM's incident (`Incident.confirmed`, the new `confirm_facts` node) — the action is raised to `isolate_vm`, whatever the model chose; a model that already isolates keeps its choice. A brute force alone, a sudo alone, two VMs, failures the correlation never saw succeed or logins it could not check keep the model's choice; so does any signal that is not a detection (a `recovery_complete`, a timeout). Every gate then judges the isolation as the model's own: the confidence, the signal, the attribution, the telemetry, the autonomy mode (in `human_only` nothing runs, the card proposes `isolate_vm`) and the agreement of the draws (each confirming draw floored alike). An action only a human approves (a restore) is never lost: the VM is contained first, then a `destructive_action` card proposes it; an escalated cycle names it in its reason. The card, the notification, the `account_compromised` card and the debug line say why the floor played, in words that fit the choice set aside. Known limits: the incident's TTL slides with every signal of the VM and `confirmed` is never cleared, so while the incident lives every later detection on the VM is raised; in `respond`, a poll longer than the TTL can lose a fact for the next detection.
  - The paired case 2/2 of the harness has failures only (no COR1 mark): the floor does not play there, and it stays as it is, a measure of the model alone. A new harness case, kind `floor` (`scripts/llm_smoke.py`, Jonathan's decision, 06/11), is the same brute force marked by COR1, its incident holding the confirmed sudo: `isolate_vm` expected, 3/3 whatever the model draws — the floor proven end to end in the CD. The harness's attestation (H3) changes with it: Jonathan will make it again at the next `make llm-compare`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_root_floor.py`; mutants M4375–M4379 and M4400–M4404 (`scripts/mutants/B3a.py`).
- **The elrond cycle keeps the maintainer's steering manual up to date at every step** (MAN0, #640 ; `docs/sessions.md`, « Le manuel de pilotage » ; `.claude/skills/elrond/SKILL.md`). The main session updates the lot's brick (state, PR, what it waits for, timestamp) and adds a line to the feed when it launches a lot, receives it, reviews, merges and prepares a release; after a release is published, the bricks on `main` become delivered with their version and the next release becomes the one being assembled. At `/elrond status` it compares the manual with the plan and GitHub (open PRs, merges since the last tag, tags, closed issues, last release), catches up the gap and says so in the feed. The manual is private: no link, identifier or address of it goes into the repository; its address stays in the main session's local memory. An unreachable manual never holds a lot back.
- **On a workstation, the test suite runs in low priority; in CI, as before** (NICE1, #620; `docs/operations.md`, « Sur un poste, la suite en priorité basse »). Outside CI (`CI` unset or empty), once pytest loads `tests/unit/conftest.py`, every process that collects (pytest alone, each xdist worker, each mutant's run) raises itself to `nice 10`, never lower than what it inherits; xdist's controller, which does not collect, keeps its priority. On 06/11, sub-agents running the suite side by side in their worktrees (one with `-n auto`, 16 workers) pushed the load to 70 on 16 cores, and VS Code, starved, killed its extension host seven times in a day.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_suite_priority.py` (the hook on a fake `os.nice` capped at 19 and never lowered, plus one real run); mutants M4480–M4481.
- **`SECURITY.md` follows the supported line, and `make release` keeps it so** (SEC-D, #636; audit P-03). It said « latest 0.4.x » at 0.5.7; the table now marks 0.5.x, and its prose names no version. For a minor release, `scripts/release.py prepare` rewrites the supported line (`| latest X.Y.x | yes |`) and the « older » line, commits SECURITY.md with the version and shows it in a dry run; a patch leaves it (`docs/sessions.md`, `docs/operations.md`). `tests/unit/test_readme.py` requires exactly one supported line, the one of `pyproject.toml`'s version.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_release.py`, `tests/unit/test_readme.py`. Mutants M4560–M4562 (`scripts/mutants/SEC-D.py`).
- **`docs/architecture.md` describes what was missing** (SEC-D, #636; audit P-04): the correlation before the decision (a diagram, `glorfindel/correlation.py`), the watch's key and its three signed uses (heartbeat, detections, the action queue's proposals and receipts) with the signed action queue behind its flag (a diagram), the identities and the identity executor (disabled by default, no verb yet), the bot and the Graph read in the processes diagram; the console's token is no longer called optional; Stratus Red Team is described as merged (R2a), not as future.
- **`scripts/setup.sh` removed** (SEC-D, #636; audit P-06): an orphan script, referenced nowhere, that installed the dependencies without the lock file nor their hashes. `make install` installs from `requirements/dev.txt`, with `--require-hashes`.

### Fixed
- **A card of the control plane or of an identity is filed on what it is about, never on a VM it has nothing to do with** (AL1c, #597 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La carte se range sur ce qu'elle vise » ; `docs/design/console.md`, « Les cartes »). Measured on the bench by ID1: six cards filed on the first VM online, none about it. The RulePoller now reads the target in the row (`glorfindel/card_target.py`) and the card keeps it (`target`): an NSG rule on the NSG, shown on each VM it covers that the discovery knows, an offline one included; a snapshot or a download access on the VM of the source disk — the row names the snapshot only, the source is read on Azure while the snapshot exists (`ReadersMixin.control_topology`, read only, cached), else the resource group, the limit said; a role on its scope (the VM, else the group; the subscription, the control plane); anything else on its resource group, or the control plane. A row routed by the address that made the call (`CallerIpAddress`: an NSG written from a watched VM) is filed on what it writes, the calling VM named as the caller (« appel depuis vm-… »), never attributed to it; only a row that names the VM itself (a role on it) is that VM's; a call from a VM the rule does not watch no longer drops the row (« appel depuis une VM que la règle ne surveille pas »). An identity card is filed on the identity (`entra:<type>:<object id>`), never a VM. What the row and Azure could not say is written on the card — only what was read: a resource missing from a listing is « deleted, or out of the watch's rights », never « deleted » alone; a VM the rule does not watch is said so (« absente de la découverte, exclue de la règle, ou d'un autre backend »), never « unknown to the discovery ». A rule of the control plane or of an identity speaks with no VM watched (every VM excluded, an empty discovery just after a start): excluding every VM no longer silences it. Unchanged: no action, no model, never merged, no incident opened or fed, nothing in the memory; the data plane keeps its anchor.
  - The console: a card about an NSG on the NSG's tile and on each VM it covers (one card, one Ack; eight VMs named, the rest counted); a group, the control plane, their own tile, named with a « : » no VM's name holds (`entra:identities`, `group:<id>`, `plane:control`) — never one tile with a VM of the same name; the identity cards on one « Entra ID — identities » tile; the card and TO JUDGE say « 🎯 about … » and the limit. A card filed before AL1c keeps its « ⚓ » and says « filed on …, may not be about it ».
  - Discord: the card names its target (« Ressource visée », « Identité visée », « Limite »; eight VMs of an NSG named, the rest counted, the field kept under Discord's 1024 characters by naming fewer VMs, each whole), one thread for every identity card, and « Pourquoi rien n'est exécuté » no longer repeats the paragraph the description shows. A card filed before AL1c says the detection may not be about its VM.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_card_target.py` (the bench's rows, forged). Mutants M4270–M4279, M4310–M4314, M4355–M4359, M4418–M4419.
- **An operation that succeeded is always read by the shipped Activity Log rules** (AL1d, #600 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`). `nsg-change-outside-glorfindel`, `role-assignment-write` and `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` keep one row per operation, its latest line, and now count its lines that succeeded in the same `summarize` (`Succeeded`, projected): an operation is set aside only when none of its lines succeeded and its latest is a failure. An operation that only failed still makes no card; the caller exclusions, the other columns and the deduplication by operation are unchanged. The card of an operation whose latest line failed after one succeeded says it took effect. `run-command-by-glorfindel-outside-isolation` keeps every operation, failures included, as before. The sha256 that AL2's tests hold for `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` follows this change (AL1d changes that rule, not its sister).
- **The same in `restorepoint-by-unexpected-caller`, AL2's sister rule, in its own form** (AL1d, #600 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Les points de restauration »). Its columns still come only from the lines that name a caller other than Azure Backup (`iif(OtherCaller, …)`, the four of them); and only a success that cannot be Azure Backup's counts: `Succeeded = iif(BackupLines > 0, OtherSucceeded, AnySucceeded)` — under a backup's correlation id taken up by someone whose try only failed, Azure Backup's Success (named or bare) makes no card for them; and otherwise the operation is read by its latest line, Azure Backup's standing for the other caller only when none of their lines failed (`OtherFailures`, then `not(AzureBackup) or OtherFailures == 0`): a backup's Success arrived last makes no « (Success) » card for another's failure alone, while another's Start, then their bare Success or nothing yet, behind Azure Backup's line, makes one — a backup's correlation id hides no one; that card carries the status of Azure Backup's latest line, the correlation id leads to theirs. Said limits: another caller's bare success followed by a failure, under such an id, makes no card; a bare latest Success there, or a bare failure of theirs followed by Azure Backup's line, makes one (too many, never one fewer). The limit AL2 wrote down (« an operation read by its latest line, whose failure is left out ») is closed in the rule's description, `docs/glorfindel.md` and the threat model.
- **A latest line that names no caller keeps the caller its operation names** (AL1d, #600 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`). In `nsg-change-outside-glorfindel`, `role-assignment-write`, `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` and `run-command-by-glorfindel-outside-isolation`, when its latest line names no one, the row of an operation carries `Caller`, `CallerIpAddress`, `CallerAppId` and `CallerObjectId` from the lines that name its caller — **only when they name one** (`Callers = dcountif(strcat(Caller, "|", CallerAppId, "|", CallerObjectId), Named)`, projected; `iif(Fill, …)`): Glorfindel's own writes stay set aside, its identity elsewhere stays critical, and a Run Command is read under the caller its lines name. A correlation id is the client's to choose: filled column by column, two callers' ids were mixed on one row, and one of Glorfindel's sufficed for the adapter to set aside another's rewrite of an isolation rule under the isolation's id, or take another's Run Command for its drain (the 5.5 review). With several callers, the row stays its latest line (bare: naming no one, the critical card of before AL1d on Glorfindel's rule or a Run Command), and the card says its lines name several callers and to read them; a success there is never said to have taken effect. Said limits: one caller named in two forms (a line with Claims, one without) counts as two, its bare latest line left bare as before AL1d; a latest line that *names* Glorfindel under an id someone else took up still decides, as before AL1d.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_activity_operation_read.py` (every shipped `azure_activity` rule is held there) and `tests/unit/test_restore_points.py` — the shipped queries run as written on forged AzureActivity lines (`tests/unit/kql_subset.py`, one reader for AL2 and AL1d: `in`, `!in`, `in~`, `!in~`, a member or an index on a column or a call, `isempty()`, `iif()`, `strcat()`, `take_anyif()`, `dcountif()`, `arg_max` among other aggregates in any order; each two-caller case run with its lines in both orders), then through the detector and the RulePoller; mutants M4380–M4389, M4490–M4494, M4500–M4506.
- **Identity cards: every command can be pasted as it is, and a consent reads in one line** (C2b, #607; `docs/glorfindel.md`). On the bench (06/11), the « Prochaines étapes » of the Entra ID cards reached Discord as `…/roleAssignments//<id>`, `…/oauth2PermissionGrants//<id>`, `…/servicePrincipals//<id>/appRoleAssignedTo/<id>`: the bot escaped the steps as prose, so Discord turned each URL into a link and showed an escape's backslash as a `/`. The bot now renders the card's plan itself, each command in its own backticks (two fields at most, an item never cut, what does not fit said); a parameter neither in the row nor read on Graph becomes a template that says what to replace (`<id-de-l-attribution>`, no space nor quote: pasted as is, the shell or Graph refuses it), never an empty id. With Graph not read, the row fills what it names: the ids of the grants a « Consent to application » adds to its client (from `ConsentAction.Permissions`, read only when the whole diff has Entra's canonical form, each entry atomic and the value at most 16 KiB: a forged line fails at once, never in a backtracking that would freeze the watch), the resource of an app role assignment, the member of a role; an eligible role is removed by an `adminRemove` schedule request, never a `DELETE` of active assignments. The consent card says the permission (or delegated scope), the application, its nature (delegated or application, from the operation, `ConsentContext.IsAppOnly` now projected as `AppOnly`, or the diff) and for whom — the raw diff is gone. IR1a's plan, checks and « Verify » are unchanged.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_identity_cards.py` (rows forged after AuditLogs); mutants M4370–M4374, M4405–M4409, M4435–M4437 (`scripts/mutants/C2b.py`).
- **A reset Azure refuses is no longer read as keeping a rule unsaid** (I3K, #602; `docs/design/preuves.md`).
  The invariant machine, during MUT3's review, failed on `main` unmutated: after the role was taken away
  (every write refused), `reset --from-azure` said `swept_partial` and named each rule by its delete error,
  but the oracle asked « gardée » of the isolation deny that held back a turned allow for the VM's own other
  NIC (one NIC's old address given to the other). Nothing kept that rule: the reset lifts the VM, the turned
  rule with it, as the code reads the VM's NICs as its own. The oracle now asks of such a rule its name in
  `failed`, by its error (a whole name, never the start of another), and « gardée » only of a rule kept
  for another VM's flows. A refused delete said done, in `deleted`, is caught: no other invariant read
  `deleted`. Not the case of the identifier: the canonical id fails the same. The reset itself is
  unchanged.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py` (`test_a_reset_refused_by_azure_names_the_rule_it_leaves`,
    red on `main` 3f6ae335), `tests/unit/test_invariants.py` (`_held_for_itself`, `_kept_said`); mutants
    M4413 (the refused delete said without its rule) and M4414 (the refused delete said done; survives the
    first version of the oracle) (`scripts/mutants/I3K.py`).
- **A tenant without a PIM license has no eligible role, and the verdict says so** (IDI1p, #618; `docs/operations.md`, « L'identité de test du banc » ; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « IDI1 » ; `docs/design/reponse-identite.md`, IR1b). Measured on 06/11 from Orodruin (IDI1's gesture 5): without Entra ID P2 or ID Governance, Graph answers the read of eligible role instances `400`, `error.code` `AadPremiumLicenseRequired`, and `make orodruin-identity` refused every identity (« identity unreadable: RuntimeError: HTTP 400 reading roleEligibilityScheduleInstances »). Without that license no eligible role can exist (Microsoft: at its expiry, eligible assignments of Entra roles are removed). That exact answer — status `400`, the code compared exactly, on the one read `/roleManagement/directory/roleEligibilityScheduleInstances`, on its first page — now reads as an empty list, said in the verdict: `note — eligible roles: none can exist, the tenant has no PIM license (Graph: AadPremiumLicenseRequired)`. The guard judges the rest as before; the note never admits anything. Any other failure still refuses: another code, another status, a body that is no JSON, the same answer on another read (`roleAssignments`, …) or on a next page after a first one read. One rule (`eregion_core.inscription.no_pim_license`) for the three readers: IDI1's guard of an application, IDI1b's of a test user (`GuardResult.notes`), and IR1b's identity executor contract (`Verdict.notes`, `glorfindel identity-contract`).
  - A refused read of Graph now says Graph's error code next to its status (`HTTP 400 BadRequest reading …`, `Graph a répondu 400 BadRequest`): data from outside, filtered — ASCII letters and `_` only (`Authorization_RequestDenied` is said), at most 64, else nothing.
  - Orodruin gets it from a release that carries IDI1p, then `make orodruin-push REF=<that release>`; H0 gets it through its CD.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_identity_inscription.py`, `tests/unit/test_user_inscription.py`, `tests/unit/test_identity_contract.py` (fake Graphs; GraphReader's own answers); mutants M4470–M4479.
- **The invariant machine sees again, on its own, three network-response mutants the 0.5.8 release gate
  found only by their frozen sequences** (MUT4, #647; `docs/design/preuves.md`). M19 (a block verified
  against the addresses recorded when it was written), M31 (one deny's priority used for both directions)
  and M38 (a block gone behind the quarantine, unsaid) were `FIXED-ONLY` at the gate (500 sequences, two
  rounds). Nothing they measure had changed since the 0.5.7: each needs three or four precise steps,
  which the machine drew together in a few sequences in a thousand. In CI, Hypothesis loads its `ci`
  profile on its own (`derandomize=True`): the machine's sequences come from a seed computed on the
  `IsolationMachine` source, so the gate's two rounds replay the same 500 sequences, and any edit of the
  class (I3K's) draws another set — the 0.5.8's held none of the three. A composite rule,
  `a_hold_moved_under_it`, makes the three states common: a VM blocked, then an address of its NIC
  changed; a VM isolated by rules, then one of our outbound denies moved behind a customer's outbound
  allow; a VM blocked then isolated by quarantine, then the block's rules taken off by a
  `terraform apply`, and the reassertion. Each is now caught by about one sequence in thirty to fifty.
  No oracle changed, none added; the CI derandomization itself is left as it is (a decision for the gate).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_invariants.py` (`a_hold_moved_under_it`).

## [0.5.7] — 2026-11-06 — "the plan on the card"

### Security
- **oauthlib 4.0.0 → 4.0.1 in the locks, nothing else moved** (DEP2, #590; `docs/operations.md`,
  « Verrou des dépendances »). The public advisory GHSA-3jp2-3mrg-2vxc (high, oauthlib 3.3.0 to
  4.0.1 excluded): the device code flow's token endpoint did not validate `device_code`, a server
  side defect. oauthlib comes through chromadb → kubernetes → requests-oauthlib; no code of Eregion
  imports it, and requests-oauthlib stays at 2.0.0. Only `requirements/dev.txt` and
  `requirements/glorfindel.txt` change: oauthlib's line, its two hashes, and their header;
  `annatar.txt` and `audit.txt` are byte for byte the same. The lock of the image changes, so H0's
  CD waits for the harness's attestation, replayed on this lock.
  - `make lock UPGRADE_PACKAGE=<name>[,<name>]` raises those packages alone, to their newest
    release before `UPGRADE_DATE` (the day by default), only in the locks that pin them; a name no
    lock pins is refused before uv runs. Each lock keeps the date it admits a package at in its
    header (`--exclude-newer-package`), and `make lock` passes it again while it postdates
    `LOCK_DATE`: without it, the next `make lock` at the same date took oauthlib back to 4.0.0,
    silently (`scripts/lock_flags.py`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_dependency_lock.py`.
- **The bot no longer writes the console's directory** (FS1c, #571; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`). Its gestures go to `runs/bot/` (`manual_actions.jsonl`; the CLI it runs carries `EREGION_RUNS_WRITER=bot`) and it no longer mounts `runs/console`: a compromised bot can no longer replace or drown the console's requests of the signed action queue, nor make a critical card blame the console. The console mounts `runs/bot` read-only; the feed, the overview's lifts and the containment analysis read both hands.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue_async.py`, `tests/unit/test_runs_mounts.py`, `tests/unit/test_profiles.py`.
- **Orodruin's Graph guarantee is strict even undeclared: a directory role makes the contract passive, `EREGION_TENANT_ID` set or not** (IDI1s, #559 ; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « IDI1 »). Measured on 05/11 by Jonathan, the first `make orodruin-check` after v0.5.6 was `armed` with no `graph` note: the token of Orodruin's managed identity carries no `wids` outside `APP_DEFAULT_WIDS`, so the hypothesis holds and the list stays that value alone. A `wids` outside it is now `graph_directory_role`, passive, where it was only said without the declaration. The declaration decides two things only: an app role in the token (undeclared, any is `graph_undeclared_right`) and a Graph token that cannot be had — passive declared, only said undeclared, since an Orodruin without a Graph right must not fall and having no token is no privilege. Mutants M4225–M4229, M4240; M3941 (IDI1), whose code is gone, is retired. Microsoft does not document the `wids` value of a principal without a role: changed on their side, the contract turns passive (closed). Undeclared, a passing failure of the Graph token alone at startup leaves Orodruin armed without its `roles` and `wids` seen — bounded: a failing IMDS fails the ARM read too, the identity guard then refuses every identity, and no injector uses a token that cannot be had.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_orodruin.py -k graph`.
- **The identity executor's contract, read at its start: armed only within a closed list of rights, else passive and it refuses to start** (IR1b, #572; `docs/design/reponse-identite.md` §3.4, `docs/operations.md`, « L'exécuteur d'identité »). `glorfindel/providers/azure/identity_contract.py` reads the executor's own Graph token first, with no call (an application's token, of the declared tenant and application; its `roles` and `wids` within the list), then its rights again on Microsoft Graph, read only: its application permissions, each named by its resource (Microsoft Graph only), its directory role assignments, active (each at its one scope) and eligible (none), its groups (none: a group can carry a role), and what it owns (applications and their principals only — never itself, never an identity of `never_target`, never a privileged one, judged by IDI1's own `privilege_refusal`: the principal of each holds no directory role, active or eligible, no group, no application permission (not even a read) and owns nothing in turn; beyond 20 owned objects its start does not read them whole and stays passive; neither its Azure roles nor its delegated grants are read, IDI1's limit). A scoped role seen in `wids` is held at the tenant: beyond. The closed list: `Application.ReadWrite.OwnedBy` and `User.RevokeSessions.All`; Directory Readers at the tenant, User Administrator at the bench's administrative unit only (its id pinned), Attribute Assignment Reader at Eregion's attribute set only. Any other right is `beyond_contract` — a refused one (`RoleManagement.ReadWrite.Directory`, `Application.ReadWrite.All`, `Directory.ReadWrite.All`… Global Administrator, Privileged Role Administrator…) named so; a contract it cannot read is `contract_unreadable`: passive both, and `start` raises `Passive`. A role is known by its definition's id, never by its name nor by the `templateId` a custom role may set; a permission is held only when the token and the assignments both say so; a right it lacks takes its verbs away (`Verdict.allows`) without disarming it. Its own certificate only, never `DefaultAzureCredential` (on H0 the VM's token endpoint is the watch's). `glorfindel identity-contract` says the verdict, exit 0 only if armed. No verb is written: IR1c.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_identity_contract.py` (the fake Graph of IR1a, `tests/unit/fake_graph.py`, now with owned objects, groups, a principal by its appId, eligible instances, who holds a role on a principal); mutants M4070–M4083, M4145–M4154, M4165–M4169.
- **No value copied into `host.env` injects a variable or breaks the file** (NT1, #583 ; `docs/operations.md`, « L'exécuteur d'identité », geste 6). `infra/glorfindel-host/host_env.py` wrote every value as `KEY=value` unchecked: a line break in one (measured with `docker compose config`) added variables compose reads — `EREGION_ACTION_QUEUE=1` and `EREGION_QUEUE_KEYS=console:…`, the signed queue switched on with a key taken from that value —, and a lone quote passed `--check`, then made compose refuse the whole file: neither the watch, nor the console, nor the bot started on H0. Now a value to write holding a line break, a carriage return, a quote, a `$` (compose interpolates it: `'a$HOME'`, unwrapped, would give the watch a path) or a backslash is refused (one pair of quotes around it is unwrapped, as compose reads it), the variable named, never the value — any variable copied, not only the never_target list's; `--check` refuses a line whose value opens a quote it never closes, or holds a backslash inside quotes (compose reads `"abc\"` as an escaped quote: the value takes the next line — `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY` arriving empty — or the file is refused).
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`.
- **The catalog decides an atomic scenario's steady-state probes too** (VM3b, #568; `docs/azure.md`, « Les sondes aussi »). A probe's `verify` is a command Run Command runs as root, three times a run: `catalog_destructive` now compares the scenario's `steady_state` probes to its entry's — in order, `verify` and `expect` included — as it already compared `engine_ref`, `engine_args` and `revert`. Another probe makes the run unknown, so destructive: refused in production, and refused everywhere when the scenario calls itself `destructive: false`. The four shipped atomic scenarios, R1's `account-creation-atomic` included, have exactly their entry's probes.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_vm3b_suites.py`; mutants M4100–M4102 (`scripts/mutants/VM3b.py`).
- **An `engine_args` value never starts with `-`** (VM3b, #568; `annatar/engines/atomic.py`). In an argument's place, `-rf` or `--help` would be an option of the command it lands in, not a value: the adapter refuses it before anything is armed, as it refuses a value outside `[A-Za-z0-9._/:-]`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_vm3b_suites.py`; mutant M4108.

### Added
- **The watch picks the signed action queue up, and the console and the bot deposit in it — behind `EREGION_ACTION_QUEUE=1` only** (FS1b, #563, second lot of FS1, #413; `docs/design/file-actions-signee.md`, §8). The flag is false by default: without it nothing changes — no queue is read, no request written, the console and the bot keep their direct call (a test holds it for each of the three).
  - **The watch's queue** (`glorfindel/queue_watch.py`): a thread of its own, never the watch's loop nor its heartbeat, reads `runs/console/queue/` and `runs/bot/queue/` every second (16 requests per service and per pass at most). Each request: its name must be its nonce; a receipt `received` first; then the verification of FS1a; its card's proposal that this watch signed, not consumed — or, for a verb without a card, what the watch holds (`nothing_to_undo`); **the console's guards replayed by the same code** (`glorfindel/approval.py`: the one-click approval, its age read from the proposal's signed `issued_at`, never from `last_seen`, a restore after the card; one host address out of `never_block`, a reach of `vm` or `subnet`; the VM in the watch's perimeter; the block of this IP on this VM; the human a bot's request names, read against the watch's own copy of `DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS` and `DISCORD_ALLOWED_ROLES`); a read-only watch refuses; the proposal consumed (one proposal, one execution, a failed one included); the receipt `accepted`; the execution in a worker, under `_vm_lock`, with the watch's functions (the CLI's release and reset moved to `glorfindel/gestures.py`, which the CLI calls too), checked after (`verification_failed` card when not `True`), the card settled; `done` or `failed`.
  - **Receipts and status signed by the watch** (`runs/glorfindel/queue/<service>-<id>.json`, `status.json`: the posture, the keys pinned, the refusals counted) under `eregion.action-queue/1`. A forged, replayed, foreign-key or other-service request makes a critical card (`queue_tampered`), decided by the code, no action, at most one per service and per hour; an unreadable register, a `queue_unavailable` card.
  - **The console's write routes** (approve, release, reset, unblock, extend to subnet, snapshot; `eregion_console/action_requests.py`) deposit a request signed by the console's key and answer from the receipt (`via: "queue"`); **the bot's Revert** deposits `reset` bound to its `verification_failed` card. Each service draws its key at its start with the flag (`~/.eregion/console/queue_ed25519`, `~/.eregion/bot/queue_ed25519`); `make queue-keys` and `make glorfindel-host-queue-keys` give the line to pin at the watch (`EREGION_QUEUE_KEYS`) — prepared, not to be run before FS1c.
  - **A new writer of `runs/`**, `bot/` (its requests); the watch mounts it read-only, the bot mounts `runs/glorfindel/queue` read-only (that subdirectory only) and `~/.eregion/bot`; the watch mounts `~/.eregion/watch-queue` (its register) and receives `EREGION_QUEUE_KEYS` and the bot's allowed lists. `make glorfindel-start` and H0's `deploy.sh` make `~/.eregion/bot`, `~/.eregion/watch-queue` and `runs/glorfindel/queue` as the operator first.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue_watch.py` (end to end on the in-memory Azure, each refusal, a replay, a forged card, another service's key, the flag absent, an unreadable register, a stop between the claim and the execution, the console's and the bot's deposits, the mounts); mutants M3975–M4014 (`scripts/mutants/FS1b.py`).
- **The signed action queue, asynchronous — behind `EREGION_ACTION_QUEUE=1`, still off by default** (FS1c, #571; `docs/design/file-actions-signee.md` §9, `docs/design/console.md`). A write route of the console deposits its request and answers at once with its id; `GET /api/queue/<id>` says its receipt (`waiting`, `received`, `accepted`, then the route's former answer, or `not_taken`, `untrusted`, `unknown`), and no thread of the console waits for the watch. The gesture is noted once, by whichever read settles it — a page closed before its receipt included. The bot's Revert says each state of its receipt in the VM's thread, holding no thread, and is noted once in `runs/bot/manual_actions.jsonl` from its receipt. The write buttons are greyed by the status the watch signs (missing, stale, read-only, another key pinned), the reason said.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue_async.py`, `tests/unit/test_action_queue_watch.py`.
- **The switch prepared, not made** (FS1c, #571; `docs/operations.md`, « La mesure au banc »). `make glorfindel-host-queue-keys` draws each service's key in its own container (`pin <service> --draw`) before the flag; `host_env.py` never copies this workstation's queue variables, and `--check` refuses the flag without the console's key (or the bot's while it runs); the watch says each missing key at its start. The order of Jonathan's gestures for the bench measure: the keys, the flag, one approval end to end, the way back.
- **A test user of Entra ID is enrolled for the bench by two halves, the way a VM is: the bench's restricted administrative unit holds it, and the attribute `Eregion.eregionTarget` / `eregionZone` on the user names the bench and the zone** (IDI1b, #573; `docs/operations.md`, « Les utilisateurs de test du banc (IDI1b) » ; `docs/architecture.md`, « Un utilisateur de test d'Entra ID »). `user_enrollment` and `GraphEnrollment.check_user` (`eregion_core/inscription.py`) refuse a half missing, two halves that disagree (another bench, a zone that is not the unit's), a unit that is not restricted, is dynamic, or is not the pinned one (`EREGION_BENCH_ADMIN_UNIT_ID`, never looked up by its name), a guest, a member of another unit, an identity of `never_target`, a directory role active or eligible, a group, an object owned, an application role; a read that fails refuses. `python -m eregion_core.inscription user <id>` says what may run on a user.
  - Hypothesis 13 settled on the locked provider (`azuread` 3.10.0, its schema): it cannot make an administrative unit restricted, nor define an attribute set, a definition or a user's attribute; it can pose a membership and a directory role at a unit's scope. `infra/terraform/scripts/entra_bench_unit.py` does the rest through Microsoft Graph, under the operator's az login: `make celebrimbor-unit-prepare` (read only without `YES=1`; `UNIT=<id>` reads the pinned unit again, restricted and not dynamic, or refused; without `UNIT`, a unit already of that name is refused, never a second one; a unit not yet readable after its creation is read again, then said, never to delete).
  - `infra/terraform/entra_bench_unit.tf`, off by default (`entra_bench_unit_enabled`), the canonical instance only: the unit read by its pinned id (output `entra_bench_unit_id`), test users with no role, group nor licence (`account_enabled` left to IR1's executor), their attribute posed before their membership, and — for IR1's identity executor (`entra_identity_executor_object_id`) — User Administrator at the unit's scope only, Directory Readers at the tenant, Attribute Assignment Reader at Eregion's set, each role template checked against the tenant's own list at plan time. `make celebrimbor-users-plan` / `celebrimbor-users-up`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_user_inscription.py` (a fake Graph), `tests/unit/test_bench_users.py` (the files, the Make targets, the script against a fake az), `infra/terraform/tests/entra_bench_unit.tftest.hcl` (`terraform test`, mock providers). Mutants M4085–M4099, M4135–M4143 (`scripts/mutants/IDI1b.py`).
- **Every Entra ID identity card carries its plan, and a Verify button that reads Microsoft Graph again, read only** (IR1a, #552; `docs/design/reponse-identite.md` §3.2, `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/console.md`). The plan is written by the code from the log's row, never by the model: the identity executor's verbs in their order (remove the added credential, or the one an unusual sign-in used; disable the consent's client application, never the resource's principal), then the steps for a human (remove a grant, an app role assignment or a directory role assignment; revoke a user's sessions; renew the other credentials), each with its exact ids read on Graph (the object, the keyId, the grant's or the assignment's id), its complete command, what to keep before, its side effect and its way back. A parameter neither in the row nor read is marked « not read » and its command said incomplete, never invented; nothing of the log that is not an id reaches a command. Glorfindel's own identity gets no executor verb, its credentials renewed first; an identity of IDI1's `never_target` list turns a verb into a human's step. Verify: the console writes the request on the card (it never reads Graph), the watch reads Graph and notes held, not held or unreadable on the card's thread — never held on a right missing, an error, a delay, an object or a field Graph did not give, a role whose name it does not read (a role is compared by its definition's id or its name, aliases folded: « Company Administrator » is Global Administrator), or a remediation step it cannot check (identifying a consent's client, renewing Glorfindel's credentials). The plan's reads of Graph have 20 s in all and stop at the first delay or missing right — the anchor VM's queue never waits longer; a redirection is never followed. Without a Graph right (H0 today) the plan keeps what the log gives and Verify says unreadable; the read permissions to grant the watch are in `docs/operations.md` (« La lecture de Microsoft Graph par la watch »). No executor exists yet (IR1c): every item is done by hand.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_identity_plan.py` (a fake Graph, `tests/unit/fake_graph.py`), `tests/unit/test_console_identity_plan.py`; mutants M3880–M3899, M3955–M3964.
- **The identity executor's application in H0's Terraform, off by default** (IR1b, #572; `infra/glorfindel-host/identity_executor.tf`, `docs/operations.md`, « L'exécuteur d'identité »). `identity_executor_enabled = true` (`identity_executor.auto.tfvars`, ignored by git) gives a single-tenant application and its own principal — never H0's managed identity — exactly the two Graph application permissions of the contract, `Log Analytics Reader` on the bench's workspace (to read the evidence again), and, when given, the public part of its certificate (a private key is refused by the plan; the key is drawn on H0). Its scoped directory roles are IDI1b's, the ownership of enrolled applications IDI1's. Applied by Jonathan, who consents the permissions; nothing is applied by a session. Outputs `identity_executor_client_id` and `identity_executor_object_id`. The module now locks `azuread` (CI4) and every one of its tests mocks it.
  - Tests: `infra/glorfindel-host/tests/identity_executor.tftest.hcl` (`terraform test`, mock providers), `tests/unit/test_identity_executor_host.py`.
- **Three more attack/detection pairs on VM: cron persistence, a Unix shell, Linux log clearing** (VM3, #548; `docs/azure.md`, « VM3 : trois paires de plus » ; `docs/operations.md`, « Les règles auditd de VM3 »). T1053.003, T1059.004 and T1070.002 move from `planned` to `implemented` in `technique_catalog.yaml`, each a pair. Red: a pinned Atomic Red Team test (`engine: atomic`, same vendored provenance as T1136.001 — repo, commit `9f6a1eab…`, sha256 in `annatar/atomics/PIN`), with a scenario in `annatar/scenarios/azure/` (steady-state probes prove the effect and the return to the initial state), played only from Orodruin on an enrolled target. The tests chosen run on Ubuntu with no outbound dependency: cron « Replace crontab » (435057fb), « Create and Execute Bash Shell Script » (7e7ac3ed) and « Overwrite Linux Log » (d304b2dc). Two are destructive — bench only, refused in production by the inscription (`safe_target: sandbox_disposable`): T1070.002 overwrites a log, T1053.003 replaces root's whole crontab and restores it from a predictable `/tmp` path, so its probes refuse a start where `/tmp/notevil` or `/tmp/persistevil` exist; the upstream cleanup leaves both, and the scenario's own revert removes them after it, which the after-probe verifies. Blue: three shipped rules that read telemetry Glorfindel already reads — `t1053-003-cron-crontab-replace` (Syslog facility cron), `t1070-002-log-tamper` and `t1059-004-shell-from-world-writable` (auditd via Syslog facility local6) — each with a threshold in its query and tested against realistic benign noise that must never fire (apt's cron, logrotate, unattended-upgrades; for `log_tamper` rsyslog's `rs:main Q:Reg` thread, sshd on wtmp/btmp/lastlog, useradd, waagent and the extensions under `/var/log/azure/`). `log_tamper` judges the writer by `exe` and the paths it touched, never by `comm`; the two auditd rules keep each record bounded and carry no free text of their own, so a writer's name never characterizes a card (useradd is no `account_creation` there). The coverage matrix (C1) shows them.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_vm3_pairs.py`; mutants M3840–M3859 and M3930–M3939 (`scripts/mutants/VM3.py`).
- **`engine_args` on an experiment: overrides for a vendored atomic's input arguments, held and vouched for by the catalog** (VM3, #548; `annatar/engines/atomic.py`, `schemas/experiment.schema.json`, `schemas/technique_catalog.schema.json`). So a pinned test runs faithfully without editing the vendored file — the shell test's ping goes to the loopback (no egress), the log overwrite to a disposable path (the cleanup restores it). The adapter refuses a key the test does not declare and a value outside `[A-Za-z0-9._/:-]` (nothing a shell would read otherwise); the parser refuses `engine_args` that are no mapping; `catalog_destructive` compares them to the catalog entry's — others make the run unknown, so destructive, never in production (SEC5, V-19). The campaign synthesizer resolves and writes the entry's own.
- **`revert` on an atomic experiment: what the upstream cleanup leaves, removed after it** (VM3, #548; `annatar/engines/atomic.py` `revert_commands`, `annatar/runner/engine.py`, both schemas). The E1 `revert` field now runs for `engine: atomic`: after the test's own cleanup, and only once it ran (a failed cleanup may still need those files), before the after-probes. One action, `remove_files`: paths, their `#{var}` filled, each a plain path inside `/tmp/` or `/var/tmp/`, sent as `rm -f --`. Anything else is refused before the contract is armed; the revert counts in the call cap; a failed or skipped revert is never a clean run, and the recovery hint gives it after the cleanup. Like `engine_args`, the catalog decides: a revert other than the entry's makes the run unknown, so destructive. T1053.003 uses it to remove `/tmp/notevil` and `/tmp/persistevil`; the vendored file is untouched.

### Changed
- **The release gate measures every mutant without the work done twice** (CI10a, #580; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « Chaque mutant mesuré, sans le travail en double » ; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »). Every mutant is still measured at every release, on the tree published, at 500 sequences; none is taken off, and CI6e's known survivors hold. The CI9 study measured where the gate's 42 runner hours went.
  - **One round where no chance plays.** A second round draws new seeds for Hypothesis only. A target keeps its rounds when one of its files imports Hypothesis (the invariant machines, the property tests), races (`tests/unit/test_locks.py`, `tests/unit/test_exercise.py`), cannot be read, or when its mutant is `TIMING`; any other target has one round (`rounds_of`). A test that starts a thread or a process — itself, or through a function or a class of its file — keeps its rounds too (M1346's stuck race, M3550's two isolations at once, `test_runs_channel.py`, `test_action_queue_watch.py`), and the control set always does. A row says its rounds (`1/1`), a shard's `--json` too (`mutant_rounds`, `mutant_targets`).
  - **The CD's mutants on the tests that catch them.** `python scripts/mutation_check.py --narrow FILE` runs each mutant whose target is FILE whole once without `-x`, and keeps its two fastest catchers as its target when they catch it in every round — else the whole file (`scripts/mutants/narrowed.json`). An entry applies only while its lot still targets that whole file; `scripts/mutant_anchors.py` says an entry that no longer applies, or a node pytest no longer collects (one `--collect-only` of their files: a parameter's id is positional). M1014 keeps its target.
  - **`--skip-machine-baseline`**: a shard runs no invariant machine unmutated, and says how many targets lost theirs — the gate's `invariants` job runs the whole suite at 2 000 sequences on the same tree. The rest of a target still runs unmutated. The Monday measure and a local run do not pass it (`tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py` holds `mutants.yml` to it).
  - **`--machines`** measures only the mutants whose target runs an invariant machine (a file defining a `RuleBasedStateMachine`); `--total DIR --machines` judges those only, and refuses a shard measured otherwise. Without it, nothing changes. The control set keeps its own.
  - **One pool** for a shard's unmutated runs and its mutants, the heaviest first; the unmutated runs in a copy of their own, so nothing they write reaches a mutant's copy. An unmutated run that fails still makes the shard no measure.
  - `--update-durations` takes each measure as this tree runs it: scaled to today's rounds, and dropped when it was made on another target than today's (a narrowed CD mutant waits for the gate's next measure). `scripts/mutants/durations.json` refreshed from the gate's three runs of 05/11, their unmutated runs included.
  - The fake `docker` of `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py` starts with `python -S`: it needs the standard library only, and the file starts it about 4 300 times.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`, `tests/unit/test_mutant_anchors.py`. Mutants M4170–M4189, M4255–M4259 (`scripts/mutants/CI10a.py`).
- **The CI in its place, on the workflows' side** (CI10b, #581; the CI9 study, taken whole by Jonathan
  on 05/11; `docs/operations.md`, « La CI au bon endroit » and « La porte de release »).
  - `ci.yml` runs `scripts/mutant_anchors.py` on each pull request and each push to `main`: a mutant
    made stale by another merge is seen at the merge, no longer at the next release gate (L16).
  - The suite runs in four processes (`pytest -n 4 --dist load`), on both Pythons, at the same budgets
    (200 sequences on a pull request, 500 on a push): pytest-xdist joins the dev extra and the dev lock
    (`make lock`; the images' locks unchanged). Measured on a shared workstation at 200 sequences: 8 223
    tests passing both ways, 152 to 183 s in 4 processes against 483 s in one (L8).
  - Components runs in one job on a pull request: each component's own two checks, the others absent,
    each run whatever the one before said, then the compose profiles; core, Annatar and Gothmog wait for
    the install alone, Glorfindel for ChromaDB's model too; its actions pinned by commit, the release
    gate's own. On a push to `main`, a job each as before. No required check changes name (L11).
  - The release gate: ten shards of mutants instead of fourteen (L5), no unmutated run of an invariant
    machine in a shard (`--skip-machine-baseline`, CI10a's harness; L3), its tests in two jobs side by
    side — Azure's machine alone, every other test in four processes — each test in exactly one (L6);
    its total no longer waits for macOS, which says itself (L7). `release-draft` takes a gate's run
    still going for a job that informs alone as green once its total and every job that blocks are; a
    run still going whose jobs cannot be read is refused with its error, never « en cours ».
  - The Monday measure keeps only the mutants whose target runs an invariant machine (`--machines`) and
    the control set, at 1 000 sequences, their total judging those alone (`--total --machines`, L13):
    the only verdicts a budget moves; the gate still measures every mutant at each release.
  - Unchanged: the night at 10 000 sequences, both Pythons, 200 sequences on a pull request, the gate's
    budget (500, two rounds).
  - Every option a workflow passes to `scripts/mutation_check.py` is one its parser knows, by its whole
    name; L3's test holds tight (the flag sought in every job's text, both invariant jobs in the total's
    needs at 500 sequences or more, no machine taken out by `--deselect` or `--ignore`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`, `tests/unit/test_release.py`,
    `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`; mutants M4190–M4199, M4235–M4239 and M4260–M4264
    (`scripts/mutants/CI10b.py`); M1441, M3597, M3604 and M3605 retargeted to the new workflows.
- **The mutants are red as soon as one survives outside the known survivors** (CI6e, #553; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « Rouge dès qu'un mutant survit hors de la liste connue »). The release gate and the Monday measure went red below `min_caught`, 14 of about 2 850 mutants: it held almost nothing, and six survivors had no written reason.
  - A known survivor is a mutant whose reason opens with a mark the harness reads, then a colon (`KNOWN_MARKS`): `EQUIVALENT` and `MULTI-HOST`, not counted; `TIMING`, counted and known. `STALE` stays uncounted.
  - Any counted mutant that survives a round (`SURVIVES`, `flaky`, `FIXED-ONLY`) without such a reason makes `--total` exit 1, said with its id and its title; so does a run of its own (`--only`, without `--json`). A shard (`--json`) writes and says it, and exits 0: its result is always uploaded, the total holds the gate. A known survivor caught in every round is said, never red, so that its reason is taken off by hand. The control set is said, never held.
  - `--min-caught` is gone, refused if given (exit 2): the harness and the workflows always come from one tree, and a flag kept without effect would hide that it holds nothing. `.github/workflows/mutants.yml` loses its `min_caught` input; both workflows' totals call `--total` alone.
  - `scripts/mutant_anchors.py` names a reason whose mark the harness does not read as a fault: its mutant would be taken for an unknown survivor; so is a `TIMING` reason that names no race test. A `TIMING` survivor caught in no round of a run is said apart by the total, never red.
  - A mutant gone `STALE` no longer passes in silence: `tests/unit/test_mutant_anchors.py` checks every mutant of the tree applies, in the ordinary suite (under a second). A meta-test, never a killer: in a copy the harness mutates, the mutant applied has left its own text, so the harness sets `EREGION_MUTANT_RUN=1` in each run of a copy and the test skips there, saying why — else it would catch M3186, M3585, and any mutant whose target holds it, by construction.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`, `tests/unit/test_mutant_anchors.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`. Mutants M3915–M3924, M3965–M3968, M4020, M4021 (`scripts/mutants/CI6e.py`).
- **Orodruin's Terraform tests run in the CI** (CI7, #554; `docs/operations.md`, « Les providers de
  Terraform, verrouillés »). `terraform -chdir=infra/orodruin test -no-color` after its `validate`,
  in `ci.yml` and in the release gate, after the module's retried `init` (CI8): the watch channel's
  writers (WC1, `watch_writers.tftest.hcl`) and Microsoft Graph's reads (IDI1,
  `graph_read.tftest.hcl`), planned against mock providers, no identifier. The test itself is never
  retried. The step « Terraform validate — Orodruin » is now « Terraform test — Orodruin (mock
  providers) », as the other modules' (a step, not a check: no required check changes).
  `TF_AWAITING_STEP` is empty.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (each module's `test` run, after its `init`).
- **The CI's downloads are retried, never a test** (CI8, #566; `docs/operations.md`, « Ce que la CI
  retente »). A repository loop, `bash scripts/retry.sh <command>`, no third-party action: three
  attempts, 10 then 30 s apart, each said (a failure as a `::warning::` of the run), the last
  failure's exit code after the third. A SIGINT or a SIGTERM (a cancelled job, its bound) stops it,
  trapped: the command is never run again, even when it catches the signal and exits 1 as pip and
  Terraform do. It wraps only the download of each network step: the locks' `pip install`
  (`ci.yml`, the release gate, `mutants.yml`, `components.yml`, `invariants.yml`, `pip-audit.yml`),
  Terraform from HashiCorp's apt repository (its key's fingerprint check outside the loop;
  `apt-get update -o APT::Update::Error-Mode=any`, so that a transient error on an index fails and
  is retried, never a `W:` and 0), each module's
  `terraform init` in a step of its own before its `test` or `validate`, ChromaDB's embedding model
  fetched in a step of its own before the tests (`scripts/chroma_model.py`), and publish.yml's
  pinned `syft` and `cosign`. Never a test, the mutation, the invariants nor a check: a flaky test is
  never masked. Actions (`setup-python`, `docker/build-push-action`) are not wrapped.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (the steps retried, listed; none that measures; every
    known download retried; retry.sh against a fake command and a fake `sleep`).
- **The watch's register of the action queue is written durably and bounds a service's claims** (FS1b, #563; `docs/design/file-actions-signee.md`, §7). A nonce claimed, and a proposal consumed (`consumed.json`, new), are fsync'd with their directory before the watch answers; a service's rate is counted before its nonce is claimed, so a compromised service key never fills the register; an unreadable register refuses whatever it holds, and never raises.
- **A card seen again keeps its proposal while it stands and is younger than 10 min** (FS1b, #563): re-signed at each refresh, its digest changed under a request just named for it (`proposal_mismatch`).
- **The console imports the queue's list of one-click approvable cards and calls the shared guards** (`action_queue.APPROVABLE_TYPES`, `glorfindel/approval.py`) instead of its own copies; their behaviour is unchanged (a card dated without its zone now reads « sans date lisible » instead of failing). A restore record of the VM there but unreadable (a list, a date without its zone, a field missing, a file that cannot be read) now refuses the one-click approval, « Restore illisible : agir depuis la CLI » — it used to fail the route, or read as no restore.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue.py`, `tests/unit/test_action_proposal.py`, `tests/unit/test_dependency_failures.py`.
- **The console refuses a fifth gesture in flight, without the flag too — visible on H0 at the next release** (FS1c, #571; `docs/design/console.md`). Its write routes (approve, release, reset, unblock, extend to subnet, snapshot) run in an executor of their own, 4 gestures at once: past them, a click is answered `503` (`busy`: « 4 gestures already in flight in this console: try again once one ends ») at once, nothing done. A gesture takes its one slot at its entry and holds it to its end: once accepted, its action, its check and its note always run — never « busy » after the isolation was made.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue_async.py`.
- **`IDENTITY_ACTIONS`, the closed list of the seven identity verbs** (IR1a, #552; `glorfindel/response/policy.py`): disjoint from `AUTONOMOUS_ACTIONS`, within `HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. The model never reads them: the prompt names `MODEL_GATED_ACTIONS`, the gated actions a decision may name, and is byte for byte what it was. An identity decision keeps `action: ""`, and both locks of C2 hold.
- **The identity executor is never a target** (IR1b, #572; `eregion_core/never_target.py`): `EREGION_IDENTITY_EXECUTOR_CLIENT_ID`, read strictly, joins the shared list — never the target of its own verbs, nor of a technique on Orodruin.
- **The auditd human-root reading (AUD1) stays the default for every T1059.004 rule, with one explicit exemption** (VM3, #548; `glorfindel/auditd.py`, `EXEMPT_RULES`). T1059.004 now carries a second shipped rule, `t1059-004-shell-from-world-writable`, whose rows (a Run Command exec, auid unset) are not of the human-root form: it alone, by name, is read as it comes. Every other rule of the technique — a client's under any name included — keeps settle, the fold and the rule window, and what is not of the human-root form stays an unreadable card (fail closed). An audit event row without a `SyslogMessage` of its own is told apart by its `AuditEvent` (`_row_identity`): two events of the same instant stay two.
- **Proposed telemetry, off by default** (VM3, #548; `infra/terraform/`). The cron facility is added to the bench DCR behind `cron_logs_enabled` (default false, `monitoring.tf`). The auditd rules the two auditd pairs read (`shell_exec`, `log_tamper`) are a script, `infra/terraform/scripts/vm3_auditd.sh`, laid on a bench VM by Run Command — not Terraform: Azure allows one CustomScript extension per VM, which AUD1 holds.

### Fixed
- **The six survivors without a reason, each killed by a test** (CI6e, #553). M1014: the CD builds nothing since CI3, and `--build-only`'s image is measured by `tests/unit/test_host_deploy.py`, now in its target. M1223, and M1224 (until now `TIMING`): a reader reads the escalations store at a quarter, a half, three quarters and all but the last character of every write, two cards of two lengths, and finds it whole — each killed by this test alone (15 of 15), the unmutated code green 30 of 30. M1461: a restore's claim refuses on its own a job started in between, or a claim a dead process left. M2915 and M3077: another process holds the flock and the write here waits, every time — the race tests saw the loss only at times. M3007: the overview says a run report it refuses in the console's log, once.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_locks.py`, `tests/unit/test_entries_calls.py`, `tests/unit/test_exercise.py`, `tests/unit/test_indicators.py`.
- **FS1b's review** (FS1c, #571). What remains synchronous of a gesture — the deposit, and the direct call until FS1e — runs in the console's own bounded executor (4 at once; past it, 503 at once); a receipt signed by another key than the watch's pinned one is said with both fingerprints and the gesture, never as « not taken »; `proposal_consumed` says « already judged, done or failed » and what to do after a failure; a `verification_failed` card born of the queue bears its request's `signal_id` (`queue-<service>-<id>`); the routes release, revert, unblock, promote and snapshot are tested without the flag.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue_async.py`; mutants M4040–M4069 (`scripts/mutants/FS1c.py`).
- **The 17 survivors that held the 0.5.7 release gate red, each caught or said** (MUT3, #599; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « Les 17 survivants de la première porte sous CI6e »). They were there at the 0.5.6 gate too, which only asked for a `--min-caught`; CI6e holds them since.
  - A FIXED-ONLY mutant is caught by the suite (Jonathan's decision, 06/11): M10, M23, M24, M34, M35, M39, M40, M1961 and M1963 take the fixed sequence of `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py` that catches them, beside the machine (`TESTS_ALSO`, `scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`); CI6e's rule is unchanged for every other mutant. M41 (flaky) takes its fixed sequence too, M36 the unit test that already held its property.
  - Tests of their own where the named test no longer went the mutant's way: M3153 (a perimeter whose write fails again narrows its baseline before a refused card), M3282 (a rule this check did not read is never read deleted), M1552 (a failed release of a deleted VM keeps its NICs for the retry, once its block's state is lost too), M613 (every write of an AWS operation holds the instance's lock), M1645 (a re-block clears the entry's marks but not the episode open in memory: an uncovered card after it never silences the pair's next disappearance).
  - M864 rewritten: it called a name `quarantine.py` no longer imports since SEC4b, and only raised a `NameError` — an isolation that failed safe, never the bug it names.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`, `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`, `tests/unit/test_aws_adapter.py`. Mutants M4290, M4291 (`scripts/mutants/MUT3.py`).
- **The hosted watch reads the never_target list: an identity card's plan is no longer every step a human's on H0** (NT1, #583 ; `docs/operations.md`, « L'exécuteur d'identité », geste 6). `docker-compose.yml` passed none of the variables of `eregion_core/never_target.py` to the watch: on H0 the list was unreadable (no operator named), `covers()` held every id, and each verb of an identity plan (IR1a) became a human's step — closed, but the plan lost its use. Now the watch, and it alone, receives `EREGION_OPERATOR_OBJECT_ID`, `EREGION_PROTECTED_IDENTITIES` and `EREGION_IDENTITY_EXECUTOR_CLIENT_ID` (neither the console nor the bot reads the list; the injectors have theirs in `orodruin.env`). `infra/glorfindel-host/host_env.py` copies them from the operator's environment into `host.env`, each one's source a Terraform output: `operator_object_id` and `protected_identities` of `infra/orodruin`, `identity_executor_client_id` and `identity_executor_object_id` of `infra/glorfindel-host` (`host.env.example`). Writing `host.env` and `--check` (run by `make glorfindel-host-push`) say a variable left empty, or an entry that is no object id, with its source and never its value — and never refuse: an unreadable list stays a refusal of the verbs, not a breakdown.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`.
- **`make release-draft` judges the release gate by its jobs, never by the run's conclusion** (REL3, #569 ; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »). At the 0.5.6 (05/11), macOS, cancelled for want of a runner, set the run to `failure` while the total « release gate » and every job that blocks were green, and the draft was refused — against H1c's rule that macOS informs and never blocks.
