Once the run is found and complete, its jobs are read (one `GET …/actions/runs/{id}/jobs?filter=latest`): the total present, complete and green; every job that blocks green, even beside a green total; of a job re-run, its latest attempt stands, never its id alone (filter=latest measured on run 42061905987: after « Re-run failed jobs », attempt 2 gives the 21 jobs, no duplicate, those not re-run copied under new ids); two jobs of one name in one attempt refuse; a job that informs (macOS) red, cancelled or absent is said after the green line, never refusing. A read that fails, an answer that is not a list of jobs or without `total_count`, a full page, a job without a name refuse. The jobs that inform have one source: `INFORMATIVE` and `GATE_JOB` in `scripts/release.py`, read against `release-gate.yml` (the total's `INFORMATIVE`, each job's name, the total's `needs`, `continue-on-error`).
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_release.py` (the fake GitHub gives the workflow's own jobs, each matrix expanded) ; mutants M4035–M4039 and M4120–M4122 (`scripts/mutants/REL3.py`), and CI6's M3593 now aimed at the total job.
- **The reassertion of an isolation reads what its write answered: an isolation that does not hold is never « reposée une fois »** (RW2, #555 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). On AWS, `isolate_vm` answers `isolated` with `verified` False or None and the ENIs in `bypass`; on Azure, a deny written behind a customer's allow answers `isolated` with `bypass`. Both made the card say « reposée une fois. Si elle disparaît encore, Glorfindel alertera », and `reasserted_at` took the isolation out of every later put-back. Now the put-back is `partial`: the card says it does not hold, with the provider's words, and promises nothing. It is put back once all the same, as a block: `reasserted_at` is set, with the original `isolated_at` (Azure's write sets it anew, and the TTL would count from the put-back), and gone again it is « une deuxième fois », escalated alone — no longer a write and a card at every episode behind an allow. An isolation its put-back leaves `partial` (PartialActionError) is no longer read by any pass, and its card says so.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_reassert_verified.py`, `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`.
- **A perimeter pair the first time of the reassertion wrote partial is said, even when its second time never comes** (RW2, #555). The first time marks the entry `partial_unsaid` before `reasserted_at`; the second time takes it off once the card is said. Skipped (the VM's lock held, the pass cut off), the next pass reads the entry and says it, writing nothing (the mode may have changed since) — its card says the pair was written at the pass before, and `reasserted_at` keeps the first time's hour — before, the provider's `partial` mark held it out of every pass, its card never written. A block written whole takes the mark off.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_reassert_verified.py`.
- **The card of a write the provider said partial, re-read whole, says « le fournisseur l'a dit partiel : à vérifier »**, no longer « une écriture a échoué » (RW2, #555). Azure's `PartialActionError` on a put-back, and `_held_partial`'s sentence on its card, are fixed by a test.
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_reassert_verified.py`.
- **The put-back of an isolation reads its effect again: « reposée une fois » only of an isolation the cloud, read again, holds** (RW3, #575 ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). After `isolate_vm`, the reassertion reads the isolation again (`verify_isolation`), as the put-back of a block does (A0). On Azure, each deny is confirmed at its write, never what holds once everything is written: a rule taken off between two writes (an apply in a loop), an allow placed after the write's plan, a read refused — the card said « reposée une fois. Si elle disparaît encore, Glorfindel alertera ». Now a re-read that does not hold (« relue, elle ne tient pas », with the NICs left uncovered or the reason) or cannot be read (`verified` None, or a read that raises: « relue, elle n'est pas lisible ») is a put-back that does not hold (`partial`): the card says it after the provider's words, if any, and promises nothing. RW2's « once » applies: `reasserted_at` set (the original `isolated_at` kept), `drift_alerted_at` after the card; gone again, « une deuxième fois », escalated alone, nothing written. A bypass the re-read finds gets its `bypassed` card in the same pass, after the put-back's card. On AWS, the re-read comes on top of the adapter's own read-back: an ENI put back in its groups in between is said.
  - Cost, measured: one `verify_isolation` more per put-back — as many reads as the check before it (on Azure 1 + 4 per NIC for an isolation by rules: 5 for one NIC, 9 for two; 2 on AWS); none on a pass where everything holds, nor in `human_only`. No write more: the fake Azure's journal of writes is unchanged.
  - The invariant machine's I3 now also judges the reassertion's isolation cards: « reposée une fois » is never said of a VM a flow still reaches (`tests/unit/test_invariants.py`).
  - Tests : `tests/unit/test_reassert_reread.py`, `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`, `tests/unit/test_reassert_verified.py`.
- **T1053.003's revert waits on a probe, not on the call** (VM3b, #568; `annatar/runner/engine.py`, `annatar/engines/atomic.py`, both schemas; `docs/azure.md`). Each step of an atomic's `revert` now names the steady-state probe it waits on (`requires_probe`, one of the scenario's probes, else refused before the contract is armed): after the cleanup, that probe runs, and the step runs only once it holds again. Run Command does not raise on a script that failed, so a cleanup whose call passed no longer lets `/tmp/notevil` — root's crontab — be removed while the evil line is still installed: the revert is skipped, the file stays for the operator, and the run says so (`checks.revert: SKIPPED`, `metrics.revert_skipped`, the run's error). A gate probe that raises is no proof. The call cap counts the gate probe. T1053.003's revert waits on a new probe, `root-crontab-restored` (root's crontab identical to `/tmp/notevil`, its only copy), in the catalog and the scenario — `cron-entry-absent` alone would hold over a crontab the cleanup emptied.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_vm3b_suites.py`, `tests/unit/test_vm3_pairs.py`; mutants M4103–M4107, M4123–M4124.
- **`t1070-002-log-tamper` sets the Azure agent aside more narrowly, and says what it cannot** (VM3b, #568; `glorfindel/rules/azure/detection_rules.yaml`; `docs/azure.md`, « L'agent Azure dans `log_tamper` »). python3 is set aside only as the system's interpreter (`/usr/bin/python3` or `python3.N` exactly, no longer any exe that begins so), on `/var/log/azure/`, `waagent.log` and `unattended-upgrades`; the agent's binaries (`/var/lib/waagent/`, `/opt/microsoft/`) on `/var/log/azure/` only. Dropping python3 would lose the real noise — waagent, the monitoring agent's handler and unattended-upgrades are scripts, their `exe` is the interpreter, and auditd does not name the script — so the limit is written in the rule and the doc: any python3 script writing those files is not seen. The same holds for logrotate, set aside on all of `/var/log` since rotating every log is its job.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_vm3b_suites.py`; mutant M4109.

## [0.5.6] — 2026-11-05 — "who is who"

### Security
- **A signature is read in its one canonical form only** (FS1a, #551; `docs/design/watch-key.md`, « Fait dans FS1a »). The base64 of a signature was decoded leniently: 16 spellings of one signature held (the unused bits of its last character), and a trailing newline too — for the queue, 16 digests of one proposal, and the register of the proposals consumed gone round once FS1b runs. `watch_key.signature_bytes` takes only the form the watch writes (`base64.b64encode`: 86 characters and `==`, matched whole, decoded strictly, encoded back the same); the heartbeat's and the detections' checks (SEC19, SEC21) use it too. A watch of today or of before writes this form only: nothing it signs is refused.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue.py`; mutant M3953.
- **An identity of Entra ID is enrolled for the bench by two keys, one per plane, and passes the same guard as a VM** (IDI1, #546; `docs/architecture.md`, « Une identité d'Entra ID » ; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`, §5). An identity carries no ARM tag: the application's own Microsoft Graph tags name the bench and the zone (`eregion-target=<bench>`, `eregion-zone=<zone>`) **and** the bench's resource group names the application (`eregion-identities=<object id …>`), the two zones agreeing — either key alone enrolls nothing (any member may register an application and tag it, not write the bench's group). The guard (`eregion_core/inscription.py`, `identity_enrollment`, `check_identity`, `GraphEnrollment`) also refuses: a scope that names no tenant (`EREGION_TENANT_ID`); a reader's token of another tenant (`tid`); a multi-tenant application. **Never a target**, the shared list of `eregion_core/never_target.py` (`NeverTarget`: `covers(object_id)`, `why(identity)` — IR1's executor will read the same): Glorfindel's, Orodruin's and Annatar's identities, the operators, the identities Terraform lists (`EREGION_PROTECTED_IDENTITIES`), Microsoft's and other tenants' applications (`appOwnerOrganizationId`), the principal of a resource (a managed identity, an API that exposes roles or scopes or that others hold roles on), an application whose principal owns one of them; unreadable — no operator named (`EREGION_OPERATOR_OBJECT_ID`), an id written wrong — every identity is refused. **Never a privileged identity** (Jonathan, 05/11: IR1's executor will own what is enrolled, and owning an application is acting as it — `privilege_refusal`): a directory role, active or eligible, at any scope; a membership of a group (a group can carry a role); an object its principal owns (owning a group or a principal with a role is holding it); any application permission — none is admitted until a technique of R2 needs one (`ADMITTED_APP_PERMISSIONS`, empty); what it owns and who holds a role on it must have been read, or it is refused; each refusal said. **Accepted on the bench** (Jonathan, 05/11): the guard reads neither the identity's Azure roles outside the bench's group nor its delegated consents; IDI2 (#556) lifts it before any identity that does not come from Celebrimbor is enrolled. In `production`, a non-destructive technique only. A read that fails refuses; each URL is built from an object id checked first, a next page outside Graph refused. `python -m eregion_core.inscription identity <object id>`, and `make orodruin-identity ID=<object id>` from Orodruin (the ID checked, never expanded by make).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_identity_inscription.py` (a fake Graph and a fake ARM, no network).
- **Orodruin may read applications in Microsoft Graph, and nothing else there: its contract reads its Graph token** (IDI1, #546; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « IDI1 »). `graph_read_enabled` (off by default, `infra/orodruin`) grants Orodruin's managed identity the Graph app roles `Application.Read.All` and `RoleManagement.Read.Directory` (the least to read role assignments, active and eligible), read-only, on Graph's principal found by its published id; the apply asks for a Privileged Role Administrator. A new guarantee of the contract, `graph`, reads the identity's own Graph token (no Graph call): an app role outside those two, a directory role (`wids`, but the value of a principal without one — a hypothesis to measure), a user's token → `passive`, as an ARM assignment outside the bench — once the Graph read is **declared** (`EREGION_TENANT_ID` in `orodruin.env`). Undeclared, as every Orodruin at the release: an app role in the token is passive (`graph_undeclared_right`), but a `wids` outside the hypothesis, or a token that cannot be had, is only said by `orodruin-check`, so no Orodruin falls at the release. The value of `wids` is measured at the first `orodruin-check` after the release; the undeclared case then turns strict. Three outputs for `orodruin.env`: `tenant_id`, `operator_object_id`, `protected_identities`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_orodruin.py`, `infra/orodruin/tests/graph_read.tftest.hcl` (`terraform test`, mock providers).
- **The watch's signed heartbeat says which detections it covers: a detection removed from the return channel no longer makes a false « missed »** (SEC21b, #535; `docs/design/watch-key.md`, « Ce que le battement couvre »). Left open by SEC21: a hand that can write `watch` could remove a detection under a true heartbeat, and the run concluded « missed » instead of « detected ».
  - **The watch**: the channel's heartbeat carries `covers` — for each window it names in `windows`, the sorted `signal_id` of every detection delivered for it (written and acknowledged before the heartbeat; an empty list for a window with none), signed with the rest under the same domain (`eregion.watch-beat/1`, unchanged: the injectors' gate of before still believes it). Each window lists its own detections, those delivered at this tick and at every earlier one. At most 256 per window (`MAX_COVERED`), and for all the windows together at most half of what an injector reads of a blob (`MAX_COVERS_BYTES`, 512 KiB): beyond, the window (the heaviest first) is left out of `covers`, said once in the watch's log, and its run cannot conclude to a miss — never a heartbeat so large that the injectors refuse it and the gate refuses every run. The local channel of verdicts (SEC18) is unchanged: its heartbeat keeps exactly its fields.
  - **The injectors** (Annatar's wait): at the end of the wait, on the storage channel, the heartbeat is read first, then the detections; a silence is a miss only once every detection the heartbeat covers for the run is read back, vouched (`watch_channel.uncovered`). A detection read that it does not list (delivered after it) is admitted. A covered detection absent is `unknown`, never `missed` (no `detection_missed`, no rule proposed), said in the run's output, in Orodruin's journal (`detection_missing`) and in the run's report (`metrics.detection_missing`, `metrics.detection_missing_total`), the first 20 only (`MAX_MISSING`), the rest counted. A covered detection replaced by a refused blob is said both refused (SEC21) and missing, the refusal first; the end of the wait concludes `unknown` itself, never through the caller. A detection of the run read back and vouched is still « detected ».
  - **The passage, strict**: a heartbeat without `covers` (a watch older than this lot) proves no miss — `unknown`, the reason and the gesture said (update Glorfindel: H0 by its CD, a workstation's by `make glorfindel-restart`); a « detected » is untouched. Bounded by the watch's update: H0 first, by its CD, then Orodruin. No new key, no new pin.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_beat_covers.py`; mutants M3740–M3749, M3760–M3767 (`scripts/mutants/SEC21b.py`).

### Added
- **The signed action queue: its format and its verification, with no change of behaviour** (FS1a, #551, first lot of FS1, #413; `docs/design/file-actions-signee.md`, §7). Nobody reads the queue yet to act: the console and the bot keep their direct call, no queue is picked up, no request is written.
  - **`eregion_core/action_queue.py`**: a request of a service (the console, the bot) — exactly its fields, at most 4 KiB, signed by the service's own Ed25519 key under `eregion.action-request/1`; the proposal the watch signs on a card under `eregion.action-queue/1` (its `kind` signed: a proposal never reads as a receipt or a status, which FS1b signs under the same domain); the closed list of verbs (`VERBS`, each service's in `SERVICE_VERBS`: the console all, the bot `reset` only; open to extension by these constants alone, no restore) and of refusals (`REFUSALS`, each with its reason; `CRITICAL`).
  - **`verify_request`**, a pure check with no network and no call to the cloud, from the cheapest to the dearest: read bounded and never through a link, the exact form (a field twice refused too), a signature, the key pinned for this directory (`EREGION_QUEUE_KEYS`, the watch's environment only), the signature that holds, the service, the time (more than 30 s ahead: `not_yet_valid`; a life over 120 s: `too_long`; from `expires_at`: `expired`), the nonce (claimed before anything runs; a register that cannot be read or written, or that holds a time without its zone, refuses), the verb and the rate (30 a minute per service). `proposal_refusal`: the card still waits, carries the proposal this watch signed, unchanged (its action, target, address, port, type), the one the request names, for its verb, target and address, never executed.
  - **The services' keys are drawn by the watch's own code**: `watch_key.load_or_create(…, name=…)` draws the console's and the bot's key (`~/.eregion/console/queue_ed25519`, `~/.eregion/bot/queue_ed25519`) under the same rules as the watch's — one draw, two uses. The watch's key, its place, its messages and what it signs are unchanged, byte for byte (a known signature computed before FS1a). No service draws its key yet (FS1b).
  - **The watch signs a proposal on each card it writes that holds an action a human may run** (a type approved in one click with its action, or `verification_failed` for its reset), with its SEC19 key and counter, signed again when the card is seen again. A card the console, the bot or the CLI writes carries none; a key unavailable never stops a card.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_action_queue.py`, `tests/unit/test_action_proposal.py`; mutants M3865–M3879, M3945–M3954 (`scripts/mutants/FS1a.py`).
- **Celebrimbor creates and enrolls a test identity of the bench in Entra ID, off by default** (IDI1, #546; `docs/operations.md`, « L'identité de test du banc (IDI1) »). `infra/terraform/entra_identity.tf`: a single-tenant application (`app-celebrimbor-test`, tagged with the bench and its zone) and its service principal, no secret, no permission, no role; `eregion-identities` on `rg-celebrimbor`; `entra_test_identity_enabled = true` in `terraform.tfvars`, the canonical instance only, then `make celebrimbor-identity-plan` / `celebrimbor-identity-up` (targeted). The `azuread` provider is added and locked in both modules (CI4); Celebrimbor's is bound to the bench subscription's tenant.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_bench_identity.py`, `infra/terraform/tests/entra_identity.tftest.hcl`; mutants M3810–M3819, M3860–M3864, M3900–M3909, M3940–M3944 (`scripts/mutants/IDI1.py`).

### Changed
- **The release gate's shards are cut on the durations measured** (CI6d, #545; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »). The gate's first two runs (05/11) took 22 to 60 min a shard in one, 26 to 72 in the other: the estimate weighed every mutant 1 (8 on an invariant machine) and each unmutated run 1, when `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`'s 97 mutants alone made a quarter of the whole (about 280 s each) and the unmutated runs a third of a shard.
  - A shard's `--json` now says each mutant's measure (its copy included) and each target's unmutated run, in seconds (`seconds`, `targets`); the rows and `--total` are unchanged.
  - `scripts/mutants/durations.json` keeps them, at the gate's budget (500 sequences, 2 rounds): `python scripts/mutation_check.py --update-durations DIR…` reads a run's artifacts (those from before CI6d too, their seconds in the rows), keeps each smallest measure (a slower runner slows a whole shard alike), only the mutants and targets of this tree, only results at that budget; nothing else. Seeded from runs 41997986094 and 42009742778 (2 851 mutants; the unmutated runs, measured from the next run on).
  - `shard_of` weighs a mutant by its measure (unmeasured: its target's median, else the old estimate, 13 s, eight times that on an invariant machine) and a piece by its mutants plus its target's unmutated run (unmeasured: one round of its slowest mutant, at least the estimate). A target is cut only when its mutants weigh more than a quarter of a shard's share, never by a count. Still deterministic — the same tree and file, the same shards — and each mutant in exactly one shard. Each shard's pools now take the heaviest first.
  - `python scripts/mutation_check.py --plan 14` says each shard's estimated time, measuring nothing.
  - Estimated on the measures: the heaviest of the 14 shards about 41 min on an ordinary runner (the shards from 36 to 41 as the file cuts them now; from 39 to 42 once the gate has measured the unmutated runs and the file is updated), the control set's about 31, for 60 and 72 measured before. A slow runner is not caught up by any cut: the slowest measured on 05/11 (1.77 times) would take about 72 min; nothing indivisible stands in the way (the longest mutant, 21 min; the longest unmutated run, about 10).
  - The Monday measure (`.github/workflows/mutants.yml`) runs in the gate's shards: 14 of the mutants, 2 of the control set, then their total at the dispatch's budget, held to `min_caught` — 17 jobs, its actions the gate's, pinned by commit. On one runner it outlasted its 180 min bound.
  - A duration that is none — at 0 (« not measured »), above a day (86 400 s), not finite or past a float (10**400) — is left out of the file and of a result, the estimate weighs it, and it is said with where it was: never a crash, nor every mutant in the first shard. `--plan` of no shard is refused as a bad `--shard` is.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`. Mutants M3790–M3799, M3805–M3809 (`scripts/mutants/CI6d.py`).

### Fixed
- **`make release-draft` counts the version's pull request's gate once it is merged** (CI6c, #541; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »). On the 0.5.5 (05/11), the gate was green on the version's pull request (run 41997986094), on origin/main's very tree, and `release-draft` refused it all the same: a `pull_request` run counted only if its `pull_requests` named a base `main`, and GitHub empties that list once the pull request is merged — `release-draft` always runs after the merge, so only a dispatch on `main` could ever pass.
  - The base is now read by every pull request of the run's head, closed ones included (`GET repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?head={owner}:<branch>&state=all&per_page=100`, the App's *Pull requests* read): measured on 05/11, `chore/release-0.5.5` gives #537, merged into `main`; `chore/release-0.4.10` gives #405, merged, and #385, closed unmerged. A commit's pulls (`…/commits/{sha}/pulls`) were not enough: they leave out a pull request closed unmerged (#385's head gives `[]`), so a pull request from the same head into another branch, closed before the version's merged, would have gone unseen (the review of CI6c).
  - Every pull request of the head must come from that branch of this repository and go into `main` of this repository, one of them with the run's commit for head, and none may ever have changed its base: each one's events (`GET repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/events?per_page=100`) hold no `base_ref_changed` (measured on #353), `automatic_base_change_*` nor `base_ref_deleted`. Any base the run still names goes into `main` too.
  - The guards hold: the run comes from this repository's `chore/release-…` head, its branch and commit checked before they go into a path; none with the run's commit, or all of them into another branch, passes the run over (said; an older run on the tree may decide, refused when there is none). Ambiguous — one into `main` beside one elsewhere, or the run and the head's pulls disagreeing —, a read that fails, an answer that does not read, a full page (100 or more), a pull request without a number, an event whose name is not a string, a base changed refuses at once, and says it — never passed over for an older run. The tree is compared; nothing passes over the gate. One read per head and per pull request.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_release.py` (a merged pull request into `main` counts, its run naming none; an open one too; into another branch, merged or open, passed over (refused when no older run on the tree); one closed unmerged into another branch beside the version's into `main`, on the run's commit or an older one, refused as ambiguous, an older green dispatch never deciding (a newer one does); an event whose name is not a string refused; a base changed, on any pull request of the head, refused; a failed, unreadable or full-page read refused and said, never an older dispatch deciding; an empty answer passes the run over (refused when no older run on the tree); another head's or a fork's pull request in the answer refused; a base of another repository, a commit no pull request's head, the run and the head's pulls disagreeing (ambiguous, refused); one read per head; a commit or a branch GitHub would not give never read; a pull request without a number; the allow-list: the second and third `gh api`, GETs of a head's pull requests and of a pull request's events, and nothing else). Mutants M3770–M3785 (`scripts/mutants/CI6c.py`).
- **The console marks each detection of the return channel signed or refused, as the injectors read it** (CON-S, #534; `docs/design/console.md`, « Les détections du canal retour »). `GET /api/channel/detections/<run_id>` judged nothing: the Runs view showed every blob of the channel as « delivered to the run », those SEC21's injectors refuse too. Each one is now judged by `watch_key.detection_refusal` with the key pinned on the console (`EREGION_WATCH_KEY`, never a key read in the channel): `signed` by the pinned key, for this run, under its signal's name, or `refused` with the run's own reason. Without a key pinned, or a value that is no key, the route says so with SEC19's gesture, and every detection is refused: none is shown as verified. The refused ones shown are `MAX_REFUSED` (20) at most, all counted (`refused`, `refused_shown`); a signed one is always shown. `injector.js` writes `SIGNED` only when the key is pinned and the server said signed, the reason and the fingerprint escaped (#254), no inline script (#257). The console's service already receives the watch's public key from SEC19, and gets no new right.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_console_signed_detections.py`; mutants M3750–M3759 (`scripts/mutants/CON-S.py`).
- **The CD's tests no longer send H0 back on a wall clock set back under load** (FL1, #540). The health compares the watch's beat (a file's mtime) with `since`, a `time.time()` taken before the switch; in the tests the fake switch is quick (the two readings as little as 14 ms apart, measured), the fake watch beats once, and `CLOCK_REALTIME` is set back (WSL2: about 65 ms every 30 s, measured) while the kernel stamps a file with its coarse clock (up to 25 ms behind `time.time()`): the beat read as from before the switch, « la watch n'a pas battu depuis la bascule », and five tests failed at random under load. The agent and the fake watch now share, in these tests only, one wall clock that never steps back (the system's monotonic clock from a base): the agent runs as on H0 (`python eregion_cd.py <mode>`, through a launcher that sets that clock), the fake watch stamps its beat on it. The CD is unchanged; the oracle stays « a beat after the switch ». Measured: a step of 65 ms made synthetic fails 85 of the file's 287 tests before, none after.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`.
- **The CD's health judges the watch's heartbeat by its identity, no longer by the wall clock** (H1d, #549; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le CD de H0 »). The health asked the beat's mtime to be no older than `since`, a `time.time()` taken before the switch: two readings of the wall clock. A clock set back during the health (about 120 s or more) read the new watch's beats as from before the switch and sent H0 back; and the old watch could beat in the few seconds before the switch stopped it — killed by a SIGKILL, its beat stayed, and a new watch that never beat passed for healthy. The CD now takes the beat (its inode, its mtime in ns) once `deploy.sh --activate` has returned — the old watch stopped — and asks another one: the watch rewrites the file by a rename (SEC17), each beat a new file. The way back and a pass taken up (the release's watch already running) take it the same way. Taken before the switch, as first thought, the old watch's last beat would still pass: measured by a mutant. A healthy watch beats at its start, then about every minute: when its first beat lands before the switch returns, the health waits for the next one. `since` stays for the journals read (V-34).
  - **The agent changes** (`eregion_cd.py` is one of its `AGENT_FILES`): the release that carries it is deployed by the agent before, which still judges the beat by the wall clock; then `#deploy` asks for `make glorfindel-host-cd-install`, from an up-to-date checkout of `main`, then `make glorfindel-host-cd-check`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py` (the fake watch now beats as the real one, by a rename, at its start and at each look of the health; a clock set back 120 s during the switch; the old watch's last beat with a silent new one, on the switch, the way back and a pass taken up). Mutants M3910–M3914 (`scripts/mutants/H1d.py`); M1019 (H1) follows the comparison it mutates.
- **The reassertion of a block reads the status its write returns: AWS's `failed` is never counted as put back** (RW1, #513; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La repose lit ce que répond le fournisseur »). On AWS, `block_suspicious_ip` answers `{"status": "failed"}` without raising when no ACL entry can be placed, and `{"status": "partial"}` when an entry does not hold at its read-back. Both times of the reassertion (the perimeter pair, then the entry) now read it. `failed` is a failure: the card says the put-back failed, with the provider's reason, and `reasserted_at` is not written, so the block can be tried again. Before, the card said « ses règles réécrites une fois » of rules never written, and the block was never tried again. `partial` is a put-back that does not hold, whatever the re-read says. Before, the entry was marked `partial` with no card: the second time skipped it, held by its own mark. An entry the provider marked `partial` is read by no later pass until it is blocked again whole or unblocked, and its card says so instead of « Glorfindel alertera ». Azure is unchanged: a partial VM block raises (`PartialActionError`), and a perimeter's `blocked_partial` is left to the re-read.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_reassert_status.py` (the AWS world of MC1, `tests/unit/fake_aws.py`); mutants M3925–M3929 (`scripts/mutants/RW1.py`).

## [0.5.5] — 2026-11-05 — "proof, start to finish"

### Security
- **The detections of the return channel are signed by the watch's key, and the injectors count only those the pinned key signed** (SEC21, #530; `docs/design/watch-key.md`, « Les détections signées » ; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`, §5 ; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`). Since WC1 (#523) other identities than H0 may write the channel; an unsigned detection could skew the measure of a run.
  - **The watch**: each `detections/<run_id>/<signal_id>.json` carries `key` (its fingerprint, said, never trusted) and `sig`, the Ed25519 signature of `eregion.watch-detection/1` — never the heartbeat's domain — and the canonical JSON of every other field: the run (`exercise_run`), the signal (`signal_id`), what was seen (`event`, `ttp`, `resource_id`, `rule`, `watch_run_id`) and when (`detected_at`). The same key as the heartbeat (SEC19); without it, the detection goes unsigned and the watch says it once.
  - **The injectors** (Annatar's wait): a detection counts for « seen » only when the key pinned in `EREGION_WATCH_KEY` — never one read in the channel — signed it under that domain, for the run read, under its own signal's name (`watch_channel.vouched_detections`, `watch_key.detection_refusal`). Unsigned, another key, a signature made for a heartbeat, a field changed, added, removed or of another type, copied from another run or another signal, or no key pinned (SEC19's words and gesture): refused, never counted, and said — in the run's output, in Orodruin's journal (`detection_refused`) and in the run's report (`metrics.detection_refused`). The first 20 of the run only (`MAX_REFUSED`, for the whole run, not each read): a writer that rotates its blobs floods neither the journal nor the report; the rest are counted (`metrics.detection_refused_total`, the most refused in one read, said in the run's output). The fingerprint a blob claims is said in printable ASCII only, for a detection as for a heartbeat. A run that saw only refused detections is `unknown`, never `missed` (no `detection_missed`, no rule proposed). A miss is concluded only on an end-of-wait heartbeat the pinned key signed (`watch_gate.miss_proof`, the gate's own proof): unsigned, another key or replayed, the run is `unknown`, said, no `detection_missed`. The heartbeat's format is unchanged; the local channel of verdicts (SEC18) too.
  - **The passage**: no option admits an unsigned detection. A watch up to date with injectors from before: the two fields are ignored, nothing breaks. A watch from before with injectors up to date: every detection refused (« unsigned … update Glorfindel »), runs `unknown`. The order: the release published, H0's CD deploys it, then Orodruin (OI3 or `make orodruin-push REF=<tag>`), then a workstation's watch (`make glorfindel-restart`). No new key, no new pin.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_signed_detections.py` (on the fake container: a signed detection believed, each refusal, the heartbeat unchanged, no key pinned, the console, the journal and the report; a false miss by an unsigned, another key's, a replayed or a changed heartbeat, and a true one that stays a miss; 25 refused say 25; a writer that rotates its blobs; an escape sequence in a claimed fingerprint), `test_watch_channel.py`, `test_annatar_detection.py`, `test_annatar_atomic.py` (their channels now signed). Mutants M3700–M3724 (`scripts/mutants/SEC21.py`).
- **The return channel names who writes it: H0's watch by default, or a workstation's or a team's** (WC1, #523; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`, §5). Each identity listed gets exactly what H0's gets — `Storage Blob Data Reader` on `exercises`, `Storage Blob Data Contributor` on `watch`, at the container's scope, a service principal —, never Orodruin's own identity nor the operator's who applies (both refused at the plan). Annatar's, Terraform does not know: the operator names the identity. The heartbeat stays signed by the watch's key, pinned in `orodruin.env`: another key is still refused (SEC19). The detections are signed by the same key (SEC21): another writer of `watch` does not skew a run's verdict — what it writes is refused and said.

### Added
- **Annatar proposes drills** (DG-e, #351; `docs/dagorlad.md`, « Ce qu'Annatar propose »; `docs/design/console.md`, « Construit : les drills d'Annatar »). A campaign of Annatar becomes a drill whose author is Annatar; the operator keeps it and launches it, as any drill — how a campaign keeps its last proof under 30 days.
  - **« Ask Annatar for a drill »** in Dagorlad (`GET /api/dagorlad/propose?target=<vm>`, read only, with Annatar's section active here): on the enrolled VM the operator chooses, Annatar lays its chain as a draft — the catalogue's implemented techniques that say they destroy nothing, in kill-chain order, five at most. Blind: the catalogue and the VM, never Glorfindel's rules nor its modes; the console never calls the model. Nothing is written, nothing starts: the operator corrects the wires, keeps them (« Save as drill », author `annatar`), then writes the request.
  - **« Import YAML »** (`POST /api/dagorlad/drills/import`, writes nothing; Jonathan's decision A, 04/11): `annatar campaign plan` writes the campaign's drill beside its manifest (`annatar-<campaign id>.yaml`, mode 600 — the scenarios synthesized, and a declared campaign's faults, in its declaration's order) and shows its path; a drill refused or not written is said, the campaign planned all the same. Pasted in Dagorlad, it is read by the drills' one exact reader and placed on the VMs enrolled now, as « Load » places it; a YAML nested past the parser's depth is refused (400), never a 500 — in `load_file` too. Orodruin never writes the console's drills.
  - **One kill-chain order**, in the shared core (`eregion_core/proposal.py`): Annatar's planner reads it from there, and the console never imports Annatar's package (D1).
  - Checked end to end on the fake: BL1's declared campaign planned, its drill imported, kept as Annatar's, then launched by `make orodruin-exercise DRILL=<name>` against a fake ssh and scp — the request carried to Orodruin holds the campaign's chain, in its order. Tests: `test_drill_proposal.py`, `test_campaign_drill.py`, `test_console_dagorlad_annatar.py`; mutants M3560–M3579.
- **A release gate: the strongest measure on the tree a release publishes** (CI6, #514; Jonathan's decision of 04/11; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »; `docs/sessions.md`, « Le cycle d'une release »). A version's pull request ran only a pull request's CI; nothing measured the published commit in full, and a commit newer than the last night could ship unmeasured.
  - **`.github/workflows/release-gate.yml`**: every test with 2 000 sequences per invariant machine (Linux), the whole mutation at 500 sequences and two rounds, control set included, ci.yml's macOS job and its Terraform tests, word for word. A last job, « release gate », waits for all of them and is green only if each one that blocks is: a cancelled or skipped job counts as red. **macOS informs, never blocks** (Jonathan's decision of 04/11, H1c, #518: only the required checks block — H0 runs Linux, and a runner GitHub never gives, as for the 0.5.4's commit, must never hold a release back): its job continues on error, and its outcome, with each job's result and role, is said in the run's summary. Every job bounded, `contents: read` and nothing else, every action pinned by commit, each job checking out the pull request's head exactly (never GitHub's merge commit).
  - **When**: on the version's pull request (`chore/release-X.Y.Z`, this repository's own; a pull request that changes `pyproject.toml` starts the workflow, any other one skips every job), so a red is seen before the merge; or by a dispatch on main. A new push cancels the run it replaces: one battery per tree.
  - **`make release-draft` refuses** until the gate's last run on a commit whose tree (`^{tree}`) is origin/main's is complete and green: a squash-merge changes the commit, not the tree, when nothing else merged in between. Otherwise it says which run, its state and its link, or that none measured this tree and how to dispatch it on main. No flag, no variable passes over it; `DRY_RUN=1` shows the check. Read by one `gh api` GET of the workflow's runs — the sessions' App reads it by its *Actions: read* (measured, 04/11). `make release` says, in its output and in the pull request's body, that the gate runs there and must be green before the merge.
  - **The mutation in shards** (`scripts/mutation_check.py --shard K/N --json`, then `--total`): one runner cannot measure 2 633 mutants (the Monday run, at 1 000 sequences, was cancelled at its 180 min bound on 02/11 before its first mutant). Each shard takes whole targets, in pieces of eight at most; the unmutated runs go in parallel; `--total` refuses a shard missing, twice, measured at another budget, or a mutant of the tree not measured exactly once, and holds the count to mutants.yml's `min_caught`. The control set is said, never held to a count.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_release.py` (refused without a gate, red, cancelled, skipped, running, on another tree, another pull request's, a fork's or one into another branch than main, unreadable, said nothing; accepted on a squash of the same tree and after a dispatch; the last started run decides, a run re-run included; no flag, no variable), `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (the trigger, the permissions, the bounds, the shards, the last job run against `needs`), `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py`. Mutants M3590–M3605 (`scripts/mutants/CI6.py`).
- **Two starting guides, replayed from zero, and `make doctor`** (GS1, #520; `docs/demarrer/`). `docs/demarrer/bleu.md` — observe read-only in 15 minutes, one path (Docker, `make profile-discover`): the Reader principal, `.envrc` with the model's key or Ollama, the workspace, `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY=1`, the noisy rule turned off in the client's layer, what to see and when, what leaves the machine, how to remove it all. `docs/demarrer/banc.md` — the full bench, from an attack to its proof: Celebrimbor, the identities, H0, Orodruin and its pinned keys, the enrollment, a first attack (T1136.001, the declared campaign) and its proof, the teardown, with the cost and time of each step and, for each, whether it is read in the code, measured, or not verified. The case without H0 is a gap, said: only H0's identity can write the return channel Orodruin reads (`infra/orodruin/main.tf`), a lot of its own. `docs/demarrer/a-rejouer.md`: what is left to replay on Azure, and what to see.
  - `make doctor [PROFILE=discover|bench]` (`scripts/doctor.py`, the standard library only, before `make install`): the tools, the variables — present, a pair complete, not an example value, never their value printed —, the model's key for its provider, `glorfindel-config.yaml` read as the watch reads it (with the venv) and its workspace, the state directories the operator's own, port 7007 free or held by the console; on the bench, the watch's key pinned in `orodruin.env`, the channels and the subscription the same in `orodruin.env` and `host.env`, `ORO_SSH_FINGERPRINT`. Each failure says the command that fixes it; exit 0 only when all is green. It runs only local commands (`docker info`, `docker compose version`, `docker ps`).
  - `scripts/doc_anchors.py`: every internal link of the README, the guides and the profiles leads to a file and to a heading of it, as GitHub names it.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_doctor.py`, `tests/unit/test_doc_anchors.py`; mutants M3620–M3629 (`scripts/mutants/GS1.py`).
- **Orodruin up to date at its start: after a publication, `make orodruin-start` is enough** (OI3, #522; `infra/orodruin/README.md`, « À jour au démarrage »; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « L'image des injecteurs »; `docs/operations.md`). H0 deployed the 0.5.4 by itself (its CD); Orodruin, stopped most of the time and without egress to the registry, stayed on the 0.5.3 until a `make orodruin-push REF=v0.5.4` by hand.
  - **`make orodruin-start`**: the VM started, then awaited over SSH (`ssh-keyscan`, nothing accepted there; 2 minutes at most), `orodruin-update` checks Orodruin's host key (`orodruin-known`) and reads the image in service in read-only, through the pinned key — docker answering (`docker info`), the tag `eregion-annatar:orodruin` and the commit its label `org.opencontainers.image.revision` says, then `~/.eregion-image` (at most 4 KB; the read bounded on the workstation too). `scripts/published_image.py age` compares it, on the workstation, to the latest release: the highest tag `v*` by version order (`v0.5.10` after `v0.5.9`, as `orodruin-push` takes it without `REF`), then by commit in git's history (`merge-base --is-ancestor`), never two strings.
  - **Older, absent or unreadable** (a verified marker whose tag says another commit included: an `orodruin-push-dev` cut before its marker): said, then `make orodruin-push REF=<the release>` as it is — digest, signature, provenance, the archive of the blobs, the load, the read-back, OI2's control run, the tag, the migration. **Equal**: nothing, in one line. **Newer** (a commit of `main` pushed by hand), **dev** (`orodruin-push-dev`) or off the release's history: kept, never replaced unless asked, the command to go back to the release said.
  - **On any error** (the VM not answering, docker not answering on Orodruin — never taken for an image absent, whose push would erase `~/.eregion-image` first —, the read, an image's commit unknown here, a `REF` off `origin/main`, the push, its verification, its control run): a non-zero exit, the cause said, nothing armed, and the VM left started — said too. `NO_UPDATE=1` starts without reading or changing anything, and says so; `REF=<tag|commit>` aims at another release. Both are given on the command line, checked before anything (`az` included), read by the shell from its environment, never expanded by make; from the environment, refused. Before `az vm start` too: `ORO_SSH_FINGERPRINT` set (but under `NO_UPDATE=1`) and `ORO_CHECK_TIMEOUT`. `make -n orodruin-start` runs nothing: the next make is never written `$(MAKE)` in the recipe. `make orodruin-update [REF=…]`: the same update, the VM already started.
  - **`make orodruin-check`** says, before the verdict, « image vX (commit), dernière release vY (commit) », and when older, absent or unreadable « image plus ancienne (absente, illisible) : make orodruin-start ou make orodruin-push REF=vY »: a warning, its exit code stays the contract's. Nothing changes to Orodruin's network, its contract, its watch guard, `orodruin-push` or `orodruin-push-dev`.
  - Tests: `test_published_image.py` (the targets run on OI1's and OI2's fakes, with a throwaway git repository — `v0.5.9`, `v0.5.10`, a commit after, a branch beside — and the host key really checked by `known_host.sh`: older pushed, absent and unreadable pushed, a marker its tag contradicts pushed, equal left in one line, newer, dev and diverged kept, a failed push non-zero with `:orodruin` unchanged, docker down, an unknown commit and a `REF` off main decide nothing, a VM that never answers, `NO_UPDATE=1`, `REF=`, thirteen values refused before `az`, the dry run; `orodruin-check`'s warnings), `test_host_ssh.py` (the update's and the check's SSH, each with the pinned key, ed25519 only, never accept-new). Mutants M3630–M3639 and M3685–M3693 (`scripts/mutants/OI3.py`): all caught.
- **Without H0, a workstation's or a team's watch talks to Orodruin** (WC1, #523; `infra/orodruin/README.md`, « Sans H0 » ; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md` ; `docs/profils/equipes.md`). Until now only H0's identity could write the return channel, so without H0 every run of Annatar or Gothmog was refused, but a development run (`WITHOUT_GLORFINDEL=1`).
  - **Terraform**: `watch_principal_ids`, one or more object ids (GUIDs, none twice whatever its case). `null` by default: H0 alone (`watch_principal_id`), at the addresses it had — the plan of H0 and Orodruin does not change on the current bench. H0 listed keeps its assignments; every other identity gets one assignment per container.
  - **Makefile**: `make orodruin-plan WATCH_PRINCIPALS=<id>[,<id>…] NO_WATCH=1` (and `-up`, `-lock`, `-down`). Given on the command line only, checked before any plan (GUIDs separated by commas, none twice), never expanded by make, passed to terraform by the shell; empty, the line is the one before WC1. A list that leaves H0 out while its state reads is refused, unless `NO_WATCH=1` (SEC7). Each `orodruin-up` and `orodruin-lock` gives the same list again: without it, the plan takes the workstation's watch's rights away.
  - **The watch**: unchanged — it already published on the channel by the path H0's takes (`watch_channel.start`, its own principal, `GLORFINDEL_AZURE_CLIENT_*`). Its network: the operator's IP, which the channel's firewall already admits. One watch at a time on a channel: the heartbeat is one blob.
  - **For Jonathan**: the plan, the apply, then a run from Orodruin with the watch on the workstation (`infra/orodruin/README.md`); and the step `terraform -chdir=infra/orodruin test -no-color` in `ci.yml` (« Terraform validate — Orodruin »), then `TF_AWAITING_STEP` emptied in `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` — a session never writes under `.github/`.
  - Tests: `infra/orodruin/tests/watch_writers.tftest.hcl` (`terraform test`, a mock azurerm: the default, one identity, several, H0 left out, an empty list, refusals — Orodruin's identity, the operator's), `test_orodruin.py`, `test_makefile_bench.py` (the variable passed, refused values, never expanded, the guard), `test_watch_key.py` (a workstation watch publishes on a fake container under its own principal, believed with its key pinned only), `test_profiles.py`. Mutants M3660–M3669, M3695 (`scripts/mutants/WC1.py`), the Terraform ones caught by `terraform test` too.

### Changed
- **No conflict between pull requests: a lot's CHANGELOG entry and its mutants in files of their own** (CI5, #512; `docs/sessions.md`, « Le cycle d'un changement »; `changes/README.md`). Every lot wrote its entry under `[Unreleased]` and its block of mutants before the harness's guard, at the same lines: each merge put the other pull requests in conflict, and their CI ran again (about 20 minutes a lot).
  - **The CHANGELOG's fragments**: one lot's entry per file, `changes/<lot>.md`, in the CHANGELOG's own Markdown — its sections as `### Security`, `### Added`, `### Changed`, `### Fixed`, each at most once. `make release` (`scripts/release.py prepare`) assembles them under `[X.Y.Z]` in that order of sections, after what `[Unreleased]` still holds (the transition), checks that no line is lost nor added, and removes them in the version's commit; `DRY_RUN=1` shows the assembly. A fragment malformed is refused, named with its line — at the release, and in the pull request that brings it.
  - **The mutants of each lot**: `scripts/mutants/<lot>.py` (`MUTANTS`, `TESTS` for its own ids, optionally `TESTS_ALSO`), loaded by `scripts/mutation_check.py` after its blocks, before its `__main__` guard; the blocks already there stay. `scripts/mutant_anchors.py` reads both places: an id two places take is a fault naming each, as is a lot's file without a mutant or whose `TESTS` names another's (the harness never applies it). `scripts/mutants/CI5.py` is the example.
  - Tests: `test_release.py` (the assembly, its order of sections, no line lost, eleven malformed fragments refused and a fragment or changes/ itself as a symbolic link, a failed `git rm` said whole with its way on, the dry run, the repository's own fragments well formed; a probe of two lots in flight from one `main`: the old way the second merge conflicts on `CHANGELOG.md` and the harness, CI5's way both merge clean, both lots' mutants are loaded and both entries released), `test_mutant_anchors.py` (every lot's mutants measured by the harness run as a script; an id two lots' files take; the loader on a copy of the harness). Mutants M3580–M3586 (`scripts/mutants/CI5.py`), and the 23 mutants already on `release.py`, `mutant_anchors.py` and the harness: all caught.
- **A faster CI: macOS off the pull requests, 200 invariant sequences there, a replaced run cancelled** (CI5, #512; `docs/operations.md`, « La CI, plus rapide »). Three pull requests pushing together queued their runs up to 15 minutes, and the macOS job, required, was often never acquired by a runner — the sessions' App cannot re-run it.
  - **macOS** (`tests (macOS, case-insensitive file system)`) runs on a push to `main` and every night (`schedule`; the night of `ci.yml` is its alone: a red audit would turn `main`'s commit red; since H1c, H0's CD says it without holding the release). Its red on `main` — or its cancel: a timeout or a runner never acquired says `cancelled` — opens the issue « CI : les tests macOS sont rouges sur main », or comments the one this workflow opened (`--author app/github-actions`): job `macos-red`, `issues: write` to it alone, never on a pull request, no checkout nor action. A macOS red or cancelled on a release's commit no longer holds H0's CD (H1c, #518): macOS is not a required check, the CD says it and deploys; the agent installed before H1c still refuses it, hence a `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` before publishing the release that carries H1c.
  - **The invariants**: 200 sequences on a pull request, 500 on a push to `main`, 10 000 at night (`invariants.yml`, unchanged).
  - **`concurrency`** on CI, Images and Components: a new push on a pull request cancels the run it replaces; on `main`, at night and in a merge queue each run is its own group, never cancelled nor held pending.
  - **Components** is not run on a pull request of prose alone (`paths-ignore`: `**/*.md`, `docs/**`, `changes/**`, `LICENSE`); its checks are not required. Images keeps its own first step, which already skips the build.
  - **For Jonathan, at the merge**: remove « tests (macOS, case-insensitive file system) » from the required checks of the rule of `main`; five remain (`docs/sessions.md`, « Les règles du dépôt »).
  - Tests: `test_workflows.py` reads each `if`, the budget and the `concurrency` as GitHub evaluates them for each event; the issue's script run with a fake `gh` (created, or the open one commented); the required checks run on every pull request and none sits behind a path filter. Mutants M3587–M3589 (`scripts/mutants/CI5b.py`): all caught.
- **H0's CD holds a release only on the checks the rule of `main` requires, and on the image it pulls: a runner GitHub never gave no longer holds it** (H1c, #518; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le CD de H0 », point 2; `docs/sessions.md`, « Les règles du dépôt »). On 04/11 the 0.5.4's commit had every check green but macOS, cancelled for want of a runner: the CD refused any red or cancelled check, whatever it was, and would have refused it. Jonathan's decision, the same day.
  - **What holds**: the five checks `main` requires — `lint + tests (py3.11)`, `lint + tests (py3.12)`, `terraform test (mock providers)`, `image (annatar)`, `image (glorfindel)` — and `publish (glorfindel)`, the image the CD pulls (CI3). Red, cancelled or timed out: refused, said with the mention, as before. Still running: waited for. Absent: waited for an hour, each with its own count, then refused.
  - **What is only said**: every other check (macOS, the components, the compose profiles, `dependency advisories`, `publish (annatar)`…). Red or cancelled, `#deploy` says it in orange, without the mention, once per tag and per check; still running, it delays nothing. A check's name is compared whole: `lint + tests (py3.12) beta` never passes for a required one. What `#deploy` says of a name loses any URL, address or host name first (‹url›, ‹hôte›), then keeps letters, digits and `+ , . ( ) -` only — no `:` nor `/`, so no link Discord would make (SEC6).
  - **What counts for a check that holds**: GitHub Actions' runs only (`app.slug` = `github-actions`, read on `main`'s commits): another app's run named as a required job, green, never fills an absent one; red or still running, it holds all the same. Two runs of one name: a red one refuses, one still running waits. Completed without a green conclusion (none, or one the agent does not know): refused, never taken for green. Every page of the check runs is read.
  - **Where the list lives**: `REQUIRED_CHECKS`, a constant of the installed agent, never read from the repository nor from the release; a test keeps it equal to the list of `docs/sessions.md`, which now holds the five (macOS was taken off the rule of `main` on 04/11).
  - **The dry run** (`make glorfindel-host-cd-check`) shows both groups, each check with its state, also when the release runs already; the journal writes them at every pass.
  - **Before publishing the release that carries this**: `make glorfindel-host-cd-install`, from a checkout of `main` that has H1c — the CD's agent never replaces itself, and the one installed before still refuses a red or cancelled macOS.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py` (macOS cancelled, failed, never started or queued while the required ones are green: deployed, the red said once; each holding check red, cancelled or timed out: refused; each required one absent: waited an hour, then refused; one still running: waited for; a check not required still running: no delay; a red one said once per tag; neighbouring names; a name with a mention, a link, a bare URL, a host or Markdown; a holding check completed without a conclusion or with an unknown one; one name run twice; another app's runs; a required one only on the second page; the list equal to `docs/sessions.md`; the dry run). Mutants M3610–M3619 and M3640–M3645 (`scripts/mutants/H1c.py`), all caught; M1007, M1666 and M1696 re-anchored.

### Fixed
- **The readiness file's lock is the shared lock of the local files** (I11g, #508, found by L1W's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « Le verrou de la préparation, hors de l'ordonnanceur »). The invariant machine failed I11 at seed 43 with 500 sequences, on `main` too: « op-1 never came back ». Two isolations at once, each refused the quarantine NSG by a policy, both record the refusal in `readiness.json`; the one stopped by the scheduler at its write held `readiness`'s own thread lock, which the scheduler does not drive, and the other blocked in it out of its sight. No deadlock in production (the holder waits for nothing else): the machine's blind spot, as the escalations store's lock was (#292). `readiness._locked` now takes `locks._file_lock` (a thread lock plus an flock), read at call time, on the same `readiness.lock` as before — an older console beside a newer watch still keeps out of its way. The machine already drives that lock; neither it nor its oracles change.
  - Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_two_isolations_at_once_that_both_record_a_quarantine_refusal` (as found, reduced to two rules, and the race alone at three orders; red on `main`), `…::test_a_race_whose_two_operations_rewrite_the_readiness_file`, `test_locks.py::test_two_processes_lose_no_readiness_record` and `…::test_the_readiness_lock_is_the_file_every_version_took`. Mutants M3550–M3553.
- **« Reposé une fois » ends its episode: a block gone again before the next pass is said « une deuxième fois »** (L1O, #507, found by L1W's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1O »; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). On `reapplied`, `_rewrite_block` (`glorfindel/reassert.py`) wrote `reasserted_at` but kept the drift marks of the episode before (`drift_alerted_at`, `drift_said`) and left that episode open in memory. A block put back once could then go again before the next pass, in the state the put-back had read. It read as « already said, no worse » and the pass stayed silent, though the card « reposé une fois », pending or acknowledged, says « s'il disparaît encore, Glorfindel alertera sans le reposer ». One way there is L1G's: a failed put-back is acknowledged, then a rule of the pair is written again. Now the episode ends in the write of `reasserted_at`. It is closed in memory first, whatever that write does, and the marks are taken off in the same write. The next disappearance is said « une deuxième fois » and never put back (L1Q), and nothing is written in `human_only`. A put-back that does not hold whole (`partial`) still marks a new episode after its card. The first time (`reassert_perimeters.py`, L1Qs) ends the episode the same way in its own write of `reasserted_at`: a pass whose second time skipped the entry (its lock held) left the marks and the episode open, and the pair gone again behind the acknowledged card stayed silent.
  - **Where it showed**: in the reassertion's own logic, with a connector that does not rewrite the block's entry. On Azure, the adapter's state write (`_save_block_state`) takes the drift marks off at every whole block (L1Q), so the episode already ended on disk. The invariant machine, which drives the real adapter, cannot produce the defect, and both ways replayed through it are green on main. The core no longer depends on that side effect (MC0).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`, `test_l1o_*`. They cover L1G's way for the VM's own block and for a merged entry, with the card pending or acknowledged, in both modes; the marks on disk; no loop; the write refused; a partial put-back still marked; a restart then a refused write (the closure in memory alone); and a pass cut between its two times. 16 of the 18 are red on main; the two green ones are guards. L1W's way, added after L1W's merge: a pair put back once, written again by another perimeter (noted in `perimeter_writes.jsonl`) and gone before the next pass is said once for each VM (`test_l1o_the_way_of_l1w_*`, red on main for the card pending or acknowledged after the write; `test_a_pair_put_back_once_written_again_by_another_perimeter_then_gone_is_said_for_each_vm_once`). `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_a_block_put_back_once_acknowledged_then_gone_again_is_said_a_second_time` checks the card's promise end to end and is green on main. Mutants M3540–M3548.
- **`annatar validate` says a failure by its exit code** (AV1, #524 ; `docs/operations.md`). It
  exited 0 even on an invalid scenario, so a script or a CI that trusted it (`make
  annatar-validate`) took the scenario for good. One invalid scenario now exits 1 with its cause;
  `--all` (and `validate` with no argument) exits 1 as soon as one is invalid, its causes under
  each and the invalid ones listed at the end; all valid: 0. An empty file, a YAML list or a
  `target` that is no mapping is a scenario said invalid, never a `TypeError` that stopped `--all`
  before the scenarios after it. What the file says — a path, a cause, a name not found — is
  printed as written, never read as Rich markup (`[bold]`) nor as an emoji code (`:fire:`), in
  `validate` and in the « Invalid scenario » and refusal lines of `annatar run`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_annatar_cli.py`, `tests/unit/test_annatar_run_exit.py`. Mutants
    M3650–M3654, M3670–M3674 (`scripts/mutants/AV1.py`).
- **The README's first attack ran `annatar run` from the workstation, where Annatar is passive since O1** (GS1, #520). The step now says attacks leave from an armed Orodruin and leads to `docs/demarrer/banc.md`; the evaluation leads to `docs/demarrer/bleu.md`, and the noisy rule is turned off in the client's layer (`~/.glorfindel/client_rules.yaml`), no longer in the shipped base.
  - `docs/profils/decouvrir.md` pointed to a README anchor gone since the heading changed (`#evaluate-on-your-infrastructure--read-only-zero-risk`), and did not name the model's key: both fixed. `docs/profils/detection.md` and the three-terminal workflow of `docs/operations.md` lead to the bench guide.
  - The refusal of `annatar run` on a workstation (`glorfindel:`, `passive:`) names the bench guide.
  - `.envrc.example` named a former default model.
- **Orodruin's first deployment: no `YES=1` on a plan that was not the one read** (OI3's review; `infra/orodruin/README.md`, « Déroulé »; `docs/banc-bl1.md`). `make orodruin-plan` computes the plan with the egress closed (`egress_locked=true`), while `make orodruin-up` applies it open: `orodruin-up YES=1` after `orodruin-plan` applied a plan no one had read. Each apply now shows its plan and waits for its confirmation; the first opens the egress, the second (`orodruin-lock`) closes it.
- **H0 requires only the key of the configured model's provider, and carries no other** (OL1, #525, found by GS1; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « La clé du fournisseur configuré, seule »). `host_env.py` required `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` whatever `GLORFINDEL_LLM_MODEL` named — even a local model (`ollama/…`), whose logs never leave the machine. It now requires what the model's provider reads, by its LiteLLM prefix: `anthropic/…` or the default, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `openai/…`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`; `azure/…`, `AZURE_API_KEY` and `AZURE_API_BASE` — or `GLORFINDEL_LLM_BASE_URL` in its place, which the watch passes as `base_url`; `mistral/…`, `MISTRAL_API_KEY`; `ollama/…` (and `ollama_chat/…`), no key but `GLORFINDEL_LLM_BASE_URL` — in the watch's container, LiteLLM's default address is the container itself; an OpenAI-compatible model served at `GLORFINDEL_LLM_BASE_URL` (self-hosted), nothing. A base URL never waives a cloud provider's own key. An unknown provider, or a model with no prefix, is refused with the list. The message names what is missing, never a value — not even the model's, where a key pasted in the wrong variable would show.
  - **One rule, in the core**: `eregion_core/models.py` (`PROVIDER_ENV`, `model_provider`, `required_env`, `missing_env`), so that `make doctor` (GS1) can read it rather than copy it (D1: the core is the only shared layer).
  - **host.env carries only the keys of the configured models' providers** (the decision model's, the analysis model's; `AZURE_API_VERSION` with `azure/…` only): another key of the operator's `.envrc` never reaches H0 nor the watch. It now carries `MISTRAL_API_KEY` and the Azure OpenAI variables when their provider is configured — `docker-compose.yml` passed them to the watch, but `host_env.py` never copied them: an `azure/…` or `mistral/…` H0 had no key. `host.env.example` lists them, and `GLORFINDEL_LLM_BASE_URL`.
  - **`host_env.py --check`** (run by `make glorfindel-host-push`) holds an existing `host.env` to the same rule, after its console token, its model's variables read as compose gives them to the watch: a pair of quotes removed, `KEY=""` and `KEY=   # note` empty. **`make glorfindel-host-model`** runs it first, on a copy carrying the new model: refused, nothing is written, here nor on the VM — it rewrote the model with no check, and H0 restarted without its new model's key.
  - **The analysis model** (`EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL`, Haiku by default) writes the campaign reports, never a decision: its provider's missing key is said when `host.env` is written, not refused — and so is a local analysis model, which would call the watch's container: the analysis does not read `GLORFINDEL_LLM_BASE_URL` yet (`analysis/campaign_report.py`, `analysis/triage.py`; outside this lot).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py` (each provider, with its oracle written there; an unknown provider; the keys carried; `--check` and compose's reading; the analysis model said), `tests/unit/test_host_console.py` (`make glorfindel-host-model` refused writes nothing), `tests/unit/test_models.py`. Mutants M3655–M3659 and M3680–M3684 (`scripts/mutants/OL1.py`).
- **The mutation harness makes ChromaDB's embedding model ready once, before any run in parallel** (CI6b, #538; `docs/operations.md`, « La porte de release »). At the release gate's first real run (the 0.5.5's pull request, #537, 05/11), two shards of fourteen failed on the unmutated code (`FileNotFoundError … onnx.tar.gz`, `InvalidProtobuf`): since CI6, the unmutated runs start at once (`--jobs 4`), and on a fresh runner each pytest fetched the model (about 80 MB) into the same `~/.cache/chroma`, reading another's half-written file.
  - **`scripts/mutation_check.py`** calls ChromaDB's default embedding function once on a word, in its own process, before the unmutated runs and the mutants' (`prepare_embedding_model`): chromadb 1.5.9 fetches the model only when one of its files is missing, checks the archive's SHA-256, and loads it as a test would. The model there, nothing goes out; ChromaDB absent, nothing to prepare. The runs inherit the harness's `HOME`, so they read the same `~/.cache/chroma`. The Monday measure (`mutants.yml`) and a local run gain the same.
  - **A preparation that fails is said** (without a network, a corrupt cache, ChromaDB that cannot start: chromadb 1.5.9 raises a `RuntimeError` on import when sqlite3 is too old), its error whole, with what to do; the runs then go on as before, each free to fetch the model itself. It is never a verdict: it neither stops a shard nor catches a mutant.
  - **`.github/workflows/release-gate.yml`**: the `mutants` and `control` jobs restore the `chroma-onnx-v1` cache, as the `invariants` job and `ci.yml` do, by the same action pinned by commit.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_mutation_shards.py` (prepared once, before any run, never by each; by the default embedding function, on a fake ChromaDB; nothing without ChromaDB; a failure said and the runs on, no mutant caught by it; ChromaDB unable to start said, never raised; the runs' `HOME` the harness's; with the model there, chromadb itself never downloads), `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (the invariants, mutants and control jobs restore the model before their tests, one pinned action, ci.yml's key). Mutants M3730–M3735 (`scripts/mutants/CI6b.py`).

## [0.5.4] — 2026-11-04 — "nothing left unread"

### Security
- **The reader of the red's mounts refuses what it cannot read** (SEC20b, #494, found by SEC20's second review; `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, « Ce que le rouge monte en écriture »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2). `tests/unit/test_red_mounts.py` read the current Makefile faithfully, but some forms make, docker or compose accept escaped it; none is in the Makefile today.
  - **Every launch, every mount**: launches are read as `docker run`, `docker create`, either as `docker container …`, whatever the spaces. Each launch's options are read as docker reads them, up to its image: a mount glued to `-v`, after `-v=`, in a group of short options (`-itv …`), by `--volume`, `--volume=` or `--mount`. `--volumes-from`, an option the reader does not know, a `--mount` key it does not read, and a launch behind docker's own options are refused. A recipe's continuation is read as the shell reads it, the two lines joined with nothing between; a comment continued onto the next line is refused, since the shell runs what follows.
  - **What a red launch is never given**, refused by name, never in the table of options read: `--privileged`, `--device`, `--cap-add`, `--pid`, `--userns`, `--ipc` or `--network` on the host (or another container), `--uts host`, a `--security-opt` other than `no-new-privileges:true`.
  - **No reference left unexpanded** in a launch's options, `$(HOME)`, `$(PWD)` and `$$HOME` aside: a variable of a `define` or of an included file, `$(shell …)`, a variable of the shell. And no `include`, `-include`, `sinclude`, `define`, `undefine`, `$(eval …)`, `SHELL`, `.SHELLFLAGS`, `.ONESHELL` or `load` in the Makefile: refused rather than read, since reading them would make the test a second make, and the Makefile needs none; nor a recipe's `$(MAKE)` told to read another makefile or directory (`-f`, `-C`).
  - **Compose reads its one file**: a recipe's compose launch given another file (`-f`, `--file`, `--project-directory`, `COMPOSE_FILE`) is refused, and so is any file beside `docker-compose.yml` that compose would take in its place or merge on its own (`compose.yaml`, `compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yaml`, their overrides, `docker-compose.override.yml` among them). The compose file includes no other (`include`), no service extends another or takes its mounts (`extends`, `volumes_from`), every image is written plainly.
  - **The shell's history with the fallback root image** (`UID=0`): appended, never truncated, as with the operator's image. The neighbour file bash truncates through is made in the container's own layer, and its rename onto the mounted file fails (`EBUSY`, a rule of the kernel, not measured here: no container); bash then removes the neighbour (read in its code, not measured); nothing reaches the host. Measured without a container (bash 5.2): a directory that refuses the neighbour leaves the history appended, never truncated. A session longer than `HISTSIZE` is not kept, with either image (the Makefile's comment and `docs/fichiers-cles.md` say it).
  - Mutants M3490–M3497, M3525–M3529 and M3535–M3536 (each form, which SEC20's reader missed, `--volumes-from` aside) and M3498–M3499 (`ANNATAR_SHELL_FILES`: the link check removed, the files made before it). The shell probe runs with its own `HOME` and `HISTFILE`.

### Changed
- **The azurerm canary says the end of a failed command's error, nothing real in its public log** (CAN1, #501; `docs/operations.md`, « Le canari azurerm, sans secret »). The 04/11 run's 5.0 line went red on « apply updating the NIC succeeds », then green on its third attempt: `run.sh` had sent the apply's output to `/dev/null`, so nobody could tell a passing 429 from a change of Azure.
  - **Said now**: a terraform command that fails (init, apply, destroy, a plan's exit 1 — its exit 2 is its answer) prints its last 30 lines, an az command its stderr (the group's read too), both on stderr.
  - **No workflow command from Azure's text**: the runner reads `::name::value` at a line's start (leading spaces aside) — each line said starts with `   | ` — and the historical `##[name]value` anywhere in a line (`actions/runner`, `ActionCommand.cs`) — each `##[` is said `# #[`. Per line rather than a `::stop-commands::<token>` around the block: nothing to close, so a block cut short leaves nothing open.
  - **Taken out first**, by `infra/canary/jit-terraform/redact.py` (a filter, not `::add-mask::`: a mask hides only a value known in advance, works in Actions only and cannot be tested offline): the canary's subscription, tenant and client identifiers, every resource id (`/subscriptions/…`, URL-encoded or escaped in JSON too), every GUID (with its dashes or without), a token (a JWT, or what follows `Bearer`), a SAS's `sig=` and `se=`, a tenant's domain (`*.onmicrosoft.com`), an e-mail address, every IPv4 and IPv6 address (after a word and a colon too). It prints once all is filtered, and `run.sh` prints what it said on its exits 0 and 3 alone: a filter that fails says « output withheld », nothing of the text, its own errors silenced. GitHub's masking of the environment's secrets stays, a second layer.
  - **A transient error said as such** (a 429 status — never a timestamp's `…:29.429` —, `TooManyRequests`, `AnotherOperationInProgress`, `RetryableError`, `ReferencedResourceNotProvisioned`), and a new exit, **3 = inconclusive**: a failed setup, or failed checks that all came after a transient error — re-run the line. A check that failed before any transient error is still a change (exit 1). In the cleanup, a transient error asks for no re-run: the verdict is said.
  - The workflow does not change. Tests: `test_workflows.py`, `test_can1_*` (the filter on a loaded sample, the NIC's failed apply, a failed setup, an az error, the group's read, a change before a 429, a filter that fails, a 429 in a timestamp, a transient cleanup). Mutants M3500–M3519; M2088 and M2114 follow the cleanup's new lines.

### Fixed
- **A perimeter pair another VM wrote again, gone again before any pass, is said for each VM** (L1W, #492; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1W »; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). Found by L1E's rule and frozen in strict `xfail`: web of rg-app2 blocked on its subnet, the pair taken off, said in human_only, the card acknowledged; web of rg-app1 blocked on the same subnet wrote the pair again (one per IP and subnet), taken off again before any pass. Azure read as the card had read, and rg-app2/web's entry stayed silent behind its acknowledged card — only rg-app1/web's card said it. **Glorfindel now notes its own writes of a perimeter pair**, before each write (`~/.glorfindel/perimeter_writes.jsonl`, `glorfindel/response/pair_journal.py`, appended, 0600): an acknowledged card that said the pair gone (absent, or there and turned) is over once that pair is noted after its acknowledgement (`reassert_said._written_since`) — what is still gone is said anew, or put back once outside human_only. A pair held at the card, a write before the acknowledgement, a pending card: nothing more. A write that reached Azure and then raised is noted too (a card more, never one less); a journal refused never stops the block, it is logged. The journal is read line by line, a byte out of UTF-8 replaced (one forged line no longer silences it), and opened only without following a link and only as a regular file (`O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat`): a link or a pipe in its place is refused, its target untouched (M3530–M3534). And `_back` reads a turned pair back only if it reads otherwise now (`was != is_`, L1H's review). The oracle and the machine unchanged (A0). Tests: the `xfail` lifted, the oracle's control (`test_a_perimeter_written_again_then_gone_with_its_journal_lost_is_refused`, which keeps M3442 caught), `test_reassert.py` (acknowledged or pending, in human_only or not, said once and not in a loop, the first time, the journal, the adapter). Mutants M3480–M3489.
- **A control run before Orodruin's tag: the layers present and unpacked** (OI2, #490, found by OI1's review; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « L'image des injecteurs »). Under containerd's image store, `docker image inspect` reads the config: `RootFS.Layers` comes from it, and `docker tag` reads nothing else. A layer lost after a load that succeeded passed the read-back and the tag, and showed only at the image's first run. `make orodruin-push` now starts the image read back as the proven one once before tagging it `:orodruin`: `timeout -k 10 120 docker run --rm` hardened as every injector's run (`ORO_HARDEN`: read-only root, `/tmp` in tmpfs, no capability, no privilege gained), `--pull never --network none --no-healthcheck --name eregion-annatar-check --entrypoint true eregion-annatar:incoming` — no mount, no `orodruin.env`, no environment. Creating the container needs every layer. If it fails or passes its bound (120 s; `ORO_CHECK_TIMEOUT=<s>` on the command line, digits, at least 1, no leading zero — `timeout 00` sets no bound —, checked before anything is sent and never expanded by make, like `REF`), its container (`timeout 30 docker rm -f`) and `incoming` are removed, the cause is said (docker's words, then the bound, docker unable to create the container, `true` that does not run, ssh, or the code), `:orodruin` is unchanged and `~/.eregion-image` stays absent, as under OI1. A check container left by a push cut short is removed at the start of the next one, with the `incoming` left. An image not read back as the proven one is never run, not even for this check.
  - **Within the contract**: no network (only loopback, so no IMDS and no token for the managed identity), never pulled, nothing injected (`true` replaces the image's command: no Python, no injector), no cloud call to journal, nothing mounted, bounded and removed. It runs in a narrower surface than the first real run, which follows the tag. The six guarantees are taken one by one in the contract.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_published_image.py` on OI1's fake docker (`tests/unit/fake_oci.py` now creates containers as docker does: the config read and every layer unpacked, else exit 125; `FAKE_LOAD_DROP=layer`, `FAKE_RUN_HANG`, `FAKE_RUN_EXIT`, a container left holds its image). The case of a layer lost after the load is red on main, under both stores (the image tagged `:orodruin`, then the migration fails). Mutants M3470–M3479, M3520–M3524. To measure, with Jonathan: `make orodruin-push REF=<the commit of main>`, then `make orodruin-check`.
- **The canary's OIDC subject is the immutable one GitHub issues** (REN1, #470; `docs/operations.md`, « Le canari azurerm, sans secret »). Since the account's rename, GitHub issues for this repository the subject `repo:<owner>@<owner id>/<repository>@<repository id>:…` (`use_immutable_subject`, read on `repos/…/actions/oidc/customization/sub`): the subject by name that REN1's entry gave (`[0.5.3]`) never matched — every line of the canary failed at its login, `AADSTS700213` (measured on 04/11). The procedure now reads the prefix from the API, so no id enters the repository; the federated credential trusts the immutable subject, and the two by name are removed (Jonathan, 04/11). Better than before: the subject no longer rests on a name someone else could take — an id is never given again. The name stays in it, so a later rename will likely change it again.
  - Tests: `test_workflows.py` — the subject read from the API, the heredoc left to expand it, no id in the section.
