A window counts as pulled from the channel only when the watch's record says so: the pull writes each window it brings down there, with its blob's creation (`note_pulled`) — a `channel_created` the file carries alone proves nothing, the window is then local. The record is read, changed and written under a `flock`, through a temporary file of its own (`mkstemp`). A record that cannot be read believes no window (its cycles stay `real`); a watch stopped more than 5 min during a local run sees its window late, refused — the safe side. A copy pulled before this version (a window still open at the deployment) is pulled once again, through `rewrite_refusal`, stamped with the listing's creation, then written down: an exercise across a deployment stays measured. Tests: `test_exercise.py` (a forged `channel_created`, six processes at once, the transition); mutants M2957, M3075–M3077, M3080–M3081.
  - **The catalog vouches for a local script's content, not its path** (V-19; « Les scripts locaux »). `catalog_destructive` recognised a scenario's script by its path, and `./scripts` is mounted writable in the local compose: each entry of `technique_catalog.yaml` that names a `reference_script` or `setup_scripts` now carries their sha256 (`script_sha256`, in the schema). A script whose content is not that one is unknown, so destructive (refused in production), and `run_script` never sends it: read once, its digest compared, that content sent. A script changed changes its pin in the same commit (a test reads every pin against its file). The path is compared normal (`./scripts/…`, `a/../`, its absolute path under the repository). Tests: `test_injector_guards.py`; mutants M2958, M3078.
  - **The advisories accepted, each with its reason; `make lock` says what follows** (R-20, N-16; `docs/operations.md`, « Les avis de vulnérabilité des verrous »). `requirements/audit-accepted.json` holds the advisories a human accepted (`package`, `id`, a written `reason`, `since`): chromadb 1.5.9's four (no fixed version yet) concern its HTTP server, and Eregion runs chromadb embedded only (`chromadb.PersistentClient`), no server started nor exposed. `make audit` and the CI say them as accepted, never red; one no longer found is said, to be removed; a list of another shape is refused. `scripts/pip_audit_issues.py pr`: red only for an advisory with a fix no one accepted — informative while none has one, a failed audit said, never red. `make lock` titles the advisories it prints and says when `make audit` could not run. Tests: `test_pip_audit_issues.py`; mutant M2959, M1159 re-anchored.
  - **The images' base pinned by its digest** (P-02; `docs/operations.md`, « Les images publiées »). `annatar/Dockerfile` and `glorfindel/Dockerfile` start from `python:3.12-slim@sha256:…` (the multi-architecture index read on Docker Hub on 02/11), the same in both: a moved tag no longer changes what is built. Its security updates come by a bump in a PR (`docker buildx imagetools inspect python:3.12-slim`). Test: `test_published_image.py`.
  - **The SBOM's tool pinned; the bot's token kept from forks; the advisories on each push and pull request** (R-14, R-15, P-03; workflows, in commits of their own). `publish.yml` no longer runs `anchore/sbom-action`, which fetched syft at run time in the job that signs: `published_image.py install-syft` installs syft v1.51.1 (the version that action ran) from its release archive, kept only under its sha256 (measured on the real archive), and the job runs it on the image loaded and proven. `notify.yml` gives `EREGION_DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` and the `#lots` forum only to a release, a closed issue, or a pull request or run whose head is this repository; `roadmap.yml` never to a fork's pull request. `ci.yml` gains a job `audit` (« dependency advisories »): read only, red on a pull request only for an advisory with a fix no one accepted, never red on a push (its step continues on error: H0's CD reads every check-run of a release's commit). Tests: `test_workflows.py`, `test_published_image.py`.
- **The root of trust: the agent sessions get their own identity, and H0 never goes back on its own** (SEC12, #418; decided by Jonathan on 02/11: a GitHub App on this repository only, merges stay autonomous, the tags `v*` reserved to the maintainer). Threat model: a new boundary, « Les sessions d'agents → main → le CD » (`docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`).
  - **The sessions' token** (`scripts/agent_token.py`): the App's JWT (RS256, issued 60 s in the past, 9 min long), its installation on the repository, an installation token restricted to this one repository, cached (0600, its directory 0700, owned by the user) until 5 minutes before it expires; a cache of another App or repository, or readable by others, is never read; a reinstalled App is looked up again. No command prints the token; a redirection is never followed (urllib would carry the `Authorization` header to the host it names). Two outputs in `scripts/agent-bin/` (100755 in Git, tested): git's credential helper (`https://github.com` and this repository only; anything else, nothing) and a `gh` wrapper (`GH_TOKEN` from the App, a config directory of its own: never the maintainer's `gh`; no token, no call). Errors are said in a sentence, never the key nor the token; a key readable by others, or not RSA, is refused. PyJWT and cryptography were already locked (through msal): no new dependency. Tests: a key generated in `tmp_path`, a fake GitHub on the loopback that checks each JWT with its public half (`tests/unit/test_agent_token.py`).
  - **The CD of H0 refuses a release older than what runs, or than its floor**, by its version (`vX.Y.Z`, compared as numbers) or by its commit — said with the mention, never taken up again on its own (before: skipped silently, by the commit only). The floor is the highest version deployed on this H0 and its commit, raised by each deployment and by what runs, never lowered. Never back by a reading that fails: what runs unreadable, or two commits git cannot order (`merge-base`: 128), is a refusal. The only way back is asked on H0, for one published tag (a pre-release refused): `make glorfindel-host-cd-rollback TAG=…` (`eregion_cd.py rollback TAG`), verified as any release; the release it leaves is marked « rolled back », a cut pass included. The CD also reads, without a token, whether an active rule reserves the release's tag (its creation, update and deletion): named with the start, said in orange when absent (with the mention) or unreadable — never a refusal, what GitHub shows without a token is to be measured on H0 (`infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le CD de H0 »).
  - **The review, traced**: the commit reviewed is noted when the reviewer is launched (`git rev-parse <branch>`), given to it and said back in its report — never the head read when the verdict is posted. The verdict of the Opus 5.5 review is posted on the pull request before the merge, for that commit (`scripts/review_verdict.py body --head`), checked back before the merge (`check --reviewed`: the pull request's head must be that commit — a push after the review asks for another —, only the authors named, the last verdict for it, a report never carries a key), and the merge is tied to it (`gh pr merge --match-head-commit`). Traced, not authenticated: it proves who posted it (the App). The step is in the elrond skill and the reviewer's definition, `docs/sessions.md` and `docs/elrond.md`.
  - **Written for Jonathan, step by step** (`docs/sessions.md`, « L'identité des sessions », « Les règles du dépôt »): the App (created by Jonathan on 02/11, without the *Workflows* permission: a change to `.github/workflows/` is his to push and merge; its ID never in the repository), switching the sessions of this project only (a git config of their own; `EREGION_AGENT_SESSION`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` in the project's ignored `.claude/settings.local.json`; the wrapper first on their PATH only; the checks), the two rulesets (tags `v*`; `main`: a pull request, no force-push nor deletion, the six required checks — the classic protection removed only once they are all in the ruleset) and how to verify them, a release published from a session refused, removing his own credentials from the sessions' machine. `CODEOWNERS` is said indicative (P-07): it requires nothing; the threat model no longer says otherwise, nor « read-only permissions everywhere ».
  - **Open**: Jonathan's gestures; the maintainer's credentials on the sessions' machine while they are there; the harness's attestation, bound to the lock's sha256 but not to its author (R-13: signing it is proposed).
- **The console reads runs/ as the watch reads it, and confronts an exercise run's T0** (SEC15, #425; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2, `docs/analysis.md`, « D'où vient chaque entrée »). runs/ is written by other containers than the console's.
  - **One reader** (`eregion_console/runs_reader.py`, on SEC8's `glorfindel.channel_reader.open_regular`) for every read of runs/ by the console: a regular file only, never through a link, never blocking, a bounded read (16 MB, 1 MB for a report, a window, a manifest, a status), a refusal said once in its log, whatever name the file is read by. A file refused reads as absent; Orodruin's status of a request refused is « unreadable », never « not carried yet ». It serves the live feed and its history, the exercise windows, the watch's cycles, a VM's history, the run reports (`run_report.read_reports` takes a reader, its default unchanged), the campaigns' manifests and report, the operator's lifts, Orodruin's contract and status, the sections. The feed and the TUI read only the end of their files (`channel_reader.tail_lines`, new, with its own tests; the watch's reading is unchanged); the TUI lists runs/ by `lstat`.
  - **No route reads on the event loop**: `/api/exercises`, `/api/campaigns`, `/api/channel`, the feed, a VM's history, a request's follow and the rest read in a thread (a test watches every read; it found Orodruin's contract read in the loop by a request's route).
  - **An exercise run's T0 confronted on Dagorlad's indicators, in the coverage matrix and in a request's follow**, as the campaign report confronts it (SEC9, `indicators.t0_confronted` on `containment.t0_refusal`): the window this host holds (believed, refused or purged), the Dagorlad request, the decision attached, the attack time the watch received, its first sight of the VM — indexed once per computation (the matrix confronts the whole history: 1 000 runs over 10 000 cycles, 13 s before, 0.06 s). A T0 not believed gives no time — to detect, to decide, to contain — and is counted apart (« N T0 not believed: no time counted ») and said on the wire's line and the matrix's run, with why; a Dagorlad request held here but unreadable is counted on the tile too. A proof whose T0 is not believed is dated by the watch's detection, said on the overview's « Last proof » tile; a computation given no windows says it did not confront them (`t0_confronted`). Consequence: a run whose window was purged (a week after its end) is no longer timed on Dagorlad's 30 days — said in the console, on the tile (« N: window purged, a week after its end ») and on the wire's line. The console's slim cycles now keep the run the signal names, the attack time received and the row's time.
  - Still open, in the threat model (#438): a campaign's page reads its files through the analysis (`analysis/`, which may not import Glorfindel's reader), outside the console's reader — in a thread, never in the loop.
  - Names of runs/ never taken raw: escaped as Rich markup in the TUI, their control characters escaped in the console's log; a file that is no file refused from its lstat alone, nothing opened; the end of a file keeps a whole line its bound falls on.
  - Tests: a real pipe (with its guard) and a link in each place the console reads runs/, the TUI's feed, a file larger than its bound, an advanced T0 refused on the indicators, in the matrix and in Dagorlad (`test_console_exercises.py`, `test_console.py`, `test_runs_channel.py`, `test_indicators.py`, `test_console_dagorlad_follow.py`); mutants M3000–M3019, M3085–M3094, M3130, M3136–M3145; M564, M1819, M2465, M2485 re-anchored.
- **The console's token is the console's alone; never printed; a session dated, lasting 12 h, ended by a rotation** (SEC10, #416; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Toujours un jeton »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`). The console's token, where the deployment does not set it, is drawn into `~/.eregion/console/token` — the console's own directory, which neither the watch nor the bot mounts (a test on `docker-compose.yml` holds it). Its former place, beside Glorfindel's state, is removed at the first start and a new token drawn, said. Earlier notes said the token was « shown once »: it is no longer printed at all — the start gives the URL without it and `make console-url` reads it. The session cookie carries its date of issue, signed by an HMAC of the token: it opens the console for 12 hours (`SESSION_TTL_S`, an operator's shift and its margin); dated ahead of the clock or rewritten, it opens nothing. `eregion rotate-token` (`make console-rotate`) draws a new token, read at the next request without a restart: every session of the former one is refused, the live feed's included (closed, 1008). A session cookie of any other form — the former one, digits of another script — is refused, never an error. Tests in `test_console.py`; mutants M2860–M2875.
- **SEC11 — rights at their narrowest** (#417; the threat model, frontier 5).
  - **A perimeter names its subscription, or refuses** (R-19; `eregion_core/inscription.py`). `Scope.check` refuses a target when its scope names no subscription; `Scope.for_bench()` — Dagorlad's plan, Orodruin's runner, the injectors' check at execution — reads `AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`, and unset it refuses every target. Before, an empty subscription let the identity's rights alone bound where an injector acted. The console's plan builds its scope the same way (the bench it names, in the subscription of its process); `make console-shots` gives its sample's. Mutants M2880–M2882.
  - **Orodruin's contract is an allow-list** (V-29; `eregion_core/orodruin.py`, `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « SEC11 »). It refused four writes on the whole group and let the rest through: Network Contributor on the bench's group armed Orodruin. Now every action a role holds inside the bench must be one the injectors need at that kind of scope — the reads on the group; `start` and Run Command on a VM; start and cancel on an experiment; Log Analytics Reader, as Azure defines it, on the workspace (its actions and its data action, `workspaces/jobs/export/action`; a test reads the role's published JSON through the contract's own reader) — written as listed (a wildcard is not expanded); the rights effective on the group are the reads and the contract's own read of role assignments. Anything else (a broad role, a wildcard, a data action, a role it cannot read, a scope below a VM) is `identity_unlisted_right`: passive. What `infra/orodruin/main.tf` grants holds unchanged. Mutants M2883–M2887.
  - **No fallback on the former shared principal** (V-17; `docker-compose.yml`, `Makefile`, `docs/operations.md`, « Identités Azure »). A service of the compose (the watch, the console, the bot) with an empty pair took `AZURE_CLIENT_*`, the former single principal — Contributor on the subscription where it still lingers. Now each takes its own pair only; empty, it has no service principal (the VM's managed identity if there is one, else none), `make glorfindel-start` says so for each (`check-azure-env`) and the watch says so at start (« Identity: no service principal » — in a container on a workstation, with no identity at all; M3036). The `make glorfindel-*` containers (`GLORFINDEL_AZURE_PICK`) the same; `AZURE_CLIENT_*` is read by the venv's watch only. `team.env.example` names the pair of its watch and of its console. **To check before upgrading**: each service's pair set in `.envrc` (`make glorfindel-start` names the missing ones). Mutants M2888–M2891.
  - **The session drain is its own role, given VM by VM to the VMs Glorfindel defends** (R-03; decided by Jonathan on 02/11: the drain stays; `infra/identities/glorfindel-session-drain.json`, `docs/operations.md`, « Identités Azure »). Run Command — the watch's strongest right, a root command on the VM — leaves **Eregion Glorfindel Response**, which goes on whole groups, for **Eregion Glorfindel Session Drain**, assigned on each VM of an explicit list, never on a group. Not the inscription tag: it says where an injector may act, not what Glorfindel defends (bree, never enrolled, is the bench's VM that meets real attacks). On H0, Terraform assigns it (`infra/glorfindel-host/`, `session_drain_vms`, kept in the committed `session_drain.auto.tfvars`: gondolin and bree; each VM read on Azure, a typo fails the plan; an ordinary `make glorfindel-host-up` removes nothing; output `session_drain_vms`), with a test against a mock azurerm (`tests/session_drain.tftest.hcl`, run by CI next to Celebrimbor's). A VM without it is still isolated; the drain refused is said on the card (« Run Command refusé sur cette VM : le rôle … n'y est pas ») and fails the isolation's verification, as any session left open. The readiness reads the right on the VM itself and names the new role (`no_drain`); the audit warns, no longer fails, when Run Command alone is missing; Run Command held on the whole group is said as beyond the response (`identity_beyond_response`). **Today** H0's response role, posed by hand, still carries Run Command on every VM of the bench. **Gestures for Jonathan, in this order**: (1) `az role definition create` of the session drain's role; (2) H0's plan read, then `make glorfindel-host-up`, without `YES`; (3) right after the apply, two VM scopes for the role (`terraform -chdir=infra/glorfindel-host output session_drain_vms`, or `az role assignment list --assignee <principal> --role "Eregion Glorfindel Session Drain" --all`) — the readiness proves nothing yet, the response's role still carrying Run Command on the group; (4) only then, `az role definition update` of the response's role; (5) after gesture 4, the readiness of gondolin and bree, without any `no_drain` (`docs/operations.md`). A listed VM that Celebrimbor rebuilds loses its assignment with it: it gets it back at the next `make glorfindel-host-up`; until then its readiness says `no_drain`, and it acts as `human_only`. Mutants M2892–M2899, M3031–M3035.
- **What was left on Glorfindel's side of the second counter-audit** (SEC13, #419; `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2).
  - **The incident registry, written by two processes, loses nothing** (V-10): the watch and `glorfindel respond` each hold an `IncidentRegistry` on `~/.glorfindel/incidents.jsonl`, whose thread lock kept only one process's threads apart. Every read-modify-write now holds an `flock` on `incidents.jsonl.lock`, and the file is rewritten whole (temporary file + `os.replace`), as the escalations store since SEC3. Measured with two real processes and a reader (`tests/unit/test_locks.py`): before, 72 incidents lost out of 120 and 77 reads of half a file; after, none.
  - **Every state of `~/.glorfindel` owner-only and written whole** (V-27): the incidents, `posture_state.json`, `discovered_assets.json`, `readiness.json` and the bot's files (`bot_*.json`) were rewritten in place or left readable by all (0644); they go through the same path as the isolation states now (0600, directory 0700, a new file put in place whole). An earlier 0644 file is tightened at its next write.
  - **The bot's `/pending` under the buttons' policy** (R-16): the command listed the waiting cards (VMs, actions) to whoever was in the channel. It answers an allowed user or role only (`DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS`, `DISCORD_ALLOWED_ROLES`); anyone else gets an ephemeral refusal, logged (« Command refused »); closed by default, as the buttons.
  - **A state directory that can't be listed is an unknown, never « no state »** (R-07): `Path.glob` gives an empty list for a directory it cannot read, and every reader that concludes from an absence read `isolation/` or `blocks/` that way — the orphan sweep (N-06), the quarantine readers (SEC4b), the reassertion; a set-aside directory that couldn't be listed made the pass raise. `unreadable_states` lists the directory itself (`isolation/`, `blocks/`, `isolation/aside/`): the sweep keeps every orphan and says why, `_held_in_quarantine` and `_recorded_original` raise `StateUnreadable`, and the reassertion says it on the `state_unreadable` card, once, closed once it reads again (`docs/azure.md`).
  - **A reassertion check that raises is said on one card** (V-16): when `verify_isolation` or `verify_block_ip` raised (a read right removed, the API down), the isolation or the block went unchecked with only a log line, at every pass. It is a card now, `unverified` (action `review_unverified`, high severity, nothing to run): one per isolation or block while the check keeps raising, not one per pass, its text kept to the last error (« first error: …; last: … »); a check that answers « can't tell » (`verified: None`: rules or NICs unreadable, precedence unknown) is said on the same card with its error and never closes it; acknowledged, silent until the next failure (read by the card's exact id); closed with a note once the check answers again, or once nothing is recorded to check (released) — not while a state can't be read. Nothing is put back nor lifted on a check that raises (`docs/azure.md`).
  - **A privilege escalation is read on sudo's own line** (R-18): `normalize_row` labelled `privilege_escalation` any Syslog line holding « USER=root », whatever program wrote it — through a client's rule or a proposed one, a characterized signal (an autonomous action allowed) named so to the model. It reads the line as the shipped rule does now (`_sudo_to_root`): tagged `sudo` (`ProcessName`), in sudo's form, not a refusal; anything else is a `syslog_event`. **`sudo-privilege-escalation` projects `ProcessName`** in its row so T1548 stays characterized — its row gains that one column, which the model sees in the signal; the T1548 rows of the LLM harness (`scripts/llm_smoke.py`) take the same shape. A client's or proposed Syslog rule whose row lacks it is no longer characterized: an autonomous isolate escalates, and a snapshot alone leaves no floor card — a client's T1548 rule that drops `ProcessName` is said when it loads (a note of `load_config`, with the fix) (`docs/glorfindel.md`, « Garde-fou déterministe »).
  - **Two sentences said more than holds**: the observe-only mode (`docs/glorfindel.md`) was « zero risk » — it writes nothing on Azure, but each decision's log extracts still go to the model's provider, the read identity sees what its role covers, and the local state and alerts say what it saw; the threat model's « a flood of lines in `runs/` » row (§2) said « no call to the model nor the cloud » — true of a refused line only: an admitted `attack_started` runs its detection and, if it finds, a decision of the model, a `detection_missed` a rule proposal — within the channel's rate, per file and per minute (SEC8). §2 also names what SEC13 holds.
  - Tests: `test_locks.py` (two real processes and a reader on the incident registry), `test_posture.py`, `test_discovery.py`, `test_readiness.py`, `test_bot.py`, `test_unreadable_state.py`, `test_orphan_sweep.py`, `test_reassert.py`, `test_detection_rules.py`, `test_forged_logs.py`; mutants M2915–M2939, each caught; M283, M1212 and M1528 follow the code they mutate.
- **The `runs/` channel is read as an input the watch does not trust, to the end** (SEC8, #414; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Le canal runs/ lu comme une entrée non fiable »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2).
  - **Regular files only, a bounded read** (`glorfindel/channel_reader.py`): each `*_signals.jsonl` is opened without blocking and without following a link, then `fstat`; a link, a named pipe, a device or a socket is refused, said once, never read — the watch's loop (reassertion, TTL, heartbeat) never waits on it. At most 256 KiB and 200 lines per file per poll, the rest at the next one; a line past 64 KiB is refused, said. The search for a restore's moment reads `runs/` the same way.
  - **A bounded rate, bounded effects**: at most 30 lines per file and 120 for the channel per minute, the surplus not admitted, said in one line at most once a minute and counted (`(débit)` in `channel_refusals.json`); one search per VM and technique, 16 in all; at most 8 of the channel's lines waiting per VM; 64 VMs named. The RulePoller's detections are never held by these bounds.
  - **Finite durations, known techniques**: `detection_timeout_s` and `detection_max_s` are finite numbers within bounds (3600 s, 86 400 s) — `Infinity`, `NaN`, text refused, in the search too. A line naming a technique neither in the shared catalog nor in the watch's rules is refused: no search, no rule proposal, no call to the model.
  - **The moment of a restore is never the channel's on a real incident** (`glorfindel/restore_plan.py`): an `attack_started` line counts only for an exercise card tied to its run, never for a moment after the card's first detection; otherwise the earliest moment the defender recorded (first detection, isolation), and the restore's field says so. `glorfindel restore` without `--before` takes the moment of the VM's first-detected pending card; without one, it asks.
  - **The workspace queried comes from the config**: a rule proposal's `getschema` reads a workspace of `glorfindel-config.yaml`; a `detection_missed` line naming another is refused.
  - **ARM calls carrying the token are built from the parts read of an id** (`eregion_core/held.py`, `vm_parts`, `arm_relative`): the enrollment read rebuilds the group's and the VM's URLs from subscription, group and name, and refuses an id that is not exactly a VM's before a token is asked for; Orodruin reads a role definition only on a path under ARM, Gothmog calls ARM paths only, and a listing's next page outside ARM is never followed (Orodruin, Glorfindel's permission check).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_runs_channel.py` (a real FIFO and real links in `runs/` with a guard delay, a huge file, a burst, `Infinity`, an unknown technique, a restore on a real incident, a foreign workspace), `tests/unit/test_arm_urls.py` (a property: whatever the id, the host is ARM's); mutants M2810–M2839.

### Added
- **The correlation, a brick of the product** (COR1, #407, 02/11; `docs/design/correlation.md`; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « La corrélation, brique du produit »; `docs/operations.md`, « Déclarer ses origines sur H0 »). Two events of a VM tied together by the RulePoller, before the decision — never in the prompt, never by the model's confidence (`glorfindel/correlation.py`).
  - **The brick**: for a line of a VM (account, instant), the SSH sessions that carry it, read in Syslog — sshd's `Accepted … for <account> from <address>` (IPv6 and `::ffff:a.b.c.d` included), sshd's pam session without its Accepted line (origin unknown), the account taken by `su`, `sudo -u`, `login`, `cron` (not followed). Every session opened in the last 7 days counts (Jonathan, 02/11; at most 1,000 lines, a reading cut at its limit is unknown, so a card); a « session closed » line is not read (any local user writes one). One query per VM, only for the lines that need it, at most 4 per rule and poll; a failed query, a reading cut at its limit, no session, the cap reached: unknown, so a card (A0).
  - **First use, ADM1's root shells**: each `expected_sudo.admins` entry may declare `origins` (CIDR ranges, read strictly). A root shell or code in line of an expected account is set aside only if every one of its sessions comes from an expected origin — journaled (`via: origin`), counted (`glorfindel report`, the console); otherwise a card that says why (`SessionOrigin`: « session depuis 203.0.113.9, origine inconnue »; a private address not declared, « mouvement latéral possible »). A sensitive command stays a card whatever the origin. Without `origins`: ADM1's behaviour. **A noise reducer, not a proof** (Jonathan, 02/11): any local user can write an sshd login line from a declared origin, so the set-aside holds only against an honest administrator and an intruder who does not know Eregion — an intruder whose real session is older than the window, or an access that writes no session line, can have a root shell set aside on its own; a sensitive command is always a card. An origin confirmed outside the VM is planned: COR2, #427. Only sshd's own `Accepted` lines are read, and only the session lines opened for the accounts in question — never sudo's own (`session opened for user root … by <account>`) nor systemd's: a week of an active administrator's sudo no longer fills the 1,000 lines.
  - **Second use**: a brute force followed by a login from the same address on the same VM, in the rule's window (`ssh-brute-force`) — the row marked `Correlation: ssh-login-after-brute-force` with its logins, a critical signal, first among the poll's cards, one signal per address and VM; a pending card for that address never absorbs it in silence (a compromise said, or a severity that rises, is a material change: reason and severity updated; a card that says a compromise keeps saying it — a plain re-fire never replaces its reason), and the compromise card carries its address (one address, one card). **Always said to a human** (the Orchestre, 02/11): an autonomous non-disruptive action may still run (the block of the address), and a card `account_compromised` follows it, never approvable in one click (`agent.compromise_card`, after `verify_action`); a card the cycle makes anyway opens with « Compte peut-être compromis ». A brute force whose logins could not be read is said the same way (`LoginCheck`, card `compromise_unchecked`).
  - **Counted**: `rule_status.json` (`correlation`, `expected_sudo.origin_count`), `glorfindel report`, the rule's detail in the console. **New key** `origins`: a version before this one refuses a config that carries it. Tests: `tests/unit/test_correlation.py`; mutants M2720–M2759, M3020–M3028.
- **Glorfindel's own identity watched: a Run Command outside an isolation it decided is a critical card** (SID1, #409, 02/11; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Notre propre identité surveillée »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §5). H0 keeps the response role on the bench, `runCommand` included (the session drain of an isolation, `ss -K`: an NSG does not cut an established connection); its managed identity is shared by the watch, the console and the bot. Rather than take the right away, Glorfindel reads its own use of it in the Activity Log.
  - **A shipped rule**, `run-command-by-glorfindel-outside-isolation` (`azure_activity`, T1651): every Run Command of the hour, one row per operation, the time of its first line (`Started`), failures kept, and `WatchedCaller = "glorfindel"` — another caller's row is not this rule's (a client's own rule on Run Command, without the column, keeps every caller).
  - **Attached, or a card** (`glorfindel/providers/azure/own_run_command.py`): `isolate_vm` now records in the isolation state when its drain asks for its Run Command — just before the call, and only if it is made (`drains_at`, the last 20; written before the drain waits up to 300 s for its result, so a row ingested meanwhile is no card; nothing on Windows, nor when the VM can't be read before the call). Glorfindel's Run Command is dropped only when it started within [drain − 120 s, drain + 420 s] of a drain recorded on that VM, one drain for one operation (by `CorrelationId`, kept across polls; drains remembered 2 h after the state is gone, forgotten by a restart). Otherwise it is kept, marked `GlorfindelRunCommand` with its reason — `no_isolation`, `outside_drain` (naming the `CorrelationId` of the operation that took the drain, `GlorfindelDrainTakenBy`), `state_unreadable`, `time_unreadable`, `identity_unknown`, `caller_unreadable` (A0: an unreadable state, time, identity, or a row naming no caller at all is a card) — and critical, the summary card past the cap included.
  - **Decided by the code**, like every AL1 row: `control_plane_change`, no model, no investigation, no action — none against the VM the command ran on, the card anchored there included; never one click. The card names the VM and the reason, recalls that a `glorfindel restore` also runs a Run Command, and gives the steps (what the command ran, the host, the right withdrawn during the inquiry, the credentials renewed).
  - **Threat model and roadmap**: the mitigation of the shared identity written in §5 (what it sees, its delay: the Activity Log's ingestion — it says, after the fact; it does not prevent), and the **signed action queue** (only the watch holds the write role) a prerequisite before any VM off the bench, in « Ouvert » and in the 1.0 criteria of `ROADMAP.md` (FS1, #413). What it does not hold, said: every container that writes the isolation state (watch, console, bot) can write a drain there — the rule catches a clumsy use of the identity, not an attacker who knows Eregion. Bench hypotheses A7, A8 in `docs/operations.md`. The rule sees nothing until the Activity Log reaches the bench's workspace (ID1, #281).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_own_run_command.py` (a fake Activity Log and real isolation states); mutants M2790–M2809, M2970–M2972; M810 re-anchored.
- **The administrators' expected sudo** (ADM1, #284, 02/11; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Les sudo attendus des administrateurs »; `docs/operations.md`, « Poser la liste des administrateurs sur H0 »). On a VM in use, most of the sudo to root `sudo-privilege-escalation` finds are its administrators' — 12 snapshots in 3 days on H0 in `non_disruptive`. A new key of `glorfindel-config.yaml`, `expected_sudo` (read strictly, A3), names the accounts expected per VM or pattern of VMs: their sudo to root opens no card, unless the command is sensitive.
  - **Set aside before the decision, never in the prompt**: by the RulePoller, once the row is routed to its VM and before any signal (`glorfindel/expected_sudo.py`, `RulePoller._judge_expected`) — not in the query (the shipped text P1 keeps intact; a row dropped on the server can no longer be counted), not in `investigate` (a graph cycle already open). The model never sees the list. The lines are read outside the poller's shared lock, and bounded (1 KB of command, linear expressions): a 8-KB line written with `logger -t sudo` no longer holds the other rules.
  - **Only when everything holds**: sudo's own form, as the rule reads it (`authpriv`, fields separated by spaces as the agent writes them, no TTY); nothing ambiguous — a line of 900 characters or more (sudo cuts at 960, the rest unread), a line break, more than one `COMMAND=` (a crafted `PWD`) stays a card; every `USER=` of the line says root (`sudo -u other` stays a card); the account, written in full, is expected on that VM; neither the command nor `LD_PRELOAD`/`LD_LIBRARY_PATH`/`LD_AUDIT` in sudo's `ENV=` is sensitive. **Sensitive** (shipped, extended by `sensitive_commands`, never replaced): `/etc/shadow`, `useradd`/`usermod`/`passwd`…, `sudoers`/`visudo`, `chmod u+s`/`4755`, `install -m 4755`, `setcap`, a supervision agent stopped or removed (`systemctl`, `dpkg -r/-P`, `rpm -e`, `rm`, `chmod -x`…), `auditctl`, the logging's configuration, logs deleted, cron, systemd units, kernel modules, the firewall emptied, `authorized_keys`/`authorized_keys2`, PAM, `ld.so.preload`, `LD_PRELOAD=`. A row no VM claims, another technique's rule, no config: nothing set aside.
  - **Root shells make a card, in their known forms** (Jonathan, 02/11): a shipped category, `root_shell`, with no exception in this lot — a shell, `su`, `runuser`, `login`, `script`, a nested `sudo` (so `sudo -i`, `sudo -s`, `sudo su -`), `screen`, `tmux`, also behind `env`, `nice`, `nohup`, `timeout`, `stdbuf`, `command`, `time`, `ionice`, `chrt`, `taskset`, `setsid`, `flock`, `chroot`, `strace`, `ltrace`, `nsenter`, `unshare`, `watch`, `systemd-run`; an interpreter with code in line or none (`python -c`, `python -m pty`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e`, `node -e`, `php -r`, `lua -e`), `gdb`; `systemd-run --shell`, `socat … exec:`, `tar --checkpoint-action=exec`, `docker exec`/`run` to a shell, `env --split-string`; `find -exec`, `xargs` to a shell, `awk` with `system()`; an editor or a pager (`vim`, `vi`, `nano`, `less`, `more`, `man` — `sudoedit` instead). **Not exhaustive**: a script or a program not listed, run by an expected account (`sudo /tmp/x.py`), passes without a card — the blind spot assumed until AUD1 (`auditd`); COR1 sets aside the root shells whose every SSH session comes from an expected origin.
  - **The shipped rule returns one row per VM and distinct line** (`… by Computer, _ResourceId, SyslogMessage`): with one row per VM, an administrator's later sudo — or a line any local user can write with `logger -t sudo` — hid another account's in the same window. Still one card per VM and poll. **A log line's dedup identity is its instant and its text's digest**: an intruder's line at an expected one's instant, ingested a poll later, was taken for it. A detection seen under the old identity is sent once more after the update — at most one card per VM and rule within ~15 min, which can launch a snapshot again in `non_disruptive`: deploy away from a recent detection.
  - **Never silent**: each sudo set aside is written to `~/.glorfindel/expected_sudo.jsonl` before it is set aside (a journal that cannot be written sets nothing aside) and counted in `rule_status.json`; `glorfindel report` gives the count per VM and account, the latest ones and since when the journal runs (`--json`: `expected_sudo`); the console shows « N expected » on the rule and in its detail. A config that cannot be read excludes nothing: the watch does not start on it.
  - **New key**: a version before this one refuses a config that carries it — deploy first, then push the list to H0. Tests: `tests/unit/test_expected_sudo.py`; mutants M2650–M2679, M2700–M2719, M2786–M2789.
- **The releases in #roadmap's message** (RM1, #406, 02/11; `docs/atelier-discord.md`, « Le bloc « Releases » »). Under the milestones' bars, a « **Releases** » block: the last one published, read on GitHub (`releases/latest`: its name, linked to its page, and its date), then the lines of a new table in `ROADMAP.md`, « Les prochaines releases », held by the Orchestre (version, title, content in one line, state: en préparation, brouillon, publiée; filled with 0.4.10, 0.5.0 and 0.5.x).
  - No release published (404), GitHub failing or an answer without a name: that line is left out, said in the log, and the job stays green; the workflow's permissions are unchanged (`contents: read`). The name and every cell of the table are made inert (SEC6, `glorfindel/chat_text.py`, now in `roadmap.yml`'s sparse checkout); a link that is not `https://` is none, and its parentheses are encoded. The section ends at a real heading (`#` to `######` and a space). The line of the version GitHub says published is shown « publiée », whatever the table still says.
  - A section missing, its header changed or a line of another number of cells: « _section introuvable dans ROADMAP.md_ », like the other sections, never a table half read. Each field cut, six lines at most then « … +N dans ROADMAP.md »; the block comes before the lots, whose lists grow: a message too long is cut at its end, never there.
  - `make release` (`scripts/release.py prepare`) sets its version's line to « brouillon » in that table, in its commit, through #roadmap's own reader: a table #roadmap would not read (its header changed) is never changed; without a line for it, nothing changes there and the command says so, a dry run refused included.
  - **The five indicators brought up to date** in `ROADMAP.md` (« Aujourd'hui »: BL1 on the bench on 31/10, bree's first autonomous blocks since 01/11 counted by the Overview, 0 % of 41 judged disruptive actions wrong, coverage per C1, last end-to-end run 31/10); #roadmap shows each whole up to 240 characters (110 before: the facts were cut off).
  - Tests: `test_roadmap_discord.py` (the block, latest absent, failing or unreadable, the section absent or of another shape, the bounds, the repository's own table), `test_release.py` (the line set, only it, shown by the dry run); mutants M2680–M2699.
- **BL1b — the choice of a grave public technique, written up** (#396; `docs/design/bl1-technique-grave.md`, pointer in `docs/banc-bl1.md`). Verifying each pinned Atomic Red Team test for the two candidates finds that **neither is playable by the R1 injector at the pinned version**: T1548.003's only non-destructive linux test has no declared cleanup and runs an interactive `sudo vim` (without a terminal it blocks, or exits in error leaving a swap file — neither faithful nor reversible); its others edit `/etc/sudoers` destructively without reverting; T1110.001's linux tests are a **sudo** brute force (not SSH), declare `dependencies` and fetch a remote payload (refused by R1, no Internet on Orodruin, and not detected by `ssh-brute-force`); all 14 tests of both techniques are refused by R1. The only nearby SSH linux test (T1110.004, « SSH Credential Stuffing From Linux ») is refused too, and an SSH attempt from the VM or Orodruin comes from a loopback or private address `ssh-brute-force` excludes. A public lead for the next bench session, submitted to Jonathan: T1136.001's « Butter » test (root-group account) is accepted by R1, and on Ubuntu its `sudo chpasswd` would probably write a sudo-to-root line the `sudo-privilege-escalation` regex accepts (it does not filter a root caller) — a hypothesis to measure, not wired here. The grave signal the measurement needs comes only from `sudo-privilege-escalation` (T1548.003), for which no reversible, non-interactive, dependency-free atomic exists yet. **Jonathan's decision (02/11): « the real + R2 »** — the « public libraries only » rule stays whole, with no exception (no home-grown script in a declared campaign, no cleanup-less atomic in R1). The contained-alone share is measured on **real traffic** (bree in `non_disruptive` since 01/11, 5 autonomous blocks), counted by the Overview with OV1 (#399); for exercises, BL1 is replayed when **R2** (#79, Stratus Red Team) brings a playable grave technique. Until then the declared BL1 campaign keeps its single public technique (T1136.001). No code or catalog change.

### Changed
- **`make glorfindel-host-push` keeps each VM's mode in place on H0** (BL1c, Jonathan's decision of 02/11; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, threat model §5, `docs/operations.md`). SEC7 forced `human_only` on every VM at each push: H0's posture on the bench (gondolin and bree in `non_disruptive`) was lost and had to be given again in the console. The push now reads the config in place on H0 through the ssh connection it already opens, and `host_config.py` carries each VM's mode (an exact name, as the console writes it) — never a bolder one (A0): the default stays `human_only` and `allow_destructive` empty whatever the local config says; a VM H0 does not know, H0's config absent, unreadable, of another shape or one the watch's strict reading would refuse — a single entry is enough: a mode it does not know (`full_auto`, deferred) or not text, a blank `match`, a key more; or a default outside the known modes — put every VM in `human_only`, never one VM dropped and the others kept; a pattern goes to `human_only`; the same name twice, the first (the one H0 resolves). It says, VM by VM, the mode sent and where it comes from (« gardé de H0 », or `human_only` and why). **`make glorfindel-host-push HUMAN_ONLY=1`** (only `1`), the emergency gesture: every VM back to `human_only`, an H0 that does nothing on its own. H0's config is read again just before the `scp` (not under `HUMAN_ONLY=1`, which carries nothing of H0): changed in between (a mode changed in the console during the push), nothing is sent. What H0's config says is shown printable only. Tests: `test_glorfindel_host.py` (H0's config readable, absent, empty, not YAML, of another shape, an unknown mode, a pattern, a new VM, HUMAN_ONLY), `test_host_console.py` (the recipe reads before the scp), `test_makefile_secrets.py` unchanged and green; mutants M2363–M2369, M2780–M2785.
- **H0 keeps writing on the bench: its posture, documented** (BL1c, #395; Jonathan's decision of 31/10, bree added on 01/11). The managed identity of H0 keeps « Eregion Glorfindel Response » on the bench's resource group alone, `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY=0`, gondolin and bree in `non_disruptive`, the CD active — BL1's exception « for the session » becomes H0's posture on the bench. The threat model (§5) says what it opens — the identity is shared by the watch, the console and the bot, so a compromised container or H0's SSH key holds the response's role too (`runCommand` as root on every VM of the bench, their NSGs and NICs written): the watch acts alone on both VMs in their mode, and an approval click or a bot button writes on the bench, by a human behind the console's token or Discord's allow list — and what bounds it: the role (never a restore, a deletion, a role), the group (never the subscription, never H0's own group), the modes (`human_only` everywhere else, the confidence gate, the guards) and the production rule, which stays the injectors'. `docs/operations.md` (« Identités Azure »: the line « H0: nothing changes » is gone), `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md` (what the VM is; the response's role given by hand, outside H0's Terraform state), `docs/banc-bl1.md` (steps 3 and 7 optional, « if H0 was put back read-only »; the measure by `make glorfindel-host-containment` with no other gesture; the result of 31/10 in one line).
- **The overview counts what Glorfindel does on real traffic; the exercises' figures go to Dagorlad** (OV1, #399, Jonathan's decision of 02/11; `docs/analysis.md`, « Le réel »; `docs/design/console.md`, « Les cinq indicateurs »). The week-end of 01/11, bree in `non_disruptive`, Glorfindel blocked five brute-force addresses alone — and the overview said « 0 of 6 »: it counted Annatar's runs only.
  - **TIME TO CONTAIN · REAL** and **CONTAINED ALONE · REAL**: an incident is a detection of the watch with a decision, on a VM it names, outside any exercise — one attacker (the rule's address) on one VM is one incident, its later cycles within 24 h repeats until its containment is lifted or said gone (a NAT folds attackers: the cautious side); it ends as BL1 says (`containment.incident`: executed in its cycle, read back on Azure, held). Its T0 is the detection's time, said so: no rule gives the first malicious event yet (a `FirstSeen` column is a Glorfindel lot apart). Beside them: the shadow, how the others ended (held, escalated, did not hold…), and how many lines are inferred real.
  - **Real or exercise**: the watch's debug line now says it (`source`, `exercise_run` — what `_cycle_source` already gave the memory and the cards; never shown to the model). Only its « exercise » is final: any other line is an exercise when it is the decision the overview attaches to a run, when a window still on the host covers it, or a run report on its VM (an attack report from before C1, its technique on any VM); else real. The doubt is said both ways (inferred real, inferred exercise). A failed cycle never loses its debug line to its label. A line dated ahead of the clock counts nowhere.
  - **Dagorlad's Runs** open on the exercises' time to contain and share contained alone (the runs, as before, with the faults taken for an attack): `GET /api/dagorlad/indicators`. The wrong rate was real already; the coverage and the last proof say « exercises ».
  - Tests: `test_indicators.py`, `test_agent_nodes.py`; mutants M2600–M2623; M1885 follows its line. Checked in Chromium on the sample data, with a real block executed alone and verified.
- **The campaign report and a campaign's console page confront the injectors' T0 and list of runs with what the watch's host holds; G1's fault rate checks a fault's effect readings** (SEC9, #415; `docs/analysis.md`, « D'où vient chaque entrée »; `docs/observation.md`). The decision was already the watch's; a run's T0, the list of runs and a fault's effect came from the injectors' host, checked in their shape only. The console's overview and Dagorlad's follow do not confront the T0 yet (a Console follow-up, with the same `containment.t0_refusal`).
  - **The T0** counts toward the time to contain only inside its run's exercise window (from its start to its end; the grace is the detection's), not before the Dagorlad request that launched it when this host holds it, not after the detection attached, not after the attack time the watch received from the injector during the run (its `attack_started`, SEC4a — what bounds the aggregated rules, whose rows carry no time), and not after the watch's first sight of the VM in the run — its detections there, or the `TimeGenerated` the telemetry gives the row its rule read. Clocks of two hosts: 10 s. Otherwise the incident keeps its ending (the watch's) and no time is counted from that T0: said in its line, the T0 column (« non retenu ») and the notes. A run without a window on the host is not timed either; a purged or refused window, an unreadable request are said. A T0 not believed bounds no other run: the next run's start and the technique's window are taken from believed T0s, else from the window the host holds.
  - **The runs** are the manifest's and those the watch saw around the campaign: a technique window this host holds, a line of the watch attached to a run (a detection, a `detection_timeout`, any cycle, the run's own debug file included) or a card. One the manifest does not list, or lists as not executed while the watch attached a line or a card to it, or that the channel's check leaves out of the facts while the host holds its window or the watch saw it, is an incident of unknown decision, said — the share becomes « at least ». « Around » starts at the earlier of an hour before the campaign's earliest start in its files and the arrival here of the campaign before it (a bound the channel does not give). A run another manifest lists, or with its own report, is counted there and said; a fault's window is never an incident.
  - **A fault is measured** (G1's rate) only when its report carries the readings of Glorfindel's telemetry that show its effect (`metrics.effect`): each target, every effect probe seen, read between the fault's start and the end of its window. Checked in their coherence, not read again by the watch (an open question for Jonathan). Readings are found by the target's full id, else its name; two targets of one name, readings kept by name, leave the fault not measured, both ids said. The check's word alone (`fault_effective`) no longer suffices. The unified run view (`read_run`) gives `effect`.
  - The campaign report's channel note says what is confronted. Tests: `test_containment.py`, `test_fault_rate.py`; mutants M2840–M2859, M2960–M2964; M2416 follows its line.

### Fixed
- **BL1's measure launched alone measures** (BL1c, #395, found at the bench session of 31/10). `python -m analysis.containment` (`make glorfindel-host-containment`) did not read the enrollment of a campaign pulled from the channel — every run was left out, « sa VM n'est pas inscrite »; it now sets it as the campaign report does (`check_enrollment`). Under `docker exec` in H0's container it kept compose's empty `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` / `_TENANT_ID` — « client_id should be the id of a Microsoft Entra application »; it now drops them as the watch and the console do (#205), with the same function, moved to the shared core (`eregion_core/identity.py`; `glorfindel.cli` keeps its name). The drop sits in `check_enrollment`, where the enrollment of a channel campaign is wired: `python -m analysis.campaign_report`, the triage and the index, launched by hand under `docker exec`, had the same defect and drop them too. The report written by the watch had neither defect. Tests: `test_containment.py`, `test_hosted_report.py` (red before); mutants M2360–M2362 (M56 follows the function).
- **A block's drift card refused on a full disk no longer silences its next return** (found by the nightly invariant machine after SEC13; `docs/azure.md`). A drift said and acknowledged, then held again while another rule was gone — worse on one side, better on the other — and the card saying it refused: the pass kept comparing with the acknowledged card, so the IP open again read « not worse », in silence (L1). What got better is the baseline before the card is written now (`reassert_cards.drift_meet`). Not a regression of SEC13: the same sequence fails on the code before it; SEC13 only changed what the machine drew. Also, this one introduced by SEC13: a write of the `unverified` card that fails is logged and said again at the next pass, never raised into a block's or an isolation's own pass. Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py` (the sequence, frozen), `test_reassert.py`; mutants M3120–M3122, M2151 re-anchored.
- **The reassertion puts the perimeters back before the VM blocks of their subnet** (L1Qr, #363; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « La règle de L1Q, entrée »). L1Q's machine rule (`blocked_again_after_an_acknowledged_drift`: a VM blocked on its subnet and for itself, the pair lifted by a neighbour's unblock, said and acknowledged, then blocked again) enters the invariant machine — a rule added, none removed nor narrowed, the oracle unchanged (A0). It shifted the draws onto an older defect, on `main` without it (seed 6 at 500): a VM block whose own rule no longer covered a NIC (its address changed) was read before its subnet's perimeter pair, turned into an allow; rewritten and read again under that allow, its card said « l'IP n'est plus bloquée sur db-nic-0, db-nic-1 »; the next entry then put the pair back, a moment later in the same pass, and the card was false once the pass ended (I3). Nothing opened; what the operator read was false. **The cause**: the blocks were reasserted in the order of a directory listing (`Path.glob`), which the filesystem decides. **The fix** (`glorfindel/reassert.py`, `_perimeters_first`): the entries holding a perimeter (`scoped: False`, merged or not) first, then the VM blocks. Tests: the sequence frozen, shrunk by hand from 7 rules to 3 (`test_a_vm_block_rewritten_before_its_subnets_perimeter_never_says_unblocked`, red on `main` 287e4ae), `test_reassert.py::test_a_perimeter_is_put_back_before_the_vm_blocks_of_its_subnet` (both listing orders, merged or not); mutants M2980–M2982.
- **The release takes off a NIC's rules on the quarantine NSG it leaves** (I3R, #431; `docs/azure.md`; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3R »). The night's seed 67 at 60 (also replayed by the macOS job on every PR): « I3: release said released, …/Db--01 is not back to its design », two failures, one cause. Db--01's isolation was put back by the reassertion while our quarantine NSG had lost its `managed-by` tag — not ours, so db-nic-0's deny pair went on the NSG the NIC carries, the quarantine NSG itself. The tag back and the state lost, the release read the NIC in our quarantine, gave it its NSG back, then swept only the NSGs that govern it now: `released`, the pair left on the quarantine NSG. Nothing opened; the release's word was false. **The fix** (`glorfindel/providers/azure/away.py`, `_left_quarantine_rules`): the sweep also looks, on the quarantine NSG a NIC leaves (named by a quarantine placement of the state, or the one the sweep takes it out of), for that NIC's rules — its hash, or an older name aimed at its addresses —, the guard asked for one aimed elsewhere (kept and said, `release_partial`). A NIC still in the quarantine (its undo failed) keeps its rules there; a bare one is looked for everywhere (D80), said once; an unreadable quarantine NSG is said. A partial release (that NSG unreadable, a 429 on a delete, a rule kept) keeps the quarantine NSG left unswept in its state (`quarantines_left`), with or without a state before: the retry, the NIC back on its own NSG, looks there again — never `released` while the pair stays. The defect is older than L1Qr; the machine reaches it since L2C (d380df5). Tests: two sequences frozen, shrunk by hand from 29 rules to 3 and 4 (red on `main` 9c4e8c5 and on 4fa6f70~1), eleven unit tests in `test_release_away.py` (the retries included); mutants M3060–M3069, M07 re-anchored.

## [0.4.10] — 2026-11-02 — "held to its word"

### Added
- **CI runs the Terraform tests, each provider checked against its module's lock** (CI4, #389, 02/11; `docs/operations.md`, « Les providers de Terraform, verrouillés »). A job `terraform-test` in `ci.yml`, on every push and pull request: Terraform at a pinned version (1.14.9) from HashiCorp's apt repository, its key trusted by HashiCorp's published fingerprint only (as the canary, no third party's action); `init -backend=false -lockfile=readonly` on the three modules — a package whose hash does not match, or a provider the lock does not list, fails `init` (both checked by hand on 02/11); `terraform test` on Celebrimbor (ID1, G1, mock providers: no Azure identifier, no secret), `validate` on Orodruin and Glorfindel's host, which have no test yet. Tests: `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (the job, its install, no identity; each lock committed with every provider its module requires and three platforms' hashes; `init -upgrade` nowhere in the repository but `make providers-lock` and the canary; the job's Terraform lines word for word, no `continue-on-error`, `if:` nor `||`, `persist-credentials: false`; every `run` of the `.tftest.hcl` with `command = plan`). `CHECKPOINT_DISABLE=1` in the job.
- **Benign noise, and the rate of faults taken for an attack** (G1, #76, 31/10; `gothmog/README.md`, « G1 »; `docs/analysis.md`, « Le taux des pannes prises pour une attaque »). Faults on which Glorfindel must neither isolate nor block, and the measure of whether it does — in code and on the bench in memory; nothing applied nor run on the bench yet.
  - **Three faults** (`gothmog/experiments/`, catalogue `azure_vm.faults`, `planned` until their first bench run): `vm-disk-write`, sustained writes by the Chaos agent (LinuxDiskIOPressure-1.1), **above** `ransomware-disk-write`'s threshold as the rule computes it — at the bench's 10-s step, two consecutive samples averaging above 45 MB/s (one above 25 at a coarse step) — decided with Jonathan on 31/10: below it, the RulePoller filters the noise and no decision is measured; `vm-cpu-pressure`, 95 % for 10 min (CPUPressure-1.0); `vm-restart`, a graceful shutdown of one minute (Shutdown-1.0).
  - **The fault's effect, read where Glorfindel reads**: each fault declares it (`effect.probes`) — the VM seen down, or a query on Perf whose peak, between the fault's start and end, is above a floor; the disk's reproduces the rule's `Rate`, its mode read from the data's step, and requires its threshold (a test keeps both texts equal). Never seen, the run fails (`fault_effective`) and measures nothing: noise Glorfindel never saw is not noise it ignored. A query read late (ingestion) is still looked for after the fault, never past its end.
  - **The VM serves during benign noise**: steady-state probes marked `during: true` are read at every poll; one that says otherwise — or is unreadable three polls in a row (a 429 twice passes) — and the experiment is cancelled, the run fails (`steady_state_during`, `metrics.steady_state_lost`) and its return is still verified.
  - **The rate** (`analysis/fault_rate.py`; `make fault-rate`, `make glorfindel-host-fault-rate` on H0, read only): over every Gothmog run, not only a campaign's, per run — a fault is taken for an attack when Glorfindel recommended or executed an isolation or a block of its target during it (BL1's `fault_run`, unchanged); over the measured faults only — started, effect seen, the Glorfindel watch live before the fault (V1) and after it (Gothmog reads it again once the fault is over, `checks.watch_after`: the watch writes only when it decides, so no file's date says it was looking), and its decisions read on this host (its files there — on Orodruin, every fault is « not measured » — none written since the fault started cut or unreadable); `None` when none is — never 0; a decision is one fault's — after the next fault on its VM started, the next one's, and if it also falls in the first one's window, the first is not measured; beside it, the disruptive recommendations, the executed ones, and the same by fault. Exposed as `measure` / `rate` for the console's overview (its line comes with the Console session).
  - **Celebrimbor** (`infra/terraform/chaos.tf`, behind `chaos.noise_vms`, empty: nothing created): the Chaos agent on the VM (a user-assigned identity with no role, the `ChaosLinuxAgent` extension), its `Microsoft-Agent` target through the **azapi** provider (azurerm cannot declare its identities; chosen with Jonathan on 31/10), CPU and disk capabilities, the three experiments, `Reader` on the VM for the agent faults and G0's custom role for the restart. Never applied. `infra/terraform/tests/chaos_noise.tftest.hcl` plans both cases against mock providers: the repository's config (`noise_vms: []`) leaves the bench's plan unchanged — no G1 resource, every VM `SystemAssigned` alone, G0 intact — and the noise on gondolin touches gondolin only (the variable `chaos_noise_vms`, null by default, overrides the config for a plan). CI runs no Terraform, as for ID1's test: by hand, `terraform -chdir=infra/terraform test`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_gothmog_noise.py` (G0's bench in memory with the agent's samples in Perf and their ingestion lag), `tests/unit/test_fault_rate.py` (a report written by Gothmog's real engine read back by the rate), `test_experiment_schema.py`; mutants M2400–M2424 (M2407 and M2419 unused).
- **Dagorlad follows a request live** (DG-c, #349; `docs/design/console.md` §7, « Construit : le suivi en direct »; `docs/dagorlad.md`, « Suivre une demande »). Once a request is written, the bottom panel becomes its chronology, one line per wire, read again every 10 s from what already reaches the console's host: the request, Orodruin's status, the exercise windows (which carry the request's id), the watch's cycles, the cards and the run's report. No new route, channel or file: `GET /api/dagorlad/requests/<id>` now also returns the request's wires, its phase (`written`, `running`, `over`) and each wire's line (`eregion_console/follow.py`); nothing is written and the watch is told nothing.
  - Each line: Orodruin's state and code, the run and its window, T0, the detection and its delay, Glorfindel's decision (action, confidence, mode, the guard that held it), the containment by BL1's definition (executed in its cycle, read back on Azure, held), the cards carrying the run and how many wait for a verdict, the run's verdict and a link to it in Runs. A fault: a decision to isolate or block on its target during it (« a fault taken for an attack »), and its return, verified.
  - **Measured as on the overview**: once the run's report is here, its decision, times and ending are the indicators' (`indicators.decision_of`, `run_report.timeline`, and `indicators.incident_of`, now shared by both). Before, the same functions on the window, each time marked `≈`. A fault's decisions already attached by the overview to an attack (of the request, or of any run of this host since it began) are never the fault's. Its reads: this host's files, and the enrollment of the targets the cycles name (cached), as the Runs view.
  - **The life of a wire** (decided 30/10): locked while the request runs (no ✕, no new wire; only « Stop all »); « New plan » drops only a request Orodruin has not heard of — a status there but unreadable, or of another request, is never « not carried yet »; over — Orodruin finished or refused it, « Stop all » closed one of its windows, or no window of it lives and Orodruin has been silent longer than it ever is between two wires (35 min: « Stop all » between two wires closes no window) — the wires unlock and keep their last result on their pill and plan line; « Run again » writes the same wires as a new request. A window counts as live within the bounds a window may hold only: forged, or on a clock off, it never keeps the wires locked. The pills follow the same line; the blue cards say what the running request does on their target. The request followed is kept in the browser: a reload finds it.
  - Tests: `test_console_dagorlad_follow.py`; mutants M2450–M2473, M2481–M2490 (the review). Checked in Chromium on the sample data (a trapped run id: nothing runs, no CSP violation).

### Changed
- **The Terraform providers are locked in the repository; `init` no longer upgrades them** (CI4, #389; decided by Jonathan on 31/10). Each module's `.terraform.lock.hcl` (`infra/terraform`, `infra/glorfindel-host`, `infra/orodruin`) is committed, at the bench's versions (azurerm 4.81.0, null 3.3.2, azapi 2.13.0), with the hashes of linux_amd64, darwin_arm64 and darwin_amd64. `celebrimbor-init`, `glorfindel-host-init` and `orodruin-init` run `init`, not `init -upgrade`: an upgrade is wanted — `make providers-lock [MODULE=…]`, the diff read, a PR, Jonathan's plan before the apply (as the Python lock, M-3). The canary (`infra/canary/`) still tests the latest azurerm, by design. **Once, on a checkout where these modules were initialised**: the local lock files were untracked and ignored, and the `git pull` that brings the committed ones overwrites them without a word (Git replaces an ignored untracked file on a fast-forward). Compare their versions with the committed ones first (`docs/operations.md`): the committed ones are the bench's, read from those local locks on 02/11; a newer local one means upgrading the committed lock first (`make providers-lock`, a PR). `annatar init` no longer upgrades either. The job « terraform test (mock providers) » is to be made required in `main`'s protection (Jonathan).
- **The experiment schema (E1) gains `effect`, for a fault only, and a probe's `during`** (G1, #76): `effect.probes` — `power_state` expecting `down`, or a query expecting `above <N>` on its `Peak` with `{target}`, `{start}`, `{end}` replaced; a technique declaring an effect is refused. `vm-shutdown` declares its effect (the VM seen down), and Gothmog refuses at load a fault that declares none. A fault's `fault_effective` check is now that effect seen (for a shutdown, unchanged); `metrics.effect` gives each reading. A fault's report says whether the Glorfindel watch was live once it was over (`checks.watch_after`; a run `--without-glorfindel` says nothing of it). The unified run view (`eregion_core/run_report.read_run`, C1) says `effective` and `watch_after`. Chaos Studio's `Microsoft-Agent` targets are read as their VM, which goes through the inscription (O0) as before.
- **One run at a time on Orodruin, a campaign, a run or a fault by hand included; the paths it reads on Azure, stricter** (#358, after DG-b's second review; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « Un run à la fois »).
  - The request's lock (`exercise_runner.request_lock`) is now taken by `annatar campaign run` too (`make orodruin-campaign-run`), and by `annatar run <scenario>` launched by hand: a campaign or a run during a request, or a request during either, is refused (`busy`) — nothing runs, nothing is ratified. A preview (`--dry-run`) takes no lock; a wire of a request never does (it runs under its request's). The lock says what holds it (« request r-… », « campaign … », « run … »), printable characters only, cut and erased with it, and the one refused names it, never as markup. The lock lives in the command's `runs/` (`--runs-dir` for a campaign): on Orodruin both commands mount the same one. `gothmog run <fault>` by hand takes it too (after G1; never a wire of a request, `--request`).
  - `held.safe_path`, the check of every path Orodruin reads on Azure Resource Manager, also refuses a `.` or `..` segment, a backslash, and any character not printable (a control, a bidirectional override, a zero-width one); a path refused is not read, and the VM is held (A0).
  - Gothmog, comparing the name typed for a destructive fault of a request to the name Azure gives: a read that fails is a refusal said and recorded (`typed_name: the target's name unreadable…`), never an exception.
  - Tests: `test_held.py`, `test_exercise_runner.py`, `test_campaign_runner.py`, `test_annatar_run_exit.py`, `test_gothmog.py`; mutants M2474–M2480, M2491–M2499.

### Fixed
- **A block card never says the IP « no longer blocked » where our perimeter still cuts it off** (I3P, #366, 31/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Un blocage fusionné »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3P »). Two cases frozen as strict `xfail` (I3: what is said is true): seed 1999 at 2 000 (I4E's review) and a sequence of I4D's harness, the card naming no NIC. Nothing opened; what the operator read was false.
  - **The causes**: (1) `_perimeter_holds` read an allow before the perimeter's deny as passing it even when a customer's deny before that allow already drops all of the attacker's traffic in its direction — our inbound block rule turned into an allow (200), before the perimeter (210), behind the customer's `deny-lateral-in` widened to every source (130): the NIC said open. (2) A bypassed block's verification dropped where each NIC stands (`held_by_perimeter`) and never asked the perimeter about the bypassed NIC; with no NIC open nor known held, the drift card said « l'IP n'est plus bloquée » of the whole VM.
  - **The fix**: an allow a customer's deny before it drops first lets nothing through (`nsg._dropped_first`: every port and protocol, the attacker on its far side, each of the NIC's addresses on its near one; ours and application security groups never count); a NIC whose own block is passed is asked the perimeter like one whose rules are gone (held, unknown, open), and the bypass returns where each NIC stands; with no NIC open nor unknown the card says « reste bloquée » where the perimeter holds, and NICs unread (429) « on ne sait pas » (`nics_unread`, A0), never « plus bloquée ». `_block_drift` moves to `glorfindel/reassert_said.py` (`reassert.py` 494 → 462 lines).
  - **Its review**: three cases where it said « holds » or « blocked » wrongly, fixed. A NIC whose rules or their precedence couldn't be read, beside a bypass, was dropped — the perimeter block kept as uncovered only the NICs said open and returned `blocked` instead of `blocked_partial`, the card left it out: it is now `nics_unknown`, named « on ne sait pas », and the perimeter block takes a NIC off its uncovered list only on a proof that it holds (`blocked_nics`, `held_by_perimeter`). A customer's deny counts as dropping the attacker's traffic first only over every source port too (the attacker picks its source port) and only when it names the attacker by `*`, `any` or a prefix holding it — never a service tag (`VirtualNetwork` was approximated by « private »: 203.0.113.7 is private for Python, outside the VNet for Azure), nor no prefix. Mutants M2189–M2196 (M2196, its second review: a deny of one of the NIC's addresses counted for all); M2186 re-anchored.
  - Tests: the two frozen sequences (their `xfail` lifted, the draws of their last rule completed), `test_glorfindel.py` (the bypassed NIC behind the perimeter, NICs unread, `_dropped_first`, `_perimeter_holds`); one L1C test now expects the bypassed NIC said open. Mutants M2180–M2188; M1625 and M1965 re-anchored.
- **A bypass is said beside a pair altered, whatever is left of it, writes refused or not** (L1R, #378, #381, 31/10; `docs/azure.md`, « La réaffirmation et les contournements »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1R »). Two sequences of the machine at 2 000, frozen as strict `xfail` by U7D and L1Q: an isolation whose `-out` rule was turned into an allow and whose inbound deny was narrowed to port 22 (seed 1006), a block whose inbound deny was narrowed to port 22 (seed 322); then a customer's rule before the deny widened into an allow of all — the reassertion said the drift, never the bypass (L1).
  - **The cause** (not the role taken away, as the titles had it: the writes refused only kept the deny from being put back): what is left of a pair (L1C) was read only through a deny still intact — still cutting the NIC, or still blocking the attacker. A deny of ours still in its place but edited (a port, a protocol, another address, another priority) was never read, so the allow before it, letting the threat through, was never said: for the isolation, both rules altered, no intact deny at all; for the block, the intact one was the `-out`, which no inbound allow passes.
  - **The fix** (`providers/azure/nsg.py`, `_deny_slot`; `verify.py`, `_half_shadowed`; `verify_block.py`, `_half_bypassed`): every deny of the pair still there, whatever it covers now, is read for an allow before it. A rule turned into an allow is no longer a deny of ours: nothing passes before it (its drift card says it). Found on the way, the same gap on a perimeter block alone (one pair on the subnet's NSG, no placement): its own path read the pair « missing » alone, as a VM's did before L1C; it reads what is left of it too. Its review: « whatever it covers » only for a name with the NIC's hash; an older name, or the VM-suffixed one, shared by homonyms, counts only as a deny that applies to the NIC — another VM's deny under it gave a false bypass card (L24).
  - Tests: the two sequences completed through the full disk and back (their `xfail` lifted, `test_invariant_regressions.py`), `test_glorfindel.py` (a port, a protocol, an address; both denies narrowed; a perimeter block; a homonym's deny); mutants M2300–M2303.
- **A release and a reset look for a bare NIC's rules away from it, and say what they keep there** (D80, #380, 31/10; `docs/azure.md`, « La levée d'une carte nue »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « D80 »). The machine at 500 sequences (seed 555), and the deep invariants of 31/10 on Linux and macOS (the Hypothesis database the macOS job of every PR replays): a release said `released`, or `release_partial` without naming it, while a rule of the VM's isolation stayed on Azure (I3).
  - **The cause**: the release's sweep looked for a NIC's isolation rules only on the NSGs that govern it now. A NIC left with no NSG of its own (someone took its NSG off; or its quarantine lifted to « none » because an isolation found it bare) has none: a rule an earlier release had kept on the NSG it carried (I4C: turned into an allow for another VM, said then) was forgotten with the state. When that NSG governed another NIC of the VM, only that NIC's rules were looked for there. The cut isolation in the issue's title is no part of it.
  - **The fix** (`glorfindel/providers/azure/away.py`): for each NIC of the VM with no NSG of its own, its rules (by the NIC's hash, first and second name) are looked for on every NSG of the subscription, as for a gone VM (I7S): kept by the guard and said « gardée » (`release_partial`), or taken off. A listing that fails is said (`release_partial`, « non cherchées »); a listing **filtered** by a role scoped to resource groups (likely ARM's answer, not measured) still reads as nothing there, as for I7S — open, to measure on the bench. Cost: one `network_security_groups.list_all` per release of a VM with such a NIC — the common case when NSGs sit on subnets only.
  - Tests: the frozen sequence of seed 555 (its strict `xfail` lifted), the two deep sequences (`test_a_bare_nics_rule_kept_on_its_mates_nsg_is_said`), `test_release_away.py`; mutants M2340–M2344.
  - **The reset too** (the macOS job of PR #391: the release fixed, the night's sequence went on to `reset --from-azure` — « swept_partial … kept without saying so »): the same search, its blocks' rules too (taken off by the reset as well); what the guard keeps said « gardée », the rest taken off with the sweep (nothing in a dry run). `verify_release` doesn't look away from the NIC (a rule there doesn't cut it): left open. Tests: `test_a_bare_nics_rule_kept_on_its_mates_nsg_is_said_by_the_reset`, two reset cases in `test_release_away.py`; mutants M2345–M2346.
- **A block written again after an acknowledged drift card says its disappearance again** (L1Q, #363, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Un blocage fusionné »; `docs/glorfindel.md`; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1Q »). A VM blocked on its subnet and for itself (one merged entry); a neighbour on that subnet blocked there and unblocked — the perimeter pair, one per IP and subnet, lifted beside the VM's block; the reassertion said it, the operator acknowledged the card. Then the VM blocked again, whole: `_save_block_state` merged it into the entry and kept its drift mark (`drift_alerted_at`, `drift_said`), so the pair, still named and still gone, read « already said » under rules rewritten since — no card, in `human_only` and outside it (L1: a drift stays said while it exists; nothing opened). A whole block is a new episode now, as a new isolation writes its state anew: the mark goes with an unblock's (L1B), and so does its re-application's (`reasserted_at`: kept, the next disappearance read « une deuxième fois (déjà reposée le <before the block>) » and was never put back outside `human_only`); a partial block keeps them. Not the quarantine, nor the perimeter's lifting, as the issue's title suggested: the quarantine's change only brought the pass. Found by I4E's machine at 2 000 (seed 42); its strict `xfail` lifted (`test_a_block_beside_a_lifted_perimeter_vanishes_under_a_quarantine_change`), its cause frozen in seven steps without a quarantine (`test_a_block_written_again_after_an_acknowledged_drift_is_said_again`, in the regressions and in `test_reassert.py`; red on `main` d05eade); mutants M2150 and M2154. A new rule of the invariant machine that makes the state common (`blocked_again_after_an_acknowledged_drift`) comes in a PR of its own, after #379, #380 and #366: it shifts the machine's draws onto those older failures.
  - **A rule back, then gone again, while the pair stays gone** (found by that rule, seed 322 at 2 000; present on `main` 48e42c8 without it): the merged entry's own rules taken off, said, the card acknowledged; put back by hand (the pair still gone, the episode went on), then taken off again — « not worse than the card », silent. What still holds of a block's drift card, at every pass better than the card — pending or acknowledged —, is now the baseline — in memory, for its episode only (a restart says the disappearance again anyway): the rules gone again are said. Narrowed only once acknowledged (a first take, undone by the second review), a drift better while the card waited, then acknowledged, then gone again before the next pass, read « not worse » for the whole episode. **Known limit, wanted**: under a pending card, a drift that comes and goes makes a card at each return — a card too many rather than a disappearance unsaid. A new episode never compares with what held of the one before. Frozen (`test_a_vm_rule_back_then_gone_again_beside_a_lifted_perimeter_is_said`, `test_reassert.py`: `…_better_under_a_pending_card_then_acknowledged_then_worse_is_said`, `…_comes_and_goes_under_a_pending_card_makes_a_card_at_each_return`); mutants M2151–M2153, M2155 (the narrowing reserved to an acknowledged card). M1646 (RB1), no longer measured once a block written again ends the episode itself, is measured again: `test_the_same_perimeter_gone_twice_is_said_twice` gains the pair put back by hand.
