  - Three older failures reached once the rule shifted the draws, red on `main` 48e42c8, none of the rule's steps in their shrunk sequences, outside the family: frozen as strict `xfail` and routed — an older version's deny-all kept at the teardown for a turned block rule (#379: I7, seed 1 at 500), a release said `released` with an isolation rule kept after a lost state and a cut isolation (#380: I3, seed 555 at 500), and a block bypassed beside its own deny altered while writes are refused (#381: L1, seed 322 at 2 000).
- **An orphan rule's card follows the VM it cuts, and says its NIC** (U1O, #372, 30/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Règles orphelines »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « U1O »). Seed 9001 at 2 000 (the machine, U1): a deny left by a deleted NIC, kept behind one of our block rules turned into an allow, had been said for batch, the VM at its address then; batch's address changed and a new VM, api, got the old one. The card stayed on batch — it named the turned rule, so I5Q's replacement never fired — and api, cut, was named by no pending card. Nothing opened; what cut was not said.
  - **The cause**: `orphan_alerts.json` keeps a rule as said by its NSG and name only; the card it points to is about the VM cut when it was said (the store merges cards by VM). Nothing compared that VM with the one the rule cuts now: an address passed to another VM, or to no one, left the card naming a VM no longer cut — for any orphan, kept or not, in `human_only` too.
  - **The fix** (`glorfindel/orphans.py`): a card about another VM than the one the rule cuts now (or its NSG, when nobody holds the address) is replaced — pending, closed (« Remplacée : … (U1O) ») and every rule it spoke for said again; acknowledged, it stays closed for the rules still about its VM, the moved rule said again for the new one. Never a removal: what is kept stays kept (I4, I5Q). The card names the VM and its NIC (« adresse de api (carte api-nic) »), and every VM a rule cuts (« Elle coupe aussi … »).
  - **Its review (31/10)**: every VM a card said is compared, not its first alone — `orphan_alerts.json` keeps, for each rule said, the VMs it cut then (`_said`): a second VM cut later by the same rule, or a VM its NSG no longer governs, replaces the card too. What comes is said at once; a VM leaving it stays named until the NIC that held the address proves it left — read by its id, gone (404), without any of the rule's addresses, or no longer attached to that VM — the VM deleted, its NIC kept, `az vm delete`'s default (`nic_left_addresses`; a card said before `_said`: the VM gone, 404, or its NICs, `vm_left_addresses`). A NIC missing from a partial listing (read rights on some resource groups only), seen elsewhere, a 403 or a 429 prove nothing: the VM stays in `_said` and in the card (« Elle coupait aussi … : non relue depuis »), even when another VM comes in the same pass; the pass says the listing is partial (`outcome: unread`). An entry of `_said` of another shape is read as absent, never a stop of the sweep. The note when nobody holds the address says so (« vise une adresse qu'aucune carte ne porte plus »).
  - Tests: `test_orphan_sweep.py`, the frozen sequence of seed 9001 (its strict `xfail` lifted); mutants M2260–M2279.
- **A partial unblock forgets the rules it took off; the seed 1006 was the teardown's operator** (U7D, #368, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, `glorfindel release` / `unblock_ip`; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « U7D »).
  - **The neighbour** (L1B's review, finding 3): an `unblock_partial` kept every placement in its entry, those whose two rules it had taken off too. A block written whole since merged them back in, and the verification read them absent — « a disparu d'Azure », a false card, and outside `human_only` rules written again that the operator had asked off. The placements whose two rules are off Azure now leave the entry; one whose rule stays, in either direction, is kept for the next unblock. Frozen: `test_a_partial_unblock_forgets_the_rules_it_took_off` (the false card and the rewrite, red without the fix), `test_a_partial_unblock_keeps_only_the_placements_it_could_not_take_off`; mutants M2210–M2212.
  - **The seed 1006 at 60** (« I7: block rules of …/Db--01 left after unblock », frozen as a strict `xfail` by L1B): the code's unblock kept Db--01's turned allow — aimed at the address another VM took, which the teardown's own release of that VM had just put back on its NSG — and our deny it waits behind, said both « gardée » and answered `unblock_partial`. The teardown's operator put the turned rules back before the releases only. It now does as said after an unblock that keeps, the kept rules first checked as said (`_kept_said`, refusing more), and the unblock is tried again; the `xfail` is lifted. On `main` d380df5 the full 29-step sequence passes with that alone.
  - **Its review**: the teardown's unblock now also requires every block rule of the VM left to be one the oracle says kept (`_kept`, the test's journal) — before, an unblock that kept rules without a reason passed once the operator had put the turned rules back (A0: it refuses more; mutant M2213, the over-guard). The same false card by another path: an unblock that took every rule off, then failed its last state write (a full disk) or died before it, left its placements with `unblocking_at`; a whole block on such an entry (or one `unblock_failed` marks) now keeps only the placements whose rules are still on their NSG (unreadable: kept); without the marks, or for a partial block, the merge is as before. Frozen: `test_a_whole_block_after_a_cut_off_unblock_forgets_what_it_took_off`; mutants M2214–M2217.
  - Two older failures found at 2 000 sequences (seeds 1006 and 322: an isolation's and a block's bypass left unsaid after a role taken away), red on `main` e75d527 too: the block's is fixed by L1C (#369), its sequence completed and no longer `xfail`; the isolation's is still red, frozen as strict `xfail` and routed to #378.
- **A bypass is said while a rule of its pair is gone; only a refusal supersedes a placement** (L1C, #369, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « La réaffirmation et les contournements »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1C »). The machine at 2 000 sequences (seed 31337): a block whose `-out` rule was gone (said, a drift card), then a customer's allow widened before its inbound deny — the reassertion never said the bypass (L1); nothing opened beyond what was already said open, an alert stayed quiet.
  - **The cause**: a pair gone or altered in one direction was read « missing » alone; what was left of it — a deny still in place, passed by an allow before it — was never checked for a bypass, so no bypass card was ever written while the rule stayed gone. And rules missing on one NIC dropped a bypass read on another. (Not a refused read nor the full disk: the machine's rule draws an `nsg.get` fault it never uses, and the sequence fails at its first pass.)
  - **The fix**: `verify_block_ip` checks what is left of a half pair (a deny that still blocks the attacker, an allow before it that lets the threat through) and returns it as `shadowed_by` beside `missing_rules` — the drift card and the bypass card both; `verify_isolation` does the same for an isolation (a NIC uncovered, its half pair passed by an allow).
  - **L1B's review's reserve**: in `_recorded_gone`, another name of the NIC supersedes a placement on an NSG only as a refusal there — its pair, both denies cutting the NIC —, never a rule merely present: the first name turned into an allow hid the second name's pair gone (the card named the wrong rule, or, the first's `-out` rule left behind, nothing gone).
  - Tests: `test_glorfindel.py`, the sequence of seed 31337 completed through the full disk (its `xfail` lifted) and the same for an isolation (`test_invariant_regressions.py`); mutants M2230–M2235, M2045 and M2046 re-anchored. Its review: beside a half pair read bypassed, everything gone on the NIC is said (another placement wholly gone was dropped, the drift card said less), and each allow is named once (M2236, M2237).
- **The seed 1 at 500 was the teardown's operator, not the code** (D79, #379, 31/10; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « D79 »). No change in Glorfindel. An older version's deny-all on `*` (an old api's isolation, its state kept) cut a new VM given its NSG; that VM's own block rule, turned into an allow by someone, waited behind it. The release of the old api keeps the deny-all (I4C's guard: taken off, the attacker reached the new VM on every port), says it « gardée » and names the turned rule to put back. The invariant machine's teardown never put back a turned rule held behind an older version's deny on `*`, and I7 refused. It now does, as U7D's unblock — after controls more (A0): every older rule left is one the oracle says held, and every rule of today's names of the VM left one it says kept (its review), none kept without a reason; each older rule is said with the turned rule behind it. The strict `xfail` is lifted (`test_an_older_deny_all_kept_for_a_turned_rule_on_a_redeployed_nic`, inbound and outbound; the release once before the teardown, the old VM isolated again by rules, or neither); mutants M2320–M2323 (over-guards of the older rules and of the placements, a keep unsaid, a keep said without its reason), caught by those controls only.

## [0.4.9] — 2026-10-30 — "held in check"

### Security
- **The azurerm canary without a long-lived secret, at least privilege** (P04, P-04 of the counter-audit, 30/10; `docs/operations.md`, « Le canari azurerm, sans secret »). The daily canary held a client secret, and its principal could create a resource group in the subscription.
  - **OIDC**: the canary's job alone asks GitHub for a short-lived token (`id-token: write`), which Entra ID exchanges for the principal `eregion-canary`, trusted for the repository's environment `canary` only (`repo:Vanyar53/eregion:environment:canary`), which only `main` deploys: no other job, `publish.yml`'s included, gets that principal. Terraform reads the token itself (`ARM_USE_OIDC`, never the az session: `ARM_USE_CLI=false`), on each line of the matrix — documented by the provider at v3.117.1, v4.81.0 and v5.12.0; `az` gets the same kind of token from `curl`. No third party's action beside it; no `CLIENT_SECRET` left in the workflow. Its identifiers are the environment's secrets (`CANARY_AZURE_*`), never variables; without them, or off `main`, the run fails.
  - **Least privilege**: `Network Contributor` on one group, `rg-eregion-canary`, created once by the maintainer — it cannot delete the group, create a VM nor grant a role (to be measured on the first run; the fallback, `Contributor` on that group, written and said); the canary creates and deletes no group and registers no resource provider. Each run's resources carry its suffix (run, attempt, line: the three lines side by side in the group) and its tag, and are destroyed at the end, success or not — a failed destroy sweeps them by the tag. What the canary checks is unchanged.
  - Closed once the maintainer's steps are done (the registration, the environment and its branch rule, the federated credential, the group, the role, the secrets, a green run, then the former secrets and principal removed). Tests in `tests/unit/test_workflows.py` (the workflow's steps and `run.sh` run against fake `curl`, `az` and `terraform`, the runner's two signals of a cancel included; the OIDC token said once, to mask it, never traced); mutants M2080–M2089, M2110–M2115.

### Added
- **Dagorlad shown: a user's guide, a README section, a home page on GitHub Pages, and images replayed from sample data only** (DOC1, #365, 30/10; `docs/dagorlad.md`).
  - **The guide** (`docs/dagorlad.md`, French): the two views, drawing a wire and reading its verdict (the stable codes, the warnings, the last proof), asking for an exercise (the button, `make orodruin-exercise`, the name typed on the operator's machine, one request at a time), « Stop all », what Glorfindel sees and does not, the guardrails. The design stays in §7 of `docs/design/console.md`.
  - **The README** gains « Dagorlad, the exercise board » under the overview's capture: the loop, the board, five lines, the guide. **`docs/index.html`**, the project's one page on Pages: static, no script, nothing loaded from elsewhere, readable on a phone.
  - **`scripts/console_shots.py`** (`make console-shots [VIDEO=…]`) starts the console on `tests/fixtures/dagorlad_sample.json` — a fictitious bench, the zero subscription, the bench's public names — in a throwaway HOME and working directory, with its own `glorfindel-config.yaml` (never the one at the repository's root), and renders `docs/images/dagorlad/` (`board.png`, `runs.png`, `loop.webp`, a 14 s animated WebP) and, with `VIDEO=`, the whole tour outside the repository (mp4 with libx264, else WebM). It refuses another sample, a sample with another GUID, an address outside the documentation's ranges or another name, a video inside the repository; the server it starts refuses an Azure variable or another HOME, and, through Python, reaches the loopback only (connections, datagrams, names) and starts no program but the system's `git rev-parse` or `git describe` (no option before the command). The workstation's Chromium (DevTools protocol) and ffmpeg — never the CI's, never a dependency; missing, they are said. `docs/images/dagorlad/shots.json` binds the images to the sample.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_console_shots.py` (the sample's identifiers, the refusals, the guard on the loopback, the board the sample gives, the images bound to the sample and their sizes, the loop's length) and `tests/unit/test_docs_home.py` (no script, nothing external, every link resolves, no identifier in the pages); mutants M2170–M2179. Its review (30/10): the sample's console reads the sample's `glorfindel-config.yaml`, never the one at the repository's root; its guard closes datagrams, every name resolution and every program but the system's `git rev-parse` or `describe`; an address ending a sentence, a GUID in capitals, a host under any domain are identifiers; the guide says the red column scrolls and that a fault asks for its target's name too — mutants M2200–M2209.
  - **The whole tour, as a video** (53 s, on sample data), attached on GitHub and played in the README and on the home page. The check that no page carries an identifier lets a GUID through only inside a GitHub attachment's URL (M2251).
- **Dagorlad asks for an exercise: the request, the name typed, « Stop all »** (DG-b, #348, 30/10; `docs/design/console.md`, §7, phase b; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « La demande de Dagorlad »). The console writes a request; the operator carries it; Orodruin judges it and runs it. No new right nor channel for H0 or the console.
  - **The request** (`eregion_core/exercise_request.py`): an id and its time; per wire, the experiment, the target and its rank — no zone, no destructiveness, no expected result, no mode, no name typed. One reader (`read`) refuses any field more.
  - **The console writes it**: `POST /api/dagorlad/requests`, behind SEC1, two identifiers per wire from the browser; judged by `exercise_plan` first (a refused wire: nothing written); written in `~/.eregion/console/requests/` only. Only the console's service mounts `~/.eregion/console`; no watch nor bot service mounts `~/.eregion` nor the console's directory. The page gives the command and says which wires will ask for their target's name; it never takes a name.
  - **`make orodruin-exercise REQUEST=<id> [DRY_RUN=1] [REQUEST_FROM=local]`**, from the operator's machine: the request pulled from H0 over SSH (host key checked), shown wire by wire, the name of each destructive (or undeclared, A0) wire's target typed there — another name or a « no »: nothing sent —, copied to Orodruin and run there.
  - **Orodruin judges and runs** (`eregion_core/exercise_runner.py`): its contract (`passive`: refused, its codes), every wire with the console's codes on what it reads now, the typed name against the name it reads (`typed_name`); one refused wire, nothing runs. Then the wires one after the other: before each, the cap left to the whole request (its calls journaled, its time), the watch, the target judged again (O0), its name again; a VM whose fault did not verify its return takes no other wire; a VM isolated by Glorfindel is waited for, then skipped — never released.
  - **The injectors run from a request**, in C1's unified run: `annatar wire <technique> <vm> --request <id> [--typed-name]` (a destructive technique only on the VM whose name was typed, checked against Azure's), `gothmog run <fault> --request <id> --target <vm>` (a VM the fault declares). The request's id is written in the window, Orodruin's journal and the report. `gothmog targets [--json]` reads, without Orodruin, the VMs each experiment aims at.
  - **Orodruin publishes** its contract's verdict (armed or passive, the codes, the time — no subscription, no identifier) and each request's status under `orodruin/` in the exercise channel (same container, same assignment); H0's watch pulls them into `runs/orodruin/`, each blob read against its shape. The console shows « Orodruin: armed / passive », or « Orodruin: unknown here ».
  - **« Stop all »** gives `make orodruin-kill`, what it will stop (the open windows and their targets) and what stays in flight; the console revokes nothing.
  - The threat model's pages for the console and the hosts say what holds. Tests `test_exercise_request.py`, `test_console_dagorlad_request.py`, `test_makefile_exercise.py` (fake `ssh`/`scp`), `test_exercise_runner.py`, `test_annatar_wire.py`, `test_gothmog.py`, `test_orodruin_word.py`, and `test_exercise_e2e.py` (from the console to both injectors, on fakes); mutants M2010–M2039, all caught.
  - **Its review (30/10)**: Glorfindel's isolations of today (`glorfindel-iso-…`, and the quarantine) are read on the NSGs of the VM's NICs and of their subnets, wherever they live, by Orodruin and by Annatar's preflight alike (`eregion_core/held.py`; unreadable holds the VM) — Orodruin's read role gains `networkInterfaces/read` and `virtualNetworks/subnets/read` on the bench, an `orodruin-lock` to apply; a fault's wire names its VM by full id, one VM per wire; the request's reader is strict (whole match, ASCII, Azure's character sets; nothing raw on the operator's terminal; no query in an ARM path); the request pulled is the one asked for; one request at a time on Orodruin (a lock); the watch, the target and the name read again after an isolation wait; an injector that cannot start fails its wire; `HOST_SSH_FINGERPRINT` required to pull from H0; a destructive or undeclared fault asks for its target's name too. The model's message is the same, to the byte, with a request present (`test_dagorlad_blind.py`). Mutants M2090–M2107.

### Fixed
- **A disappearance is said behind a bypass, and after an unblock that failed** (L1B, #355, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « La réaffirmation et les contournements »; `docs/glorfindel.md`; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1B »). Two cases where the reassertion did not say that rules were gone (L1: a drift stays said while it exists); nothing opened, an alert stayed quiet. Both were frozen as strict `xfail` by L2C; both now pass.
  - **An isolation bypassed and gone at once**: placed on the subnet's NSG (an allow before our deny on the NIC's), an older version's pair bypassed on the NIC's NSG, our rules taken off the subnet's — `verify_isolation` returned « bypassed » alone, the missing rules dropped. The bypass now carries the rules the state's placements record that are absent from an NSG that governs their NIC (`missing_rules`; an unreadable listing claims nothing): put back once outside `human_only`, or said, next to the bypass card — as I4C did for a block. The invariant machine's oracle no longer counts a bypass card for a vanished isolation (L2C's loosening of `_gone_said`, taken back: it refuses more).
  - **A block written again after a failed or cut-off unblock**: `_save_block_state` merged it into the entry and kept `unblock_failed` / `unblocking_at`; the pass left the entry to the operator for ever, its rules gone unsaid. A whole block supersedes the unblock: its marks go (and `stale_alerted_at`); a partial one keeps them. The machine's exemption now reads the test's own journal (a whole block seen since the last unblock or reset), never the code's marks alone.
  - **Its review**: only the bypassed NICs answer for what is gone, and a placement whose NSG carries another name of the NIC (a rewrite superseded it, the merge kept its record) is never said gone — an isolation that held was said vanished, with a re-isolation more outside `human_only`. The machine's journal of whole blocks now drops only the VM an unblock or reset acts on.
  - Tests: `test_glorfindel.py`, `test_reassert.py`, frozen sequences of the machine; mutants M2040–M2047. Two older failures of `main`, outside the lot, frozen as strict `xfail` (#368, #369).

- **An isolation or a block written again under the NIC's second name no longer opens the VM** (I4D, #356, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Sous le second nom, jamais plus bas qu'une allow »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, I4D). Written again while one rule of its first pair is kept for another VM (I4A, I4B: a turned rule aimed at the NIC's old address, now another VM's), an isolation or a block goes under the NIC's second name, at the first free number, and took the first pair's other rule off. That rule could precede a customer's allow the new one comes after: Db--01's inbound isolation deny at 100, before `allow-ssh` at 101, replaced at 102 — taken off, the attacker reached the NIC on port 22 (I5; the invariant machine, seed 1618 at 2 000 sequences; the reassertion outside `human_only` and an isolation by hand alike). Such a deny now stays, and is said: `left_rules` and the note of the isolation or the block, the reassertion's card (`kind: before_allow` for a block). The release and the unblock take both pairs off. The block's side was found by reading the code (the machine's customer allows never lie among our block numbers). The machine now checks that an isolation and a block never open the VM they act on, bypassed or not (before, a bypassed one was not looked at; A0: it refuses more, never less). Frozen: L2C's `xfail` lifted (`test_a_quarantine_put_back_never_opens_a_vm_given_a_turned_rules_address`), the three steps of the cause (`test_an_isolation_written_again_never_moves_its_deny_past_an_allow`, red on `main` d380df5), the kept deny said for an isolation, the reassertion and a block; mutants M2060–M2066. Its review: a deny is kept only while its own side still holds a current address of one of the VM's NICs (any of them: an address may have moved to another NIC of the same VM — second review, M2072–M2074) (aimed only at old ones it closes nothing of the NIC, and goes as before); an unreadable number keeps it; the kept deny names the VM it cuts off on the addresses no longer the NIC's; the release asks its guard with the VM's live NICs (`mates`), so a rule aimed at the VM's other NIC is no longer kept « for another VM », `release_partial` for ever (seed 9001 at 2 000); the machine's teardown operator puts back an older version's turned rule too, as the release asks (its VM from the test's journal); a second case of #366 frozen `xfail`. Mutants M2067–M2071.
- **A perimeter lifted by a neighbour's unblock is said, even behind a bypass** (I4C, #344, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Un blocage fusionné »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, I4C). A VM's entry holds the subnet's perimeter pair with its own rules (RB1); another VM of the subnet unblocked, the pair (one for the subnet) goes. When the VM's own rules were there but bypassed (a customer allow before them), its verification said the bypass alone and dropped the pair's disappearance: the reassertion, finding nothing missing, neither put it back nor said so (L1). Now the bypass carries what is gone (`held_missing`), and the reassertion puts it back or says it. Found by the invariant machine once I4C made the state common; frozen (`test_a_perimeter_lifted_by_a_neighbour_is_said_behind_a_bypass`, red on `main` f2b4333); mutants M1965–M1966.
- **An isolation's deny on every address that holds back a turned rule stays at the release and the reset** (I4C, #344; `docs/azure.md`, « Le déblocage, le reset, la levée et l'isolation, sur une adresse reprise »). An older version's deny-all on a VM's own NSG (or ours, `*`, on an NSG that governed that VM alone) cuts every NIC the NSG governs — a VM given that NSG and an old address since included. The release (its older-state path asked nothing) and the reset took it off by name: one of our rules turned into an allow, waiting behind it for that VM, let the attacker reach it (I4). Now both ask the guard, which counts every NIC behind a `*` deny of an isolation (`turned._cuts_all`; never a block's: there `*` is the perimeter); kept, said. Found by the machine at 500 (seed 7), on `main` f2b4333 too; frozen (`test_an_older_isolations_deny_all_holding_back_a_turned_rule_stays`, release and reset); mutants M1967–M1969. Kept, the older version's state stays one (the review: written with `placements: []`, the next release no longer read it as such, its deny-all « VM inconnue : laissée » for ever): the turned rule put back, the next release takes it off (`test_an_older_isolations_kept_deny_all_goes_once_the_turned_rule_is_put_back`, M1970). The same reading (`_aims_elsewhere`) serves the isolation written again and the release's sweep: an isolation pair on `*` (today's isolation addresses the NIC's IPs, L24; `*` comes from a version before it, or a NIC with no address) now asks the guard there too — left and said when a turned rule waits behind it, the isolation then under the NIC's second name (`test_an_isolation_on_every_address_written_again_leaves_it_for_the_vm_behind`, M1971).
- **The invariants' oracle, hardened** (I4C, #344; `docs/design/preuves.md`, I4C; A0: it refuses more, never less): what the unblock, the reset and the release keep (taken off, another VM's flows would change) is never `unblocked`, `swept`, `released`, and is named, « gardée », where the operation looks (`_kept_said`; a deleted VM's release answers for what its state recorded, as I3 judges it); a perimeter lifted in an operation exempts only the attacker's flows it alone closed (`_lifted_alone`), no longer every opening of the VMs behind it. Two rules make these states common. Mutants M1960–M1964, caught by the machine alone; none by the oracle before.
- **An older version's isolation survives a new isolation of its VM, and its release takes it off** (I4E, #361, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Une isolation d'une ancienne version, la VM isolée de nouveau »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, I4E). A VM isolated by an older version (its single-NSG state, the fixed-name deny-all) and isolated again wrote its new state over the old record: on an NSG shared since, nothing tied the deny-all to its VM any more — « isolation d'une ancienne version, VM inconnue : laissée », every release `release_partial` (the reset `swept_partial`), the VM cut off for ever. The new isolation, complete or partial, now keeps the record (`legacy`), read wherever the old state was: the release and the reset take off the new pair and the old deny-all (under I4C's guard, which may keep it and say so), `verify_release` looks for it, `_legacy_claimed` ties it to its VM, the Activity Log's reader counts its rules as ours. Found by I4C's second review; frozen (`test_an_older_isolation_kept_through_a_new_one_goes_with_its_release`, release and reset, complete and partial isolation; `test_an_older_record_kept_by_a_new_isolation_stays_while_its_deny_all_does`; red on `main` 91dae76); mutants M2130–M2137. A record already written over before this fix is not recovered: the release and the reset say « VM inconnue », and now give the `az network nsg rule delete` command that removes the deny-all, if it is the operator's.
- **The invariant machine's teardown no longer reads the code's state** (I4E, #361; A0): it removed an older version's fixed-name rule on a shared NSG when the state « did not name it » — read in the state the code writes, which the defect above had written over; it reads the test's journal now (the old state the test wrote and never lost since). The oracle checks that a partial release of an older version's state leaves it one (M1970's defect), and a new rule makes the state common (`an_old_isolations_vm_isolated_again`: an older version's VM, a new VM on its NSG, the VM isolated again under a fault or not, then released or reset). On `main`, the teardown so bounded and the new rule fail at 500 on seeds 1, 7, 42, 31337, 101, 555, 8675 — that defect only. No rule removed or restricted.
- **A bypass, an unreadable local state, the rules a block left: said again when they come back** (L2C, #343, 30/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L2C »). L2Q's pattern, found by its review on the other cards of the reassertion. Each keeps its own mark of what was said (`bypass_said`, `state_alerts.json`, `left_said`); a write of it that failed (a full disk) kept a former change, and once the situation came back to it, the pass read « said or acknowledged » with no card left: an isolation or a block still bypassed, a state file still unreadable, in silence. A new set of left rules was also folded by the escalations store into the former card, which kept naming the former rules. Only the operator's last word now silences them, as for the quarantine: the card is pending, or the latest card of that resource is this change's, closed by a human; Glorfindel's closures carry a note (a new set of left rules closes the former card). Tests in `tests/unit/test_reassert.py` (`-k "bypass or unreadable or left_rules"`); the invariant machine draws it (`said_then_back_after_a_full_disk`) and finds the unreadable state on its own at 500 sequences on `main`, on every seed tried; mutants M1930–M1941. The same for a disappearance (its review): an isolation or a block gone, its card acknowledged, held again while its drift mark could not be taken off (a full disk), then gone again was read « already said » — the VM open again, no card. A drift mark now silences only while the alert's latest card is pending or closed by the operator (`_alert`'s closures carry a note), and an episode seen over is remembered by the watch even when its mark stays; the episode ends once its rules are back, a customer's allow passing them or not (only a hold ended it); a state write refused on one VM no longer stops the pass for the others, and is said in its report (« état local non écrit »); after a restart of the watch, an acknowledged disappearance still there is said again once, then silent (a noisy restart, decided 30/10); mutants M1942–M1949, M2120–M2125. Out of the lot, all present on `main`: two older L1 on a merged block (its bypass read, its missing perimeter dropped) — settled by I4C, their sequences pass —; frozen `xfail` for lots of their own: an I5 (seed 1618 at 2 000; settled by I4D), and a block written again after a failed unblock that keeps its mark and goes unchecked.

## [0.4.8] — 2026-10-30 — "held in sight"

### Added
- **The time to contain and the share contained alone, computed from a campaign's traces** (BL1, #280, 30/10 — prepared; measured at the bench session with Jonathan, `docs/banc-bl1.md`). The two indicators of the finality that were still empty: H0 observes in `human_only`, and no response had been executed then verified in the loop since 5–6 October.
  - **The definitions** (`analysis/containment.py`, `docs/analysis.md`, « Temps pour contenir et part contenue seule »). An incident is an executed technique run; its T0 is the injector's `attack_time`. It is contained alone when Glorfindel's decision on it (attached as the campaign report attaches it, #157) is an isolation or a block executed in its own cycle, read back true on Azure, and that held: no card of the reassertion saying, on that VM, that it disappeared or is bypassed, until the end of the run's window (never into the next run on the VM). The time to contain runs from T0 to the read-back — C1's `run_report.timeline`, one definition with the coverage matrix; BL1 adds that the containment held and was executed in the cycle — and the campaign's is the median of the contained ones (none: `None`, never 0). A fault's end and targets join C1's unified view of a run (`read_run`: `ended_at`, `targets`). The share is over every incident, missed ones included; an unknown decision makes it a lower bound, said. Every other ending is said: missed, held by a guard, escalated, action failed, verification failed, isolation that did not hold, a snapshot, a dry run. A Gothmog run attached to the campaign (named, or on one of its VMs within the hour around it) is never an incident: a decision to isolate or to block on its targets during its window is « a fault taken for an attack », counted apart.
  - **Glorfindel stamps the read-back**: `verify_action` adds `outcome.verified_at`, the end of the time to contain (the debug line is written later, after the memory and the notification).
  - **Published** in the campaign report (a « Réponse » section computed by the code, never by the model; a failure to compute is said, never stops the report), in a campaign's page of the console (`GET /api/campaigns/<id>`, `containment`; the RESPONSE box) and on the overview, which now uses the same definition (end at the read-back, an isolation that did not hold is not contained, the attack runs of the period beside the detected ones, the faults taken for an attack in red). `make campaign-containment CAMPAIGN=<id> [FAULT_RUNS=…] [JSON=1]`, and on H0 `make glorfindel-host-containment`. The signal contract is unchanged.
  - **The declared campaign of BL1** (`annatar/campaign/declared/bl1-reponse.yaml`): the fault `vm-shutdown`, then T1136.001 by the pinned Atomic Red Team test, then the recovery, on gondolin (`bench`, `non_disruptive`). `annatar campaign plan --declared <file>` plans exactly its techniques, after checking it against the catalog (`annatar/campaign/declared.py`: implemented, a public library's engine — never a home-grown script —, not destructive; the fault declared on the same targets, in the zone `bench`); `make orodruin-campaign-plan CAMPAIGN_DECLARED=…`.
  - **Where the responding watch runs**, proposed and not applied (`docs/banc-bl1.md`): H0 with the « Eregion Glorfindel Response » role on the bench's group alone, for the campaign, recommended over a watch on the operator's machine (it breaks the return channel) and a principal of the watch's own on H0 (a secret on H0).
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_containment.py` (every edge case, and a campaign replayed end to end: the injectors simulated, Glorfindel's real graph deciding with the model mocked and acting on the invariant machine's in-memory Azure, its reassertion seeing an isolation removed, read back by the report and the console), `tests/unit/test_campaign_declared.py`, `tests/unit/test_indicators.py`, `tests/unit/test_makefile_campaign.py`; mutants M1770–M1789.
- **Dagorlad's Runs, and Dagorlad in the header** (DG-a, end, #347, 30/10; `docs/design/console.md`, §7). `/dagorlad?view=runs` is Annatar's and Gothmog's former page, common to both: the exercise windows and their detail (cycles, cards, run report, a fault's probes, the return channel), the campaigns, and C1's coverage matrix, techniques then faults — one injector kept by a plain link (`?injector=`). `/annatar` and `/gothmog` lead there, filtered, their query kept (308, until 1.0); `/runs/<id>` too. The header has three tabs: Overview, Glorfindel, Dagorlad (greyed while neither injector is set up here). Each red card of the Board carries its last proof from the matrix. Mutants M1995–M1999.
- **Dagorlad's Board, read only** (DG-a first part, 30/10; `docs/design/console.md`, §7): `/dagorlad`. The catalogue's techniques and faults on the left, Glorfindel and the bench's enrolled VMs on the right (their zone, their effective mode, what holds them now, whether the watch sees them); the operator draws wires — a red card then targets, or its handle dragged onto one; several to one target, each removed by its own ✕ — and the plan below says, for each, whether it may run and what to expect. The verdict is the shared core's (`eregion_core/exercise_plan.py`, stable codes: `unknown_experiment`, `not_implemented`, `wrong_target_type`, `outside_scope`, `not_enrolled`, `zone_forbids`, `no_watch`, `no_engine`, `over_cap`), built on O0's perimeter, enrollment and zone, from two identifiers the browser sends — never what it says of them; the targets read with the console's identity (`TagEnrollment.vms`). What to expect comes from the configuration — the rule that detects, its delay, what Glorfindel's guards allow at most in the target's mode — never from the model. Two GETs (`/api/dagorlad/board`, `/api/dagorlad/plan`): nothing launched, nothing written; the wires stay in the browser. Rendered in Chromium at 1440 and 1600 px with a VM named `<img onerror>`: no JS error, no CSP violation, nothing injected. Tests `tests/unit/test_exercise_plan.py`, `tests/unit/test_console_dagorlad.py`; mutants M1980–M1994. The Runs view, the redirects of `/annatar` and `/gothmog` and the tab come after C1.
- **The design of Dagorlad, the console's exercise board** (30/10, decided by Jonathan question by question; `docs/design/console.md`, §7). One page, `/dagorlad` (« the Battle Plain », where the Last Alliance faced Sauron's army — Annatar is Sauron disguised), replaces Annatar's and Gothmog's: the red cards (the catalogue's techniques and faults) on one side, the blue ones (Glorfindel and the targets Celebrimbor enrolled, with their zone and autonomy mode) on the other; the operator draws a wire from a red card to blue targets. The server judges each wire from two identifiers, never from what the browser says, with the rules Orodruin applies at each injection (scope, enrollment, zone, the catalogue's destructiveness, the production rule, the watch's heartbeat), and says what to expect: the rule that detects, the delay, and what Glorfindel's guards allow it at most in the target's mode — computed from the configuration, never by the model. Three phases: (a) a plan and its simulation, read only; (b) a request the operator carries to Orodruin (`make orodruin-exercise REQUEST=<id>`, over SSH; no new channel, no new right for H0's identity), the wires one after the other, a destructive wire's target name typed in that command, after it shows the wires, never stored by the console, and checked by Orodruin, « Stop all » through Orodruin's emergency stop; (c) the run followed live from what already reaches the host, measured with BL1's definitions. A request never goes where Glorfindel's decision reads. Two views: **Board** and **Runs** (the windows and campaigns of both injectors); `/annatar` and `/gothmog` will lead to Runs. A **drill** — wires kept and replayed — belongs to the client's instance, in layers like the rules (shipped reference drills in `drills/`, the client's own in its instance, the draft in the browser, one YAML to export); Annatar's campaigns become drills Annatar writes, its planner a proposal on the board; targets by name, or by selector in a shipped drill; shared within the instance, its author noted. Nothing built yet: lot DG-a, the Board first.
- **The cycle of a session, written** (`docs/sessions.md`, `CLAUDE.md`). How a Claude session finds and talks to the Orchestre, asks for a lot's issue and a mutant range, works on a branch in a worktree, pushes, gets its PR reviewed by Opus 5.5 and merged, and what stays with Jonathan. `scripts/mutant_anchors.py` checks the mutants and the control set before a push: every id unique, and every mutant one the harness can apply (each replaced text found once, in a file git tracks).

### Fixed
- **The unblock, the reset, the release and the isolation no longer cut off nor open the VM that took an address** (I4B, #333, 30/10; `docs/azure.md`, « Le déblocage, le reset, la levée et l'isolation, sur une adresse reprise »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, I4B). After an IP change and the old address handed to another VM, these operations took off (or rewrote) one of our rules whatever it had become: a rule someone turned into an allow, aimed at the other VM — it lost the flows it gave it; and an isolation rewritten moved our deny off the old address, where one of our turned rules waited behind it — the other VM opened to the attacker.
  - **One guard for every removal by name** (`turned._held_by_turned`, I5Q's, extended to a turned allow): such a rule stays, said — `unblock_partial`, `swept_partial`, `release_partial`, the rule named; read only when a rule aims elsewhere than the VM's addresses (`_aims_elsewhere`).
  - **The isolation's rewrite** reads its pair first, leaves what the guard keeps (`left_rules`, `left_note`, on the reassertion's card too) and writes under the NIC's second name, as I4A's block does.
  - **The block's guard** reads every turned rule of ours on an NSG that governs the address's holder (`_waits_for`), not only the NIC's own block rules (the lot's fourth point).
  - **A NIC with no NSG of its own that nothing records**: the unblock looks for its block on every NSG (`_unplaced_blocks`) — it said `not_found` while the block stood (the CI's macOS run, a seventh case, on `main` before I4B).
  - **The oracle, hardened** (A0: it refuses more, never less): an unblock or a reset lifts a perimeter, the attacker's flows of another VM were left out of I4; they may now open only behind a perimeter it lifted, never be cut. It found the unblock's case on seeds 42 and 8675 at 500 on `main`.
  - `verify_block_ip` split out of `unblock.py` (`verify_block.py`, moved without change). Six `xfail` lifted, the seventh case frozen; the reset's preview asks the guard too (it showed « supprimer » what the reset then kept); PR #342's CI sequence frozen; mutants M1890–M1906 (16/16 by their sequences; the harness before/after on the 150 mutants of the modules touched: none lost). Invariants at 500 on six seeds and 2 000 on three, green.
- **An altered quarantine NSG whose change comes back is said again** (L2Q, #331, 30/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L2Q »). Found by the invariant machine (seed 101 at 500, L2), present before RB1. Our quarantine NSG stripped, a card said it; a forensic access added changed the diff, its card closed and a new one written, but `quarantine_alerts.json` could not be written (a full disk) and kept the former change. The access taken off — or the configuration unreadable, which hides it —, the diff was the former change's again, read « said or acknowledged »: the NSG, still stripped and holding NICs, had no card. Only the operator's last word now silences a change: the latest card of that NSG is this change's, closed by a human. Glorfindel's own closures of a quarantine card now carry a note (« changed », or whole, empty, gone). Tests in `tests/unit/test_reassert.py` (`-k "comes_back or could_not_be_written or last_word or own_nsg or unreadable_config"`), the 14-step sequence no longer `xfail`; the invariant machine now draws it (`quarantine_change_comes_back`, with a disk that fills up mid-pass) and finds the bug again at 500 sequences; mutants M1860–M1866.

## [0.4.7] — 2026-10-30 — "held to proof"

### Security
- **LiteLLM 1.86.1 → 1.100.4** (DEP1, #282, 29/10; `docs/operations.md`, « Verrou des dépendances »). The first release that fixes the three advisories on the locks: GHSA-3cv6-jpf6-8222 (authenticated SSRF through request-body routing parameters, #316), GHSA-g5ff-637f-6q2m (arbitrary local file read through `vertex_ai_credentials`, #317) and GHSA-7hp6-4w63-5g45 (high: privilege escalation to proxy admin through the salt key reused across domains, introduced in 1.91.0 — 1.95.1, the first candidate, carried it). All three sit in LiteLLM's proxy, which Eregion does not run.
  - **What enters with it**, same resolution date as the lock before (26/10): 1.100.4 declares `boto3` and `pydantic-settings` on top of 1.86.1's dependencies. boto3, botocore, s3transfer and jmespath enter the three locks; pydantic-settings, python-dateutil and six enter the injectors' too (chromadb already brought them to the others). python-dateutil goes back from 3.0.0 to 2.9.0.post0 in `dev.txt` and `glorfindel.txt` (botocore requires `<3.0.0`), and tzdata, pulled by 3.0.0 only, leaves. The AWS SDK is installed by litellm, never imported outside a provider's adapter (`tests/unit/test_provider_boundary.py`).
  - **The call proven in each image before the harness** (CI1): a tool call through litellm, the way the decision makes it (`eregion_core/llm_call.py`), answered by litellm's own mock — no key, no model, no network (`--network none`). `import litellm` was not enough: 1.106.0 (19/10) imported, then failed on every call (« No module named 'mcp' »); measured again on 29/10 in a throwaway venv, the check fails on it. Tests `tests/unit/test_image_check.py`, mutants M1610–M1614.
  - The four advisories of chromadb 1.5.9 have no fix yet. The release that carries this lock waits for the harness's attestation in H3's format (`requirements/harness.json`, `eregion.harness/2`: two passes, the lock in service then this one, judged case by case): H0's CD refuses it without one. A committed attestation on another lock, without its reference, or that misses the default model fails in CI first (`tests/unit/test_llm_smoke.py`, mutant M1616).

### Added
- **The coverage matrix, its evolution and the non-regression, in the console** (C1, #80, 30/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Matrice de couverture »; `docs/architecture.md`, « Le run, unifié »; `docs/design/console.md`).
  - **One run format for an attack and a fault.** Annatar and Gothmog write the same report, `runs/<run_id>.json`, from one class in the shared core (`eregion_core/run_report.RunReport`; each injector keeps only how it prints it). The report now says what it is, in `metrics`: `run_format` (1), `kind` (`technique` or `fault`), `injector`, `experiment_id` (the ATT&CK id or the fault's id). A compatible addition: a report written before reads as before, `schemas/run_report.schema.json` already allows other fields, and the signal contract (`eregion_core/signals.py`) does not change. One reader (`read_run`) gives the view the console shares; `timeline` joins the watch's decision and gives an incident's times — attack, detection, decision, verified action (`outcome.verified_at` when the watch writes it, else the record's time) — the format BL1 measures the time to contain on.
  - **The matrix** (`eregion_console/coverage.py`, `GET /api/coverage[?kind=technique|fault]`): for each technique of the catalog and each declared fault — and a run outside the catalog, said so — its last run (date, detected or not, time to detect, Glorfindel's decision, time to contain when an isolation or a block was executed and verified), its last proof (a technique detected **and** with its decision attached; a fault PASS with the watch running), its last ten outcomes and its state: `proven` (under 21 days), `aging` (21 to 30), `stale` (over 30: the alert), `regressed`, `unproven`, `never`. **The evolution**: twelve weeks, the techniques detected in the 30 days before each. Read from every run report the host keeps (campaigns included) and the watch's cycles, over the whole history — never from the cloud, never from the exercise windows (7 days).
  - **The non-regression**: a technique detected before whose last measured run missed (a fault PASS then FAIL) is `regressed`, red, with the run that held and the run that lost; a run that measured nothing (unknown, `--without-glorfindel`, a fault refused) neither proves nor regresses. `eregion coverage [--json] [--check]` prints the matrix from `runs/` and, with `--check`, exits 1 on a regression — after a replay of the catalog on the bench.
  - **In the console**: the right column of Annatar's and Gothmog's pages is the matrix (it replaces the catalog list); on the overview, the coverage says what regressed (red, a link to its page) and the stale proofs, and the age of the last proof is read over the whole history — over 30 days it is said, red, instead of « none in 30 d ».
  - **After the review**: a run dated more than 5 minutes ahead is left out of the matrix and the indicators, and said (a `missed` of 2076 kept a row regressed for ever); the decision attached is the watch's record on the run's VM (Annatar's reports now name it, `metrics.resource_id`), of the run's own technique, the closest to its detection; a fault whose start failed is neutral; the detections are indexed once (bisection), each report read once per file version, the matrix computed again only when a file changes or after 30 s (2 000 runs and 40 000 cycles: 0.2 s, 6.4 s before); the overview's time to contain goes through `timeline` too; `eregion coverage` prints no control character; `schemas/run_report.schema.json` declares the new fields.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_coverage.py` (fake runs replayed), `tests/unit/test_run_report.py` (the core alone), the injectors' (`-k unified`); mutants M1800–M1829, M1880–M1889 (M560 retargeted on the indexed search).

### Changed
- **The bench's Activity Log and Entra ID logs, ready to switch on and to measure** (ID1, #281, preparation; nothing planned nor applied — Jonathan applies and measures). AL1 and C2 ship detection only, but blind: their tables reach the bench's workspace only through two diagnostic settings, one on the subscription, one on the tenant.
  - **Switched by Terraform variables**, off by default: `activity_log_enabled` (category `Administrative` → `AzureActivity`) and `entra_logs_enabled` (`AuditLogs`, `ServicePrincipalSignInLogs` → `AuditLogs`, `AADServicePrincipalSignInLogs`), set in `infra/terraform/terraform.tfvars` — no longer `activity_log.enabled` / `entra_logs.enabled` in `config.yaml` (tracked and public: a fork applying it would send its own tenant's logs). Canonical instance only; another one asking for them is told (a Terraform `check`).
  - **`make celebrimbor-logs-plan` / `make celebrimbor-logs-up [YES=1]`**: plan and apply targeted on those two settings alone, so a pending drift of the bench never rides along; refused on another instance before any call; never a `-var` of their own (the next apply without it would remove the setting). Back: the variable to `false`, the same two commands.
  - **Rights, cost and latency, with their sources** (Microsoft Learn, the Azure retail prices API, the azurerm documentation), in the Terraform and in `docs/operations.md`, « Les journaux d'activité et d'Entra ID au banc »: Monitoring Contributor on the subscription; an Entra Security Administrator for the tenant's setting, under `az login` (a service principal would need `Contributor` at `/providers/Microsoft.aadiam`); `AzureActivity` free; Entra's tables at 2.99 USD per GB in West Europe beyond the monthly 5 GB, estimated under 2 USD a month on the bench. The licence is corrected to a hypothesis: Entra's licensing page gives audit and sign-in logs on Free and says nothing of their export (C2 assumed P1 or P2).
  - **The measurement, written**: the checks before the plan, what the plan must show and nothing else, the known test operations (a harmless NSG rule, a Reader assignment, a disk snapshot and its download access, an Azure Backup on demand; a test application, a secret, an admin consent, Security Reader assigned, the test principal's sign-ins), the KQL measuring the end-to-end ingestion latency and each rule seeing its operation (the rule's own query, as written), the cost (`Usage`), and the hypotheses to settle (D1–D2, A1–A6, E1–E9), each with its measurement and what changes either way. What R2 (#79) expects from ID1, in one line.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_bench_logs.py`: the categories sent are exactly those the shipped rules' tables need (a table a rule reads without its category fails, a category no rule reads fails), the Activity Log rules read only Administrative operations, both off by default and on the canonical instance only, the Make targets against a fake `terraform`; `infra/terraform/tests/diagnostic_settings.tftest.hcl`, the same contract planned against a mocked azurerm (`terraform test`, by hand: the CI has no Terraform). Mutants M1790–M1799.

### Fixed
- **The orphan sweep no longer opens a VM that took a deleted VM's address** (I5Q, #327, 29/10; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I5Q »). A VM blocked for itself and then on its subnet — its own rule at 200, the perimeter one at 210 —, that rule turned into an allow by someone, then the VM isolated by rules and deleted, and a new VM given its address and its NSG: the reassertion's orphan sweep took the deleted VM's isolation off the new VM, and the attacker, let through the subnet's NSG by the turned rule, reached it on 22 — the perimeter block standing behind. Found by the machine (seed 101 at 500 sequences, RB1's fourth review), present before RB1. A deny of a NIC that no longer exists now stays when one of our rules turned into an allow, on the same side and aimed at an address it cuts (or at all), sits on an NSG that governs that NIC: the card says which rule and why; once that rule is put back or removed, the next pass takes the deny off. The new VM stays cut off meanwhile — never opened. The release of a deleted VM had the same hole, found by the machine once its new composite rule (`turned_then_address_reused`) made the state common (seed 322 at 500, I4): the denies of its placements, and those I7S looks for on every NSG, are kept the same way — `release_partial`, the turned rule named; a live VM's own turned rule never holds its release. Tests in `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py` and `tests/unit/test_orphan_sweep.py`; mutants M1730–M1747. A deny kept that way no longer loses its card when its sibling (the other direction, cutting the same VM) is taken off: the card they share stayed resolved, the kept deny unsaid (`glorfindel/orphans.py`). A card said before the rule was turned (in `human_only`, or shared with the sibling) no longer keeps telling to delete the deny, which would open the VM: it is replaced by one naming every turned rule the VM's kept denies wait behind, whose first steps put them back as denies. The NSGs that govern the VM cut are read again just before a removal, and the guard played again.
- **A block written again no longer cuts off the VM that took over the old address** (I4A, #328, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Found by RB1's invariant machine (seed 8675), present on main before it. A VM blocked; someone turns one of its block rules into an allow; its IP changes and Azure hands the old address to a new VM, which the turned rule now lets the attacker reach. The block written again (a second `block`, or the reassertion outside `human_only`) rewrote that rule by its name for the VM's new address: the allow was gone, and an operation on one VM changed another VM's flows (I4). Now such a rule is left as it is: an allow of ours aimed at an address that is no longer the NIC's, held by another NIC, or by nobody knows whom (the NICs unreadable, a range), or at any address on an NSG others share; and our own deny on such an address while a rule of the NIC's block turned into an allow waits behind it, on one of the NIC's NSGs — aimed at the address, any, a range holding it, or a tag (moved, it let that allow open the other VM — seed 42 at 2,000; those NSGs unreadable, it is kept). A rule of the first pair that can't be taken off is said (`drop_failed`). The block is written under the NIC's second name (same NIC hash: found by the verification, the unblock, the reset and the orphan sweep), the first pair's other rule taken off once it holds. The block's outcome says what it left (`left_rules`); the reassertion says it once on a card (`rule_left`), and writes nothing. An old address nobody holds: rewritten as before. Tests `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py` (the frozen sequence, no longer `xfail`); the invariant machine draws the sequence (`address_reused_under_a_turned_rule`); mutants M1755–M1769; the isolation's side of the same defect is frozen as a strict `xfail` for I4B (#333). **Still open** (I4B): the unblock and the reset take off a rule with the NIC's hash whatever it has become — the turned allow the block now leaves, too —, so the VM given the old address still loses those flows there; frozen as strict `xfail`s, unseen by the invariant machine (it drops the attacker's flows at unblock and reset).

## [0.4.6] — 2026-10-29 — "held at the source"

### Security
- **The images published, signed and pulled by their digest** (CI3, #267 step 2, 29/10; findings P-02 — no SBOM, no signature — and V-22 — unsigned tags; `docs/operations.md`, « Les images publiées »; the threat model, frontier 6).
  - **The head of every push to `main` publishes its two images** to GHCR, `ghcr.io/vanyar53/eregion-glorfindel` and `-annatar`, tagged `sha-<commit>` (`.github/workflows/publish.yml`). Built as CI1 builds them (plus UID/GID 1000, the hosts' user, and the version and commit as labels), proven in the image **before** the push, then attested — a SLSA provenance (`actions/attest-build-provenance`) and an SPDX SBOM (`actions/attest-sbom`), both pushed next to the image — and signed keyless (cosign, a sigstore certificate on GitHub's OIDC token: no long-lived key, no secret). The workflow then verifies them as H0 does. Every commit, not the release: a draft targets a commit of `main` and H0 deploys it as soon as it is published, so its image must already be there; the CD waits for the commit's `publish (glorfindel)` check like the main CI's. That workflow alone may write a package, mint an OIDC token or write an attestation, at its job's level, on a push to this repository's `main` only — never a pull request, a fork's included (`tests/unit/test_workflows.py`). The images are public: the repository is, and an image holds no secret.
  - **H0 no longer builds** (its CD, `eregion_cd.py`): the release's image digest is read anonymously on the registry (the sha256 of the manifest read: a tag is a hint), then its signature and its provenance are verified by a pinned cosign (v3.1.3, its sha256 checked before each use): the publishing workflow of this repository, on `refs/heads/main`, triggered by a push, **at the tag's commit**; the provenance is read again (subject, workflow, repository, ref, event, source commit, a GitHub-hosted runner). Pulled by that digest, its labels must say the tag and the commit, and it must run as H0's user. An image absent, private, unsigned, signed by another workflow, repository, branch or for another commit, without provenance, or a digest that moved since the release was first seen: refused, said in `#deploy` with the mention — never built instead; a private or absent package is refused at its anonymous token (GHCR's 403). A registry or sigstore that does not answer (cosign's own words classified: an absent or other signature refuses, the network or TUF waits) is said once, in blue, and waited for an hour, then refused; `docker pull` and cosign are bounded. A « signature » that is only an attestation is refused (cosign v3 takes one for the other, measured). Measured on 29/10, without any token, on public GHCR images: a build provenance verified with these flags, another commit and another workflow refused. `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` brings `published_image.py`, and has the VM fetch cosign (its sha256 kept only if pinned). The transition, step by step: `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Ce que CI3 change ».
  - **Orodruin gets the published image, verified on the operator's machine** (`make orodruin-push [REF=<tag|commit>]`): resolved, verified and pulled by digest there, loaded over SSH — Orodruin's egress stays closed, GHCR's blobs are served under *.githubusercontent.com, GitHub's domain for user content too, and an NSG filters by address only — under a waiting tag, its image id read back on Orodruin against the ids computed before it left, and only then tagged `eregion-annatar:orodruin`; `~/.eregion-image` is cleared first and written (« commit digest ») on success only. No Terraform change. `make orodruin-push-dev` keeps the former path, the image built on the machine, unverified, said as development (`docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « L'image des injecteurs »).
  - What stays open (the threat model): whoever can merge to `main` publishes an image H0 accepts; the SBOM is attested, not read by the CD; Orodruin's image is verified only on the machine that sends it; a deployment of H0 by hand still builds on the VM.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_published_image.py` (a fake registry, a fake cosign answering as cosign v3.1.3 does, a fake docker), `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`; mutants M1650–M1669.

### Changed
- **H0's CD judges the harness's attestation against the lock in service, not in absolute terms** (H3, #324, 29/10; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « L'attestation du harnais »). The model H0 runs refuses a temperature, and the same lock (litellm 1.100.4) drew 51/51 and no overconfident error, then 50/51 and one: the pair « brute force after root » drew `isolate_vm` twice, then `block_suspicious_ip` at 0.80. Only 15 cases out of 17 drew one action on each pass. The absolute rule (everything right, no overconfident error, one pass) refused at random and asked for the harness again until green.
  - **Two passes, one file.** `make llm-compare MODELS=<H0's model> RUNS=3 ATTEST=1 [BASE=<tag>]` (`scripts/harness_attest.py`) runs the harness on the reference first: the lock of the release in service (the latest `v*` tag, or `BASE`), installed with `--require-hashes` in a throwaway venv under `~/.cache/eregion/`. Then it runs on the candidate (the checkout's `.venv`, refused when it does not follow the lock). The code and the cases are the same; only the dependencies differ. `requirements/harness.json` (`eregion.harness/2`) keeps each pass's `lock_sha256` and `runs`, and per case every draw: right or not, the action, the confidence. Nothing of the logs. It costs twice one pass, about 260 calls to the model (306 at most).
  - **What the CD refuses:** an attestation of the old format (said, with the command), incomplete or for another lock; a broken integration on the candidate; no reference, or one on another lock than the one in service; a regression (right on every draw on the reference, wrong on every draw on the candidate); overconfidence the reference does not have (one case on every draw, or more than one case — one is the noise measured on 29/10); a decision steered by an injection or the memory on the candidate only, even once. A case that varies, or is wrong on both sides, is said in orange on `#deploy`, without the mention, never a refusal. After the review: any case losing 2 draws of 3 to the reference is refused, of every kind (Jonathan's choice, 29/10 then 30/10: p ≈ 1/6 measured: about 4 % of refusals at random per noisy case, up to 9 % if p ≈ 1/3; from 4 to 20 % per attestation with the noise — accepted), and so is a case gaining 2 overconfident draws (it replaces « overconfident on every draw, never on the reference », which it contains), and 3 draws lost or more to the reference, max(0, right before − right now) summed over every case (the same lock against itself lost 1): 31/51 against 51/51, or against 41/51, no longer passes. What still passes, said in orange: per case, one draw lost and one overconfident draw gained at most; in all, 2 draws lost to the reference (over every case) and one case newly overconfident at most; and a regression of the code (both passes run the new code: it shows as « wrong on both sides », counted in the orange when `glorfindel/` or `eregion_core/` changes) — the threat model and the README write it down.
  - **The order** (README): the release that carries H3 does not change `requirements/glorfindel.txt`, so the CD of before deploys it without an attestation. Then `make glorfindel-host-cd-install`, then DEP1's release with the attestation in the new format. Out of order, it is a refusal said on `#deploy`, never a silent deployment.
  - DEP1's CI check (`test_the_committed_attestation_is_the_locks_and_covers_the_default_model`) reads the new format: the candidate carries the committed lock's sha256, the reference the lock of its release (when the checkout has the tag), and both cover the default model. Tests `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`, `tests/unit/test_llm_smoke.py`; mutants M1670–M1689, and M1010–M1012 retargeted.

### Fixed
- **`make llm-compare ATTEST=1` writes its attestation** (DEP1, #282, 29/10). A `from pathlib import Path` in the harness's `--json` branch made `Path` local to its `main`: without `--json`, the attestation of a full, paid run was lost to an error at its very end. Test `test_make_llm_compare_attest_writes_the_attestation_without_json`, mutant M1617.
- **The reassertion of a merged block puts back all of it, and its card says exactly what is back and what still holds** (RB1, #321, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Found at the review of I5R, present on 0.4.5; nothing opened by Glorfindel, but cards that said wrong. A `block_perimeter` and a `block` of one VM share one state entry. Someone turned the VM's own rule into an allow: the reassertion put back the perimeter rule alone. On one subnet, the perimeter still cut the attacker off, and the next pass said « l'IP n'est plus bloquée » (in `human_only`, at once). On a VM with NICs on two subnets, the perimeter covers the primary NIC's subnet only: « reposé une fois » was said while the other NIC stayed open. Now:
  - the verification counts a perimeter block of the IP NIC by NIC — the entry's own or another VM's on the subnet: a NIC whose own rules are gone, behind it (present, whole, not bypassed on the threat port), is still blocked (`held_by_perimeter`); one it doesn't cover is open (`open_nics`); one whose perimeter can't be read is unknown (`perimeter_unread`) — and checks the perimeter's own pair: deleted, it was gone in silence for every other VM of the subnet;
  - the reassertion writes back the perimeter **and** the VM's rules, then reads Azure again: « reposé une fois » only for a block that holds whole; otherwise the card says what still doesn't hold (outcome `partial`, said once per episode);
  - a card never says « l'IP n'est plus bloquée » of an IP our perimeter (or the quarantine) still cuts off: « l'IP reste bloquée sur … par la règle de périmètre … »;
  - a write that failed is never « reposé » (`partial`, the error on the card), and a perimeter pair that can't be read makes the verification unknown (found at the review).
- **A perimeter block no longer says `blocked` with a NIC left open, and its promotion no longer opens one** (RB1, review). The perimeter lies on the primary NIC's subnet. A VM with a NIC on another subnet: `blocked` was said while that NIC stayed open, and a promotion (`replace=True`) took the VM's own block off both NICs — the second one open, `verify_block_ip` true, no card. Now the promotion keeps the block of a NIC off the perimeter's subnet (of every NIC when they can't be read); a perimeter block names the NICs it leaves open (`uncovered_nics`, status `blocked_partial`; `nics_unread`), what blocks a NIC off its subnet read on Azure, never from the state; a perimeter block alone is verified on the VM's NICs too, and the reassertion's card says, once and with nothing written, that the perimeter never covered it (« non couverte depuis le blocage (autre subnet) — bloquer aussi la VM »). A NIC a promotion left to the perimeter is covered by design while the perimeter holds: the next pass no longer says it vanished nor writes the VM's rules back over the promotion (second review). That card has its own mark (`uncovered_said`, said again when the set of NICs changes): acknowledged, it no longer silences the perimeter's later disappearance (third review). A drift card keeps what it said (`drift_said`) and is said again when it worsens — a NIC held turned open, a rule more gone — never when the same or better (fourth review: acknowledged, « reste bloquée par la règle de périmètre » silenced that perimeter's deletion).
- **The unblock of a deleted VM says how to take what it leaves, its perimeter block too** (RB1). A rule it can't attribute is left with the `az network nsg rule delete -g … --nsg-name … -n …` that takes it off, and the unblock says it again while it stays (`unblock_partial`) — taken off, the next unblock is done. Its entry lost, a perimeter block of the IP (`glorfindel-block-<ip>`) that no other VM's entry records was neither looked for nor said (`not_found`): it is now said and left, for it covers a whole subnet.
- **Our outbound block rule turned Inbound by someone no longer makes the rewrite collide** (RB1). Its number counted as free, the inbound deny was written there, Azure refused two rules of one direction at one number, and nothing was put back. Only a rule of ours in its own direction frees its number (`_pair_priorities`).
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`, `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`; the invariant machine draws a second subnet for Db--01's second NIC (about half the worlds; the examples a `GLORFINDEL_INVARIANT_DB` holds replay shifted), checks what the reassertion's block cards say (« reposé », « reste bloquée », « n'est plus bloquée ») NIC by NIC against the world, and I3 now judges a perimeter block on every NIC of its VM — but the ones it names uncovered, off its subnet — and a perimeter block alone's verification; mutants M1620–M1649. `glorfindel/reassert.py` was split first (`reassert_quarantine.py`), unchanged in behaviour.

## [0.4.5] — 2026-10-29 — "held in place"

### Security
- **Identities and hardening** (SEC7, #296; the threat model, frontiers 3, 5 and 6).
  - **One Azure principal per service of Glorfindel, with only what its code calls** (V-17; `docs/operations.md`, « Identités Azure »). The watch, the console and the bot each take their own (`GLORFINDEL_AZURE_CLIENT_*`, `EREGION_CONSOLE_AZURE_CLIENT_*`, `GLORFINDEL_BOT_AZURE_CLIENT_*`); empty, the former single `AZURE_CLIENT_*`. Two custom roles (`infra/identities/`): the response's writes (isolate, release, block, snapshot) for the three, the restore for the console and the bot only — never for the watch. Readers and the roles on the enrolled resource groups. README, `.envrc.example` and the operations guide create them that way, with the rotation of a secret and the removal of a former shared principal; nothing grants Contributor or Owner on a subscription any more, in the docs, the scripts or what the audit and the readiness advise — they name the two roles (`tests/unit/test_azure_roles.py`). The shared `AZURE_CLIENT_*` is the watch's in `.envrc.example`, never the console's; the red container (`make annatar-*`) never falls back to it. `make glorfindel-start` says a principal whose id or secret is missing (the fallbacks are drawn one variable at a time); the readiness says, and the watch logs once, a principal that holds a restore's rights. The watch's `GLORFINDEL_OWN_CALLERS` gets the console's and the bot's ids from compose, so their approved writes are no control-plane cards. H0 is unchanged (its managed identity, a reader).
  - **The bot's buttons** (V-08): after a restart, only a card that still waits gets its buttons back — pending, or a decision still to judge. At the click the card is read again: acknowledged, judged, expired, or its VM no longer in the discovery, the button answers « carte réglée » and runs nothing; the restore's form and confirmation too. The same rule greys the buttons: an acknowledged decision keeps its verdict buttons until it is judged. A posted message not re-armed gets an inert view: even shown live, its buttons answer « carte réglée », never « interaction failed ».
  - **The containers hardened** (V-34): every compose service runs with no capability, no privilege gained, a read-only root filesystem and `/tmp` in tmpfs; what each writes is a volume (measured on Docker; `tests/unit/test_profiles.py`). H0's CD reads each container's journal after the switch: a « Read-only file system » the release before did not show rolls back (in the CD's agent, after `make glorfindel-host-cd-install`). What to check at H0's next deployment: `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`.
  - **H0's host key pinned** (V-32): `HOST_SSH_FINGERPRINT`, read on the VM and kept in the operator's environment, writes a dedicated `known_hosts`; every command toward H0 trusts it alone, and another key refuses before anything runs. Without it, the key is still accepted at first contact, and each command says so (`infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « SSH »).
  - **The control plane read from the query** (N-10): a rule of another source that reads `AzureActivity` is the control plane whatever it projects — decided by the code, never by the model, as an Entra query already was.
  - **The follow-ups of the reviews of 29/10.** Orodruin: a subscription without Chaos Studio holds no experiment whatever the status of the answer (to measure); `orodruin-plan`, `-up` and `-lock` refuse when the hosted Glorfindel's state does not read (`NO_WATCH=1`, and 1 only); a pulled window whose local copy no longer reads is never replaced. A restore's claim holds for as long as its process lives (a lock on its file), says where it is and until when when it is left behind, and each restore removes its own only. The images' CI builds on a missing decision, on a HEAD that is not the merge commit and in a merge queue. The release's allow-list refuses a renamed `os` or `subprocess`, `importlib`, and a write method in one word, in `release.py` too; `issues: write` is the dependency audit's alone (`tests/unit/test_workflows.py`).
  - **Choices written down** (the threat model, « Décisions assumées »): the design review without a named owner (T-05), the bench open to the Internet by design (V-37).

### Fixed
- **The release of a deleted VM no longer leaves its isolation rules on a shared NSG** (I7S, #303, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Found by the invariant machine (seed 322 at 500 sequences), present on `main`. Take a VM isolated by rules on an NSG it shares, whose state is lost, whose NIC is then left bare and isolated again on its subnet's NSG; the VM is deleted. Its release undid what the state named, found the VM gone and stopped there: `released`, while the first isolation stayed on the shared NSG, aimed at the address Azure hands to the next VM — which is then cut off (a disruptive action by mistake, never an opening). Now, the VM gone, the release looks for the rules named for the NICs its states name (the isolation's placements, the blocks', those a partial release kept) on every NSG of the subscription and takes them off. A listing that fails gives `release_partial`, the state and its NICs kept for the retry. A state of that VM that can't be read (isolation or block) gives `release_partial` too, the file kept as it is: the release used to clear the only file that named the NIC. Each known NIC is read first, since `az vm delete` and Terraform keep it, detached, by default. When it is gone, detached, or on a VM of another name, its rules are looked for. When it is attached to another VM of the same name, that VM's isolation has the same names: they stay, and the release says so. When it can't be read, the release is not verified. Only the current hashed names are looked for, by design (I2). Without any state naming the NIC, the orphan sweep (A3) is still the net.
- **A reassertion no longer opens a VM: a rule written again keeps its place** (I5R, #311, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Found by the invariant machine, present on 0.4.4. A VM blocked on its subnet (priority 200) and for itself (210); someone turned the VM's rule into an allow; the reassertion put the block back and rewrote the perimeter rule at the first free number, its own counted as taken — 220, under that allow: the attacker reached the VM on every port. A block or an isolation by rules written again now keeps each rule's number, or a smaller one in its direction, never a larger one. What the reassertion can't put back is said by its drift card, as before.
- **The unblock of a deleted VM looks for its block** (I5R). The VM gone, `unblock` answered `not_found` without looking, and a block whose state entry was lost stayed on a shared NSG, cutting the attacker's address off the next VM given it. As the release of a deleted VM now does (same code), the rules named for the NICs the local state names are looked for on every NSG of the subscription and removed; a NIC Azure kept and attached since to another VM of that name is left, and said. A rule named for that VM whose NIC nothing names is left and said (`unblock_partial`), as is a state that can't be read.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py` (and a composite rule of the invariant machine, `block_turned_then_reasserted`); mutants M1590–M1599.

### Added
- **A `#releases` channel: a release's story in one place** (REL2, #309, 29/10; `docs/atelier-discord.md`, « `#releases` »). A new webhook, `EREGION_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_RELEASES`, gets one card per step, silent, never deleted, each with a link button: the version's pull request (`chore/release-X.Y.Z`, from this repository: a fork's branch of that name stays in `#ci`) opened and merged; the draft ready, posted by `make release-draft` with its notes in short (section titles, first entries); the release published, with « le CD de H0 la déploie : voir #deploy ». The published card and the version's pull request leave `#ci`, which keeps the other pull requests, red runs and closed issues. `#agents` keeps the draft's line and ping (REL1), unchanged; the CD of H0 stays in `#deploy`.
  - **A draft's link that survives an edit.** Measured on the 0.4.4: GitHub gives a draft a new `…/releases/tag/untagged-<hash>` URL at each edit, and `make release-draft` edits it right after posting it (#agents' ids in its notes), so the link on `#agents` led nowhere. The link posted, on `#agents` and `#releases`, is now the repository's list of releases, where the draft comes first for its members.
  - **Text from outside stays inert** (SEC6): a release's notes and name and a pull request's title go through `glorfindel/chat_text.py`, brought by `notify.yml`'s sparse checkout; no mention allowed, a button only to an `https://` link. A card refused with its button (400) is sent again without it. The secret is given to `release` events and to the repository's own version pull request only (`pull_request_target` whose head is this repository's `chore/release-…`, checked in `notify.yml`, then again by the script); never to a fork's pull request nor to a run — a red run on `chore/release-*` stays in `#ci`, with its ping. Without `chat_text`, a strict filter shows every mark as « · », `_` included.
  - **Without the webhook, nothing changes**: the pull request and the publication stay in `#ci`, the draft has no card; a Discord error is one line, and the command or the job succeeds. `notify_discord.py check` names the new key.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_notify_discord.py`, `tests/unit/test_release.py` (the fake `gh` moves a draft's URL at each edit), mutants M1540–M1549, M1580–M1584.

## [0.4.4] — 2026-10-29 — "held at the core"

### Security
