- **The advisories of the locked dependencies, one issue per advisory** (CI2, #268, 29/10; `docs/operations.md`, « Les avis de vulnérabilité des verrous »). Findings P-03 (no scanner) and N-16 (a lock refreshed unchecked).
  - **A weekly workflow, also run by hand** (`.github/workflows/pip-audit.yml`). It runs `pip-audit` on `requirements/dev.txt`, `glorfindel.txt` and `annatar.txt`, each read as locked (`--require-hashes --disable-pip`): nothing is resolved, and only the advisory service is asked. pip-audit has its own pinned and hashed lock (`requirements/audit.txt`) and runs in a venv of its own. The workflow uses no third-party action and no secret but the repository's token (`contents: read`, `issues: write`).
  - **One issue per advisory, label `security`** (`scripts/pip_audit_issues.py`, standard library). It gives the package, the version in each lock it touches, the fixed versions and the identifiers.
    - The same advisory never opens two issues. A key kept in the issue's body (the package, every identifier seen, its locks) finds the issue, open or closed, whatever its title or labels became. Every page is read, and pull requests are skipped. pip-audit's duplicates and an advisory found in several locks are merged.
    - Only the issues the workflow opened (`github-actions[bot]`) are read back, and each field of their key is checked against its shape. An issue by anyone else that carries a key never silences an advisory, is never edited, and never puts its identifiers in the bot's issue.
    - An open issue is rewritten when what it says changes. An issue closed as done while the advisory is still there is reopened. An issue closed as not planned, or as a duplicate, is left alone.
    - An advisory no longer found gets one comment, only if every lock of its issue was read in that run. The workflow never closes the issue.
    - The text of an advisory is checked against its shape, and its description is never written.
  - **Never a pull request.** A bump goes through `make lock`, then the harness.
  - **The run goes red when there is something new**: an issue opened or reopened, an advisory back, or an audit that failed. `notify.yml` announces it in `#ci`, because an issue opened by the workflow's token triggers no workflow. A GitHub error on one advisory or issue does not stop the others; the run says each error and exits 2. The runner is pinned (`ubuntu-24.04`): the locks' markers are read for its Python, which must not drift. pip-audit's own lock is audited too.
  - **`make lock` reports the advisories of the locks it just wrote** (`make audit`), and never fails on them.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_pip_audit_issues.py` (fake `pip-audit` and `gh`, a test lock with `requests==2.19.1`, CVE-2018-18074); mutants M1150–M1159, M1490–M1497.
  - The first audit (29/10, run locally, no issue created) finds 6 advisories. Four are for chromadb 1.5.9 (`dev.txt`, `glorfindel.txt`), with no fixed version yet. Two are for litellm 1.86.1 (all three locks); 1.95.1 is the first version that fixes both (DEP1, #282).
- **The local state, the reassertion and the proposed rules** (SEC4b, #275, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, « État local illisible » and « La réaffirmation et les contournements » ; `docs/fichiers-cles.md` ; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »).
  - **An unreadable state file is an unknown for every reader that concludes from an absence**, not only the orphan sweep (SEC3). While an isolation state file can't be read — unparsable, or not the shape Glorfindel writes — the release, `reset --from-azure` and `verify_release` take nothing off a NIC in quarantine or left without an NSG, and never say it released: they name the file. The fixed names of an older version's isolation on an NSG are left and said. A failed release no longer writes over that file. An unblock behind the quarantine ends `unblock_partial`, naming the file. An isolation is never stopped by an unreadable state: what it would have recorded is read as unknown — our tag when it says the original, else left unwritten, and the release keeps the NIC in quarantine until it is known; the VM's own unreadable file is set aside (`isolation/aside/`), never written over and never a state again, repaired or not — listed as unreadable until removed by hand. An isolation that leaves a NIC's original unknown says it at once (`original_unknown`, in its result, its state and on the console's card), not first at the release. A state with placements and no resource id is unreadable. The cost, assumed: while a file can't be read, the release, the reset and the verification of such a NIC wait until it is repaired or removed by hand.
  - **The reassertion says what it can't read**: one card `state_unreadable` per set of unreadable files, said once (acknowledged, it doesn't come back while that set stands), closed once every file reads again (`~/.glorfindel/state_alerts.json`).
  - **The reassertion says a bypass.** A rule of someone else's that lets traffic through before Glorfindel's deny, on the NIC's or the subnet's NSG: one card `bypassed` per isolation or block and per change — not at every pass, not again once acknowledged — closed once the deny holds again. Glorfindel never removes that rule.
  - **The proposed detection rules' store is locked and rewritten whole** (`~/.glorfindel/proposed_rules.jsonl`), as the escalations' since SEC3: each `record`, `approve` and `reject` under an flock, through a temporary file. Measured between processes: 28 proposals out of 60 lost before, a reader seeing half a file; none now.
    - **The console** makes no VM tile for the local state's card (`~/.glorfindel`): it showed a VM `.glorfindel`, counted as unprotected.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_unreadable_state.py`, `test_reassert.py`, `test_proposed_rules.py`, `test_locks.py`, `test_console.py`; mutants M1250–M1259, M1390–M1394, M1520–M1531.
- **The decision facing a model, a memory or a line it did not expect** (SEC4a, #275, 28/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « gardes de `decide` », « Source d'un cycle », « Le canal runs/ »).
  - **The model's answer is read field by field, for its type.** An action that is not text, a confidence that is not a number of [0, 1] (a boolean, NaN, an infinity, 5.0: unknown, held by the confidence gate — no longer brought back into the range), steps that are not a list: the field is unreadable and the cycle escalates — it no longer raises. Only the decision schema's fields are kept from the answer. A property test over arbitrary JSON keeps it so.
  - **Every signal, an exercise too, is given real past cycles only**: an exercise's cycles, or cycles of unknown source, are no longer retrieved — so what the model is shown never tells it whether the current signal is an exercise. A cycle's identifier in the memory is drawn by the memory: a cycle is added, never overwritten; a replayed `respond` is retrieved once.
  - **An `attack_started` from `runs/`**: the detection row the rule finds is read as the rule poller reads it (its resource, its host name, the private IP that made the call — one shared reading) and must name the VM the line names — a row naming another VM is never taken for it, and a row naming no VM leaves the actions aimed at that VM (isolation, block) held for a human (`unattributed_signal`). Its `attack_time` must be a moment within 2 h back and 5 min ahead, or the line is refused (counted with the other refusals).
  - **What the model sees changes**: fewer past cycles (real ones only, for every signal), and the attribution of an `attack_started`'s detection in its context — an end-to-end run comes with BL1 (#280).
- Tests `tests/unit/test_unexpected_decision.py`, mutants M1240–M1249, M1330–M1339, M1360–M1364.
- **The entries and the calls: restore, queries, timeouts, names** (SEC4c, #275, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/operations.md`).
  - **A restore takes the newest recovery point before the attack on that VM.** Without `--before`, `glorfindel restore` reads when the attack began from the `attack_started` lines of that VM the `runs/` channel admits — the earliest, within the bounds of SEC4a around the VM's isolation — instead of the last line in `runs/`, of any VM. When nothing says when, it asks for `--before` instead of taking the newest point; the connector refuses a restore without that moment. The command says the point chosen and why.
  - **A value read from a log line enters a query as a checked literal.** The host name and the source address the investigation queries with, and the VM's id and name in the audit, are checked for what they are, then escaped; a value that is not one is not queried with, and the investigation says so (counted as a failed query, which holds a disruptive action). A property test: no value leaves its literal.
  - **Every HTTP call has a timeout**: the vault's snapshot and restore triggers (they had none), and a test that refuses a `requests` call without one in the repository.
  - **A short VM name held by two VMs is refused wherever they are known** — isolated, blocked or discovered — with the list; the full id is asked for.
  - **The account-creation rule (T1136.001) reads useradd's own lines**, like the SSH and sudo rules since SEC2 — measured on a host's Syslog over 30 days: every real « new user » line came from it.
  - **A restore from the console, the bot or the TUI asks for its moment.** The field is filled with the moment the `runs/` channel gives for that VM (an exercise), else the card's first detection — the attack began before it; the recovery points from before it are shown, read only; the restore runs with that moment once confirmed. A refusal of the command is always shown: on the card in the console, in the thread for the bot, on screen for the TUI. The bot acknowledges a card only once its command succeeded.
  - **One restore at a time per VM**: a restore is refused while another one of that VM is running or about to start — two forms, a double click, two tabs, the TUI.
  - **A moment to restore before must be one**: a moment in the future, or at the calendar's edge, is refused on every path without a crash; a moment after the VM's isolation is said. The proposed moment never falls after the isolation, and a point taken during the incident is marked.
  - **What the model sees changes** only when a value is refused: its queries show as empty, as a failed query did. Unchanged, for BL1: the system prompt still describes `restore_from_backup` as « from last recovery point ». An end-to-end run comes with BL1 (#280).
- Tests `tests/unit/test_entries_calls.py`, `tests/unit/test_restore_moment.py`, mutants M1370–M1389, M1396–M1409, M1460–M1469.
- **The injectors and their channels** (SEC5, #276, 29/10; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « SEC5 »).
  - **A refusal is not followed by a reset.** A campaign run the engine refuses before it touches its target (its inscription, its zone, the production rule, Orodruin passive), or that raised, resets nothing, and a refused target is refused for the rest of the campaign; the manifest says so.
  - **The catalog decides what is destructive**: the technique and its pinned source (the atomic's `engine_ref`, the catalog's scripts); a scenario that calls itself non-destructive against it is refused, unknown is destructive — in a single run and in a campaign's destructive policy.
  - **Never without Glorfindel, more strictly.** The watch's heartbeat names its perimeter (a digest of the subscription it reads), compared with the injector's; the gate is read again before each action of a run, not only at its start; `annatar clean` goes through it. `--without-glorfindel` is refused in zone `production` and written to Orodruin's journal. The documentation says what the gate proves and what it does not.
  - **Exercise windows bounded by what the watch knows**: a window pulled from the channel is bounded by its blob's creation time, not by what it declares; once pulled, it is replaced only by its closure; on a production VM, a window marks nothing but a non-destructive technique.
  - **Bounded pulls**: a blob's size, a listing's size, how many names and downloads per pull — read as a stream cut past the bound, and said when exceeded; a retention on the channel's account keeps the listing under its bound.
  - **The channels send their storage token only to an Azure Storage blob endpoint** (public or sovereign clouds); any other URL is refused before a token is asked for.
  - **Orodruin's rights per enrolled VM**: it reads the bench's group, and writes only on each enrolled VM and each experiment; the contract refuses a write right on the whole group, and asks the chaos rights only when the group holds an experiment. Its journal is also pushed off the host, to the exercise channel. The channel's storage account denies every network but Orodruin's and H0's (service endpoints) and their and the operator's addresses. Terraform written and validated, to be applied.
  - **Vendored atomics are checked against their pinned sha256 at run time**, not only in CI.
  - **The TUI**: « unblock » removes the VM's IP blocks (it ran a full reset); Restore is refused on the same cards as the console and the bot.
  - Mutants M1260–M1279, M1450–M1456.
- **The security process: where to report, the boundaries, a README that says true, a hardened CI** (SEC6, #277, 28/10).
  - **`SECURITY.md`**: the supported version (the latest 0.4.x), how to report (a private advisory on GitHub, « Report a vulnerability » — no e-mail address), indicative delays, the scope (this repository's code; not the maintainer's bench nor hosts), and what is not a vulnerability (`human_only` by default, observation, a wrong model a guard holds).
  - **The threat model** (`docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`): one page per boundary — the console, `runs/` and the local state, Discord and the webhooks, the model's provider, the hosts, the CI and the CD — who is trusted, what crosses it, STRIDE, what holds it (with the code and the lot), what is still open, said as « planifié : #n ». I8S's choice (the quarantine tag wins over the state's « none ») is written as an assumed decision. `.github/CODEOWNERS`: the maintainer on everything, named again on `glorfindel/response/`, `glorfindel/providers/`, `infra/`, `.github/`.
  - **Text from outside is inert in the chats.** An Entra application's display name, the model's words, a rule's or a VM's name reach the Discord bot's cards and the webhook escaped: no Markdown, no masked link, no `@everyone`, no backtick closing Glorfindel's own (`glorfindel/chat_text.py`); the bot allows no mention but the role it pings. A card's steps are escaped whole: a command shows its backticks, without a code style; an empty value between Glorfindel's backticks shows « — ».
  - **The workshop's Discord against forks and outsiders.** A fork's red CI run is announced in `#ci`, never in a lot's thread (its head checked, not only its branch's name); an issue whose author holds no role in the repository, once closed, neither tags nor archives a lot's thread.
  - **The CI.** A workflow that holds a secret, or runs on an event a fork can trigger, pins every action by commit; the canary installs Terraform from HashiCorp's signed apt repository, its key checked by its fingerprint, instead of a third party's action; `actions/checkout` is pinned by commit everywhere. `mutants.yml` passes its inputs through `env:`. A test refuses an action named by its tag in such a workflow (and any new one elsewhere) and a `run:` that takes `inputs.*`, `github.event.*` or `github.head_ref` in its text (`tests/unit/test_workflows.py`).
  - Mutants M1280–M1289.

### Added
- **A release in two commands** (REL1, #269; `docs/operations.md`, « Packaging et version »). `make release VERSION=X.Y.Z TITLE="…"` cuts `[Unreleased]` into `[X.Y.Z] — <date> — "<title>"` (an empty `[Unreleased]` kept above), bumps `pyproject.toml` and the README's status, and opens the pull request `chore/release-X.Y.Z`. It refuses a modified tree, a HEAD that is not an up-to-date `origin/main`, a version not greater than the current one, an `[Unreleased]` empty or with headings only, and a branch or tag that exists. A step that fails after the branch is created (commit, push, pull request) is not said as a refusal: it says what is left and how to go on or back. `make release-draft VERSION=X.Y.Z [NOTES=file]`, after the merge, creates the **draft** on `origin/main`'s full sha (which must hold the version), with the notes given or the CHANGELOG's section. It refuses a tag or a release that exists. **No path publishes**: publishing deploys H0, and Jonathan alone publishes. A test reads the code against an allow-list: four `gh` commands, never `gh api`, no `draft` but `create`'s `--draft`, a `releases` URL only ever read. `DRY_RUN=1` shows everything and writes nothing. `scripts/release.py`, standard library only.
  - **The draft seen on `#agents`**: a 🙋 line on the board, with a link to the draft (visible to the repository's members only), and a ping. Both are deleted when the release is published: `notify.yml` (`release: published`, with the new secret `EREGION_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_AGENTS`) finds their ids in an HTML comment of the draft's notes and reads each message first: only this draft's ping is deleted, only the board rewritten. The secret is given to release events only. The local board asks GitHub as well, at once when the ping is gone. Without Discord's configuration, nothing is sent and the command succeeds (`docs/atelier-discord.md`).
  - **One source of version**: `annatar.__version__` reads `pyproject.toml`; it no longer holds a copy.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_release.py` (a throwaway git repository, a fake `gh`, the fake Discord), mutants M1160–M1169, M1430–M1439, M1470.
- **The images built and proven in CI** (CI1, #267, step 1, 29/10; `docs/operations.md`, « La CI des images »). A new workflow (`.github/workflows/images.yml`) runs on pull requests and on `main`, and builds when what goes into an image changes: a Dockerfile, the lock, `pyproject.toml`, the packages and files the images copy. It builds Glorfindel's image and the injectors' (Annatar, Gothmog) with GitHub's cache, never pushed (no registry, no login, no secret), then checks inside each, with `scripts/image_check.py`: `--version` is `pyproject.toml`'s version; the watch, the bot and the console import (the same imports as H0's CD preflight); `glorfindel-config.yaml.example` passes A3's strict reading; Glorfindel, the console and their dependencies are absent from the injectors' image (D1); neither image runs as root. Each check is a `docker run --network none` with no environment from the host, and nothing starts. A broken Dockerfile or lock now fails in the pull request, not at H0's preflight, where it burnt a release. The workflow has no `paths` filter, so its checks `image (glorfindel)` and `image (annatar)` can be required: its first step reads the pull request's diff and skips the build, green, when nothing that goes into an image changed (a push to main, or an unreadable diff, always builds). The cache is written by main's pushes only. A red run is announced in `#ci` (`notify.yml`), and a test requires every workflow run by a push, a pull request or a schedule to be in its list, exceptions written. `make images-check` builds and checks both images the same way, locally, tagged `:ci` (the `make build` images do not move). A test reads each Dockerfile's `COPY` lines and fails when the script's list of image paths (`IMAGE_PATHS`) misses one. A test reads every `docker run` of H0's preflight and requires its check here. Tests `tests/unit/test_image_check.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflows.py`, mutants M1140–M1149, M1480–M1489. Making the job required by branch protection is the maintainer's choice. Publishing the image (GHCR, provenance, H0 and Orodruin pulling it by digest) is step 2.
- **H0's CD proves that Glorfindel detects, and rolls back only on a regression** (H2, step 1, #266, 29/10; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le CD de H0 », `docs/operations.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`).
  - **`glorfindel rules check [--live] [--json]`**, a command of its own: every rule loaded as the watch loads it (the shipped base, the client's layer, the approved rules) is said bound or not — no workspace resolved, an inline workspace with no backend (the form of before P1: no VM), a static rule on no resource, a source no detector reads are errors. With `--live`, each bound rule's query runs **once**, read-only, on the detector and in the window the rule poller would use: `ok` (zero rows included); `error` only for what lasts, on explicit evidence (an invalid query or a table absent — a 400, Log Analytics' own words and codes —, a 403, a 404); `unproven` for what may pass (a 429, a 5xx, a 401 or a credential not available, the network or DNS, a partial result, anything else); `timeout`. The HTTP status is read by attribute, the SDK never imported (MC0). Each query is bounded (`--query-timeout`, 30 s), the whole too (`--total`, 120 s: the rules left are not run, and said); never a retry. No model, no write, no action. Exit 0, 1 (a lasting error) or 3 (unproven or timeouts only). `--json` gives each rule its kind, its code (`SEM0100`, `429`) and one reduced line without a workspace, a subscription, a resource path, a host name or an address; the full words go to stderr.
  - **The CD runs it twice**: in what runs, just before the switch (outside the candidate's health: the old release still serves; at most `EREGION_CD_RULES_S` + `EREGION_CD_RULES_MARGIN_S`, 105 s by default, written down with the switch for a cut pass), and in the candidate's watch, with its identity, once the three containers are healthy, within `EREGION_CD_HEALTH_S` — the containers are looked at once more after it. **Only a regression rolls back** (Jonathan's decision, 29/10): a rule in a lasting error after the switch that was ok, or absent, before; or a check that cannot load or be read where what ran could. It is said in `#deploy` with the rule's name, its kind and its code, never the error's words (the journal keeps them), escaped (SEC6). A rule broken before as after (a table the workspace lacks, a client rule) is said in orange with the mention; an unproven rule, a timeout, a reference without the command (« non comparé ») in orange, never a rollback. A release from before H2 (click's « No such command ») is kept, its rules said unverified; another code 2 is said in orange. `EREGION_CD_RULES_S` and `EREGION_CD_RULE_QUERY_S` are bounded to what the command accepts. The way back runs no rule check.
  - **The agent changes** (`eregion_cd.py`, and `chat_text.py` installed beside it): `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` once the first release carrying H2 is deployed — `#deploy` asks for it. Before it, `docker exec eregion-watch glorfindel rules check --live` on H0 shows the rules already in error.
  - Tests `tests/unit/test_rules_check.py` (a fake backend), `tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`; mutants M1120–M1139, M1500–M1519. Step 2 (a technique on the sentinel VM, with J2 #83) is not in it.

### Changed
- **`glorfindel restore` resolves a short VM name** like the other commands (SEC4c), and reads `--before` without an offset as UTC; an unreadable `--before` is refused.
- **The README says true**: the status is v0.4.3 (a test reads it against `pyproject.toml`), about 3,000 tests (a test bounds every count by the suite's size); the read-only evaluation is « read-only on Azure », with what still leaves the machine — the detection's rows, the investigation, past decisions, to the model's provider; a local model or `--model` to keep them in — and a new « Remove Eregion » (state, memory, images, Azure's identities and quarantine NSGs). Installing the console is `pip install -e '.[console]'` from the clone, in the README, `docs/operations.md` and the console's own messages. The release pull request updates the README's status with the version (`docs/operations.md`, « Packaging et version »).
- **`make celebrimbor-up` shows the plan and asks**, like the other Terraform targets; `YES=1` applies it unread. It passed `--auto-approve` unconditionally.

### Fixed
- **A characterized detection answered by a snapshot alone now leaves a card** (SEC4a, #275): the snapshot still runs on its own, and a card `uncontained_detection` (no action to approve) asks a human whether to contain — unless the VM is already isolated, or the detection's source already blocked, by an earlier cycle. It used to end with the snapshot's notification only.
- **A detection on a VM whose isolation is being lifted, or whose lifting failed halfway, leaves its card** (SEC4c): such an isolation is partial; the card says its state. It was read as contained.
- **The watch lists the VMs' private IPs once every 5 minutes**, for every `attack_started` line and the rule poller alike (SEC4c); each line listed them all again.
- **`verify_release` no longer says "released" for a NIC left bare whose original is unknown** (I3F, #287, 28/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Take an isolation by quarantine whose state and tags are lost. It is isolated again, so its original is now unknown (Q7), and then someone leaves the NIC with no NSG. `verify_release` read that as released (`verified: True`). `glorfindel release` trusts that check, so it then cleared the state that still held the NIC. The NIC was left without its NSG, and an older version's isolation stayed on that NSG. Now the check answers not verified, and `original_unknown` names the NIC. The release already kept the placement. It now says the NIC carries no NSG (it used to say the NIC "stays in quarantine") and how to get out: put the NSG the NIC must carry back on it, or set our tag. The invariant machine no longer exempts such a NIC when a state held it bare in quarantine, with no original, after the test last lost that state. Mutants M1340–M1342.
- **The invariant machine drives the escalations store's lock too** (I3F, #292, 29/10). Since SEC3, the store is rewritten under a lock that the machine's scheduler did not drive: in a race where both operations leave a card, one stopped at its write inside that lock left the other blocked in it, out of the scheduler's sight. That was I11's « op-2 never came back » (#292, not load), and the hang of the macOS job, where the teardown's sweep then waited behind it. `_file_lock` takes a `timeout`, as `_vm_lock`; the machine tries it without blocking, as the VM and quarantine locks. A regression replays the race (`tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`); mutant M1395. The macOS job is bounded to 25 min, and a test still running after 8 min writes every thread's stack to the log.
- **`unblock` no longer answers `not_found` while a block rule stays on Azure** (U3F, #293, 29/10; `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`). Take a block cut off before its state was written (a crash), then an isolation by quarantine, then someone takes our quarantine NSG off the NIC and leaves it with none. The block lies on the NIC's own NSG, away from the NIC. The unblock looked for it there only behind a quarantine, so it answered `not_found` and the IP stayed blocked; the release then put that NSG back, the block with it. Now a NIC left bare is looked up as the release does: the original the state records, else our tag on the region's quarantine NSG. The block found there is removed (`unblocked`). When a state holds the NIC in quarantine with no original and no tag names it (Q7), the unblock answers `unblock_partial` and names the NIC. Mutants M1343–M1345.
- **A race of the invariant machine that never comes back fails; it no longer hangs the job** (U3F). When I11 failed, the thread waiting for its turn stayed there with its locks, and the teardown that takes them next waited forever. The scheduler now abandons the race: each waiting thread unwinds (an exception that no `except Exception` of the code catches) and releases its locks, then the failure is raised. A deadlock between two locks the scheduler drives (each operation holding one and wanting the other) no longer runs forever: each gave its turn away at every try, so no turn ever timed out; past 2,000 turns in a row spent only on taken locks (a real race: 10 to 16, depending on the seed), it fails as a livelock, in under a second. A thread blocked out of the scheduler's sight fails the race after one turn's timeout (20 s); it cannot be unwound, so the failure names it, the machine's teardown skips the recovery that would wait behind its locks, and what comes next — the next example, a replay while shrinking — gets lock registries of its own and thread names of its own, sharing nothing with it. Still seen only by the job's bound: a thread that loops forever, giving its turn back or not. The Linux test job of the CI is bounded to 30 min (4–10 min usually) and writes every thread's stack when a test outlasts 8 min, as on macOS; the nightly invariants are bounded to 150 min (about an hour on Linux, 33–41 min on macOS — a manual run with more sequences stops there too, its input says so). Mutants M1346–M1349, M1440–M1442.
- **Tests no longer depend on when they run**: the five files that took `NOW` at import take it when each test starts, and the two tests that compared it with the clock of the code under test — the console's heartbeat, a window declared for later — no longer fail when the suite runs longer than a minute.

## [0.4.3] — 2026-10-28 — "held at the edges"

### Fixed
- **In read-only, the buttons that write are greyed again** (regression of 0.4.2; SEC1). The guard read the inline handlers #257 removed; it reads each button's action now, the modals' own buttons included. The actions themselves kept refusing.

### Security
- **The runs/ channel and Glorfindel's local state** (SEC3, #274, 28/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Le canal runs/ » ; `docs/fichiers-cles.md` ; `docs/operations.md`, « Montages »).
  - **An unreadable state file is never read as "no state".** While an isolation or block state file can't be read, the orphan sweep removes nothing and its cards say why (`unreadable_states`); it reads the state again just before a removal. `remove_orphan_rule` now goes through the connector's write guards like every other write: the subscription of the NSG, the lock of each VM the rule cuts.
  - **The watch takes from `runs/` only what the injectors emit** (`glorfindel/signal_origin.py`): `attack_started` and `detection_missed`, each with the fields an injector writes; any other line is refused — logged, counted in `~/.glorfindel/channel_refusals.json`, never decided. A run id must be a plain name.
  - **A `recovery_complete` releases an isolation on its own only when Glorfindel's own path emitted it**: built by `glorfindel restore --wait` in its process, or read by `glorfindel respond` from `~/.glorfindel/recovery_signals/` (no longer `runs/recovery/`). Otherwise the release is held for a human.
  - **The escalations store keeps every card under concurrent writers**: each read-modify-write holds an flock, and the store is rewritten whole (temporary file + `os.replace`); a webhook is posted after the lock. Measured between real processes (`tests/unit/test_locks.py`): no card lost, no truncated read.
  - **State files are owner-only** (0600, directories 0700), written whole: isolation and block state, the escalations store, the refusal counter, the recovery signals; an earlier version's isolation and block state is tightened when the watch starts. The other files of `~/.glorfindel` keep the umask for now.
  - **The injectors no longer mount `~/.glorfindel`**: Annatar's and Gothmog's containers (compose, `make annatar-*`) see `~/.glorfindel/injectors` only, read-only, where the watch writes a copy of its heartbeat; the watch (and `make glorfindel-start`) creates it. Annatar's watcher of Glorfindel's blocks, and the `attack_adapted` signal it emitted, are removed.
  - **`make annatar-simulate` goes through `glorfindel respond <file> --dry-run`**: the simulated signals are written to a temporary file, not `runs/`. Tests and mutants M1210–M1239 (M1224, an append under the lock, caught only at times).
- **Detection rules hardened against log lines Glorfindel did not write; addresses it never blocks** (SEC2, #273, 28/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/operations.md`).
  - `ssh-brute-force` reads the lines tagged sshd only (`ProcessName` `sshd` or `sshd-session`) and takes the peer address from sshd's own field at the end of the line. `sudo-privilege-escalation` reads the lines tagged sudo only, in `auth` or `authpriv`, in sudo's form (fields separated by « ; » or by spaces, as the agent writes them), a failure excluded. The investigative queries read the same lines (what the model sees around a detection changes). Thresholds unchanged. Annatar's SSH and sudo simulations now tag their lines as the daemons do (`logger -t`).
  - **`never_block`** (new key of `glorfindel-config.yaml`, read strictly): addresses never blocked, on top of the shipped ones (Azure's platform addresses, the private ranges, carrier NAT, IPv6 unique local, the loopback; an IPv4-mapped address is read as its IPv4). A private address is never blocked: lateral movement is answered by isolating the source VM. `decide` holds such a block (a `protected_address` card) and the Azure and AWS connectors refuse it, whatever the path; an unreadable list refuses every block.
  - One escalation card per address: two addresses on one VM are no longer folded into one card.
  - A proposed rule never takes a shipped rule's name (suffixed `-proposed`); `pending` and `approve-rule` say when an approval replaces a client rule; a client rule in a shipped rule's place, or turning one off, is said loudly (watch banner, `glorfindel rules`).
  - `interval_s` is at least 30 s (a rule asking less is polled at 30 s, said); `${VAR}` is expanded only in `workspace_id`, `resource_id`, `endpoint`.
  - Data-source rules are capped per poll like the control plane (5 cards, the gravest first, then one card per VM naming the 50 gravest of the rest and their number).
  - Tests run the shipped rules as written on in-memory lines (`tests/unit/kql_subset.py`). Mutants M1190–M1209, M1310–M1324.
- **The console's boundary: always a token, its own host and origin only** (SEC1, #272, 28/10; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Console — exposition »).
  - **A token always.** Without `EREGION_CONSOLE_TOKEN`, the first start draws one, keeps it in `~/.glorfindel/console_token` (mode 600) and prints the URL that opens the console once (`http://localhost:7007/?token=…`, in `docker compose logs war-room` too); later starts read it again. `make console-url` (new; called by `make glorfindel-start` and its neighbours) prints it from the environment or that file. No route answers without it but the token page. With the token set (H0), nothing changes.
  - **Its own host and origin.** The console answers under a loopback name, or a name listed in `EREGION_CONSOLE_HOSTS` (new, passed by compose), any port. A change (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, the live feed's WebSocket) from another origin is refused; a browser's change carries its origin.
  - **A change carries a JSON object** (`Content-Type: application/json`); the pages send theirs so, and encode every name in a path.
  - **The cookie is a session derived from the token**, never the token, named by the port the browser reached (`eregion_console_<port>`: two consoles of one host keep theirs); `Secure` over HTTPS (the tunnel on `http://localhost` still works). A browser asks for the token once after the upgrade.
  - **Headers.** The CSP adds `default-src 'self'`, `style-src`, `img-src`; `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; the API schema and its pages are no longer served. Config receives the Azure identifiers masked.
  - **Restore from the console is its card's**: the server refuses an anchored card, as the page and the bot did.
  - Checked in Chromium: no resource blocked, every button of Glorfindel's page works, the tunnel's URL opens the console. Mutants M1170–M1189 caught.

## [0.4.2] — 2026-10-28 — "the CD of H0, a console that runs only its own scripts"

### Added
- **The CD of H0: the release Jonathan publishes reaches H0 on its own** (H1, #244, 28/10; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Le CD de H0 »). A systemd timer on the VM runs `infra/glorfindel-host/cd/eregion_cd.py` every 5 minutes. The agent is installed in `~/.eregion-cd/`, never in the checkout it deploys, and never replaces itself.
  - **H0 stays closed.** It only goes out, to GitHub's public API (no token) and to #deploy's webhook. Publishing is the gesture: sessions prepare drafts (`CLAUDE.md`).
  - **Verified first.** The tag is on main and never older than what runs; every check run of its commit is completed, none red (a CI still running is waited for, and said once).
  - **A new lock needs the harness.** `make llm-compare … ATTEST=1` writes `requirements/harness.json`: the lock's sha256, the runs, and per model the integration, the judgment and the overconfident errors. The CD refuses a changed `requirements/glorfindel.txt` without an attestation for that lock and H0's model, complete and with no overconfident error.
  - **Built aside, preflighted, then switched.** The candidate `eregion-glorfindel:<tag>` is built in a worktree of its own. In it: `glorfindel --version` is the tag, the watch, the bot and the console import, H0's config passes A3's strict reading, compose renders. Then `deploy.sh --activate` switches.
  - **Health for 3 minutes, else back.** The three containers run, never restarted, on the tag; the watch's heartbeat is written after the switch; the console answers.
    - Unhealthy: back to what ran by a retag; the release is marked and never tried again on its own (`make glorfindel-host-cd-retry`).
    - Before switching, the running image is tagged by its reference (one built before H1 had none). Without an image to go back to, there is no switch.
    - The old images are pruned only once the health is green, keeping the new one and the one before.
    - A switch is written down before it happens: a pass cut after it (a reboot, a timeout) is taken up by the next one, read against what runs.
      - The release running: its health, then kept or back.
      - Still what ran, or the watch gone (cut before or during the containers' move): H0 put back in one piece, the switch « interrupted », never called sick, tried again — refused, with the mention, at the second interruption.
      - Something else (deployed by hand meanwhile): left alone.
    - A tag seen before on another commit is refused.
  - **Said in #deploy.** The start and the success silently; a refusal, a rollback and a failed rollback mention Jonathan; a release carrying another agent says to reinstall it. A refusal's detail (A3's words, compose's output) stays in the journal: it may name the infrastructure.
  - **Installed disabled.** `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` (sudo on the VM; the #deploy webhook and the mention by stdin, never the bot's token), then `-cd-check` (a dry run: built and preflighted, never switched), then `-cd-enable`; also `-cd-status`, `-cd-retry`, `-cd-disable`, `-cd-uninstall`.
  - **Tests and mutants.** The agent runs installed, as on H0, against real git, a fake docker, a fake GitHub and Discord (`tests/unit/test_host_cd.py`). Mutants M1002–M1019 and M1050–M1052, 21/21 caught.

### Changed
- **`deploy.sh` builds aside and switches by a retag** (H1). `--build-only REF` builds `eregion-glorfindel:REF` from the CD's worktree without touching what runs. `--activate REF` checks out, retags and restarts, without building. Every image is also tagged by its reference. `--prune REF…` keeps the three newest and every one named; `--activate` never prunes, and a deployment by hand keeps the image it replaces. So going back is a retag. `--activate` tolerates a failed `git fetch`: the rollback must not depend on the network. A tag rewritten since its image was built: the image's own commit is checked out instead, and said — never one commit's compose and rules beside another's image. One deployment at a time: by hand, it is refused while the CD's pass runs (`~/.eregion-deploy.lock`).
- **`#agents` is what waits for Jonathan, and nothing else: one board, pings that delete themselves, links** (N3, #262, 28/10). The first real elrond run with N1 (I1S, I4S, I8S, H1) gave 10 alerts in `#agents` and one was right. A reviewer waiting for its mutants ends its turn, so `SubagentStop` fired, and the pause was taken for a return. The report was read from the transcript, where the `SubagentHandback` is not yet written when `SubagentStop` runs: « sans verdict » five times (both `blocking` reviews were never said as such), and once the previous verdict (an `ok` said `changes`). Corrections and second reviews, which elrond sends by `SendMessage` to the agent that already ran, were seen by no hook (« Rien ne tourne » during a review). And no alert had a link.
  - **elrond's part moves to `scripts/agents_discord.py`.** `notify_discord.py` keeps GitHub to `#ci` and `#lots`.
  - **The report comes from `SubagentHandback`'s `PreToolUse` (`tool_input.message`),** as Claude Code documents it. A pause (`background_tasks`) is nothing. A resume by `SendMessage` is followed. A fallback counts only in the stretch it happened in.
  - **`#agents` holds one board, rewritten in place** (N1's pinned « En cours » message becomes it): 🙋 what waits, 🔄 running, ✅ returned lately, 🏁 the last run, each line with its links (the lot's thread, the pull request, the issue, the report).
  - **One ping per thing that waits, deleted once settled:**
    - a lot blocked, or a review not obtained;
    - the elrond session waiting for a permission (`Notification` `permission_prompt`) or for an answer (`AskUserQuestion` left unanswered for 30 s);
    - a pull request to merge by hand (`agents_discord.py lot <ID> merge --pr <n>`, settled when GitHub says it is merged — asked at each event, and by a detached watcher every five minutes while one waits, two days at most: the merge often comes after the run);
    - the end of a run.
  - **No longer pings:** `changes` / `blocking` (elrond fixes alone), a worker's fallback, a return.
  - **Each worker's and reviewer's report lands in the lot's thread:** the card holds a report of up to 1500 characters, or its head; a longer one is attached whole as a `.md` (median 7.8 KB, measured on 28/10; cut beyond 1 MB). A fallback in the reviewer's last turns, reaching the transcript after its hand-back was read, is said at its stop.
  - **The hooks only queue the event and return;** one process delivers the queue in order. `PreToolUse` and `SubagentStop` are synchronous, so a hand-back is queued before its `SubagentStop`, and that one before elrond can resume the agent; an agent that hands back is never read from its stop. A failing `#lots` never keeps a ping from Jonathan; a ping whose deletion fails is tried again. A board re-created after being deleted is no longer pinned (a webhook can't pin).
  - **After merging, run `bash scripts/elrond-hooks.sh install` again.**

  To measure on a real run: the payloads, from Claude Code's documentation (the auto-mode classifier refused a measuring hook). `~/.local/state/eregion/discord.trace` keeps one line per event, its fields' names only. Tests: `tests/unit/test_agents_discord.py` (I8S's sequence of 28/10 replayed); mutants M1060–M1076 (M1070–M1076 after the two 5.5 reviews), and M978, M979, M990 and M994 moved with the code they bite.

### Fixed
- **The CD of H0 asks to reinstall its agent only for a release newer than it** (H1b, #263, 28/10, after the first real pass to v0.4.1). `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` notes the commit it installs from (`~/.eregion-cd/installed-from`, a full commit id). The « 🔧 Agent de CD à mettre à jour » message needs a release that descends from it and carries other agent files. v0.4.1 had no H1 and asked anyway: installing from it would have broken the CD, its `deploy.sh` having no `--activate`. An older release says nothing in #deploy (a line in the journal). An installation without a valid `installed-from`, or installed off main, says once, without the mention, that it cannot tell when to update. Mutants M1053–M1056.
- **The small follow-ups of the isolation reviews of 28/10** (ISU, #264; after I1S, I8S and I4S).
  - **An unblock behind the quarantine follows the release's rule for the original.** For a NIC in quarantine, the state's « none » yields to our tag when it names an NSG (`_tag_over_none`); a tag that can't be read is `unblock_partial`, never « none » on a guess. Before: two hosts, host 2's block on X answered `not_found`, and the IP came back blocked with X at the release.
  - **An original NSG gone says how to get out.** A release or a reset whose NIC's original NSG no longer exists still fails and keeps the NIC as it is. It now says how to get out: recreate it (same group, same name), or put by hand on the NIC the NSG it must carry, then run again. When the original comes from our tag (no local state, or the state's « none »), it also offers setting the tag `glorfindel-orig-<hash>=none` on the quarantine NSG. The NSG is read again by its id: only a 404 gives this message, never a 403 or a 429.
  - **A quarantine card of another subscription is said once.** It is filtered before being read again (`outside_subscription`), kept, and said once per NSG in the watch's log. Before, a warning was written at every pass, without end. A refused NSG (403) of this subscription is still said at every pass. These messages name the NSG in its own case, not by the lowercase key of an acknowledgement.
  - **A plain-named block on a NIC's NSG that is also its subnet's** is said « d'une ancienne version ou de périmètre » when the NSG's sharing can't be read.
  - Docs: `docs/azure.md` (a stale X in another quarantine group can win over a correct « none » for a bare NIC: marginal, on the closed side); `docs/design/preuves.md` (M1020 caught by the machine alone only from a few hundred sequences: 1/6 at 60, 4/4 at 200 — a remeasure: 2/3 — and 4/4 at 500, the safe budget). Tests on the fake Azure; mutants M1100–M1115, 16/16 caught.

### Security
- **The console runs no script but its own files** (#257, 28/10; with S1–S8 of #256's review). The CSP gains `script-src 'self'; object-src 'none'`: an inline script, an `on…` handler, a `javascript:` URL or `eval` no longer runs, even where escaping would miss it. The pages' scripts are files (`glorfindel.js`, `overview.js`, `injector.js`), moved unchanged in a commit of their own. The 95 inline handlers of the Glorfindel page are gone: an element names its action (`_on` writes `data-on-click="showEscModal"` and its arguments as JSON, escaped), and one listener per event, on the document in the capture phase, calls it from `ACTIONS`, a closed table; the nearest element takes the event, as `stopPropagation()` did. `_js` is gone. The reader (`scripts/console_escaping.py`) refuses an inline handler or script, `javascript:`, `srcdoc`, and the APIs it does not follow (`document.write`, `setAttribute` of a handler or a URL, `.href =`, `el['innerHTML']`, `eval`, a string to `setTimeout`…); it reads a concatenation like a template; a `.length` is a number only on what the page built (from the server: `Number(…)`); the start of a URL includes a leading `/` (the overview linked a section by `/${esc(id)}`); it counts the `${…}` it reads, compared apart. Checked in Chromium on a local console seeded with payloads, `main` alongside: every element declares the same call with the same arguments (89/89), drag and drop and the modal behave as before, no CSP violation from the pages, no JS error; a forced `<img onerror>` runs on `main` and is blocked here. After the 5.5 review: an action is always a name the page wrote (a `data-on-*` value takes a literal only; `setAttribute`/`dataset` of a `data-on-*` refused); what an injected element could still make the page do is bounded — arguments are strings, numbers, flags or null, never an object (an approval's scope window would be skipped), and a mode is read only from the page's own `<select>`; the reader also refuses `setAttributeNS`, a navigation (`location.assign`, `location =`), a timer given what may be a string and a `<meta>` written by a script, follows a name bound again (`[a] = …`, `for (a of …)`), reads a const string before a value as text, and an `<object data>` as a URL. A click outside the capability popover now always closes it. Mutants M1080–M1099, M1290–M1299.

## [0.4.1] — 2026-10-28 — "nothing raw in the console, three loose ends of the isolation path tied"

### Fixed
- **A reset no longer removes the older isolation of a VM in quarantine from the NSG it left** (I4S, #239, 28/10; the symmetric case of I4F). An older version's isolation of `api` — its fixed names (`glorfindel-isolation-deny-all`, any/any), its state lost — stays on `api`'s own NSG. `api` is then isolated by quarantine, so its NIC moves away, and a new VM is attached to that NSG, alone there now. Counted from the NICs attached, the NSG read as the new VM's; `glorfindel reset --from-azure` (or a release) of the new VM took the fixed names for its own and deleted `api`'s older isolation. No flow opened, since the quarantine holds `api`; but had its NIC come back to its NSG outside Glorfindel (an ARM or Bicep redeploy), the rule would have been gone. A NIC of another VM away from the NSG — in our quarantine, or left bare — now counts among the NSG's NICs when our quarantine tag (`glorfindel-orig-<hash>`) names that NSG as its original, or, the tag lost, its VM's local state does. A stale tag (a NIC deleted since, or given back an NSG outside Glorfindel) ties nothing, so a VM really alone on its NSG still removes its fixed names. The same holds for an older block under the attacker's address alone (`glorfindel-block-<ip>`): the unblock of the new VM took it too. When the NSG, or the subscription's NSGs or NICs, cannot be read (403, 429), whose the fixed names are is unknown: they are left, and the report says so — for the isolation names, `swept_partial` / `release_partial` (« NSG … au partage illisible : isolation d'une ancienne version »); for the plain block name, `unblock_partial` (« … blocage d'une ancienne version … laissée »), where the unblock used to answer `not_found` while the IP stayed blocked. These reads happen only on an NSG that holds a fixed name and governs a single NIC, but there on every read of the VM's NICs, `verify_isolation` included (`docs/azure.md`). Limit: a NIC whose original nothing records any more (tags and state lost) ties no NSG. The invariant machine now also reads I4 from the test's journal: an older version's isolation is never deleted by an operation on another VM (`_old_isolations_kept`), with a composite rule that draws the case (`an_old_isolations_vm_goes_away`). Regressions in `test_invariant_regressions.py`, mutants M1030–M1039.
- **Two Glorfindel hosts with separate states: a stale « none » no longer leaves a NIC without its NSG** (I8S, #245, 28/10; follows I8F). Host 1 isolates a bare NIC (state and tag: « none »); someone takes our NSG off, the customer then gives the NIC an NSG X; host 2 isolates it (tag: X); our NSG taken off again; host 1 isolates it again and releases it. The release followed host 1's own « none »: with a 429 at 10 of the 26 calls of that re-isolation, it said `released` and left the NIC with no NSG, our tag X deleted. Now, on a NIC whose local state says it had no NSG, **our tag wins when it names an NSG** — at the release, in `reset --from-azure` (NIC in quarantine or bare) and in `verify_release`, which said released while X was off the NIC; and when the two hosts use different quarantine groups, a tag naming an NSG wins over a « none » found first in another group. No tag, or our quarantine NSG gone: the state's « none » stands (a NIC bare by design whose tags were lost is released bare, as before). A tag that can't be read fails the release (`release_partial`, `swept_partial`), never « none » on a guess, and the NIC is never put back bare on it (the reset recorded the « none » before reading the tag: found by the 5.5 review); the re-isolation no longer writes the state's « none » over a tag a 429 kept it from reading. Option (a) of the issue: (b), an unreadable or missing tag always taken as unknown, would keep in quarantine every NIC bare by design whose tags were lost. Not changed: the unblock still reads the state's « none » to find a NIC's own NSG behind the quarantine (`unblock.py`, a follow-up). Tests: `test_two_hosts_a_stale_none_never_leaves_the_nic_bare` (259 frozen cases, two state directories, I1 and I2 checked after each lift; 189 fail on 0.4.0, 10 of the issue's 26); mutants M1040–M1049. Docs: `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`.
- **A quarantine card no longer closes on its NSG's mere absence from the listing** (I1S, #231, 28/10, found by the third and fourth reviews of I1F). The reassertion closed a `quarantine_altered` card — and forgot its change in `quarantine_alerts.json` — as soon as its NSG was missing from the subscription's listing, while the NSG could still be altered and hold NICs: Glorfindel's role removed or expired (PIM) on its group, which ARM would leave out of a subscription-wide listing without an error (a hypothesis, to measure on the bench), our `managed-by` tag taken off as well as our rules, or a card of another subscription (`AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID` changed with a shared `~/.glorfindel`). Silence until the rights came back. Now the NSG of a card, or of an acknowledged change, that the listing doesn't show is read again by its id (`read_quarantine`, a `get` for those alone — never every NSG at every pass): not found (404) closes it, any other error (403, 429…) keeps it, found is judged on its content **with or without our tag** (the card was raised on our NSG at that id; requiring its NICs to be named by our states would miss I1F's own case, a state lost), and an NSG of another subscription is never read here and keeps its card. The fake Azure can leave an NSG out of a listing without an error and refuse any call on it (`unreadable`, written as a hypothesis); the invariant machine draws it, and our tag taken off, after L2 held (`_out_of_sight`). Mutants M1020–M1026.

### Security
- **The console writes nothing from the outside raw into its pages** (#254, 28/10; the known issue of 0.4.0). A card, a rule, a feed entry, an error — a resource's or an Entra application's name from Azure, the model's text, a rule's query, a run report pulled from the exercise channel — written raw into `innerHTML` ran in the operator's console and could call its routes (#250 fixed one card's detail; the CSP of #222 has no `script-src`). Now escaped everywhere it lands: the live feed, a rule's window and rows, the decisions, the cards' titles (the `|| e.action` fallback), the coverage window, the NSG, backup and cluster tiles, the configuration, the overview's indicators. `_attr` takes any value (`String(s ?? '')`) and escapes `'` and `>` too; an argument of an `on…` attribute goes through `_js` (a JSON string, then escaped for the attribute — the browser decodes `&#39;` before the handler runs), never between quotes: 74 handlers. `scripts/console_escaping.py` reads each page's scripts and follows what every `${…}` of an HTML template and every value given to innerHTML can hold — through ternaries, `||`, variables and their assignments, a `Map`, a page function's returns, a parameter's calls and a callback's array — and `tests/unit/test_console_escaping.py` fails on any unescaped value in an HTML template's `${…}` or in a value given to innerHTML (string concatenations, `document.write`, `setAttribute` and `srcdoc` are not followed yet — none is used in the pages), on the start of a URL escaped only for HTML, or on an attribute not in double quotes; it holds the reader to small pages too. Checked in Chromium on a local console seeded with payloads: on `main`, 14 injected images and payloads run on load and on click; after, none, no JS error, every handler's argument intact. Mutants M580–M599.

## [0.4.0] — 2026-10-27 — "the isolation path, held to its invariants"

### Added
- **The console shows what Glorfindel closed itself** (#248, 27/10, Jonathan's choice): `/api/state` gives `handled`, the cards resolved with a note in the last 24 h — today an orphan rule A3 took off alone, a write on Azure no open card says. A green line on the VM's or the NSG's tile, the note on hover; a card a human acknowledged carries no note and is never shown. Limit: a card Glorfindel closes because the rule is no longer an orphan (« retirée, ou sa carte est revenue » — often a human who ran the card's command) shows too: the note says which. Mutants M574, M575.
- **The road to 1.0 in Discord** (N2, #243, 27/10; `docs/atelier-discord.md`). `scripts/roadmap_discord.py`, run by `.github/workflows/roadmap.yml` on every push to main, every day, and when an issue or a pull request opens or closes.
  - **`#roadmap`**: one message of the Eregion bot, rewritten in place: a bar per milestone (its issues closed over all), the lots in progress (an open pull request of the repository, never a fork's, or elrond's state), reviewed, blocked and still to do, the 1.0 criteria and the five indicators, from `ROADMAP.md`. The bot finds its own message among the pins, then the last 50 messages, and never takes someone else's for it. A `ROADMAP.md` that changed shape loses that section, and the message says so; the message stays under Discord's limits.
  - **`#lots`**: a thread per open lot of the four milestones — an issue whose title starts with its lot id (Jonathan, 27/10); follow-up issues and findings have none —, « `<ID> · <title> · #<n>` », created when missing, with its milestone and state tags. « fait » stays while a merged lot's issue is still open, unless it was reopened; a thread Discord archived for idleness stays archived unless it must change. A closed issue gets no thread, and its existing one turns « fait » and is archived. elrond's finer states are kept, and a thread elrond made is found and renamed. Two issues of one lot id (I1F, #226 and #231) get a thread each: N1's thread matching now looks for the issue number first.
  - **Configuration and tests.** One more secret, `EREGION_DISCORD_ROADMAP_CHANNEL`, and no webhook. The roadmap and N1's notifications each run one at a time, in their own `concurrency` group (GitHub keeps one pending run per group: a roadmap run never cancels a notification), and the roadmap creates threads only on a push, the daily run or by hand — on an issue or pull request event, N1 creates the lot's thread. The roadmap follows only pull requests to main. When its message cannot be looked up, nothing is posted rather than a second, stale one. `--dry-run` prints the message and the forum's changes without writing. Tests run against a fake Discord and GitHub, now shared with N1 (`tests/unit/fake_discord.py`); mutants M980–M989, and M995–M999 and M1000–M1001 for the fixes of its two reviews: 17/17 caught.
- **The workshop's news in Discord** (N1, #242, 27/10; `docs/atelier-discord.md`). It sits apart from the defense: Glorfindel's bot, its cards and its channel do not change.
  - **`#ci`**: from `.github/workflows/notify.yml`. A pull request opened or merged, a closed issue, main green again after red: silent. A red run (pull request, main, nightly) and a published release notify. The workflow never checks out nor runs a pull request's code.
  - **`#lots`**: a forum with one thread per lot, found by its id (elrond's branch `lot/<id>-…`, the title, or the issue number), archived threads included. Every event of the lot lands there, silent. A GitHub event creates a thread only for a known lot; a fork's pull request reaches `#ci` only. A dedicated bot, « Eregion », keeps the thread's milestone and state tags true; it is never Glorfindel's bot, and its token never goes to H0.
  - **`#agents`**: one « En cours » message rewritten in place, plus what needs Jonathan, mentioning him: a model fallback seen by palantir, a review with `changes` or `blocking`, without a readable verdict or not obtained, a blocked lot, the end of a run.
  - **elrond's lots are followed by user-level hooks**, never the repository's (`bash scripts/elrond-hooks.sh install`, now with `notify_discord.py hook` on `PreToolUse` for `Agent` and on `SubagentStop`). Launch, return, verdict and fallback need no call from the model. The review's verdict is read from the report the reviewer hands back through the `SubagentHandback` tool, in its transcript (measured on the real transcripts); elrond itself sends only `lot <ID> blocked` and `run-end`. Every hook command ends in `|| true`: exit 2 from a hook would block the tool or hold the agent.
  - **`scripts/notify_discord.py` never breaks its caller.** Nothing configured: nothing sent, exit 0. A Discord error is one line on stderr, without the URL or the token. `allowed_mentions` is empty on every message, so a title with « @everyone » stays text. The hook returns at once and delivers from a detached process.
  - **Configuration** comes from the environment, or from `~/.config/eregion/discord.env`: `EREGION_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_CI` and `EREGION_DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` as GitHub secrets, `EREGION_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_AGENTS` on the operator's machine. No URL, token or id is in the repository; `host_env.py` copies none of them to H0.
  - **Tests and mutants.** Tests run against a fake Discord on the loopback (`tests/unit/test_notify_discord.py`); mutants M970–M979, and M990–M994 for the fixes of its review (verdict, forks, HTTP errors, a blocked lot without configuration): 15/15 caught.
- **Entra ID's logs as a detection source, detection only — the defensive half of C2** (#199, 27/10; stacked on AL1). A source `azure_entra` (`glorfindel/providers/azure/entra.py`, `plane = "identity"`) reads `AuditLogs` and `AADServicePrincipalSignInLogs` from the same workspace (backend `azure_monitor`). Four shipped rules, each with its thresholds and exclusions: a credential added to an application or a service principal (`entra-credential-added`, T1098.001 — secret, certificate, federated credential; a removal is no finding), a consent to an application (`entra-app-consent`, T1528 — delegated or application, admin consent said), a privileged directory role assigned (`entra-privileged-role-assigned`, T1098.003 — permanent, eligible or scoped; PIM activations left out), a service principal's successful sign-in from an address never seen in its 14-day baseline (`entra-sp-unusual-signin`, T1078.004). The adapter applies in code what a query cannot hold alone, tested on fake logs: failures dropped, a role kept only if Entra marks it privileged (`PRIVILEGED_ROLES`, by display name; a role whose name the row lacks is kept), a sign-in kept only for a principal seen on at least 3 days of its baseline. **Nothing is dropped under Glorfindel's identity** (it writes nothing in Entra ID; its usual sign-ins are left out by the baseline, like anyone's): as the actor (`GlorfindelIdentity`) or the target (`GlorfindelManaged`, a credential added to its service principal) a finding is critical, its card first; a customer's identity is never silenced nor marked by it — a name is no proof. **An identity detection is decided by the code, never by the model** (`_identity_decision`, on `context.plane`, the source — `respond` too — or, in depth, the row's Entra columns or table): no `investigate`, no incident, no decision memory — **no UPN reaches the model's provider**, on that cycle or later through `past_cycles` —, no action recommended; escalation `identity_change` whose reason says what, who, on which identity, the grave marks and the severity, and whose **steps say how to check and how to revoke** (remove the credential, revoke the grant, remove the role assignment, block the principal's sign-in — commands for a human). A second lock: the route after `decide` never sends an identity detection to an action, and `escalate_to_human` always types it `identity_change` (never approvable in one click, never folded, no button nor command in Discord). One card per finding (`EventId`), capped per poll with a summary card; an identity rule not projecting `EventId`, or another source reading an Entra table, is said at load. The tables reach the workspace only through a **diagnostic setting on the tenant**: `infra/terraform/entra_logs.tf` proposes it, disabled (`AuditLogs`, `ServicePrincipalSignInLogs` only; disabled, nothing of the tenant is read). Hypotheses to measure on the bench with Jonathan: operation names and the shape of `TargetResources`, latency, licence (sign-in logs in a workspace need Entra ID P1 or P2). After the 5.5 review: under `az login` the token's oid is the operator's, so in Entra ID only an application's token marks Glorfindel (`app_identities`; the Activity Log keeps the oid); a rule of another source reading an Entra table carries `plane: identity` from its query, whatever it projects (the RulePoller and `respond`); the role rule no longer drops an assignment without `Role.DisplayName` (fallback: the role's target; `Company Administrator` added, hypothesis); a first credential's old value is an empty array; a consent card proposes to block only the client application, never the resource's principal; an identity card, or a control-plane one the RulePoller says `unattributed`, is always `real`, never an exercise (a `respond` signal on the Activity Log, with no attribution, keeps its declared window). Out of this lot: the attack side (a public library's technique from Orodruin, R2) and the inscription of an identity. Tests: `tests/unit/test_entra_detection.py`; mutants M920–M949, M955. Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/azure.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`.
- **Azure's Activity Log as a detection source, detection only** (AL1, #225, 27/10; detached from R2, a prerequisite of C2 and C3). A source `azure_activity` (`glorfindel/providers/azure/activity.py`) reads `AzureActivity` from the same Log Analytics workspace as Perf and Syslog: a rule `source: azure_activity` binds to the `azure_monitor` backend. Three shipped rules, looking back one hour: an NSG or one of its rules written or deleted (`nsg-change-outside-glorfindel`, T1562.007), a role assigned (`role-assignment-write`, T1098.003), a disk snapshot or download access to a disk or a snapshot (`snapshot-by-unexpected-caller`, T1578.001); one row per operation, failures left out. **Glorfindel's own writes** (isolation, quarantine, blocks, release) are dropped by the adapter: its identity — read from its token's claims, `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` and `GLORFINDEL_OWN_CALLERS`, never written in code; from an application's token its `appid`/`azp` and `oid`, from a user's (`az login`) its `oid` only, since there `appid` is the Azure CLI's, the same for the whole tenant (said once) — doing an operation its capabilities declare, **on a target that is its own**: a quarantine NSG, or what an isolation or block state records — the rules it placed (a `glorfindel-*` name on a customer's NSG is no proof: no state names it, the row is kept, critical), a customer rule whose priority it moved, a NIC it put in quarantine; keyed with the state's subscription, remembered two hours after the state is gone. Its identity doing anything else (assigning a role, writing a customer's rule) is kept, marked `GlorfindelIdentity`, `critical`; anyone else writing one of Glorfindel's rules (`glorfindel-*`, its quarantine NSGs) is marked `GlorfindelManaged`, `critical`, its card first. **Each operation has its card** (control-plane rules only): up to five per rule and poll, the gravest first, then one summary card naming every operation left — never a row marked sent without a card; data-plane rules keep one card per cycle. No identity known: nothing is dropped. `normalize_row` gives a control-plane row its operation, target, `caller`, `caller_ip` and both marks; every other row keeps its three keys. The RulePoller deduplicates an operation by its correlation id, operation and target: a row arriving late is a new detection, an operation's Start and Success lines are one; a row on a VM's sub-resource (a role scoped on the VM) is attributed to that VM. A control-plane rule looking back less than 30 minutes, or not projecting `CorrelationId` (two operations at the same instant would fold into one), is said at load. **A control-plane detection is decided by the code, never by the model** (`_control_plane_decision`, on `context.plane` or the source — `respond` too — and, in depth, on the row's `OperationNameValue`: an AzureActivity row read by a rule of another source): no `investigate` on the anchor VM, no call to the model (it would only add text, cost, and send an administrator's name to the provider), **no action recommended**; escalation `control_plane_change` whose reason gives the operation, its status as read (an operation seen at `Start` may fail afterwards: said), the caller, its address, the target, the grave marks and the severity; never approvable in one click, never folded into the previous card, no button nor command on the VM in Discord (nor on `unattributed_signal`). An unattributed control-plane signal opens no incident on its anchor VM, and no control-plane cycle enters the decision memory (ChromaDB). `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, the few-shot, `_build_user_message` and the data-plane path are unchanged. The `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` rule rests on a **hypothesis, to measure on the bench**: Azure Backup (Glorfindel's snapshot, a `glorfindel restore`) may produce `disks/beginGetAccess` or `snapshots/write`. `AzureActivity` is **not** in the workspace by default: a diagnostic setting on the subscription sends it there — `infra/terraform/activity_log.tf` proposes it for the bench, disabled (category Administrative only — `Security` is read by no rule; disabled, not even the subscription is read). The replay of a real window of the bench, read only, is left to do with Jonathan. Tests: `tests/unit/test_activity_logs.py` (a fake AzureActivity, no Azure, no LLM); mutants M810–M829, M850–M859, M890–M894. Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/azure.md`, `docs/operations.md`.
- **The analysis AI triages a campaign, with its own memory and model, and nothing applies without a human** (IA1, #88, 27/10, code side). `python -m analysis.triage runs/campaigns/<id>` writes `triage.md` and `triage.json`: what needs a human, computed by the code and sorted by a fixed priority — a missed technique first, then an unknown outcome, an escalation, a scenario in error, a detection slower than the catalog expects. Each item gathers what the code read: the runs (as the report reads them), the technique catalog (checked against its schema when `jsonschema` is there; an invalid one is not believed), Glorfindel's rules (the shipped base and the client's layer over it, read only) and the infrastructure code (is the technique's log source collected? Terraform read without its comments, the evidence cited as `file:line`; a source or a code it cannot read is « unknown », never « not collected »); and the causes of a miss (not in the catalog, no enabled rule, source not collected, collection unknown, or a rule and its source both there). The analysis model explains each item and proposes one of a closed set (`author_rule`, `review_rule`, `check_collection`, `review_decision`, `rerun`); the check removes an entry that names no item, a second one, a proposal the computed facts do not support (`author_rule` while a rule watches the technique…), anything that carries a rule (a KQL query, a rule in YAML), and on an unknown decision an explanation saying Glorfindel acted or did not. Every kept proposal is written `pending` by the code, whatever the model wrote; `python -m analysis.triage decide <campaign> <tri-N> approve|reject --by <name>` records a named human's decision, once, and nothing else — an approved proposal is work for a human, and a triage holding a decision is never written over. The analysis never uses Glorfindel's model nor its memory (`analysis/separation.py`): its model (`EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL`) is refused when it is Glorfindel's decision model, compared without case or provider prefix, and its index (`EREGION_ANALYSIS_INDEX`) when it lies in `~/.glorfindel`, path resolved — for the campaign report too; no module of `analysis/` imports Glorfindel or a way to run a command (a test reads the code). Five analysis cases for the harness (`analysis/triage_cases.py`: a miss without a rule, one whose source is collected only in a comment, one with rule and source, an escalation sorted after a miss, a planted line asking for a rule and an approval), run against the real model by `python scripts/analysis_eval.py` (Jonathan) and in the tests with a simulated model. After the review: the triage reads the client's layer as the watch does — an entry the watch skips (no query, `enabled` not a boolean, `interval_s: 30s`, a `ttp` or `name` not a string) no longer replaces the base rule, so a technique the watch still watches is never « no rule » (`tests/unit/test_analysis_rules_parity.py` compares with the watch's own loader on a table of layers); `decide` and the writing of a triage take a `flock` on `runs/campaigns/<id>/.triage.lock` — two decisions at once are both kept (tested across processes); an unreadable `triage.json` (not JSON, an unknown key, kind or status) is refused in one line, exit 1, nothing written and no model called; no turn of the model's prose is filtered (a regex on prose removed legitimate proposals such as « doit être validée par un humain »): what counts is the status the code wrote in `triage.json` — `pending`, or the decision `decide` recorded with the human's name — shown in bold beside each proposal in `triage.md`, whose header says that no decision is taken by the model; the model's text is quoted as its text, on one line (whatever ends a line — `\r` alone, `\r\n`, U+2028… — becomes a space), its Markdown escaped so that it cannot draw a status of its own (tested through a real CommonMark engine, markdown-it, on a table of hostile entries); what the check removed is shown on one line each, its reason written by the code and never quoting the model, escaped too; a new triage written while a decision is taken no longer loses it (the save takes the lock too); Annatar's container receives `GLORFINDEL_LLM_MODEL` (the end of `campaign run` writes its report there, and the refusal compared with the default); `host_env.py` copies `EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL` to H0's `host.env`, and `make glorfindel-host-model` says when the model chosen for Glorfindel is the analysis's (`python -m analysis.separation` in the watch exits 2: every report would be refused; 1 is an error, said apart; the target never fails on it); a refusal says what to set first, so the cut message of `campaign run` keeps it. Tests: `tests/unit/test_analysis_separation.py`, `tests/unit/test_analysis_triage.py`, `tests/unit/test_analysis_rules_parity.py`; mutants M780–M799, M840–M842, M844, M871–M878. Docs: `docs/analysis.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`, `docs/operations.md`, `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`.
- **Usage profiles, and each component runs alone in CI** (D1, #81, 27/10). Glorfindel is in every profile. The profiles are combinations, each with a page in `docs/profils/`:
  - Glorfindel alone, to discover in observation (`make profile-discover`);
  - Glorfindel and Annatar, « does my detection work? » (`make profile-detection`);
  - Glorfindel and Gothmog, « does my infrastructure hold? » (`make profile-resilience`);
  - everything, like H0 (`make profile-proven`);
  - one Glorfindel per team (`make team-init` and `make team-start TEAM=<name>`).

  **docker compose.** It gains two profiles, `annatar` and `gothmog`, for the injectors (`docker compose --profile annatar run --rm annatar …`):
  - each depends on the watch, reads its heartbeat in `~/.glorfindel` read-only, and shares its `runs/`;
  - each gets its own service principal (`ANNATAR_AZURE_CLIENT_*`), never Glorfindel's (block `x-injector`);
  - Orodruin's contract stays empty, so on a workstation an injector stays passive.

  **One instance per team.** Four variables name it: `EREGION_INSTANCE`, `EREGION_CONSOLE_PORT`, `EREGION_RUNS` and `EREGION_CONFIG`. Left empty, the file renders as before, the names H0 reads included (checked with `docker compose config`).
  - A team keeps all its state in `~/.eregion/teams/<name>/`: `team.env` (from `team.env.example`), its config, its runs, and the `HOME` compose reads.
  - `make team-start` runs compose under `env -i`, so the operator's shell (their subscription, their keys) never reaches a team. It refuses a team with no subscription or no console port.

  **The signal contract moved to the shared core.** It was `annatar/signals/schema.py` and is now `eregion_core/signals.py`. Glorfindel imported it from Annatar, so it could not run without Annatar's package.

  **Each component runs alone.** `scripts/component_check.py` imports and tests Glorfindel, Annatar, Gothmog and the core with the other components absent from the interpreter, and Glorfindel's dependencies absent for an injector. The CI does the same (`.github/workflows/components.yml`, which also runs `make profiles-check`), and so does `make components-check`.
  - Only the crossings listed with their reason are allowed. `tests/unit/test_components.py` refuses any other import of one component by another, and every test file must belong to a component or to `TOGETHER`.
  - The analysis AI (`analysis/`) is scanned too: the watch loads it (IA0). The campaign report reads a run's detections with Annatar's reader on load, a listed bridge, so a Glorfindel installed without Annatar starts with the campaign reports off and says so once: the watch imports the report before it starts their thread (M870).
  - `tests/unit/conftest.py` skips the fixtures of an absent component; a test checks that in the full suite none is skipped.
  - The profile targets create, as the operator, the directories the services mount (`~/.annatar` too): left to docker, they would be root's. `depends_on` starts the watch without waiting for its heartbeat, so in the first minute an injector may refuse (V1), as the pages now say. `team.env.example` carries the two channels, empty.
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` now opens on the adapters as the way in for contributors.

  Tests: `tests/unit/test_components.py`, `tests/unit/test_profiles.py` (the team targets run against a fake `docker`), `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`. Mutants M800–M809, M845–M849, M870. The harness no longer reads a failure of the component check as a broken copy, line by line: a module made absent on purpose never covers another missing one.
- **The console says what it runs, beside its logo** (#206, 27/10, Jonathan's request): the tag H0 deployed, else the package's version and the short commit (« v0.3.0 · 79f220f »); on hover, the full commit. `deploy.sh` passes the reference it builds from to the image (`EREGION_REF`, `EREGION_REVISION`, beside the labels `--show` reads); a local console reads its checkout's commit. In `/api/sections`. Mutants M566, M567.
